This is Home 2013's Eq. 19, whose √(ħ/(2m_jω_α)) prefactor is correct only with orthonormal mass-weighted eigenvectors, which Run 4 verified together with the eigenproblem (Home Eqs. 7-9) and the quantization (Eqs. 10-13), with one misprint to override: Home's Eq. 13 writes the mass-weighted momentum as i(ħ/(√2σ′_α))(a_α − a_α†), which gives [ζ̂′, p̂′] = −iħ against his own Eq. 12; the sign-corrected (a_α† − a_α) is implemented **[verified]**, **[corrected]**. Home's other printed slips, each confirmed by the run: Eq. 4 carries a cos(Ω_rf t) that his Eq. 1 already supplies (the drive counted twice); Eq. 14 drops the 1/√2 of Eq. 12, so his σ′_α = √(ħ/ω_α) is √2 times the rms zero-point amplitude; Eq. 21 writes the interaction-picture phases in the complex-conjugate convention, so mixing it with a standard frame swaps the red and blue sidebands; Eq. 24's carrier prefactor is Ω, not Ω/2, and its validity condition is η²(2n + 1) ≪ 1; Eq. 27 prints √m_i inside a sum over j; Eq. 28 indexes η by the wrong ion; Eq. 29's second term is an energy that needs division by ħω_α; his "50× weaker" 24Mg+ sideband coupling is 91× on the 12.11 MHz x mode once √(m_Mg/m_Be) is included, 8300× in rate; and his Table I caption transposes the Mg x and y frequencies (below) **[corrected]**. Every eigenvector in every source (Kielpinski's v, Morigi and Walther's β′, Wübbena's b, Home's e′, the columns of Landa's U) is an eigenvector of the mass-weighted Hessian, orthonormal in u_i = √m_i z_i and never in raw displacements, the one exception being Morigi and Walther's oblique Eq. 9 (convert with β′_i = √(m_i/m) q_i); renormalizing in raw displacements is a 20 to 160% error that vanishes identically at μ = 1, so no equal-mass test catches it (b₁² = 0.40641 instead of 0.31608 at μ = 40/27, the module's negative control). Mode order is not stable in μ (Kielpinski's three-ion spectrum crosses exactly at μ = 17/3) and "in-phase is lowest" holds axially but not radially, where the in-phase mode is the upper one (Wübbena Eqs. 15-16: ω_i² − ω_o² = a/μ² > 0, with the discriminant identity a² = Y² + μ³), so the module sorts by value and identifies modes by eigenvector structure; the overall sign of an eigenvector is a gauge fixed by the rule of Section 13 and the relative signs within a mode are physical, so published η tables (Morigi and Walther's are −1 times their printed Eq. 9; Home's Table II uses the opposite choice of which ion sits at +z) are compared up to a per-mode sign and a global relabelling. Equilibria are mass independent only for equal charges, and the solver must handle multiple minima and off-axis equilibria under a stray field (Home's Table II case sits 399 and 1583 nm off axis, and an on-axis start converges to a saddle), so it minimizes from a perturbed start and checks the Hessian's positivity. Kielpinski's and Wübbena's transverse closed forms assume equal dc curvatures in x and y (their α_dc = ½), an explicit parameter in the module because every transverse anchor moves with it (Kielpinski's 27.6 and 14.7 MHz become 30.2 and 16.3 at α_dc = 1), and ω_r0 there is the bare pseudopotential frequency, the observed ω_x = ω_z√(ε² − ½) being 26.7 MHz for ω_r0 = 27.6 MHz. Mass weighting is the primary but not the only difference from the equal-mass treatment: the per-ion pseudopotential term is itself mass dependent (Φ_pond ∝ 1/m), which is what makes the two species' radial modes nearly decouple. The two-ion closed form is the unit test: for masses m₁, m₂ with μ = m₂/m₁ and ω_z1 the axial frequency of ion 1 alone, the axial modes are ω±² = ω_z1²[1 + 1/μ ± (1 − 1/μ + 1/μ²)^{1/2}], which the mass-weighted Hessian reproduces to machine precision (0.500847 and 2.250636 ω_z1² for 9Be+-24Mg+ with the isotope masses 9.0121822 u and 23.985042 u, μ = 2.66140, satisfying ω₊² + ω₋² = 2(1 + 1/μ) = 2.751484; the natural-abundance mass 24.305 u would give 0.495185 and 2.246406, a 1.1% difference that a fixture must not hide; and 1 and 3 for equal masses), with the eigenvectors orthonormal in the mass-weighted metric, Σ_k m_i (c_i^{(k)}/√m_i)² = 1. The transverse modes follow from the same construction with the species-dependent single-ion transverse frequencies (the rf part of ω² scales as 1/m², the static part as 1/m; Home 2013 Eq. 6), and the participation of the coolant species in each mode is what sets the sympathetic cooling rate of that mode: in the Lamb-Dicke, low-saturation limit the one-mode rate equation of Section 4.2.2 is unchanged except for an overall factor W_k = Σ_{j∈cooled} η_{j,k}², so participation changes the rate and not the steady state provided W_k ≠ 0, a mode dark to the cooling beam being not cooled at all (Morigi and Walther 2001 Eqs. 15-17) **[verified]**; their closed form W_k^max = ω_R/Ω_k for all ions driven holds only for a single-species chain, and a mixed chain must evaluate the sum (their own Mg/In pair gives W₋^max = 0.193 where the closed form gives 0.72 with the Mg mass and 0.155 with the In mass) **[corrected]**. Three consequences the sources establish and the module enforces **[verified]**, **[corrected]**: a mixed crystal has no exact centre-of-mass mode, because the uniform vector solves the eigenproblem only for equal masses, so every mode with a non-vanishing mass-weighted sum Σ_j c_j^{(k)}/√m_j couples to uniform electric-field noise and heats at ṅ_k = e²S_E(ω_k)/(4ħω_k) × (Σ_j c_j^{(k)}/√m_j)² in the unit-mass normalization of Kielpinski et al. 2000 Eq. 20; in a mixed pair both modes couple, with a strength set by μ, while in a reflection-symmetric mass array every mode has a definite inversion parity and exactly the even-parity modes (p = +1 in Morigi and Walther's convention, whose displacement patterns are antisymmetric about the centre with the central ion at rest, (N − 1)/2 of them for odd N) are exempt; the in-phase, centre-of-mass-like lowest mode is odd under that parity, so reading 'even' as 'in-phase' inverts the rule, which the first draft of this paragraph did; those same even-parity modes are the noise-decoupled bus candidates and the modes a centre-ion coolant cannot cool at all, W_k = 0 (Section 4.1.5's uniform-noise limit is therefore the equal-mass special case, and the first version of this plan's "every mode" was the two-ion statement over-generalized) **[corrected]**; the heavier ion dominates the lowest axial mode (in-phase) and the lowest radial mode (out-of-phase), and for mass ratios below 0.25 or above 4 the radial modes decouple into nearly single-species motion for typical traps (Wübbena et al. 2012), which by itself does not raise the Doppler limit, since a mode with a small coolant amplitude has its photon cooling and heating reduced by the same factor and only reaches the steady state more slowly; it is the external heating S_E, with no compensating cooling term, that leaves a poorly participating mode hot, and Wübbena attributes the radial failure of sympathetic cooling to strong radial confinement (large ε = ω_p/ω_z, quoted for the cooling ion, ε_clock = ε/√μ) rather than to the mass-ratio threshold as such; and in a mixed radial mode the static linearized Coulomb spring adds to the dc quadrupole and rescales the Mathieu a parameter by 1 + (1 − β)/(2α), β the amplitude ratio of the two ions, which shifts the mode frequency (Wübbena Eqs. B5-B9), while keeping the rf drive rather than the pseudopotential gives the mean-square velocity an intrinsic-micromotion term of the same order as the secular one (Eq. B10); nothing in that derivation modulates the Coulomb term at Ω_T, so it is distinct from the rf-modulated coupling of the Floquet correction below, which needs ions displaced from the rf null (the first version of this plan conflated the two); Wübbena's Eq. B7 is also written with +2q cos, so its micromotion phase is inverted relative to the plan's Mathieu sign, and his Sec. II decoupling window (μ < 0.25 or μ > 4) is an ε-dependent rule of thumb, since b₁² = 0.0035 at (μ, ε) = (0.675, 3) is full decoupling inside the supposedly coupled window while μ = 4 at ε = 1.05 gives b₁² = 0.75.

Fixtures the module reproduces **[recomputed here]**: Home 2013's Be+/Mg+ two-ion crystal, single-ion frequencies 2π × (12.26, 11.19, 2.69) MHz for 9Be+ and 2π × (4.82, 3.72, 1.65) MHz for 24Mg+, gives modes at 12.11 and 4.67 MHz (x), 11.03 and 3.53 MHz (y) and 4.04 and 1.90 MHz (z) with the two axial eigenvectors (∓0.926, 0.379) and (0.379, 0.926) and radial modes that are single-species to 2%, the off-species amplitudes being 0.018 in x and 0.020 in y as printed; Home's Table I caption gives the Mg frequencies as (3.72, 4.82, 1.65) MHz, a transposition of x and y that the table body contradicts (the x-y symmetric pseudopotential makes ω_x² − ω_y² carry the same sign for both species, and the printed order gives off-species amplitudes 0.017 and 0.022 instead; `check_home_table.py`) **[corrected]**; Kielpinski's three-ion closed forms with the impurity at the centre, ζ_{1,3} = [13/10 + (21 ∓ √(441 − 34μ + 169μ²))/(10μ)]^{1/2} and ζ₂ = √3, agree with the numerical eigenproblem to 10⁻⁶ at μ = 1, 24/9 and 9/24 (0.7811, 1.7321, 1.8881 at μ = 24/9); Wübbena's axial forms are the two-ion case above, and Morigi and Walther's 25Mg+-115In+ anchors (Ω₊/Ω₋ = 2.638 at μ = 4.6, η_Mg = 0.22 and 0.5, η_In = 0.38 and −0.06) are the test of the per-species η.

**The quantized multi-ion time-dependent frame** (Landa, Drewsen, Reznik, Retzker, J. Phys. A 45, 455305 (2012); Kaufmann et al. 2012) **[verified]**, **[corrected]**. The coupled Mathieu system ü + [A − 2Q cos 2t]u = 0 (A and Q the matrices of Section 4.1.6 in dimensionless time) is solved for each stable mode by a Fourier-Floquet series u = Σ_n C_{2n}[b e^{i(2n+β)t} + c e^{−i(2n+β)t}], whose coefficient vectors obey the three-term matrix recursion Q C_{2n−2} = [A − (2n + β)²] C_{2n} − Q C_{2n+2}, folded into two infinite continued matrix inversions whose consistency condition fixes β (the multi-mode generalization of Section 4.1.1's scalar recursion); the coefficient vectors then follow C₀ by continued inversions in both directions, C_{2n} = T_{2n}QC_{2n−2} upward and C_{−2n} = T̃_{−2n}QC_{−2n+2} downward with T̃_{−2n} = [R_{−2n} − QT̃_{−2n−2}Q]⁻¹, where the paper's text after its Eq. 25 writes the first downward step with an undefined inverted operator, a typo an implementer must not copy **[corrected]**. The π-periodic matrices U(t) (columns Σ_n C_{2n,β_j} e^{2int}) and V(t) (the same with factors i(2n + β_j)) form the Floquet-Lyapunov transformation φ = Γ(t)χ, which is canonical only under the normalization Vᵗ(0)U(0) = i/2 (the identity the Run 3 verifier flagged); with it the inverse of Γ is given by transposes and conjugates, the transformed Hamiltonian is a sum of decoupled constant-frequency oscillators Σ_j β_j ξ_j ζ_j plus a c-number ordering term, and the quantized ladder operators evolve freely, ζ̂_j(t) = ζ̂_j(0)e^{−iβ_j t}, with exact oscillator commutators; the Floquet number states are closed-form multidimensional Hermite functions of U and V, and the single-ion reduction reproduces Leibfried et al. 2003's Eq. 36 (the RMP, which Landa's citation lists as a Wineland review) with t → Ωt/2 and ν → νΩ/2, the RMP's printed form itself carrying a sign misprint in its x′² coefficient. U(t) is diagonal, and the frame reduces to constant normal modes with factorized wavefunctions, only when a constant matrix diagonalizes the equations of motion, for instance when A and Q commute; otherwise the modes are irreducibly time dependent, and Γ⁻¹(0) is not unitary in general (under Landa's own normalization it is non-unitary for every admissible β ≠ 1, including the decoupled case, so unitarity is never a sanity check; the closed form Γ⁻¹ = [[iV†, −iU†], [−iVᵗ, iUᵗ]] is) **[verified]**. Landa's Eq. 1 has no mass matrix and requires symmetric A and Q, so a crystal enters in mass-weighted coordinates, A = (2/Ω)²M^{−1/2}K_dc M^{−1/2} and Q = −½(2/Ω)²M^{−1/2}K_rf M^{−1/2} with the Coulomb curvature in K_dc, the construction of Landa et al.'s companion paper (New J. Phys. 14, 093023, read in Run 2) that Run 4's verifiers re-derived independently and validated at f = 1 and f = 2 with unit masses: for a = 0.10, q = 0.20 the continued inversions, accumulated inward from the truncation order, give β = 0.350217935570 against the monodromy to 10⁻¹⁵, C₂/C₀ = −0.03689084456182 and C₋₂/C₀ = −0.07637216741375, where the paper-literal inverted step gives −0.52375, 6.9× wrong; for f = 2 with A = [[0.30, 0.07], [0.07, 0.22]] and Q = [[0.25, 0.05], [0.05, −0.18]] the roots 0.45236768 and 0.62270851 match the monodromy to eight digits, and the sign changes at 1.40837 and 1.56830 are poles of det Y that a determinant-based root finder must exclude, which is why the module roots on the smallest singular value of Y and de-duplicates {β, 2 − β}; the principal branch β ∈ (−1, 1] is stated in code because the exponent aliases near β = 1, the Fourier truncation test is ‖U(π) − U(0)‖, Landa's Eq. 48 prints H̃ where KB is meant, and the C_{2n} are not orthogonal and must not be orthonormalized **[verified]**, **[corrected]**. No source treats a mixed-species crystal in the Floquet frame, so the per-species Floquet η_{j,α}(t) built from U(t) is a derivation validated against the static limit only, and the time-periodic Coulomb Hessian along the micromotion trajectory that Kaufmann's mechanism needs is printed nowhere; the module builds it from the closed-form Coulomb Hessian evaluated along r̄_n[1 − (q^{(n)}/2)cos Ωt] with per-ion q^{(n)} ∝ 1/m_n, K(t) being periodic through both the explicit drive and the moving evaluation point, and treats Kaufmann's one crystal as its only measured anchor **[background]**. The measured anchor: a three-ion 40Ca+ zigzag crystal at q ≈ 0.2, anisotropy α ≈ 0.53 and Ω/2π = 35.07 MHz has zigzag modes at 714(2) and 1078(2) kHz, where the static-Hessian pseudopotential prediction seeded from the measured centre-of-mass frequencies (1238(2) and 1695(3) kHz) gives 730(14) and 1041(12) kHz and the Floquet-Lyapunov treatment gives 715.1 and 1078.5 kHz, a 2 to 3% shift at q ≈ 0.2 that grows to a calculated 20% at high q, while the centre-of-mass modes are decoupled from the Coulomb coupling, obey the single-ion Mathieu equation exactly and are therefore unshifted by this crystal correction (their pseudopotential values β ≈ √(a + q²/2) still differ from the exact Mathieu exponent at O(q⁴), by 5.4 × 10⁻³ in y and 9.1 × 10⁻³ in x at Kaufmann's q = 0.206, 9 and 27 kHz, which is Section 4.1.1's monodromy correction and not this one **[corrected]**); the mechanism is that each ion's micromotion amplitude is −(q/2) times its mean radial displacement to O(q²/4), so the Coulomb coupling is rf-modulated wherever ions sit off the rf null. For a linear chain on the rf null with no stray field that displacement vanishes to lowest order, so the pseudopotential modes of Section 4.1.3 are exact at that order and the correction applies to zigzag and planar crystals, to chains displaced by stray fields (Section 4.1.1's excess micromotion) and to mixed radial modes (above). The simulator therefore keeps the pseudopotential frame as its default for linear chains, implements the Floquet-Lyapunov modes as the validation option of Section 4.1.1 with the Kaufmann table as its acceptance test, and reports the pseudopotential error estimate (q/2 times the configured off-null displacement, in units of the mode spacing) with every device.


### 4.2 Laser cooling and state preparation

The preparation module produces the initial motional and internal states of a run. It offers three levels of description for every cooling stage, all sharing one parameter set: (A) closed-form rate equations that return a steady-state mean phonon number and cooling rate per mode, used by default because they are what the sources validate against, each mode's rate carrying the participation weight W_k = Σ_{j illuminated} η_{j,k}² of Section 4.1.7 and the beam projection cos²θ_L of Section 4.2.8 rather than a single-ion value, and raising rather than returning a steady state when a mode's projection on every cooling beam is below threshold, because such a mode is not cooled at all (added after the 2026-09-04 experimentalist critique); (B) the Lamb-Dicke birth-death rate equation on Fock populations, used when the time dependence or non-thermal distributions matter; (C) the full master equation of the internal levels plus one mode, solved with `mesolve` or `mcsolve`, used to validate (A) and (B) and to reproduce the multi-level effects the closed forms miss. The output of every stage is a per-mode thermal density matrix (or the Fock distribution from level B) and an internal state with a preparation error, handed to the next stage.

#### 4.2.1 Doppler cooling

Semiclassical picture (RMP 2003 Eqs. 96-107) **[verified]**: an ion in the pseudopotential ½mν²x² with velocity v(t) = v₀cos(νt) feels the average radiation force F_a = ħkΓρ_ee with the saturated Lorentzian excited-state population ρ_ee = (s/2)/[1 + s + (2δ_eff/Γ)²], δ_eff = Δ − k·v. Linearizing in v gives F_a ≈ F₀(1 + κv): a constant push F₀ = ħkΓ(s/2)/[1 + s + (2Δ/Γ)²] that displaces the ion, and a friction term that cools only for Δ < 0. The balance of friction against recoil heating gives the Doppler limit, minimized at Δ = −(Γ/2)√(1 + s) **[verified]** (in the review's Eq. 105 the factor (1 + s) sits inside the bracket multiplying only Γ/(2Δ), and in Eq. 106 the radical covers 1 + s alone; both were verified symbol by symbol). The RMP's Eq. 106 with its own angular factor ξ = 2/5 (the recoil-pattern average of Section 4.2.8) gives k_B T_min = 0.35ħΓ, a factor 1.43 below the textbook ħΓ/2 that Berkeland uses; at Γ/2π = 20 MHz that is 0.336 mK against 0.480 mK, and the Bose occupation of a 1 MHz mode 6.5 against 9.5, so the two limits are not interchangeable and neither closed form is used as the Doppler stage **[corrected]**. The force model itself is valid only for ν ≪ Γ, because it linearizes in the velocity and treats the Doppler shift as quasi-static; the 9Be+ anchor below sits at ν/Γ = 0.58, 0.94 and 1.54, in the resolved-sideband regime where the floor scales as (Γ/2ν)² rather than Γ/2ν and the optimum detuning moves from −Γ/2 toward −ν, which is why Monroe's best detuning was −30 MHz, 1.55Γ, and the measured n̄ lie near half the unresolved-limit values. Level A's Doppler stage therefore uses the rate framework of Section 4.2.2, A_± = W(Δ ∓ ν) + (η̃²/η²)W(Δ) with W from the multi-level Bloch steady state, valid at any ν/Γ, and optimizes Δ numerically, once per beam over the whole mode set: a Doppler beam has one detuning, one saturation parameter and one direction for all 3N modes, so Δ, s and k̂ are beam parameters chosen to minimize the participation-weighted mean of the per-mode steady-state n̄ (weights b_{i,m}² of the gate modes the schedule uses next, uniform when none is scheduled), and the per-mode residual n̄ is reported, never optimized mode by mode, since a per-mode optimum is a state no single beam can prepare (the optimum Δ for a 0.5 MHz axial and a 4 MHz radial mode differ by more than Γ); the acceptance test is Monroe's three modes from his single Δ = −30 MHz beam, whose three measured n̄ one shared detuning has to reproduce together (2026-09-04 experimentalist critique) **[corrected: critique, 2026-09-04]**; the force model is the ν ≪ Γ cross-check, invoked only when ν/Γ < 0.1 (the first version of this plan made the force model the default; caught by the 2026-09-04 experimentalist critique) **[corrected]**. The textbook limit k_B T_D = ħΓ/2 at low saturation and the corresponding n̄ ≈ Γ/(2ν) up to angular factors are **[background]**; valid in the unresolved-sideband regime ν ≪ Γ **[verified]**.

Anchors the simulator must reproduce: NIST 9Be+ (Γ/2π = 19.4 MHz, best detuning about −30 MHz) reached n̄ = (0.47, 0.30, 0.18) on modes at (11.2, 18.2, 29.8) MHz, consistent with the theoretical 0.484 for x **[verified]** (Monroe et al. 1995). For 40Ca+ on the 397 nm line at −20 MHz with an 866 nm repumper, the two-level estimate (0.5 mK, n̄_z ≈ 3, n̄_x,y ≈ 6) fails: the measured starting points were n̄_z = 6.5(1.0) and n̄_y = 16(2), because the multi-level structure invalidates the two-level assumption **[verified]** (Roos et al. 2000). For 171Yb+ at 0.5-0.8 MHz trap frequencies Doppler cooling leaves n̄ ≈ 20 **[verified]** (Ejtemaee and Haljan 2017). Level A therefore uses the two-level closed form only for near-two-level species and otherwise takes n̄_D from a level-C solve of the actual level scheme (S₁/₂, P₁/₂, D₃/₂ with repumper for Ca+; the F = 0, 1 hyperfine manifolds for Yb+), which is the reason the species modules carry full branching ratios.

#### 4.2.2 Resolved-sideband cooling

**Single-photon scheme.** With the cooling laser on the first red sideband, δ = −ν, each cycle removes ħν and the ground state is dark. Rate-equation treatment (RMP 2003 Eqs. 110-112, 116) **[verified]**: the rate out of |n⟩ is R_n = Γ̃ P_e(n) = Γ̃ (η√n Ω)²/[2(η√n Ω)² + Γ̃²], with Γ̃ the effective linewidth (possibly repumper-broadened); the ground-state population obeys

ṗ₀ = p₁ (ηΩ)²/Γ̃ − p₀ [ (Ω/2ν)² η̃² Γ̃ + (ηΩ/4ν)² Γ̃ ],

where the two loss terms are off-resonant carrier excitation followed by a sideband decay (η̃ is the Lamb-Dicke parameter of the spontaneously emitted photon, η̃² = α(k_em x₀)² with the angular factor α of Section 4.2.8 and k_em the emitted photon's wavenumber, which is the drive's only for a closed two-level cycle: in a quenched or Raman scheme the recoil photon is the fast level's decay, 393 nm for 40Ca+ cooled on the 729 nm quadrupole line and quenched at 854 nm, so (η̃/η)² = α(k_em/k_L)²/cos²θ_L = 0.4 × 1.85² = 1.37 there and the bracket below is 1.62, not 0.65 **[verified]**) and off-resonant blue-sideband excitation. The steady-state limit is ⟨n⟩_min ≈ (Γ̃/2ν)²[(η̃/η)² + 1/4] in the review's form and ⟨n⟩_min ≈ (γ/2ω_z)² ≪ 1 in Wineland 1998 **[verified]**, valid for γ ≪ ω_z, ω_z ≫ ω_R, with the recoil condition ω_R/ω_z = η² **[verified]**. Two printed errors in the review must not be copied: Eq. 109 as printed is non-Hermitian (i η(σ₊a + σ₋a†) must read i η(σ₊a − σ₋a†)) and would give a growing norm in any solver **[corrected]**; Eq. 113 lacks a 1/(n̄+1) normalization and a factor 1/2 in its sine arguments **[corrected]**. The general Lamb-Dicke framework for any scheme with known scattering rate W(Δ) at rest **[verified]** (RMP Eqs. 117-121):

R_{n→n+1} = (n+1) η² A₊,  R_{n→n−1} = n η² A₋,  A_± = W(Δ∓ν) + (η̃²/η²) W(Δ),  η̃²/η² = α k_em²/(Δk·ê_m)²,

with the intermediate state |e, n±1⟩ paired with detuning Δ∓ν (anti-correlated) **[corrected]**; this is level B's engine, with W(Δ) = Γρ_ee(Δ) from the Bloch steady state, α the recoil angular factor of Section 4.2.8 and k_em the emitted photon's wavenumber, so that the carrier term carries the emitted photon's Lamb-Dicke parameter η̃ while the sideband terms carry the drive's η, the two coinciding only for one resonant beam along the mode axis, where the weight reduces to α (the participation b_{i,m} multiplies both η̃ and η and cancels from the ratio, so the sideband-cooling floor of a given mode is independent of N, which is the regression; the second revision carried b_{i,m} in the denominator, inflating the floor by 1/b² for every multi-ion mode while every single-ion test passed at b = 1; 2026-09-04 experimentalist critique) **[corrected: critique, 2026-09-04]** and the expression to Stenholm's A_± = P(Δ ∓ ν) + αP(Δ) (Stenholm 1986 Eqs. 5.49-5.54); the first two revisions of this plan wrote the weight as α in general while computing (η̃/η)² = 1.376 for quenched 40Ca+ a few lines above, an inconsistency the derivation audit of 2026-09-04 caught **[corrected: derivation audit, 2026-09-04]**. Two conventions are fixed here, after a correction found by the 2026-09-04 critique: A_± are the bare coefficients with units of rate and the Fock rate equation carries η², where the RMP's Eqs. 117-119 place η² inside A_± and the EIT sources (Morigi, Roos, Lechner) outside; and the carrier term W(Δ) is kept, weighted by η̃²/η², because it is the recoil heating that accompanies carrier scattering and produces the (η̃/η)² piece of the floor above (equal to α only in the single-beam, on-axis case). The EIT papers omit it because at the dark resonance W(Δ) = 0, a property of that scheme and not a convention. The carrier term cancels in the cooling rate A₋ − A₊ = W(Δ+ν) − W(Δ−ν) but not in the steady state ⟨n⟩ = A₊/(A₋ − A₊) = [W(Δ−ν) + (η̃²/η²)W(Δ)]/[W(Δ+ν) − W(Δ−ν)], the opposite of what the first version of this plan stated **[corrected]**. W(Δ) is the same object as the readout scattering rate R_∘ of Section 8.1 and comes from one shared multi-level optical-Bloch steady-state solver, because the two-level Lorentzian saturating at Γ/2 over-predicts the 171Yb+ detection-beam rate by 2× against the three-ground-plus-one-excited ceiling of Γ/4 **[corrected]**.

**Stimulated-Raman scheme for hyperfine qubits** (Monroe et al. 1995; Wineland 1998) **[verified]**. Two beams detuned Δ from the P level with difference frequency ω₀ − ω_ν drive |↓,n⟩ → |↑,n−1⟩ with two-photon Rabi frequency Ω = g₁g₂/Δ, g₁ and g₂ the single-photon couplings in the half convention g = Ω_single/2 (in Monroe's convention P = sin²(Ωτ), so a π pulse is Ωτ = π/2, half the usual sin²(Ωt/2) convention, and in plan units the same quantity is Ω₁Ω₂/(2Δ), Section 13's two-photon row and its g-symbol row **[verified flag]**); a resonant repump |↑⟩ → |3⟩ ⇝ |↓⟩ returns the spin with Δn ≈ 0 when ω_R ≪ ω_z. The 9Be+ sequence: about 50 µs Doppler precooling with beams D1, D2 and D3 on, about 7 µs of optical pumping into |2,2⟩, then repeated cycles of a 1-3 µs Raman red-sideband pulse and about 7 µs of repumping, the whole sequence at about 4 kHz **[corrected]** (the extracted prose had dropped D3). Five cycles per axis in xyz order gave n̄ = (0.033, 0.022, 0.029), about 92% three-dimensional ground-state occupation; five cycles on x alone gave 0.014(10) **[verified]**. Two intrinsic floors are order-of-magnitude scalings, off-resonant stimulated Raman heating ⟨n⟩ ≈ (g₁g₂)²/(ω_νΔ)² and spontaneous Raman scattering, both about 10⁻³ in that experiment, and the ambient heating was about 1 quantum/ms **[verified]**. Lamb-Dicke parameters η_ν = δk_ν r_ν with r_ν = √(ħ/2mω_ν) were (0.21, 0.12, 0.09), and the geometry that reproduces them is not counter-propagating: at 313 nm and 11.2 MHz, r_x = 7.08 nm, so δk = 2k gives 0.284 while a 90° crossing with δk = √2 k along x gives 0.2008, the quoted 0.21, and the y and z values follow to 8% and 3% from a projection k on each axis, a second 90° pair whose δk bisects y and z (`check_critique_v3.py`; the second revision's '2k for counter-propagating beams' does not reproduce the anchor and left the test unwritable, so the fixture records |Δk| = 2k sin(θ/2) and its direction cosines per axis; 2026-09-04 experimentalist critique) **[corrected: critique, 2026-09-04]**, **[recomputed here]** **[verified]**. A failure mode the simulator must reproduce: because the red-sideband Rabi frequency Ω_{n−1,n} depends on n, a fixed pulse area traps population in any |↓,n⟩ for which Ω_{n−1,n}t = 2mπ in this plan's Rabi convention (transfer probability sin²(Ω_{n−1,n}t/2)), which reads Ω_{n−1,n}t = mπ in Wineland's and Monroe's half-Rabi convention; laboratory sequences vary the pulse duration between cycles for this reason **[verified]**, and a missed conversion places the stranded population at a different n **[corrected]**. Cooling of higher sidebands first, as in the Home 2009 sequence (Doppler to n̄ ≈ 15, then second-sideband cycles, then first-sideband cycles, reaching about 0.94 ground-state fidelity and ⟨n⟩ ≈ 0.06 per mode in a four-ion mixed crystal) **[verified]**, is supported as a schedule option.

#### 4.2.3 EIT cooling

In a Λ system a strong blue-detuned coupling beam (Rabi frequency Ω_r, detuning Δ_r > 0) dresses the excited state and produces a Fano absorption profile for a weak cooling beam (Ω_g ≪ Ω_r): an exact zero at the two-photon resonance Δ_g = Δ_r that cancels carrier scattering, and a narrow bright resonance displaced by the light shift δ = (√(Δ_r² + Ω_r²) − |Δ_r|)/2 ≈ Ω_r²/(4Δ_r) **[verified]** (Morigi, Eschner, Keitel 2000, Eq. 1; the narrow dressed state shifts by +δ and the broad one by −Δ_r − δ **[corrected]**, and δ is the light shift of the ground-like dressed state **[corrected]**). The tuning rule is Δ_g = Δ_r and δ ≃ ν, which puts the red sideband on the narrow peak **[verified]**. With Δ_r = Δ_g = Δ the rate coefficients are

A_± = (Ω_g²/γ) · γ²ν² / { γ²ν² + 4[Ω_r²/4 − ν(ν ∓ Δ)]² },

the upper sign (heating) pairing with ν − Δ and the lower (cooling) with ν + Δ **[verified]**, and the Fock populations obey Ṗ(n) = η²[A₋((n+1)P(n+1) − nP(n)) + A₊(nP(n−1) − (n+1)P(n))] with η defined against the two-photon wavevector difference **[verified]**. The mean phonon number relaxes exponentially at rate η²(A₋ − A₊) to

⟨n⟩_S = A₊/(A₋ − A₊) = [γ²ν² + 4(Ω_r²/4 − ν(ν+Δ))²] / [4Δν(Ω_r² − 4ν²)],

which equals (γ/4Δ)² exactly at δ = ν **[verified]** (δ is not a free parameter once Ω_r and Δ are chosen; the calibration direction is Δ and target ν → Ω_r = 2√(ν(ν + |Δ|)) **[corrected]**), has poles at Δ = 0 and Ω_r = 2ν where cooling vanishes, and requires Δ > 0 **[verified]**; the RMP's (Γ/4Δ_r)² is approximate, the exact value at the RMP's own approximate tuning Ω_r² = 4νΔ_r being (Γ² + 4ν²)/(16Δ_r(Δ_r − ν)) **[corrected; the earlier flag printed (Δ_r + ν), a sign error caught by the 2026-09-04 critique: at γ = 1, ν = 0.3, Δ = 5 the formula gives 0.00361702, the (Δ − ν) form 0.00361702 and the (Δ + ν) form 0.00320755]**. The bandwidth advantage comes from the dressed-state linewidth Γ′ ≃ Γ(2δ/Ω_σ)² ≃ Γν/Δ_σ **[verified]**, wide enough to cool modes 1.7 MHz apart at one setting. Validation anchors: Roos et al. 2000 on 40Ca+ with Δ_σ = Δ_π = 2π × 70 MHz, Ω_σ = 2π × 21.4 MHz, Ω_π = 2π × 3 MHz, δ/2π = 1.6 MHz, reaching n̄_y = 0.18 and, at δ/2π = 3.3 MHz, n̄_z = 0.1 in about 250 µs, insensitive to the π-beam intensity only while I_π ≪ I_σ (their ratio about 100) **[corrected]**; Lechner et al. 2016 with Ω_σ = 2π × 30 MHz, Ω_π = 2π × 6.2 MHz, light shift 2.2-2.3 MHz, cooling an 18-ion 40Ca+ string's radial modes to n̄ ≈ 0.01-0.02 at R = (19 ± 3) × 10³ s⁻¹, with a fitted heating term of (65 ± 7) s⁻¹ on a nine-ion COM mode and per-mode rates from 5 to 17 × 10³ s⁻¹ across 1.13-3.84 MHz in a two-ion crystal **[verified]**. Level C for EIT is a four-level (Zeeman-resolved) master equation, not the idealized three-level one, because the experiments use the S₁/₂ and P₁/₂ Zeeman manifolds **[corrected]**.

#### 4.2.4 Polarization-gradient (Sisyphus) cooling

Two counter-propagating, cross-polarized beams blue of the 171Yb+ S₁/₂(F=1) → P₁/₂(F=0) line (Δ/2π = +310 MHz, s₀ = I/(51 mW/cm²)) in a 5.9 G transverse field cool all three axes from one beam pair because each beam projects onto the axes with factors {1/2, 1/2, 1/√2}, giving η = (0.052, 0.053, 0.090) at (0.790, 0.766, 0.525) MHz **[verified]** (Ejtemaee and Haljan 2017). In the Lamb-Dicke regime without Zeeman coherences the cooling rate scales as s₀² (measured exponents 1.98(6) axial, 1.91(3) transverse, over about a factor 7 in intensity) **[corrected]**; single-ion steady states reach n̄ ≈ 1.5-2 only at the optimum intensity and rise on both sides, all twelve modes of a four-ion string reached n̄ ≤ 3, and the cooling must be pulsed with an optical-pumping-plus-microwave reset because 15-20% of the time the ion is pumped into the dark |0,0⟩ state **[verified]**. The paper's quantum model (1D Monte Carlo wavefunction with hyperfine structure and Zeeman coherences, 20 or 200 Fock levels, 40-80 trajectories) is the template for level C here, including its documented truncation sensitivity **[corrected]**. Blue detuning is required for this F → F′ = F − 1 scheme with a transverse field; it is not a universal Sisyphus rule, though the paragraphs below show it is required for the same reason on any inverted j_e ≤ j_g line, where the most strongly coupled sublevel is the optical-pumping source rather than the sink **[corrected]**.

**The analytic polarization-gradient model, and why it is a second model rather than a refinement of the first** (Joshi et al. 2020, verified in Run 5) **[verified]**, **[corrected]**. For a j_g = ½ ↔ j_e = ½ (S₁/₂ ↔ P₁/₂) line driven lin-perp-lin by two counter-propagating beams **along** the quantization axis, so the ion sees only σ light of constant total intensity with a rotating polarization, the two ground Zeeman states acquire U_± = U_trap + (ħ/3)Δs ∓ (ħ/3)Δs sin(2kz + 2φ), with U_trap = ½mω_z²z² (ħ restored: the light shifts are frequencies and the trap term an energy, which the second revision's ħ = 1 form mixed; 2026-09-04 theorist critique), Δ > 0 the detuning, s the **single-beam** saturation parameter (Ω²/2)/(Γ²/4 + Δ²) referenced to the S₁/₂-P₃/₂ stretched transition, and φ the gradient phase at the trap centre. The position dependence is printed inside Eq. (1) already, so the substitution 2φ → 2kz + 2φ belongs to the **pumping rates only**; applying it to Eq. (1) too gives sin(4kz + 2φ) and halves the gradient period, destroying the Sisyphus geometry **[corrected]**. The state-independent +Δs/3 exerts no force but keeps U₊ + U₋ position-independent, and the well depth is 2Δs/3 = 2ω_zξ with ξ ≡ Δs/(3ω_z), so "depth equals the trap frequency" is ξ = ½. The pumping rates carry the same sign of the sine as the potential - fastest pumping out of a state at its own potential maximum, the sign invariant a refactor must preserve - Γ_{±→∓}(z) = (1/9)Γs(1 ∓ sin(2kz + 2φ)), whose 1/9 is (2/3) × (1/2) × (1/3): the squared Clebsch-Gordan ratio of a D1 σ transition to the D2 stretched transition, the low-saturation ρ_ee = s_eff/2, and the π branching out of |P₁/₂, ∓½⟩. Detailed balance with the z-substituted rates reproduces the source's own p_± = ½(1 ± sin(2kz + 2φ)) identically while the printed rates leave a residual, which is the internal-consistency argument for the substitution **[corrected]**. The energy rate equation Ė = Σ_± p_±⟨Γ_{±→∓}(z)(U_∓ − U_±)⟩_± + H_sc ħω_z adds recoil diffusion H_sc = H_carr + H_sb with H_carr = (α/3)η²Γs(1 − sin²2φ) and H_sb = (1/3)η²Γs(1 + sin²2φ), α = 1/3 being the **isotropic** ⟨cos²θ⟩ (a sin²θ pattern's 2/5 in its place fails internal consistency with the linearized heating rate by 5%, the discriminating test) **[recomputed here]**, and linearizing to first order in kz gives W(φ) = (16/9)η²Γs ξ cos²2φ and H(φ) = (2/9)η²Γs(8ξ²cos⁴2φ + 2 + sin²2φ). With E = ħω(⟨n⟩ + ½) the rate equation is d⟨n⟩/dt = −W(⟨n⟩ + ½) + H and the steady state H/W − ½: at fixed phase φ = 0 it is ⟨n₀⟩ = ξ + 1/(4ξ) − ½ with minimum exactly ½ at ξ = ½, and phase-averaged, ⟨n⟩ = ⟨H⟩_φ/⟨W⟩_φ − ½ = (3/4)ξ + 5/(8ξ) − ½ with minimum √(15/8) − ½ = 0.8693064 at ξ = √(5/6) = 0.9128709 **[recomputed here]**, a form valid only for a gradient moving fast against W (the W < δ < ω_z window below), where every ion sees the averaged coefficients; for a static gradient sampled by many ions at fixed distinct φ each ion has its own steady state H(φ)/W(φ) − ½, W(φ) ∝ cos²2φ vanishes at φ = π/4, so ions near a node are not cooled at all and the ensemble mean diverges rather than reading 0.87, and the module averages H/W − ½ over the ions' actual φ and raises when any |cos 2φ| falls below the cooling threshold, the uncooled-mode failure Section 4.2 promises to raise on (the second revision applied the moving-gradient average to 'many ions sampling the gradient'; 2026-09-04 theorist critique) **[corrected: critique, 2026-09-04]**. Three things not to read into those formulas: the fixed-phase limit is the φ = 0 **value**, not a minimum over phase (φ = 0 minimizes the occupation only for ξ ≲ 0.612 but always maximizes the rate); cooling requires **blue** detuning, since W ∝ ξ ∝ Δ and on an inverted j_e ≤ j_g transition the most strongly coupled sublevel is the pumping source rather than the sink, so the sign flip turns cooling into heating; and a moving gradient has no steady state, so the inter-beam frequency difference δ must satisfy W < δ < ω_z, which the source's operating point holds with about a decade of margin each side **[verified]**, **[recomputed here]**.

**The Lindblad layer and the namespacing.** The full model is a four-level atom (the S₁/₂ and P₁/₂ Zeeman manifolds, no D branch) tensored with one truncated mode: two circular standing waves offset by ∓π/4 about the common phase, so their **relative** phase is π/2, a λ/8 displacement and not π/4; a coupling amplitude √(1/3) = √(2/3) × 1/√2 that is a Clebsch-Gordan ratio times each linear beam's projection onto one circular component and **not** a bare Clebsch-Gordan factor, since √(2/3) doubles the optical potential; and kẑ = η(a + a†) kept as the full exponential when η√(⟨n⟩ + ½) is not ≪ 1 **[corrected]**. Its dissipator is the recoil-resolved kernel of Section 4.2.8 in discretized form: **twelve** collapse operators Ĵ_mq = p_mq C_m √Γ e^{−ik_q ẑ} σ_m⁻ over four decay channels m and three recoil classes k_q ∈ {−k, 0, +k}, with C_m = √(1/3) for Δm_j = 0 and √(2/3) for Δm_j = ±1, Σ_q p_mq² = 1 per channel and second moments Σ_q p_mq²(k_q/k)² = 2/5 for σ and 1/5 for π, matching α_m for the (1 + cos²θ)/2 and sin²θ patterns exactly **[recomputed here]**. The printed master equation carries three defects the module overrides: the −i/ħ is missing from the commutator, the jump operators are scaled by Γ rather than √Γ, and the three recoil classes are summed **coherently** inside one operator, which inflates the decay rate at the origin by (Σ_q p_mq)² = 2.7856 for σ and 2.3314 for π even as η → 0, corrupts the σ:π branching and creates spurious coherence between momentum classes **[recomputed here]**, **[corrected]**. Two namespacing rules follow. The two saturation parameters are different objects, stored separately: Joshi's s is per beam, already detuning-suppressed and referenced to the P₃/₂ stretched transition, while Ejtemaee's s₀ = I/(51 mW/cm²) is per beam **on resonance** in the two-level I_sat = πhcΓ/(3λ³) convention, which recomputes to 50.78 mW/cm² at 369.5 nm, so the printed 51 is that same convention to 0.4% **[recomputed here]**; the bridge s_Joshi = (3/2)s₀(Γ/2)²/((Γ/2)² + Δ²) is derived in the Run 5 consolidation and printed by neither paper, so it is tagged derived, **[background]**, and used only to check that s₀ = 11-15 at Δ/2π = 310 MHz lands inside Joshi's bound s < 0.07. And the analytic prefactors do **not** transfer: 1/3, 1/9, 16/9, 2/9 and the 8ξ² are specific to j_g = ½ ↔ j_e = ½, the sources warn they fail for j_e = 3/2, and whether they carry to the 171Yb+ F = 1 → F′ = 0 scheme is asserted by nobody, that source printing no display equations at all. The two layers are therefore separate models with no shared coefficients until a source for the second is transcribed, and a simulator must not validate one against the other's steady-state number: 0.87 quanta analytic phase-averaged, about 1 as the 171Yb+ expectation, 1.5-2 measured there, 7.7(1.1) outside the Lamb-Dicke regime in the analytic model's own experiment **[contested]**. The analytic model also drops the Zeeman term on which the 171Yb+ CPT-free window depends (g_F μ_B B/ħ at 5.9 G is an adjacent-m_F splitting of 2π × 8.27 MHz against a scattering rate of order 10⁶ s⁻¹), so merging them means adding it - and the trade-off is real, CPT-negligibility pushing to high field and low intensity while the s₀² rate law pushes to high intensity **[verified]**.

#### 4.2.5 Sympathetic cooling

A second species (or, in the omg scheme, a metastable manifold) is cooled with light the qubit species does not see; Coulomb coupling shares the energy through the normal modes. Home et al. 2009 fix the archetype: a 9Be+-24Mg+-24Mg+-9Be+ crystal whose order is preserved through separation and recombination, Doppler cooling on Mg+ to n̄ ≈ 15, then second- and first-sideband cooling on Mg+ only, reaching ⟨n⟩ ≈ 0.06 per axial mode in 5.1 ms per composite operation against 3.6 ms of transport **[verified]**. The mode structure of the mixed crystal (two highest axial modes 251 kHz apart, gate detuning 83.6 kHz, force duration 2π/δ, phase-contribution ratio 2.72) **[verified]** is the reason the crystal module works from a per-ion mass list from the start. In the first release the sympathetic stage is modelled at level A: the coolant's Doppler or sideband cooling sets each shared mode's n̄ through its participation eigenvector, and the qubit ions' internal states are untouched; the dual-species mode solver is the extension that makes level C possible.

#### 4.2.6 Optical pumping and internal-state preparation

Optical pumping drives every level except the target out of the manifold until population lands in the one dark state; it takes a few microseconds and leaves the ion in a definite internal state with a residual error set by off-resonant excitation of the target **[extracted]**. The laboratory order is Doppler cooling first, then sideband or EIT cooling with its own repump, then a final optical pump immediately before the circuit, with a re-pump after any interleaved recooling: Doppler cooling runs on a repumped cycling transition and scrambles the internal state (171Yb+ over S₁/₂ F = 1 through the 14.7 GHz sideband, 40Ca+ over both S₁/₂ Zeeman states), so a pump done first is erased, which is the order Section 2 and the 9Be+ anchor of Section 4.2.2 (50 µs Doppler precooling, then 7 µs of optical pumping, then Raman sideband cycles) already state; the second revision printed the reverse order here (pump to |↓⟩, cool to about 1 mK leaving ⟨n⟩ ≥ 1, sideband cool), which an implementer following it would have turned into a scrambled initial state and a preparation error orders of magnitude too large, so the stage order is a scheduler constraint (Section 7.3) and not prose (2026-09-04 experimentalist critique) **[corrected: critique, 2026-09-04]** (Wineland 1998 prints ⟨n⟩ ≥ 1 after Doppler cooling, not ≳). The simulator models preparation at level C by the multi-level master equation of Section 4.2.8 on the internal manifold, with the pumping beam's polarization-resolved Rabi frequencies and the branching ratios of Section 4.5.2 and the recoil operators for the emitted photons, from which the residual population in non-target states (the SPAM preparation error of Section 8), the pumping time and the motional heating during pumping follow. Species anchors: 171Yb+ pumps to |0⟩ = |F=0, m=0⟩ with a 2.1 GHz sideband on S₁/₂|F=1⟩ → P₁/₂|F=1⟩ (1/3 branching into |0⟩ per excitation), with a 3.07 GHz sideband on the 935 nm repump essential **[extracted]**; 9Be+ pumps into |2,2⟩ in about 7 µs **[verified]**; 43Ca+ prepares the stretch state with error below 10⁻⁴ **[extracted]**.

#### 4.2.7 Thermometry and hand-off

After every stage the simulator can run the same diagnostics the laboratory runs and compare them with the state it actually prepared: (i) sideband-asymmetry thermometry, P_e^{rsb}/P_e^{bsb} = [n̄/(n̄ + 1)]^k for k-th-order sidebands, exact in η, in pulse duration and for any coupling matrix element because the same Ω_{m+k,m} appears on both sides (Turchette 2000 Eqs. 8-11; verified to 6 × 10⁻¹⁵ at η = 1.5, n̄ = 8), inverted as n̄ = R_k^{1/k}/(1 − R_k^{1/k}); the constancy of the ratio under varying pulse time is itself the test of thermality, since constancy forces a geometric distribution **[verified]**; (ii) blue-sideband Rabi flopping, P_↓(τ) = ½(1 + Σ_n P_n e^{−γ_n τ} cos(2Ω_{n+1,n}τ)), inverted by cosine transform for the Fock distribution P_n **[verified]** (Wineland 1998 Eq. 42, with Ω defined by ħΩ = −μB₁/4 so that the flopping frequency is 2Ω); and (iii) the thermal Debye-Waller reduction of a carrier Rabi frequency by spectator modes, Ω̄ = Ω^{(0)} Π_p Σ_{n_p} P_{n_p} e^{−η_p²/2} L_{n_p}(η_p²) ≈ Ω^{(0)} Π_p exp[−η_p²(n̄_p + 1/2)] with P_n = n̄^n/(1 + n̄)^{n+1} **[verified]** (Wineland 1998 Eqs. 122-124), which is what the carrier-Rabi calibration of Section 7.5 fits.

The hand-off state is a product of per-mode thermal states (level A or B) or the level-C reduced motional state, with the internal state from Section 4.2.6; Section 9.3 requires that the diagnostics recover the prepared n̄ within the statistical error a laboratory would quote, and that the level-A closed forms agree with level C where both apply.

#### 4.2.8 Spontaneous emission with recoil, and the multi-level master equation

Run 3 named the spontaneous-emission recoil kernel as unsupplied by any source (Section 12): the emitted-photon Lamb-Dicke factor η̃ in the sideband-cooling floor of Section 4.2.2, the angular factor ξ = 2/5 in the Doppler limit of Section 4.2.1, the Δn ≈ 0 claim for repump photons and the recoil of Rayleigh scattering during gates (Section 6.5) all depend on it. This subsection specifies it and the multi-level master equation it belongs to. Run 4 checks the forms against Stenholm 1986, Morigi 2003 and Wineland and Itano 1979, and the recoil follow-up run (Run 4R, Appendix D) reconciled them across Cirac, Blatt, Zoller and Phillips 1992, Marzoli et al. 1994, Itano and Wineland 1982, Eschner et al. 2003, Steck's Quantum and Atom Optics and the cooling chapter of Roos's 2000 thesis, read from the papers themselves after the first attempt found them paywalled; Javanainen and Stenholm 1981 was not obtained and its content is covered through Cirac 1992 and Stenholm 1986. The physics is textbook **[background]**, the angular integrals below were recomputed on 2026-09-04 **[recomputed here]**, and the cross-source paragraph below carries the follow-up's verdicts.

**The recoil term.** A photon of wavevector k_eg emitted in direction k̂ carries momentum ħk_eg k̂ away from the ion, so the decay |e⟩ → |g⟩ displaces the motional state by the operator e^{−ik_eg k̂·x̂}. Averaged over the emission pattern N_q(k̂) of the decay's polarization type q, the spontaneous-emission part of the master equation for an ion of internal levels plus motion is

𝓛_se ρ = Σ_{e→g, q} Γ_{e→g,q} [ ∫ dΩ_k N_q(k̂) e^{−ik_eg k̂·x̂} |g⟩⟨e| ρ |e⟩⟨g| e^{+ik_eg k̂·x̂} − ½{|e⟩⟨e|, ρ} ],

with Γ_{e→g,q} the channel rates of Section 4.5.2 and, for a quantization axis along B̂, N₀(k̂) = (3/8π) sin²θ for π decays and N_{±1}(k̂) = (3/16π)(1 + cos²θ) for σ decays, θ the angle between k̂ and B̂. Writing the emitting ion's position as k̂·x̂_i = Σ_m b_{i,m}(k̂·ê_m) x₀,m (a_m + a_m†) over the modes, with b_{i,m} the ion's participation of Section 4.1.3 (c_{i,m} for mixed species), the operator inside the integral is a product of displacements Π_m D_m(−i k_eg b_{i,m} x₀,m k̂·ê_m), built exactly as in Section 5.1.1, one operator set per emitting ion and decay channel (the first version of this plan dropped b_{i,m}, which over-weights recoil into a centre-of-mass mode by N; caught by the 2026-09-04 critique); the recoil kernel over Δn is the distribution of |⟨n + Δn|D|n⟩|² averaged over N_q, exact in η through the Laguerre elements of Section 4.3.1.

**Angular factors.** To second order in the recoil the average enters only through α_m ≡ ∫ dΩ N_q(k̂)(k̂·ê_m)², the mean squared projection of the emission direction on the mode axis, and the recoil heating per photon scattered by ion i into mode m is α_m b_{i,m}²(k_eg x₀,m)² quanta, so that the effective emission Lamb-Dicke parameter of Section 4.2.2 is η̃_{i,m}² = α_m b_{i,m}²(k_eg x₀,m)²; summed over the modes of one axis the recoil energy per photon equals the free-ion recoil energy (ħk_eg)²/2m times that axis's α, which is the module's unit test. For a π decay α = 1/5 along B̂ and 2/5 perpendicular to it; for a σ decay 2/5 along B̂ and 3/10 perpendicular; for an isotropic average 1/3 (all recomputed by quadrature, `check_recoil.py`). This is Stenholm's factor: with the one-dimensional emission kernel (Γ/2)∫₋₁¹ dx W(x) ρ(k + qx), the pattern normalized as ½∫W dx = 1 and α ≡ ½∫x²W dx, the pattern W = (3/4)(1 + x²) gives α = 2/5 along the beam and the isotropic W = 1 gives 1/3 (Stenholm 1986 Eqs. 4.16-4.24) **[verified]**; Stenholm's α weights the emission recoil alone, the induced recoil supplying the 1 in his free-particle diffusion coefficient ∝ (1 + α) (Eqs. 4.40-4.42) and his trapped-ion coefficients carrying α as the additive carrier term of Eq. 5.41, and his Eqs. 4.31b-c carry two misprints (a plus for an equals sign, and ρ₂₂ at P + ħq/2 for P + ħq) that Eqs. 4.34-4.38 of the same paper fix **[corrected]**. Stenholm's Lamb-Dicke sideband-cooling rate equation then has A_± = P(Δ ∓ ν) + αP(Δ), the carrier scattering entering both rates through the recoil factor, with the steady state ⟨n⟩ = (γ/ν)²(α + 1/4) for γ ≪ ν (γ the half-width; 7/12 and 13/20 in units of (γ/ν)² for α = 1/3 and 2/5, Eqs. 5.49-5.54): this is the RMP's ⟨n⟩_min ≈ (Γ̃/2ν)²[(η̃/η)² + 1/4] of Section 4.2.2 with (η̃/η)² = α, which reconciles the two sections. Wineland and Itano 1979 give the same kernel exactly (their Eq. 31 is the Laguerre matrix element of Section 4.3.1 with the three-dimensional factorization over modes, checked by Run 4 against the matrix exponential to 10⁻¹⁵ for η up to 2.5; the factor they name Debye-Waller is the cross-section-level exp(−k²⟨x²⟩), the square of the amplitude prefactor), the general bound-atom rate equation as a Lorentzian-weighted sum over the initial and the intermediate, absorbed, motional states with the mean emission recoil R added per event (Eq. 48, one beam, low saturation), the free-atom Doppler limit ⟨E_K⟩_min = ħγ/4 per degree of freedom, k_B T_min = ħγ/2 (Eq. 11; 0.24 mK at γ = 2π × 10 MHz), the weak-binding bound-atom treatment that shares the emission recoil isotropically (1/3 per axis) and needs three orthogonal beams, one beam along (1, 1, 1) when the three frequencies differ, or collisional thermalization, the resolved-sideband floor ⟨n⟩ = (5/16)(γ/Ω_x)² for a carrier-plus-two-sideband emission spectrum, and the exact first and second moments of the emission recoil for isotropic emission, ⟨ΔE⟩ = R and ⟨(ΔE)²⟩ − R² = (2/3)R⟨H_t⟩ (Eqs. 36-37, from a commutator identity valid for bound and free atoms; the invariant form (ħ²k²/M²)Σ_iα_i⟨P_i²⟩ generalizes it to an anisotropic pattern and the printed ⟨H_t⟩ form under-predicts the free-atom Doppler variance by exactly 2) **[verified]**; their per-axis bookkeeping silently charges the full 2R per event to the driven axis, that is α = 1, which is why their ħγ/2 Doppler total is the (1 + α)/4 form at α = 1 and their 5/16 sideband floor is (α + 1/4)/4 at α = 1, and their isotropic partition (4R/3 along the beam, R/3 transverse) is replaced by the trace-one emission tensor diag(α_x, α_y, α_z), R(1 + α_∥) on the driven axis and Rα_⊥ on each transverse one **[corrected]**; the RMP's ξ = 2/5 is thus the σ pattern along the beam, and Rasmusson et al. 2021's n̄_D ≈ Γ/(2ω) is the bare form without it, so a test that compares Doppler limits must state which α it carries. The RMP's ξ = 2/5 in the Doppler limit of Section 4.2.1 is therefore the σ pattern along the field or the π pattern perpendicular to it, and the 1.43 fork between k_B T_min = 0.35ħΓ and ħΓ/2 recorded there is the difference between including this factor and not; n̄_D comes from the Section 4.2.2 rate coefficients evaluated with the configured α and cos²θ_L, as Section 4.2.1 requires, and the force-model value is reported only as the ν/Γ < 0.1 cross-check (the second revision's sentence here still named the force model as the producer, against 4.2.1; 2026-09-04 critique) **[corrected: critique, 2026-09-04]**. Sizes: for 171Yb+ at 369.5 nm on a 3 MHz mode, k x₀ = 0.053, so a scattered photon deposits about 1.1 × 10⁻³ quanta with α = 2/5; the few photons of optical pumping (Section 4.2.6) or a repump step therefore leave Δn ≈ 3 × 10⁻³, which is the quantitative content of the sources' "Δn ≈ 0", while the 10⁴ photons of Doppler cooling deposit tens of quanta, which is the Doppler limit itself; a Rayleigh event during a gate (probability of order 10⁻⁴, Section 6.5) heats by about 10⁻⁷ quanta, present but negligible.

**Cross-source reconciliation (Run 4R) [verified], [corrected].** The follow-up run established the following, each by reading the equation on the page and recomputing it. (i) The exact recoil term above is what Cirac et al. 1992 Eqs. 1-3, Steck Eqs. 5.416-5.417 and the one-dimensional reduction Γ∫₋₁¹ du f̃(u) 𝓓[e^{−ik_em u ẑ}]ρ all write, with three normalizations in the literature that must not be mixed: Itano's and Steck's three-dimensional density integrates to one over the sphere, Steck's azimuthally reduced f̃(u) integrates to one on [−1, 1], and Cirac's W(x) carries the explicit measure dx/2, so that f̃(u) = 2πf(θ, φ) = W(u)/2, and dropping Cirac's ½ doubles the recycling rate and breaks trace preservation. Cirac's W = (3/4)(1 + x²) is not a generic dipole pattern despite the paper's wording: it is the σ pattern with the mode axis along the quantization axis, and the two published values of 2/5 are different geometries (Itano Eq. 22 is the π pattern with the mode perpendicular to the dipole axis; Cirac's and Itano's 24Mg+ f_sz are the σ pattern along it) that coincide numerically **[corrected]**. (ii) The emission diffusion coefficient in the adiabatically eliminated motional master equation is D = ½ Σ_{i→j} ρ_ii^SS Γ_{i→j} α_{ij,m} η_ij², one Lamb-Dicke parameter per decay channel built from that channel's own wavenumber and one angular factor per channel and mode axis, and it multiplies Cirac's half-rate dissipator L_em μ = D(2XμX − X²μ − μX²) = 2D·𝒟[X] with X = a + a†, so its contribution to the phonon heating rate is 2D = Σ ρ_ii Γ_ij α_ij η_ij², which collapses to α η_em² R_sc only when every channel shares one pattern and one wavenumber (an implementer who feeds D to the plan's global 𝒟[L] convention halves the heating, and one mean α is up to 37% wrong for mixed π and σ channels; derivation audit of 2026-09-04) **[corrected: derivation audit, 2026-09-04]**; Cirac's γ_ji is a half rate and Marzoli's printed Eq. 4 reads as full rates and is twice this, while Marzoli's Eq. B4, an independent Lamb-Dicke expansion of the exact V-system equations, confirms the half-rate form **[corrected]**. α multiplies only the spontaneously emitted photon's recoil; the absorption recoil enters through e^{ik_L·x̂} in the Hamiltonian and never through a collapse operator, and the V-system form D = (s₁₀/4)ρ₀₀[Γ₁₂(η₁₀² + αη₁₂²) + Γ₁₀η₁₀²(1 + α)] is the test that catches both an α on the absorbed photon and a missing 1 on the closed cycle. (iii) The rate coefficients are A_± = 2Re[S(∓ν) + D] in general, with η² inside S and D, and, below saturation, W(Δ) → Ω²Γ/(4Δ² + Γ²) = (Ω²/Γ)L(Δ) with the dimensionless Lorentzian L(Δ) = Γ²/(4Δ² + Γ²), so that A_± = (Ω²/Γ)[L(Δ ∓ ν) + (η̃²/η²)L(Δ)], which is Eschner et al. 2003 Eq. 6 once their unprojected η² and explicit cos²θ_L are folded into the plan's η_{i,m} (Δk·ê_m inside η, Section 4.1.3) and their α into (η̃/η)²; the code stores the bare A_± in s⁻¹ with W(Δ) = Γρ_ee from `light/bloch.py`, which returns a rate for both of its consumers, and η² stays outside A_± in the rate equation Ṗ(n) of Section 4.2.3 (the second revision printed (Ω²/Γ)η²[cos²θ_L W(Δ ∓ ν) + αW(Δ)] here, η² inside and the projection twice, which fed into 4.2.3 would have multiplied every cooling rate by a second η² while leaving every n̄ test unchanged; the test is that η²(A₋ − A₊) reproduces Lechner's 19 × 10³ s⁻¹; 2026-09-04 critique) **[corrected: critique, 2026-09-04]**: this is Section 4.2.2's form, and Cirac's printed Eqs. 50 and 52 have the sideband sign correlated with the rate index (heating at red detuning) and lack a factor 2η², both corrected by three independent routes **[corrected]**. (iv) The steady state is not a recoil floor: η² = ω_R/ν cancels identically from A₊/(A₋ − A₊), so the recoil frequency sets the cooling rate and not n̄; at Δ = −ν with Γ ≪ ν, n̄_SB = (Γ/2ν)²[α(k_em/k_L)²/cos²θ_L + 1/4] = (Γ/2ν)²[(η̃/η)² + 1/4], which is Roos's Eq. 3.20 with (η̃/η)² = α(k_em/k_L)²/cos²θ_L, and α → 0 leaves (Γ/4ν)² from off-resonant blue-sideband excitation, not zero; at α = 2/5 with k_em = k_L and one on-axis beam the recoil term is 62% of n̄ and the blue sideband 38%, the closed-cycle special case, while the quenched 40Ca+ scheme of Section 4.2.2 (729 nm drive, 393 nm recoil photon, (k_em/k_L)² = 3.44, (η̃/η)² = 1.37) has bracket 1.62, the regression (the second revision's bracket here dropped the (k_em/k_L)² its own sentence names and returned 0.65 for that case, the very number 4.2.2 marks as the uncorrected value; 2026-09-04 theorist critique) **[verified]**, **[corrected: critique, 2026-09-04]**. In the Doppler regime at Δ = −Γ/2 the same coefficients give n̄_D = (Γ/4ν)(1 + α/cos²θ_L), Itano and Wineland's E_K = (1 + f_s)ħΓ/8 (ħΓ/6 for α = 1/3, 7ħΓ/40 for 2/5), their three-beam optimum γ_si/γ_tot = √f_si/Σ_j√f_sj, and a single beam along one axis leaves the other two heating linearly with no three-dimensional steady state **[verified]**. (v) For a quenched or repumped upper level the effective two-level parameters are Γ′ = ΠΓ₁₀ and γ′ = Π(Γ₁₀ + Γ₁₂)/2 with Π = (Ω_aux/2)²/[δ_aux² + ((Γ₁₀ + Γ₁₂)/2)²] = s_aux/2 (Marzoli Eqs. 12, 15; Roos Eq. 3.21, whose (Γ_aux + Γ) is the total fast-level linewidth), so γ′/Γ′ is (Γ₁₀ + Γ₁₂)/(2Γ₁₀) and not ½, the resolved-sideband criterion is ν ≫ Γ′ and ν ≫ γ′ separately, and in the V configuration the effective roles swap so that cooling needs blue detuning δ₂₀ = +ν **[verified]**; Cirac's Λ-plus-repumper system gives ⟨n⟩_min = 1/√β − ½ at Ω_φ² = 4Δν/√β inside the window R ≪ ν ≪ Γ ≪ Δ (0.554 at β = 0.9, 2.66 at β = 0.1 for Γ = 1, ν = 0.1, Δ = 10), which reproduces the paper's Fig. 8 **[verified]**. (vi) Printed errors the implementer must override, not copy: Cirac Eq. 7 (operator ordering, which would weight diffusion by the lower level), Eq. 28 (index order), Eqs. 50 and 52 (above), Eq. 54 (ρ_g omitted); Itano and Wineland Eq. 14 (a plus for an equals); Steck Eq. 5.522 (the pattern weight omitted, so it evaluates to 4π/3), Eq. 19.214 (a spurious tilde) and Eq. 19.212 (the integral-sign subscript on Ω); Roos Eq. 3.11 (Laguerre index n rather than n_< for m < 0, a factor 2 at (n, m) = (1, −1) and 2.9 at (8, −3)), the exponent of Section 4.3.1's form being already correct **[corrected]**. (vii) Validity conditions the module asserts at run time rather than documents: η_ij²(2n + 1) ≪ 1 per transition including the emitted photon, Ω ≪ Γ for every closed form, cooling rate W ≪ ν and ≪ every internal rate for the adiabatic elimination, Γ′, γ′ ≪ ν for resolved sidebands and Γ > ω_R for Doppler cooling, s_aux ≪ 1 and |δ| ≪ Γ₁₀ + Γ₁₂ for the three-level reduction, and the reduction's neglect of the ion's motion during its stay in the fast level, whose error no source quantifies and which the level-C solve measures. Two gaps the sources leave and the simulator fills by construction: no source works out the joint multi-mode recoil dissipator, which the product of displacements over modes with one sampled direction k̂ is, tested by the Cartesian sum rule Σ_axes α = 1, the single-kick expectation and Itano's per-event invariant Σ_i⟨ΔE_i⟩ = 2R + ħk·v; and no source treats a mode axis oblique to the quantization axis or a decay that is a coherent superposition of q components, for which the scalar patterns are insufficient and the jump operator per direction and polarization must be the vector form Σ_q(ε̂*_{k̂λ}·ê_q) d_q e^{−ik_em k̂·x̂}, which sums over the two polarizations to the scalar patterns when one q decays and carries Steck's tensor f_qq′ otherwise **[background]**; the module builds that form and derives α from it, so no scalar α is ever hard-coded.

**Implementation as collapse operators.** QuTiP needs a finite set of collapse operators, not an integral over directions. The module discretizes each decay channel's emission pattern on a quadrature of K directions with weights w_k (a product Gauss-Legendre grid in cos θ and trapezoid in φ, or Lebedev), giving K operators √(Γ w_k) |g⟩⟨e| ⊗ Π_m D_m(−i k_eg b_{i,m} x₀,m k̂_k·ê_m) per channel and emitting ion, exact to the order of the quadrature in η̃; for a mode along a principal axis the reduction to one dimension uses 16 Gauss-Legendre nodes on [−1, 1] with p_j = w_j f̃(u_j), which is exact to 10⁻¹⁰ for η_em < 0.3 because the integrand is a low-order polynomial times a smooth exponential; the one-dimensional marginals for the principal orientations are (3/4)(1 − u²) for a π pattern along the axis (α = 1/5), (3/8)(1 + u²) for a σ pattern along it or a π pattern perpendicular to it (α = 2/5; the same marginal, not merely the same α), (9 − 3u²)/16 for a σ pattern perpendicular (3/10) and ½ for isotropic emission (1/3), and two Gauss-Legendre nodes return α = 1/3 for every quadratic marginal, so three nodes are the minimum for the second moment and six to eight for the full Δn kernel at η_em ≤ 0.9 (a Run 4 brief's claim that two nodes suffice was refuted by its own consolidation) **[verified]**, **[corrected]**. Morigi's EIT figure is the closed form's test in the other direction: at her Fig. 3 parameters (ν = 2.0068, γ = 20, Ω₁ = Ω₂ = 17 MHz, Δ_Morigi = −70 MHz, which is Δ = +70 MHz in the plan's sign, Section 13, because her optimum reads Ω² = 4ν(ν − Δ_Morigi) where Section 4.2.3's reads Ω_r² = 4ν(ν + Δ), so Δ = −Δ_Morigi) the rate coefficients give n̄ = 0.005102 against (γ/4|Δ|)² = 0.0051020 and her caption's 0.005, while the caption's printed Δ_Morigi = +70 MHz (Δ = −70 in the plan's sign) returns −1.005 from the Section 4.2.3 closed form, a negative occupation that trips its own A₋ − A₊ ≤ 0 guard, so the module is parameterized from her body text; the two Rabi frequencies enter that closed form as the quadrature sum Ω_r = √(Ω₁² + Ω₂²) = 24.04 MHz, the value that satisfies Ω_r² = 4ν(ν + Δ) = 578 at Δ = +70 (a single 17 in its place returns 0.147, 29 times the target; `check_critique_v3.py`), and since Ω₁ = Ω₂ sits outside the weak-probe regime Ω_g ≪ Ω_r of Section 4.2.3, level C rather than the closed form validates the figure (the second revision quoted the fixture in Morigi's sign with neither the sign map nor the composition rule; 2026-09-04 experimentalist critique) **[corrected: critique, 2026-09-04]**, **[recomputed here]**; her optimum Ω² = 4ν(ν − Δ_Morigi) is leading order in γ/|Δ| (the exact stationary point is 0.5% below) **[verified]**, **[corrected]**. Three assertions run before any solve: Σ_j p_j = 1 and Σ_j p_j u_j² = α to 10⁻¹², so that a failure is a quadrature bug and never a physics one; Σ_k C_k†C_k = Γ|e⟩⟨e| ⊗ 𝟙 to machine precision, because the kicks are unitary and the internal decay rate must not depend on the motion; and the single-kick expectation, the Kraus map applied once to |e, 0⟩ giving ⟨n_m⟩ = α_m b_{i,m}² η_em,m² exactly with ⟨p⟩ = 0. The minimal quadrature that reproduces the first and second moments exactly (for each emission axis a ±k̂ pair with weights α/2 each, plus the complementary weight on a no-recoil operator, each axis resolved over its modes through b_{i,m}) is the default for Lamb-Dicke-regime cooling and the sideband floor, which need nothing beyond second order; the full quadrature is the option for hot ions, where the Δn distribution's tails matter. Trajectories (`mcsolve`) then produce discrete recoil jumps whose direction-by-direction cross term −2R k̂_i k̂_si averages to zero but contributes shot-to-shot variance that `mesolve` cannot see, and `mesolve` the averaged kernel; the semi-analytic path solves the internal-only steady state with `steadystate`, obtains S(±ν) from `spectrum` or `correlation_2op_1t` of the dipole-force operator on the internal Liouvillian, forms D from the populations, and raises rather than returning a negative n̄ when A₋ − A₊ ≤ 0. With recoil switched off the operators reduce to the internal-state collapse operators of Section 4.5.5, which is the d > 2 scattering model of Section 6.5.

**The multi-level optical Bloch equations.** With the levels and matrix elements of Section 4.5 the cooling, pumping and detection stages share one Hamiltonian builder: H = Σ_p E_p|p⟩⟨p| + Σ_beams Σ_{(p,q)} (ħ/2) Ω^{(beam)}_{pq}(t) e^{−iω_beam t} |q⟩⟨p| + h.c., with E_p the field-dependent energies of Section 4.5.1 and Ω_{pq} the polarization-resolved Rabi frequencies of Section 4.5.2, plus the motional Hamiltonian and the drive's displacement operators when a mode is included (level C). A rotating frame that removes the optical frequencies exists when each fine-structure manifold can be assigned one frame frequency such that every beam's detuning from every transition it drives is small: the builder assigns frame frequencies manifold by manifold, checks each beam against the manifolds it connects, moves the residual detunings onto the diagonal, and flags any beam whose residual is inconsistent with an already assigned manifold (two beams on one transition at different frequencies, or a closed loop of beams whose frequencies do not sum consistently), in which case the Hamiltonian is explicitly time periodic and is integrated as such or handled with `FloquetBasis`. The steady state of this system (`steadystate`) is the single scattering-rate object of Section 13 that supplies W(Δ) for cooling (Section 4.2.2), R_∘, R_d and R_b for readout (Section 8.1) and the dark-state physics of coherent population trapping; its time evolution from a given initial state gives the optical-pumping fidelity and duration of Section 4.2.6 from first principles, which fills the gap Run 3 named from both ends of the stack; and with one motional mode and the recoil operators above it is the level-C cooling simulation of Sections 4.2.1 to 4.2.4. The effective two-level descriptions of those sections (repumper-broadened linewidth Γ̃, the three-level EIT Fano profile, the two-level Doppler force) are recovered from it by adiabatic elimination under the conditions Marzoli et al. 1994 state, which Run 4 supplies; the simulator tests each closed form against the level-C solve in its stated regime.

**Pulsed sideband cooling with exact matrix elements** (Che et al. 2017; Rasmusson et al. 2021) **[verified]**, **[corrected]**. The rate picture of Section 4.2.2 is completed for pulsed schemes with the exact Ω_{n,n−k} of Section 4.3.1: one cycle, a k-th-order red-sideband pulse of duration t (a free parameter, not a π time; Rasmusson's worked example is t = 1.016 × 2π/Ω) followed by fast optical pumping, is a column-stochastic transfer matrix W_k(t) on the Fock populations, one matrix per sideband order with diagonal a_n = cos²(Ω_{n,n−k}t/2) and k-th upper diagonal b_n = sin²(Ω_{n,n−k}t/2), coherences being discarded between pulses because the pumping erases them; N pulses compose as an ordered matrix product, and the mean occupation after a schedule is Σ_n n[W(t_{N−1})⋯W(t₀) p_th]_n. Three optimizations are supported: a single shared pulse time, independent pulse times by gradient descent, and one pulse time per sideband order with higher orders applied first, which the sources find optimal (Che's 75-pulse schedule for 25Mg+ from n̄ = 17 at η = 0.3: fifteen second-order pulses, fifteen first-order, fifteen second-order, thirty first-order, 9 µs each at Ω₀/2π = 150 kHz). The exact matrix elements are what make higher orders necessary: because Ω_{n,n−k} ∝ L^{(k)}_{n−k}(η²) has zeros, single-order cooling strands population at the Rabi node. At η = 0.3 the first zero of L^{(1)}_{n′}(0.09) lies at n′ = 39.8 and that of L^{(2)}_{n′}(0.09) at n′ = 71.8 **[recomputed here]**, so with the index n′ = n − k of Che's Eq. 2 the stranded states are n = 41 and n = 74, whereas Che's Fig. 2 and simulations report 40 and 72, the roots indexed by the initial n rather than by n − k, an off-by-k inconsistency internal to the paper; the simulator uses the exact element, reproduces Che's accumulation centres (43, 41, 40 after 40, 150, 500 first-order cycles and 75, 73, 72 for the second order) only with the shifted index, and treats that comparison as a test of the index convention rather than of the physics **[corrected]**. Two more of Che's printed forms are not copied: his Eq. 4 prints the thermal distribution without the exponent n (the Fig. 2 inset is the geometric n̄ⁿ/(n̄ + 1)^{n+1}), and his carrier Ω₀e^{−η²/2} is the n = 0 value only, the general carrier Ω₀e^{−η²/2}L_n(η²) having a zero between n = 15 and 16 at η = 0.3, next to his n̄ = 17 **[corrected]**. Repumping heats by off-resonant Raman scattering during the pumping interval, Δn̄ = t_re s_re Γ_re³η²/(4Δ_HF²) (0.03 quanta per 10 µs repump for 25Mg+), which caps the cooling at about 0.97 quanta per cycle and is the recoil term of this subsection in its Λ-system form. Thermometry after such cooling must not assume a thermal distribution: Rasmusson's time-averaged red-sideband signal P̄^{RSB}_{↑,m} converges to half the tail sum Σ_{n≥m} p(n) independently of the decoherence rate, and stepping the sideband order m extracts individual populations, while the ratio method r = n̄/(n̄ + 1) of Section 4.2.7 holds for thermal states only, so the simulator's thermometry experiments offer both and the double-thermal model p = αp_th(n̄_l) + (1 − α)p_th(n̄_h) as the fit for post-cooling distributions. Anchors: Rasmusson's 171Yb+ at ω_z = 2π × 0.670 MHz and η = 0.18, where 50 multiorder pulses (first, second and third order, W_m built from Ω_{n,n−m} for each) from n̄ = 15.36 reach n̄ = 0.06 while single-order cooling leaves about 0.3 quanta stranded above the first-order zero near n = 112, and where 25 first-order fixed pulses from n̄ = 14.6 predict n̄_sim = 3.57(58) against measured thermometry of 0.58(56) (ratio), 8.0(1.3) (SVD) and 4.1(7) (time-averaged), so that only n̄_sim is a target and the three measurements are anchors of the estimator bias; Che's 25Mg+ at 2π × 1.5 MHz with η = 0.3 and a 500 Hz decoherence rate on 151 Fock levels. This also supplies the second-sideband cooling rate that Run 3 listed as missing: it is the same expression with k = 2 and the exact matrix element, not a Lamb-Dicke expansion. Morigi 2003 completes the EIT picture of Section 4.2.3 at the master-equation level: with the Λ system's recoil kernel included, the second-order recoil-diffusion term vanishes identically at two-photon resonance, so EIT cooling has no recoil floor and its optimum ⟨n⟩ = (γ/4|Δ|)² stands unchanged, while the effective Lamb-Dicke parameter of the scheme is the projected difference η₁cos φ₁ − η₂cos φ₂ of the two legs (each η_j = k_j x₀ from the full wavevector, the projection applied separately, and the Lamb-Dicke expansion parameter being the thermally inflated η_j√(2⟨n⟩ + 1)), zero in a Doppler-free geometry **[verified]**; two of Morigi's printed forms are not copied: her Eq. 22 with her own α = ∫N cos²φ overstates the recoil diffusion by a factor 2 (the Liouvillian prefactor is α/2, the half-rate form of the diffusion coefficient above), and the arXiv v1 Eq. 29 has a plus for a minus that breaks probability conservation; the recoil-free property itself, P̃₀L̃₂P̃₀ = 0 because the dark steady state has no excited population, stands **[corrected]**.


### 4.3 Spin-motion interaction Hamiltonian and single-qubit control

This is the core of the simulator: one Hamiltonian builder that every pulse goes through. The conventions below are the simulator's; Section 13 lists the alternatives the sources use.

#### 4.3.1 The Hamiltonian the builder implements

For N ions with internal transition frequency ω₀, modes m with frequencies ω_m and ladder operators a_m, and a running-wave drive on ion i with Rabi frequency Ω_i, effective wavevector projection δk along the motional axis, frequency ω_L and phase φ_i, the lab-frame Hamiltonian is (Monroe 2020 Eqs. 6-7; RMP 2003 Eqs. 60-63; Wineland 2003 Eq. 2.1) **[verified]**

H_lab = (ω₀/2) Σ_i σ_z^i + Σ_m ω_m a_m†a_m − Σ_i d̂_i · E(X̂_i),  E_i = E_{i0} cos(k X̂_i − ω_L t − φ_i),

with ħ = 1 and X̂_i = X̄_i + Σ_m b_{im} ξ_m^{(0)}(a_m + a_m†), ξ_m^{(0)} = √(ħ/2Mω_m). Moving to the frame of the qubit and applying the optical rotating-wave approximation (dropping e^{±i(ω_L+ω₀)t}, valid for ω_L, ω₀ ≫ Ω) leaves the exact spin-motion interaction (Monroe Eq. 8, in the interaction picture for the motion) **[verified]**

H = Σ_i (Ω_i/2) σ₊^i e^{i(δk X̄_i − μ_i t − δφ_i)} Π_m exp[iη_{im}(a_m† e^{iω_m t} + a_m e^{−iω_m t})] + h.c.,

where μ_i = ω_L − ω₀ is the detuning from the carrier and η_{im} = b_{im} δk ξ_m^{(0)} the Lamb-Dicke parameter of ion i on mode m (Section 4.1.3). The simulator keeps the motion in the Schrödinger picture instead (Section 5.2), so the operator Π_m exp[iη_{im}(a_m + a_m†)] = Π_m D_m(iη_{im}) is time independent and the time dependence sits in the scalar coefficient (Ω_i(t)/2) e^{−i(μ_i t − φ_i(t))}. Resonances occur when μ_i = Σ_m ℓ_m ω_m for integers ℓ_m, the sideband of total order p = Σ|ℓ_m| carrying a prefactor of order η^p/√(p!), since ⟨p|D(iη)|0⟩ = e^{−η²/2}(iη)^p/√(p!) **[corrected; the earlier η^p/p! understates the third and fourth sidebands by 2.4 and 4.9 times at η = 0.1, and the truncation and mode-dropping heuristics use the exact element rather than the scaling]**. The RMP 2003 form of the same expansion carries the micromotion sidebands through the Floquet function u(t); as printed, its Eqs. 67-68 and the sentence "β_x ω_rf ≈ ν" drop a factor 1/2 relative to the paper's own Eq. 37 (ν = β_x ω_rf/2), which would double the secular sideband spacing in the micromotion comb **[corrected]**.

**Exact matrix elements.** For a single mode the coupling between |↓, n⟩ and |↑, n′⟩ is (Wineland 1998 Eq. 18; RMP 2003 Eq. 70) **[verified]**

⟨n′| e^{iη(a+a†)} |n⟩ = ⟨n′|D(iη)|n⟩ = e^{−η²/2} (iη)^{|n′−n|} (n_<!/n_>!)^{1/2} L^{|n′−n|}_{n_<}(η²),  Ω_{n′,n} = Ω |⟨n′|D(iη)|n⟩| = Ω e^{−η²/2} (n_<!/n_>!)^{1/2} |η|^{|n′−n|} |L^{|n′−n|}_{n_<}(η²)|,

exact in η for one running-wave drive and one mode, the signed element being what the builder uses and the modulus what a Rabi frequency means (the first two revisions equated the modulus to the signed Laguerre expression, which is negative for odd |n′ − n| at negative η and beyond the polynomial's first zero, already at n = 6 to 8 for η = 0.5; caught by the derivation audit of 2026-09-04) **[corrected: derivation audit, 2026-09-04]**. In the Lamb-Dicke regime, η√⟨(a+a†)²⟩ ≪ 1 (a state-dependent condition), this reduces to Ω for the carrier, ηΩ√n for the first red and ηΩ√(n+1) for the first blue sideband **[verified]**, and the first-order Hamiltonian is (RMP Eq. 72) H_LD = (ħ/2)Ω₀σ₊{1 + iη(a e^{−iνt} + a†e^{iνt})}e^{i(φ−δt)} + h.c., with the red sideband a Jaynes-Cummings and the blue an anti-Jaynes-Cummings coupling **[verified]**. Because the builder uses D(iη) exactly, the Debye-Waller factors are included automatically: the carrier operator on each spectator mode is D̂_{im} = e^{−η_{im}²/2} L_{n_m}(η_{im}²) ≈ 1 − η_{im}²(n_m + 1/2) + … (Monroe Eq. 11) **[verified]**, its thermal average is exp[−η_p²(n̄_p + 1/2)] per mode, and Wineland 1998 Eqs. 122-127 give both the mean and the rms shot-to-shot fluctuation of the Rabi frequency when spectator modes are thermal and frozen during a run (their worked example: 100 modes at n̄ = 0.1, η = 0.01) **[verified]**. The sideband Hamiltonians with driven-mode and spectator-mode Debye-Waller operators are Monroe Eqs. 16-19 **[verified]**.

**Two-level propagators for tests.** Within the subspace {|↓,n⟩, |↑,n′⟩} at detuning Δ from the (n′−n)-th sideband the exact propagator has generalized Rabi frequency (Δ² + 4Ω²_{n′,n})^{1/2} in Wineland's convention and f_n^l = √(δ′² + Ω²_{n+l,n}) in the RMP's, with the drive phase entering as φ + (π/2)|n′−n| (Wineland Eq. 21; RMP Eq. 84) **[verified]**; these closed forms are the unit tests for the builder. The resonant map is |↓⟩|n⟩ → cos(Ω_{n,n′}t)|↓⟩|n⟩ − i e^{iφ} sin(Ω_{n,n′}t)|↑⟩|n′⟩ in Wineland's half-Rabi convention (Wineland 2003 Eq. 2.4), which reads cos(Ω_{n,n′}t/2) and sin(Ω_{n,n′}t/2) in the plan's convention of the next paragraph, the conversion made once at ingest (2026-09-04 critique); the companion Eq. 2.5 is misprinted in the source (its left side should read |↑⟩|n′⟩) and must not be implemented as printed **[corrected]**.

**Rabi-frequency conventions.** Wineland 1998 defines ħΩ = −μ_M B₁/4, half the usual Rabi frequency, so its carrier π pulse is at Ωt = π/2 and its blue-sideband flopping appears at 2Ω_{n+1,n} (Eq. 42) **[verified]**; Monroe 1995 uses P = sin²(Ωτ) likewise **[verified]**; the RMP, Monroe 2020, Lee 2005 and Mount 2015 write H = (ħΩ/2)σ₊ + h.c. so that a π pulse is Ωt = π. The simulator uses the latter, Ω defined so that the resonant carrier flops as sin²(Ωt/2), and converts imported numbers.

#### 4.3.2 Stimulated-Raman drives

Two beams (fields E_j cos(k_j·x − ω_{Lj}t + φ_j), j = 1, 2) coupling |↓⟩ and |↑⟩ through far-detuned excited states |i⟩ produce, after adiabatic elimination (Δ_i ≫ γ_i and Δ_i ≫ the two-photon coupling), an effective Hamiltonian of the single-photon form with the substitutions (Wineland 2003 Eqs. 2.2-2.3; RMP Eq. A12; Wineland 1998 Eqs. 37-41) **[verified]**

k → Δk = k₁ − k₂,  ω_L → ω₁ − ω₂,  φ → φ₁ − φ₂,  Ω e^{iφ} → e^{i(φ₁−φ₂)}/(2ħ²) Σ_i ⟨↑|d·E₂ε̂₂|i⟩⟨i|d·E₁ε̂₁|↓⟩/Δ_i,

which is twice the coupling matrix element that Wineland 2003 Eq. 2.3 prints with 1/(4ħ²), because Ω here is defined by H = (ħΩ/2)σ₊ + h.c. while that paper's Ω is defined by ħΩσ₊ (Section 4.3.1); for a single intermediate state it is Ω₁Ω₂/(2Δ) in single-photon Rabi frequencies of the same convention (Section 4.5.4), and the same 1/(4ħ²) prefactor is correct for the ac Stark shift below, because a level shift and a half-Rabi-frequency share it (the first version of this plan substituted the 1/(4ħ²) form into the (ħΩ/2) slot; caught by the 2026-09-04 critique) **[corrected]**.

The Lamb-Dicke parameter becomes η = Δk z₀ (counter-propagating beams: |Δk| ≈ 2k; co-propagating: Δk ≈ 0, no motional coupling **[verified]**). Factor-of-2 traps: with single-photon Rabi frequencies defined through (ħΩ/2)-type couplings, the RMP's Eq. A13 reads Ω = −2|Ω_{g3}Ω_{e3}|/Δ_R, which is twice "the product divided by Δ_R" and four times a coupling-strength reading **[corrected]**; the arXiv v1 preprint of Lee et al. 2005 (quant-ph/0505203v1) prints the ac Stark shift χ_{m,i} with denominator 2 where 2Δ is required, dimensionally a frequency squared, and swaps the beam-to-level assignment in its Eq. 2, whereas the published J. Opt. B version prints both correctly; the errata check of 2026-09-04 traced the first revision's reading to the preprint (pdftotext also drops the Δ glyph from the journal PDF, which is the likely route in) **[corrected: preprint only]**. The simulator computes the two-photon Rabi frequency and the light shifts from the dipole matrix elements, detunings and intensities in one function, with tests against the 9Be+ closed forms below.

**ac Stark shifts.** Each beam j shifts each qubit level m_S by δ(m_S, j) = (1/4ħ²) Σ_i |⟨m_S|d·E_jε̂_j|i⟩|²/Δ_i (Wineland 2003 Eq. 2.10) **[verified]**; for the 9Be+ |2,2⟩ ↔ |1,1⟩ qubit the eight-term differential shift (Eq. 2.11) collapses for ω₀ ≪ Δ, ω_F to Eq. 2.12 and has a polarization null **[verified]**. For clock qubits the differential shift is the difference of four detuning-weighted diagonal amplitudes, Δ_St = g²[a^{(1/2)}_{↑↑}/(Δ+Δ_hf) + a^{(3/2)}_{↑↑}/(Δ+Δ_hf−Δ_f) − a^{(1/2)}_{↓↓}/Δ − a^{(3/2)}_{↓↓}/(Δ−Δ_f)] (Ozeri 2005 Eq. 3) **[verified]**; for far-detuned clock-qubit Raman drives the ratio of total scattering to Stark phase saturates at 0.9579 γ/Δ_hf ≈ 0.0154 photons per radian for 9Be+ **[verified]**. In a bichromatic drive with asymmetric detunings μ_{i+}, μ_{i−} the Stark term is s_i = μ_{i+} − μ_{i−} (Monroe Eq. 20) **[verified]**.
