Stark shifts enter the builder as time-dependent σ_z terms proportional to the instantaneous intensity envelope, so amplitude-shaped pulses shift the qubit frequency during the pulse exactly as in the laboratory; the third-tone cancellation used by Innsbruck (Section 4.4) is a scheduled drive like any other.

**Clock-qubit Raman coupling and scattering** (Ozeri et al. 2007) **[verified]**. For the |F = I−1/2, m_F = 0⟩ ↔ |F = I+1/2, m_F = 0⟩ transition driven by a blue/red beam pair detuned Δ from P₁/₂ (Δ − ω_f from P₃/₂):

Ω_R = (g_b g_r/3)(b₋r₋ − b₊r₊) ω_f/[Δ(Δ − ω_f)],

so the coupling interferes destructively between the two fine-structure paths and falls as 1/Δ² for |Δ| ≫ ω_f, exactly like the Raman scattering amplitude **[verified]** (Ozeri 2005: the P₁/₂ and P₃/₂ Raman amplitudes are equal in magnitude and opposite in sign, while the Rayleigh amplitudes share a sign but not a magnitude **[corrected]**). The probability of any scattering event during a π rotation depends only on γ, ω_f and Δ, with all power and waist dependence cancelling:

P_total = (πγ/ω_f)(2Δ² + (Δ − ω_f)²)/|Δ(Δ − ω_f)|,  minimum at Δ = (√2 − 1)ω_f,  P_∞ = 3πγ/ω_f **[verified]**;

the single-qubit gate error is the Raman-scattering probability, ε_S = P_Raman (Eq. 11), whose minimum inside the fine-structure interval is 8πγ/(3ω_f) **[corrected]** (the extracted item called it a maximum), and which in laboratory quantities reads ε_S = 2π|Ω_R| ħ ω_{3/2}³ w₀²/(3c²P) with P the power per beam and w₀ the waist (Eq. 17), the paper's "λ_{3/2} = c/ω_{3/2}" being a typo for 2πc/ω **[verified flag]**. Scattering into low-lying D levels is not protected by the interference: ε_D = f P_total with branching ratio f, giving an irreducible floor ε_D∞ = 3πγf/ω_f (Eqs. 19-21); Table I of that paper gives f⁻¹ = 17 (43Ca+), 14 (87Sr+), 3 (137Ba+), 290 (171Yb+), 700 (199Hg+), and no D level for 9Be+, 25Mg+, 67Zn+, 111Cd+ **[verified]**. Rayleigh scattering at large detuning does not decohere a hyperfine qubit but imparts recoil, which matters for gates through motion (Section 4.4) **[verified]**. The species module stores Ozeri's Table I constants (for 9Be+: I = 3/2, γ/2π = 19.6 MHz, ω₀/2π = 1.25 GHz, ω_f/2π = 0.198 THz, λ = 313.1/313.0 nm; 43Ca+: I = 7/2, 22.5 MHz, 3.23 GHz, 6.68 THz, 396.8/393.4 nm) and the η convention warning that Ozeri's z₀ carries a factor 4 under the root **[verified flag]**. The scattering channels are implemented as Lindblad operators on the extended internal space (d > 2) with rates Γ_{i,f} = g²γ|a^{(1/2)}_{i→f}/Δ + a^{(3/2)}_{i→f}/(Δ − Δ_f)|² summed over final states **[verified]**, and as an estimated per-pulse error when d = 2.

**Frequency-comb Raman drives** (Mount et al. 2015) **[verified]**. A mode-locked comb drives the qubit through the resonant tooth pair and, with weights 1 : 2 : 1, through off-resonant pairs at δ′ and 2δ′; a second-order Magnus average turns these into a static detuning tilt, so the imperfect pulse propagator is R = exp[−(i/2)θ(1 + ε)(σ_φ + δZ)] with a multiplicative area error ε and a dimensionless detuning δ **[verified]**. This is the error model the simulator uses for composite-pulse tests, and it is all that Section 4.3.5 needs; Section 4.3.7 builds the comb drive itself from first principles, as a set of tones over tooth pairs of which Mount's three-term model is the truncation, together with the comb's own resonance conditions, ac Stark shifts and noise channels.

#### 4.3.3 Microwave drives

A microwave field drives the hyperfine transition directly with η ≈ 0 (no motional coupling without a field gradient). Harty et al. 2014 fix the archetype **[verified]**: 43Ca+ clock qubit S₁/₂^{4,0} ↔ S₁/₂^{3,+1} at B₀ = 146.094 G, f₀ = 3,199,941,077 Hz (after removing a −5 Hz differential ac Zeeman shift from trap rf currents), curvature d²f/dB² = 2.4 mHz/mG², μ_I = −1.31535 μ_N with inverted ordering (F = 3 above F = 4); drive from a frequency-octupled 400 MHz DDS with Ω/2π = 21 kHz, t_{π/2} = 12.1 µs, 14 µs dead time for DDS phase switching, deliberate detuning +4.5 Hz and amplitude stability |ΔΩ/Ω| ≲ 5 × 10⁻⁴. The imperfectly polarized field couples off-resonantly to spectator Zeeman transitions Δ_Z/2π ≈ 50 MHz away, giving spectator flopping of order (Ω/Δ_Z)² ≈ 10⁻⁷ and an ac Zeeman shift of the qubit transition of −1.0 Hz; with the convention δ = ω_drive − ω₀ a negative transition shift increases the detuning, δ_eff = δ − δ_ac = +5.5 Hz **[corrected]** (the extracted item had the sign wrong). The paper's own error model, a product of imperfect π/2 propagators and 14 µs dead-time propagators over the longest randomized-benchmarking sequences, predicts 0.81(14) × 10⁻⁶ per gate against the measured 1.0(3) × 10⁻⁶ **[verified]**; the simulator reproduces this as a test (Section 9.2). Memory: Ramsey contrast exp(−t_R/T₂*) with T₂* = 50(10) s at the clock point, without shielding or decoupling **[verified]**.

#### 4.3.4 Phases, frames and virtual Z

The drive phase φ = φ₁ − φ₂ (plus the geometric term δk X̄_i for the ion's equilibrium position) may be set to zero once per ion, after which every operation on that ion must be referenced to that phase **[verified]** (Wineland 2003). The carrier, red-sideband and blue-sideband Hamiltonians all carry the same optical phase factor e^{i(ΔkX_{0,i} − Δφ)} (Lee 2005 Eqs. 6-8) **[verified]**, so a carrier rotation axis at angle β_i in the xy plane is set by the beat-note phase (Monroe Eq. 10) **[verified]**. In a bichromatic drive the two optical phases recombine into a spin phase, the half-sum of the tone phases, that sets the axis σ_{φ_s} of the spin-dependent force, and a motion phase, the half-difference, that sets the phase of the force on the mode (Lee Eqs. 28-30; Monroe Eqs. 20-22) **[verified]**. The simulator's convention is Haljan's, φ_s = +(φ_b + φ_r)/2 for the half-sum (Sections 4.4.2 and 13); Lee writes φ_S = −(half-sum), whose minus sign the extracted prose had dropped **[corrected]**, and it is converted at ingest (the first version of this plan adopted Lee's sign here and Haljan's in Section 4.4.2, a contradiction caught by the 2026-09-04 critique). The axis of the force is not the half-sum itself: expanding the plan's own drive factor e^{i(Δk·x̂ − μt + φ)} to first order gives the sideband coupling an explicit i (iη(a + a†)), and i[σ₊e^{iψ} − h.c.] = σ_{ψ+π/2}, so in the same-Δk (phase-sensitive) geometry the force axis sits at π/2 + Δk·X̄_i + (φ_b + φ_r)/2, a quarter turn from the carrier axis at the same beat-note phase, while in the crossed-Δk (phase-insensitive) geometry η_r = −η_b adds a further π and Δk·X̄_i cancels; the constant offset is what the phase-frame alignment of Section 7.5 step 3 measures and the native MS(φ₀, φ₁) definition absorbs, so it changes no gate, but the Section 9.4 pin must carry it: an MS pulse with φ_b = φ_r = φ on ions at the frame origin entangles about the azimuth φ + π/2 in the same-Δk geometry (the second revision's '+φ' would have certified a quarter-turn error; derivation audit of 2026-09-04) **[corrected: derivation audit, 2026-09-04]**. Whether the gate is phase sensitive or insensitive is decided by beam geometry: with two beat notes on the same beam pair both above (or below) the third beam's frequency, Δk_r = Δk_b and the optical phase enters the spin phase (sensitive); with one above and one below, Δk_r = −Δk_b and the optical phase enters only the motion phase, which cancels when the loop closes (insensitive) **[verified]**. Virtual Z rotations are frame updates: RZ(θ) on qubit i shifts the phase reference of every later pulse on i by −θ in time order (Section 7.6), costing no error beyond bookkeeping and no time beyond the hardware's phase-switching dead time **[corrected]**, and identity gates in benchmarking are implemented as delays of one pulse duration so that idle errors are counted **[verified]** (Harty 2014).

#### 4.3.5 Single-qubit gates and composite pulses

A native GPi(φ) or GPi2(φ) is a resonant carrier pulse of area π or π/2 with phase φ relative to the ion's frame; the target unitary is R(θ, φ) = exp(−iθσ_φ/2), σ_φ = X cos φ + Y sin φ **[verified]** (Mount 2015 Eq. 1). The half-Pauli generator is load-bearing for everything below: a 2π segment is exactly −I, and R(π, φ)R(α, β)R(π, φ)† = R(α, 2φ − β) is the in-plane reflection every corrector phase descends from, so halving the tabulated angles to "pulse areas" θ/2 breaks the 2π quantization and with it every sequence **[verified]**, **[recomputed here]**. Products run right to left, so a scheduler emitting (θ_l, φ_l) in time order folds them in reverse (Section 13), and a negative nominal area is played as a positive area at phase φ + π - exact under amplitude error and **not** under detuning error, the σ_z term not reversing with the drive phase.

**The static error primitive and the four channels.** Run 5 reconciled five composite-pulse sources onto one object **[verified]**: M(θ, φ; ε_a, ε_d) = exp[−iθ{(1 + ε_a)ρ̂(φ)·σ + ε_d σ_z}/2], ρ̂(φ) = (cos φ, sin φ, 0), exact for a square segment under time-independent errors. It is Section 6.4's R = exp[−(i/2)θ(1 + ε)(σ_φ + δZ)] (Mount 2015) and also Cummins' quaternion pulse, whose axis (cos φ, sin φ, ε_d)/√(1 + ε_d²) and angle θ√(1 + ε_d²) identify ε_a = g and ε_d = f = δ/ν₁ with no rescaling on ingest **[verified]**. Four systematic channels map onto it (Merrill and Brown Sec. 2.2.1) **[verified]**: **amplitude** ε_A = Ω′/Ω at fixed duration, M = R(θ(1 + ε_A), φ); **pulse length** ε_T = δt/Δt, the same propagator for a square pulse but kept separate so the coupled model v_{x,y} = u_{x,y}(1 + ε_T), v_z = (u_z + ε_D)(1 + ε_T) is reproducible; **addressing** ε_N = Ω′/Ω on a spectator, whose zero is **inverted** (ε_N = 0 is ideal, U_T = I, M = R(θε_N, φ)), so R(θ(1 + ε_N), φ) by analogy with ε_A is wrong; and **detuning** ε_D = Δ/Ω, whose error generator +ε_D θ H_z scales with pulse area and keeps its plus sign even where the drive phase is π and the in-plane term is negative - flipping it along with the middle CORPSE pulse makes all three generators commute and returns the bare-pulse infidelity to six digits **[verified]**. Amplitude and addressing errors have δu ∝ u, so the leading error is a vector sum; detuning does not commute with the ideal generator, which is why CORPSE needs a Magnus expansion and BB1 does not.

**Order bookkeeping and the fidelity measure.** Order n means residual O(ε^{n+1}), hence distance slope n + 1 and **infidelity slope 2(n + 1)**, never 2n; two source abstracts write O(ε^n) loosely against their own equations **[corrected]**. Three measures appear and differ by 2 and 4/3 in infidelity: F_C = |Tr(U_id†U)|/2 (Cummins' quaternion fidelity and Merrill-Brown's minimized-state form are the same number), F_K = ¼|Tr(U_id†U)|² = F_C², and F_avg = (2F_C² + 1)/3, so 1 − F_K = 2(1 − F_C) and 1 − F_avg = (4/3)(1 − F_C) to leading order **[verified]**, **[recomputed here]**. The simulator adopts F_K internally, the normalization Section 6.9's filter functions are built to, and converts on ingest; every published c-coefficient carries the same factor 2. Two printed "fidelities" must be replaced rather than implemented: Low-Yoder-Chuang's F = ‖UV†‖ is identically 1 for a qubit under its own normalized trace norm and Brown-Harrow-Chuang's 1 − ‖V†U‖ identically 0 for unitaries **[corrected]**, **[recomputed here]**. Below E ≈ 10⁻⁸ a subtracted 1 − F hits the float64 floor, so sequences are scored by the operator distance and converted as E²/2, or run in mpmath as the check script does.

**The verified library.** Each family is a list of (θ_l, φ_l) in time order, folded right to left, with the target phase φ_t added rigidly to every entry **including** the zeroth pulse; adding it reproduces Z(φ_t)U_T^{(0)}Z(φ_t)† exactly, where subtracting it (the direction one source's prose suggests) leaves O(ε) **[corrected]**, **[recomputed here]**. The table gives the sequence, the quadrature it corrects, its order, and the printed defect the module repairs; k = arcsin(sin(θ/2)/2) and φ₁ = arccos(−θ/4π) throughout.

| Family | Sequence, time order (θ_t at phase 0 first) | Corrects | Order | Printed defect repaired |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| SK1 (BHC Eqs. 9-10; MB Eq. 30) | (θ, 0), (2π, +φ₁), (2π, −φ₁) | amplitude, addressing | 1 | Eq. 10 prints U_1X(+θ): **first** order, exactly twice the bare-pulse error. Use U_1X(−θ); keep Eq. 9's own U_1X(a) = R₀(+aε) and take the ceiling on ∣a∣ **[corrected]** |
| SKn (BHC Eqs. 13-14; MB Eqs. 36-38) | M_{A_n}⁻¹ U_nX(‖A_n‖^{1/n}) M_{A_n} SK(n−1) | as SK1 | n | ‖·‖ is the **spectral** norm (trace norm is 2× and breaks cancellation by 2^{1/n}); M_{A_n} is the **imperfect** pulse; the index k of A_k, U_kX, M_{A_k} is never mixed with k + 1; U_nY conjugates with π, not the printed π/2; the SK2 rhombus needs φ_γ = arcsin(−sin 2φ_SK1/2) **[corrected]** |
| BB1 = B2 = W1 (Wimperis; MB Eq. 43) | (θ, 0), (π, φ₁), (2π, 3φ₁), (π, φ₁) | amplitude | 2 | none in Eq. 43; the **minus** in the arccos and the **3φ₁** middle phase are both load-bearing **[verified]** |
| NB1 = N2 (MB Eq. 42) | (θ, 0), (π, φ₁), (2π, −φ₁), (π, φ₁) | addressing | 2 | differs from B2 only in −φ against 3φ, the commonest transcription bug; one code path with a phase-map argument **[verified]** |
| PB1 = P2 = PD2 (MB Sec. 4.2.1) | (θ, 0), (2π, φ_P2), (2π, −φ_P2), (2π, −φ_P2), (2π, φ_P2), φ_P2 = arccos(−θ/8π) | both, reduced efficacy | 2 | **five** pulses, not four: the (1,2,1) three-pulse closure does not apply, and forcing it is 3700× worse than P2 and 16× worse than no correction **[corrected]** |
| P2j, N2j, B2j (MB Eqs. 44-50) | T_{2j}(1, φ_{P2j}) after (θ, 0), φ_{P2j} = arccos(−θ/(2πf_j)) | amplitude (P, B), addressing (N) | 2j | **f_j = (2^{2j−1} − 2)f_{j−1}**, f₁ = 4 (P) or 2 (N, B): 4, 24, 720, 90720 and 2, 12, 360, 45360. The printed −1 destroys first-order compensation and drops the slope from 10 to 2. The odd-k B2j bottom layer needs a fourth pulse, M(kπ,φ)M(kπ,3φ)M(kπ,3φ)M(kπ,φ); as printed it is not palindromic and is 1200× worse than no correction **[corrected]** |
| CORPSE (CLJ Eqs. 13-15; MB Eq. 55) | (2π + θ/2 − k, 0), (2π − 2k, π), (θ/2 − k, 0) | detuning | 1 | none; n = (1,1,0) gives 420°/300°/60° at θ = π and only n₁ − n₂ + n₃ = 0 returns +R(θ,0) **[verified]** |
| short-CORPSE | (θ/2 − k, 0), (2π − 2k, π), (θ/2 − k, 0) | detuning | 1 | as CORPSE, n = (0,1,0) **[verified]** |
| SCROFULOUS (CLJ Eqs. 18-23) | (θ₁, φ_S), (π, φ_S − arccos(−π/2θ₁)), (θ₁, φ_S), θ₁ = arcsinc(2cos(θ/2)/π), φ_S = arccos(−π cos θ₁/(2θ₁ sin(θ/2))) | amplitude | 1 | arcsinc branch (0, π] with arcsinc(0) = π, and the **minus** in φ₂ is load-bearing: the plus branch gives 180° at θ = 180°, against the known 180₆₀ 180₃₀₀ 180₆₀ **[corrected]** |
| reduced CinSK (Kabytayev Table I) | CORPSE block, then (2π, −φ₁), (2π, +φ₁) | **both at once** | 1 in each | none; slope 4 under ε_a = ε_d where SK1, BB1 and CORPSE fall to 2 **[verified]** |
| reduced CinBB (Kabytayev Table I) | CORPSE block, then (π, φ₁), (2π, 3φ₁), (π, φ₁) | **both at once** | 1 in each, 2 in amplitude | none; retains BB1's sixth-order amplitude coefficient 9.388566 while holding slope 4 under simultaneous error **[verified]** |

Leading coefficients at θ = π in 1 − F_K, all **[recomputed here]**: primitive (π²/4)ε_a² and ε_d² exactly; SK1 22.830256 ε_a⁴ = (π² sin 2φ₁)²; BB1 and CinBB 9.388566 ε_a⁶; SCROFULOUS 4.566051 ε_a⁴ but 4.000000 ε_d², four times the primitive detuning sensitivity, where BB1 pays essentially nothing there and is the safe default when both channels are live; CORPSE 6.5008 × 10⁻³ ε_d⁴, degraded 115-fold to 0.75 ε_d⁴ by either concatenation. CORPSE is asymptotically fourth order but has a wide intermediate regime where the ε_D³ term dominates, so a fit at ε_D ≈ 0.05 legitimately returns 4.5 to 6.0 and the exponent must be fitted below 10⁻⁴ **[verified]**. The module root-finds every Trotter-Suzuki phase on the leading ε coefficient rather than hard-coding f_j, asserts the printed anchors φ_P2 = arccos(−θ/8π) and φ_P4 = arccos(−θ/48π), and caches T_{2j−2} as one 2 × 2 matrix raised to integer powers (P4 = 37 pulses, P6 = 1189); palindromic ordering kills every even-order term, so only even orders exist in that ladder.

**The Low-Yoder-Chuang construction** supplies the general-θ closed forms the plan previously had from nowhere. A length-L = 2n sequence 𝒮 = ∏_j M_{φ_j}[θ₀] at θ₀ = 2π multiplies the target M₀[θ_T], with γ = θ_T/θ₀ and x = εθ₀/2. **PD_n** is palindromic (φ_k = φ_{L−k+1}, killing Im Φ for **even** j) and **AP_n** antipalindromic (φ_k = −φ_{L−k+1}, killing it for **odd** j) - a parity easy to invert - and only half the phases are tabulated. Under t_k = tan(φ_k/2) each family is at most quartic in t₁² and so closed-form solvable: AP1 gives cos φ₁ = −γ/2 = −θ_T/4π (**this is SK1**), PD2 gives cos φ₁ = −γ/4 = −θ_T/8π with t₂ = −t₁ (**this is PB1**), AP2 and PD4 have printed coefficient polynomials, and **ToP_n** gives exact seeds at γ = 2b from which arbitrary γ follows by numerical continuation wherever det J ≠ 0, so the standard names are aliases of these forms rather than reimplementations **[verified]**, **[recomputed here]**. The printed reality domain |γ| ≤ 2⌊n/2⌋ + 2 is a necessary outer bound only (PD4 is real only for |γ| ≤ 4), and PD_n exists only for even n **[corrected]**. The **ε-free certificate** is Eq. 11: a sequence is order n if and only if **Φ_L^j(φ) = f_L^j(γ) for 0 < j ≤ n**, where Φ_L^j = Σ_{h₁<…<h_j}exp(−iΣ_k(−1)^kφ_{h_k}) and f_L^j(γ) = Σ_k(−1)^k C(T, k)C(L − T, j − k) with T = (γ + L)/2 a generally non-integer generalized binomial. The alternating sign runs over the **position** k within the chosen subset, not over the pulse index, and the sum is over combinations, not permutations - the most error-prone point in the formalism; fill Φ by its dynamic-programming recursion, never by enumerating subsets. This certifies a candidate with no ε sweep and no propagator solve, and it **fails** at j = n + 1, pinning the order at exactly n **[verified]**, **[recomputed here]**. Broadband BB_n at θ₀ = π comes from the PD_n phases through the toggling map ψ_k = −Σ_{h<k}(−1)^hφ_h + Σ_{h>k}(−1)^hφ_h, which forces L ∈ 4ℤ and hence even n, while narrowband uses the original phases unchanged; the strongest cross-paper check available is that toggled PD2 at γ = 1 gives ψ = (φ₁, 3φ₁, 3φ₁, φ₁) with φ₁ = arccos(−1/4), which is Wimperis's BB1 at θ = π (distance slope 3.000 against 1.000 untoggled) **[verified]**, **[recomputed here]**.

**PDn, the Mount anchors, and the order ladder as a test.** The plan's PDn row stays: the palindromic family with 2n π pulses cancels area error to O(ε^n) and its tabulated PD6 phases are indexed by the target angle θ_t, not the target phase (a source typo) **[corrected]**. Run 5 supplies the general-θ_t construction Section 12 listed as missing, through PD_n above, so PD6 is no longer a background entry, although its phase vector at arbitrary γ still needs the numerical continuation and is not a printed formula. Measured anchors are unchanged: B2-compensated gates stay below 1% error for |ε| < 0.4 and PD6 for |ε| < 0.6; randomized benchmarking F(L) = A₀p^L + B₀ with error (1 − p)/2 gave 3.6(3) × 10⁻⁴ per Clifford on that comb-driven system **[verified]**, and 1.0(3) × 10⁻⁶ for Harty's microwave gates **[verified]**. Duration at constant amplitude is τ_seq = Σ_l θ_l/Ω, so τ_SK1 = τ_BB1 = (4π + θ)/Ω, τ_CORPSE = (4π + θ − 4k)/Ω and τ_CinSK = τ_CinBB = (8π + θ − 4k)/Ω (the CORPSE list sums to 4π + θ − 4k and each concatenation appends 4π of correctors, so at θ = π, k = π/6 the pulse list 420° + 300° + 60° + 360° + 360° = 1500° = 8π + π/3; the second revision printed 8π + 2θ − 4k, 12% long at θ = π, and `check_composite.py` now asserts every family's Σ_l θ_l against these forms; 2026-09-04 experimentalist critique) **[corrected: critique, 2026-09-04]**, **[recomputed here]**, and the scheduler quotes both infidelity and duration because total rotation sets the scattering and heating exposure of Sections 6.5 and 6.2; no source gives a rule for choosing a family from a measured (ε_A, ε_D, ε_N) triple, so that rule is the simulator's own (Section 12). The shipped regression is the measured order ladder in 1 − F_K at θ = π (`check_composite.py`, Section 9.15): primitive 2 in both quadratures, SK1 4 amplitude and 2 detuning, BB1 6 and 2, CORPSE 2 and 4, SCROFULOUS 4 and 2, reduced CinSK and CinBB 4 under simultaneous ε_a = ε_d **[verified]**, **[recomputed here]**. These residuals are the ideal-limit **floor** of the 2 × 2 algebra: none of these papers has a Hamiltonian, an ħ or a time, so off-resonant sideband coupling, the Debye-Waller factor, leakage, spontaneous emission, the correctors' own ac Stark shift and heating over a five- to eightfold longer sequence are all outside them, and are separated from the composite-pulse residual by running the same pulse list through the Section 4.3.1 Hamiltonian and differencing.

#### 4.3.6 Micromotion in the drive

For an ion displaced from the rf null by a stray field, the drive seen by the ion is phase modulated at Ω_T with index φ_Ω = (q_x/2)[k_x Δ_x − k_y Δ_y] (Wineland 1998 Eqs. 71-72; the symbol is φ_Ω, which pdftotext renders as φ_V **[corrected]**), reducing every carrier matrix element by J₀(φ_Ω) and adding micromotion sidebands **[verified]**, consistent with Berkeland's J_n²(β) spectrum in Section 4.1. The builder applies this as an optional multiplicative modulation of the drive coefficient; the intrinsic-micromotion factor C₀ (Section 4.1.1) is already inside the η the builder receives from `Crystal.lamb_dicke` and is never reapplied here (2026-09-04 architect critique). The modulation e^{iβ cos(ω_rf t + δ)} also needs the pulse start time relative to the trap rf: a drive flagged `rf_locked` carries an rf phase reference and keeps the coherent sideband, an unlocked one averages δ per shot so that only J₀ and the J_n² weights survive (`Drive.rf_phase`, Appendix E), and the default without a declared reference is the unlocked average, because a result that depended on the integrator's time origin would be an artifact (Section 12 had listed the rule as absent; 2026-09-04 experimentalist critique) **[corrected: critique, 2026-09-04]**.

#### 4.3.7 Frequency-comb (mode-locked) Raman drives

Every 355 nm Raman system on 171Yb+ drives the qubit with a mode-locked pulse train, not two continuous-wave tones, so the chain of Sections 4.3.2 and 4.5.4 becomes a sum over comb-tooth pairs. Run 5S (Appendix D) read six clusters for this - Hayes 2010, Islam 2014, Inlek 2014, Mizrahi 2013 with its Appl. Phys. B companion of 2014, Campbell 2010, Lee 2016 - for 63 model entries, a third of them corrected on transcription or on the sources' own printed errors. The set is one species at one wavelength; the module takes (ν_rep, τ, Δ, species, geometry) as inputs, and Section 9.15 holds its numbers.

**The idealized train and its comb.** A train of N identical pulses at a fixed point is E(t) = Σ_{n=1}^{N} f(t − nT) e^{iω_c t} (Hayes Eq. 1) **[verified]**, whose unnormalized forward transform f̃(ω − ω_c) Σ_n e^{−i(ω−ω_c)nT} has teeth at ω_c + 2πmν_R spaced by ν_R = 1/T. The carrier exponential sits **outside** the sum: that is the zero-carrier-envelope-offset idealization, and writing e^{iω_c(t−nT)} instead reintroduces a per-pulse phase e^{−inω_cT}, a nonzero offset frequency **[verified]**. A Λ-configuration Raman process needs no carrier-envelope phase at all - the qubit sees only tooth-frequency *differences*, and f_ceo cancels between two copies of one comb **[verified]** - so the simulator carries only the inter-comb (AOM) phase, which is the spin-dependent-force phase. Tooth width follows the Dirichlet kernel |sin(Nϑ/2)/sin(ϑ/2)|: exact FWHM 1.206716 ν_R/N in amplitude and 0.885899 ν_R/N in power at N = 256 **[recomputed here]**; the widely quoted 0.886 is the intensity value, so code holding amplitudes uses 1.2067. The sources state only the scaling and never a linewidth (Section 12).

**Tooth amplitudes and the intensity sum rule.** Tooth k from the comb centre has g_k = g₀ √(πν_rep τ) sech(2πk ν_rep τ) with g₀² = Σ_k g_k² = γ²Ī/(2I_sat) (Lee Eq. 1, identical to Mizrahi Eq. 20 under ω_rep = 2πν_rep) **[verified]**, γ **angular** and Ī the **peak on-axis time-averaged** intensity 2P̄/(πw₀²) with w₀ the 1/e² intensity radius - unstated but forced by reproducing that source's own second-order shift, since P̄/(πw₀²) halves g₀² **[corrected]**. τ is the sech **field**-envelope parameter, never a width, and its envelope is sech(πt/(2τ)): the tooth formula sech(2πk ν_rep τ) is the Fourier transform of a field sech(πt/(2τ)) (FT[sech(at)] ∝ sech(πω/2a), and a = π/2τ gives sech(ωτ) = sech(2πkν_repτ) at ω = 2πkν_rep), so the field FWHM is (4/π) arccosh(2) τ = 1.6768 τ and the intensity (sech²) FWHM is (4/π) arccosh(√2) τ = 1.1222 τ; the second revision printed 0.8384 τ and 0.5611 τ, the widths of sech(πt/τ), whose teeth would read sech(πkν_repτ), so a τ inferred from a measured pulse width was off by 2 and with it every sech(ω_qτ/2) suppression and the 27% pair-versus-single test (`check_critique_v3.py`, which also checks the transform pair numerically; 2026-09-04 theorist critique) **[corrected: critique, 2026-09-04]**, **[recomputed here]**. The sum rule is asymptotic in ν_rep τ, not algebraic (1.000000000000 at ν_rep τ = 1.68 × 10⁻³, 3.14 at 1) **[recomputed here]**, so the module builds the sech profile to |k| ~ several hundred and renormalizes to it rather than trusting the √(πν_rep τ) prefactor. Hayes' equivalent E₀ is **fluence**-normalized, ∫|f|²dt = E₀²τ against a peak f(0) = √(π/2)E₀ = 1.25331414 E₀, so a measured peak passed as E₀ overstates Ω₀ and Ī by π/2 = 1.5708 **[corrected]**.

**The tooth-pair sum and the two-photon Rabi frequency.** All pairs of separation l add coherently at the beat note l(2πν_rep): Ω^{(l)} = Σ_k g_k g_{k+l}/(2Δ) ≈ Ω₀ sech(πl ν_rep τ), Ω₀ = g₀²/(2Δ) **[verified]**. The *pair* argument is half the *single-tooth* one - at l = 105, ν_rep = 120 MHz, τ = 14 ps, sech(πlν_repτ) = 0.863911 against sech(2πlν_repτ) = 0.595332, a 27% error and the cheapest flag for a factor-2 bug **[recomputed here; corrected]** - and is itself 5% high against the exact convolution, so the module records the ratio as a unit test rather than asserting equality **[corrected]**. Three closed forms circulate and must not be harmonized: sech(ω_qτ/2) = 1 − x²/8 (Mizrahi Eq. 22, from the exact time-domain Rosen-Zener solution), (ω_qτ)/sinh(ω_qτ) = 1 − x²/6 (the exact convolution of two sech spectra), and (ω_qτ/2)/sinh(ω_qτ/2) = 1 − x²/24 (Hayes, whose field-sech τ is twice the Lee-family τ for one pulse) **[contested]**; the first two give 0.925994 and 0.902128 at 80 MHz and 10 ps, a 2.65% gap **[recomputed here]**, and the plan implements sech(ω_qτ/2) and reports the gap. **Chain I against Chain II.** One source prints the intensity-to-Rabi calibration two ways, Ω = g²/(2Δ) with g = γ√(I/(2I_sat)) and Ω = g²/Δ; both are internally self-consistent, differ by exactly 2 in every Rabi rate, and no source resolves it **[contested]**. The plan's Section 13 row Ω_R = Σ_e Ω₂*Ω₁/(2Δ_e) selects **Chain I**, a choice derived from the plan's own convention and endorsed by no paper (**[background]**; Section 12 keeps it contested), so the printed π-pulse energy is a factor 2 too small: E_π = π²I_sat w²Δ/γ² is 13.561 nJ on Chain II, matching the printed ~12 nJ, against 27.122 nJ on Chain I at I_sat = 1500 W/m², w = 10 µm, γ = 2π × 19.6 MHz, Δ = 2π × 22.11 THz **[recomputed here]**, and Hayes' Ω₀ = sγ²/(2Δ) is halved at ingest. The fine-structure detunings are **signed**, Δ_{1/2} = +33 THz and Δ_{3/2} = −67 THz at 355 nm, so 1/Δ = 1/Δ_{1/2} − 1/Δ_{3/2} **adds** to Δ/2π = 22.1100 THz where magnitudes give 65.029 THz, a factor 2.941 in Ω **[recomputed here]**.

**Resonance conditions.** For a single comb, ω_q = n ω_rep with n integer, equivalently q ≡ ω₀/(2πν_R) ∈ ℤ **[verified]**, and the integer/half-integer dichotomy under a pulse picker is the test: q = 156.5090, 313.0181 and 469.5213 at Hayes' 80.78 MHz and its 1-in-2 and 1-in-3 picked rates, with qubit evolution only at the integer **[recomputed here]**. Integer q is necessary, not sufficient; the rate is suppressed unless the pulse bandwidth exceeds ω_q. Two comb copies offset by an acousto-optic difference frequency give ω_q = n ω_rep ± ω_A (Mizrahi Eq. 29) and, with a sideband, |2πjν_R + Δω| = ω₀ ± ω_t, j a signed integer and Δω = 2π(ν₁ − ν₂) signed **[verified]**; the sign of Δω selects red from blue and is the primary control input, so the module root-finds this rather than hard-coding a tooth index. In ordinary-frequency (AOM/PLL) form the same statement is |jν_rep + Δν_M| = ν_ab ± ν_t, Islam's branch being ν_sb = nν_rep − |Δν_M| = ν_LO + ν_M2, time independent because ν_LO and ν_M2 are synthesizer frequencies, subject to nν_rep > ν_LO (stated) and ν_M1 > ν_M2 (implicit, and what lets the outer absolute value be dropped) **[corrected]**. The lock arithmetic is the test: 157 × 80.6 MHz = 12.65420 GHz, ν_ab/ν_rep = 156.8588 so n = 157, ν_M1 = 216.200 MHz (printed ~217) and ν_M2 = 204.819 MHz (printed ~205) **[recomputed here]**. Inlek's two-tooth Mølmer-Sørensen conditions close with n = 160 on the **red** leg and m = 154 on the **blue**, swapped in extraction with a 483.42 MHz miss **[corrected]**, and the feed-forward chain ν_PLL = n[ν_rep + δ_r(t)] − ν_MO gives 285.20, 198.22 and 43.49 MHz for the red, blue and copropagating-carrier loops at ν_MO = 12.606 GHz, with loop-closing AWG frequencies 111.83 and 38.19 MHz **[recomputed here]**.

**How the drive enters the builder, and when the mapping is legal.** A comb drive is a **set of tones** in the sense of Section 5.2: H = Σ_j (Ω_j/2)[e^{i((jω_rep + ω_A − ω_q)t + φ₀)} σ₊^{(i)} ⊗ Π_m D_m(iη_{im}) + h.c.] with Ω_j = Ω₀ sech(jω_rep τ/2) - one drive term per beat note, all sharing the same time-independent operator σ₊ ⊗ Π_m D_m(iη), each with its own scalar coefficient **[verified]**. The resonant tooth pair is the tone at j = n; the off-resonant pairs at multiples of the repetition rate are the remaining tones with sech² weights. Near-resonant tones stay explicit as `QobjEvo` coefficients while only far-detuned ones fold into the static fourth-order shift below, never both **[verified]**; the cut is explicit: a tone pair stays explicit when |μ_j − ω_res| ≲ 10/t_g (about 100 kHz for a 100 µs gate) and everything beyond folds into the l-sum shift, with a convergence test that moves the cut by 2× and reports the change in the gate phase, because the off-resonant beat notes sit at multiples of ω_rep = 2π × 120 MHz and retaining even the nearest one raises the highest frequency in the frame from about 3 MHz to 120 MHz, a 40× step-count multiplier that turns the Section 11.1 cost of a 100 µs gate at dimension 2048 into hours (2026-09-04 numerics critique) **[corrected: critique, 2026-09-04]**. Mount 2015's three-term model (Section 4.3.5), the resonant pair plus off-resonant pairs at δ′ and 2δ′ with weights 1 : 2 : 1, is that tone set truncated to three beat notes with the sech² envelope flattened, which its 0.53% variation per tooth index licenses **[recomputed here]**; Mount 2015 is outside the Run 5S source set, so the identification is derived (**[background]**) and the Mount propagator stays the composite-pulse error model of Section 4.3.5. The **validity hierarchy** of the pulse-train-to-continuous-wave mapping is a runtime guard logged before every solve **[verified]**: N ≫ 1 (teeth resolved); ω_q τ ≪ 1 (the pulse bandwidth must straddle the qubit splitting or no pair drives the transition); ω_q ≪ 1/τ ≪ |Δ| ≲ ω_FS (adiabatic elimination legitimate, and Δ not far above the fine structure or the D1/D2 amplitudes cancel); θ ≪ 1 per pulse; ω_rep τ ≪ 1 (the sum rule); and ω_t t_N ≫ 1 with η√(n̄+1) ≪ 1 for the resolved-sideband picture against ω_t t_N ≪ 1 for the ultrafast kick. The selector between the two pictures is **train duration against the trap period plus the Lamb-Dicke condition**, not the Bessel-order ratio J₂ ≪ J₁, which holds throughout the ultrafast source **[corrected]**; no source bounds the crossover, so the guard routes to the exact pulse-by-pulse propagator instead of interpolating (Section 12). The single-pulse ceiling is the exact Rosen-Zener result P_{0→1} = sin²(πu) sech²(πν_HF T_pulse) (Campbell Eq. 6; Mizrahi Eq. 18), reproducing the measured 72% and 91% as 0.720860 at 14.8 ps and 0.914142 at 7.6 ps, where reading ν_HF as angular gives 0.002474 - the discriminator that pins that source's H/h ordinary-frequency convention **[recomputed here]**. There is **one** sech(ω_qτ/2) factor, not three: the Rosen-Zener contrast, the finite-duration reduction of the kick area and the tooth-pair suppression are one amplitude factor seen in the time and frequency domains, all 0.925994 at τ = 10 ps, and applying it twice or three times multiplies the suppression spuriously **[corrected]**. The pulse-count map is N = f_rep t and Ω₀ = f_rep θ, the printed 2π-prefactored forms being wrong by (2π)² = 39.478 under that paper's own angular convention **[recomputed here; corrected]**.

**Spin-motion coupling in the two regimes.** In the slow regime the exact one-period propagator is V = e^{−iH₀T} exp[−i(θ_p/2)(σ₊e^{iΔk x̂} + h.c.)] with H₀ = ω_t a†a + ½(ω₀ + Δω)σ_z, the beat-note offset absorbed into the qubit term and the kick left Δω-free (Hayes Eqs. 4-6) **[verified]**; at first order in θ_p and inside the Lamb-Dicke regime the pulse sum becomes a grating, Σ_n Q_n ≈ iη [sin(Nϑ_r/2)/sin(ϑ_r/2)] e^{iϑ_r(N−1)/2} σ₊a + h.c. with ϑ_r = (ω₀ + Δω − ω_t)T = 2πj - an N-slit selection rule of peak value N **[verified]**. The θ_p/2 belongs to the V^N expansion, not the Dirichlet factor, so the sideband coupling over the train is Ω_sb = θ_p η N/2, not ηN **[corrected]**. In the fast regime a simultaneous counterpropagating pulse pair is an impulsive Kapitza-Dirac kick, H = −Θ_B δ(t − t₀) sin[Δk x̂ + φ(t₀)] σ_x, whose propagator is exact in η by Jacobi-Anger, U_{t₀} = exp{iΘ_B sin(Δk x̂ + φ)σ_x} = Σ_n e^{inφ} J_n(Θ_B) D(inη) σ_x^n, needing no Lamb-Dicke assumption because D(ia)D(ib) = D(i(a+b)) exactly **[verified]**. The printed pairing of an Ω/2 Hamiltonian prefactor with Bessel argument θ = ∫Ω dt cannot hold: the identity closes to 10⁻¹⁴ only when the Bessel argument equals the exponent coefficient **[recomputed here; corrected]**, so the plan carries **one** symbol Θ_B *defined* as the Bessel argument, with a perfect kick at total train area Σ_k Θ_{B,k} = π - read literally the printed Hamiltonian moves that to 2π and saturates near 50% at π. The ideal kick U_SDK = e^{iφ′}D(iη)σ_∓ − e^{−iφ′}D(−iη)σ_± is unitary for *either* relative sign (σ_±² = 0 kills the cross terms); the minus makes it anti-Hermitian with U² = −𝟙, the algebraic reason the area must be π **[recomputed here; corrected]**. No source gives η numerically except Hayes' 0.1, and Hayes writes e^{ikx̂} with a bare k, yet 0.1 closes only with Δk = √2 k (0.1063 against 0.0751 for bare k at 1.64 MHz), so that paper's "k" *is* the two-photon Δk **[recomputed here; corrected]**; with Δk = 2k, η = 0.2233 at 743 kHz, and η√(n̄+1) = 1.74 at the assumed n̄ = 40 makes the Lamb-Dicke branch illegal and the full cos(Δk x̂) drive mandatory **[recomputed here]**. The blue-sideband generator M̂ = ie^{iφ₀}a†σ₊ − ie^{−iφ₀}aσ₋ is Hermitian with M̂² = n̂ + 1 on one spin block and n̂ on the other, so its printed cos/sin closed form is exact only on the n = 0 → 1 manifold and the rate is Θη√(n+1) per {|n,↓⟩, |n+1,↑⟩} block, never Θη **[recomputed here; corrected]**.

**Mølmer-Sørensen gates and the phase structure.** With two tones symmetrically detuned by δ from the red and blue sidebands, single-loop closure is δ = 2ηΩ with t_g = 2π/δ, that is ηΩ/δ = ½ **[verified]**, Ω being the per-tone resonant *carrier* Rabi frequency; this is the plan's own closure ηΩ/ε = 1/(2√K) of Section 4.4.1 at K = 1, which the spin-operator normalization does not change (Section 13; the second revision's 'halved on ingest' rule was the factor-2 error the derivation audit of 2026-09-04 removed). In the phase-insensitive geometry the two legs carry separate wavevector differences, Δk_r = k_A − k_{B,r} and Δk_b = k_{B,b} − k_A, with φ_G = φ_{S,i} + φ_{S,j}, φ_{S,i} = −½(Δk_r·X_i − Δφ_r + Δk_b·X_i − Δφ_b) and φ_{M,i} = +½(Δk_r·X_i − Δφ_r − Δk_b·X_i + Δφ_b) (Inlek Eqs. 4-5, whose two printed lines of Eq. 4 disagree by a factor 2; both self-consistent readings give identical φ_S and φ_M, so the second line and Eq. 5 are implemented) **[contested]**. Because Δk_r + Δk_b = k_{B,b} − k_{B,r} contains no k_A, the k_A rf phase and any k_A path drift cancel identically and φ_G ≈ φ_{B,b} − φ_{B,r}, whereas the sensitive geometry gives φ_G ≈ φ_{B,r} + φ_{B,b} − 2φ_A and keeps the drifting position term **[verified]** - the geometric criterion of Section 4.3.4 and the spin/motion split of Section 4.4.2, with the plan's Haljan sign φ_s = +(φ_b + φ_r)/2 applied after conversion. The path dependence is *transferred* to φ_{M,i}, not destroyed, so each ion carries its own φ_{M,i} from its own equilibrium position and the displacement is driven with e^{iφ_{M,i}}; the regression test is that a global path drift leaves fidelity unchanged, which bites only if φ_M is per-ion and is not folded into the spin phase. Wave-front alignment enters through λ′ = 2π/Δk ≈ λ/√2 = 251.0 nm at a 90° crossing with Δφ_M = 2πl sin θ/λ′, and the source's own triple does not close - 30 µm at θ = 0.02° gives 15.0° end-to-end against a stated "< 10°", and θ < 0.0133° is what is needed **[recomputed here; corrected]** - so the module implements the formula with an explicit end-to-end convention rather than an angle.

**ac Stark shifts, second and fourth order.** At second order E^{(2)}_{00} = (g₀²/12)(1/Δ − 2/(ω_F − Δ)) and E^{(2)}_{10} is the same with Δ → Δ + ω_HF, so the differential enters the comb only through g₀² by the sum rule and is comb-shape independent **[verified]**. The 1/12 is a second-order 1/4 times a 1/3 scalar polarization average, and the −2 is the (2J′+1) line-strength ratio *times* a sign from writing the P_{3/2} detuning as the positive ω_F − Δ; in signed form both weights are positive, (g₀²/12)(1/Δ_{1/2} + 2/Δ_{3/2}), and pairing −2 with one's own signed Δ_{3/2} double-counts it, inflating E^{(2)}_{00} by a factor 133 at the near-total D1/D2 cancellation of 355 nm (1/33 + 2/(−67) = 0.00045 THz⁻¹ against 1/33 + 2/67 = 0.0602 THz⁻¹, both positive; the second revision printed this as a sign flip from about +0.1 to −2 MHz, which neither reading gives; `check_critique_v3.py`) **[corrected]**, **[corrected: critique, 2026-09-04]**, **[recomputed here]**. The printed −7.3 kHz is an **ordinary** frequency δω^{(2)}/2π, recomputed as −7.63 kHz **[recomputed here; corrected]**. At fourth order (second order in intensity) E^{(4)}_n = −Σ_{a≠n} Ω_{n,a}²/(4δ_{n,a}) with δ_{n,a} = ω_a − j(2πν_rep) the residual detuning of the *nearest* beat note, j = argmin_j|ω_a − 2πjν_rep|, and δ_{a,n} = −δ_{n,a}; the printed plus sign paired with the printed δ is self-contradictory, disagreeing with that paper's own three-level result, so exactly one of (minus, δ = splitting − beat note) or (plus, δ = beat note − splitting) is implemented **[corrected]**. The comb generalization multiplies each term by C_{n,a} = Σ_k sech²((j+k)πν_repτ)/(1 − k(2πν_rep)/δ_{n,a}), with Ω_{n,a} the polarization-weighted Ω₀ carrying **no** sech factor - the sech² weights live inside the k sum, so applying sech twice is a silent factor-0.75 error **[corrected]** - and C is neither normalized to one nor small, its k = 0 term alone being 0.746343 at j = 105. Its convergence is the trap: at ν_rep = 120 MHz, τ = 14 ps and ω_HF/2π = 12.642821 GHz (j = 105, δ_{00,10}/2π = +42.8210 MHz) the partial sums run 0.746343, 0.453162, 0.450920, 0.669087, 0.940671, 0.943780, 0.943789 for |k| ≤ 0, 5, 10, 100, 500, 1000, 5000 **[recomputed here]**, because the sech² weight is centred at k = −j with half-width 1/(πν_repτ) ≈ 189: the |k| ~ 5 to 10 plateau is a factor 2.093 **low** and a successive-partial-sum test converges falsely there, so the module sums to |k| ≳ 1000 and validates by doubling. The guard is δ_{n,a} ≠ 0, **never** j ≠ 0: j = 0 is the operative case for the Zeeman partners (ω_Zee/2π ≈ 7 MHz < ν_rep/2), where C_{10,11} = C_{10,1−1} = 0.988841 exactly, and that equality makes the elliptical-polarization cancellation E^{(4)}_{10} = 0 survive the comb generalization; a j ≠ 0 guard deletes exactly those terms **[recomputed here; corrected]**. The module implements the algebraically identical single sum over the separation l = j + k, E^{(4)}_n = Σ_{a≠n} (Ω_{n,a}²/4) Σ_l sech²(πlν_repτ)/(ω_a − l(2πν_rep)), which removes j and δ_{n,a} entirely so the guard error cannot be made (**[background]**, derived by reindexing). For a **single comb** with the resonant beat note excluded, the conditionally convergent bilateral sum δ₄ = −Σ_{j≠n} Ω_j²/(2(jω_rep − ω_q)) must be symmetrized in ±j pairs, and that symmetrization produces the closed form δ₄ ≈ 0.853 Ω₀²ω_qτ/ω_rep with 0.853 = ∫₀^∞ sech²v tanh v/v dv = 7ζ(3)/π² = 0.852556797635 **[recomputed here]**; the signed sum already contains the counter-rotating Bloch-Siegert term as its j = −n member and must not double-count it **[verified]**, and the closed form is leading order only (effective coefficient 0.756 at τ = 10 ps) **[recomputed here]**. For **two offset combs** the fractional offset σ = ω̃_A/ω_rep, ω̃_A = ω_A mod ω_rep, can null the shift; the printed counter-rotating sum needs a plus, not the printed minus, and the closed form's smooth term is exactly twice the single-comb coefficient, 3.410227 = 4 × 7ζ(3)/π². Its nulls are roots of a three-pole truncation, not of the exact sum - 0.353353 at τ = 5 ps, 0.399342 at 10 ps, 1/√12 = 0.288675 as τ → 0, against the exact 0.315397, 0.366248 and 1/4 - so the Stark-nulling AOM offset is root-found on the exact sum, subject to the excluded offsets ω_A ≠ kω_rep and ≠ (k+½)ω_rep that keep a spin-dependent force available **[recomputed here; corrected]**. The series is **marginal at full power**: the source never states |Ω_{n,a}| ≪ |δ_{n,a}|, and at its own operating point Ω₀/2π = 45.7 MHz *exceeds* δ_{00,10}/2π = 42.8 MHz, so the expansion parameter is order unity and nothing bounds the error; the module offers a non-perturbative check by direct diagonalization of the tooth-pair-coupled ground manifold and does not claim the closed form at full power (Section 12) **[contested]**.

**Comb noise channels and where they enter.** Three channels are comb-specific, each mapping onto one entry point of Section 6.1.

- **Harmonic-amplified repetition-rate drift.** The beat note is a tooth-index *difference*, so ν_sb = nν_rep − |Δν_M| has ∂ν_sb/∂ν_rep = n and δν_sb = n δν_rep: at n = 157 a free-running 1 Hz/min becomes 157 Hz/min = 2.617 Hz/s at the qubit **[verified]**, and a 10⁻⁷ fractional drift at 80 MHz becomes 1.26 kHz **[recomputed here]**. It is driven from a ν_rep(t) process, not an ad-hoc qubit-frequency noise term, so the gain emerges rather than being asserted: the slow part is option (c), a quasi-static per-shot sample, the 1 Hz to 10 kHz band option (d), a sampled trajectory. Drift alone is insufficient - it accumulates 0.008 Hz over the measured 3 ms unlocked Ramsey time - so a fast jitter is fitted to that 3 ms, ~53 Hz rms on the beat note (derived, not sourced) **[corrected]**.
- **Feed-forward per-tooth residuals.** The lock cancels drift algebraically to all orders, but only for the selected tooth's lower sideband: on tooth m the residual is (m − n) δν_rep for the lower sideband, (m + n) δν_rep for the upper (worse than unlocked) and m δν_rep for a bare tooth **[corrected]**. A tooth-pair sum for a Stark shift or a spin-dependent force applies these coefficients pair by pair, not as a global lock - no source does this - and for the off-resonant Stark drive the sensitivity exceeds the bare tooth-index lever, 360× fractional on δω^{(4)}, of which jν_rep/δ_{00,10} = 294× is the detuning **[recomputed here]**.
- **Intensity noise.** Ω(t) = Ω₀(1 + ξ(t)) with one-sided white S_ξ = α_lin is option (d), with error ε ≈ (π²/2) α Ω₀² T and **Ω₀ in Hz, never rad/s**: at α = −115 dB/Hz, Ω₀ = 600 kHz, T = 1 ms the Hz reading gives 5.6179 × 10⁻³, consistent with the source's own ε < 1%, while rad/s gives 0.2218, wrong by (2π)² = 39.478 **[recomputed here; corrected]**. The relation is dimensionally consistent either way, so only this test settles it; structurally π²/2 = (2π)²/8 = 4.9348022005, the conversion already absorbed. The PLL-achieved −120 dB/Hz is the default, and the prefactor is order-unity-loose, strict power-to-field propagation giving π²/8 **[contested]**.

The static fourth-order shift and any retained off-resonant tones are option (a); the shot-constant beat-note phase φ₀ is option (c), sampled once per shot and never averaged inside a trajectory. Option (b), a Lindblad rate, gets nothing new from this layer, which is the first gap.

**What the comb sources do not supply.** **No spontaneous-emission rate exists in the comb layer at all**: every Hamiltonian in all six sources is post-adiabatic-elimination and carries no Lindblad term (γ is not even defined in one of them except inside Ω₀ = sγ²/(2Δ)), so per-gate Raman and Rayleigh probabilities, branching ratios and P-manifold decay during the pulse are absent. The plan does not take them from these sources: the scattering channels of Section 4.5.5 are computed from the *same* tooth sums, the residual excited population being Σ_k |g_k|²/(4Δ²) over teeth exactly as Section 4.5.4's adiabatic-elimination residual, so drive and dissipator cannot drift apart; until that is validated the simulator bounds rather than computes the fidelity ceiling of a 355 nm comb drive. **No multi-ion comb Mølmer-Sørensen construction exists**: every comb equation in the set is single-ion and single-mode, so modes, participation factors, per-mode η_{im} and phase-space closure come from Sections 4.1.3 and 4.4.3, and the reported two-ion fidelities are consistency checks, not targets. **No wave-front phase-gradient tolerance closes**: only ΔL ≪ cτ ≈ 3.0 mm (printed as c/τ, dimensionally an acceleration **[corrected]**) and the λ′ formula above, whose own numbers do not close. Section 12 carries these, plus the missing noise spectra, the missing numerical η, the missing fourth-order shift for a *resonant* carrier drive, and the (τ, bandwidth) inconsistency that moves C_{00,10} from 0.9438 to 0.7174 **[recomputed here]**.

**Printed source errors corrected here**, for the errata record: the pulse-count-to-Rabi map N = 2πω_rep t and Ω₀ = 2πω_rep θ, wrong by (2π)² = 39.478 under its own angular convention; the multi-level fourth-order Stark sign, needing an overall minus with the antisymmetry δ_{a,n} = −δ_{n,a}, its guard being δ_{n,a} ≠ 0 and never j ≠ 0; the two-comb sum's counter-rotating term, which must carry a plus, and its null offsets, roots of a truncation (0.353, 0.399, 1/√12) rather than of the exact sum (0.315, 0.366, 1/4); the Kapitza-Dirac Hamiltonian and its Bessel expansion, printed inconsistently by a factor 2 in three places; Inlek's red and blue tooth orders, swapped in extraction with a 483.42 MHz miss, and its printed AWG frequencies, each high by exactly 2ν_α = 5.00 MHz; Hayes' E₀, fluence- and not peak-normalized, a factor π/2 = 1.5708; the blue-sideband closed form, which assumes its generator squares to the identity and so omits the √(n+1); and two ordinary-frequency values quoted inside otherwise-angular papers, δω^{(2)}/2π = −7.3 kHz and δω^{(4)}/2π = 23 and 12 MHz, the latter needing an explicit 2π before entering any angular equation, which turns a comfortable 435 ns addressing-raster margin into a marginal 69 ns at ten ions. Smaller ones are listed per source in Section 15, including the path-matching condition printed as c/τ and the three printed clock splittings that are second-order-Zeeman-shifted values rather than the zero-field 12.642812118466 GHz (Section 13).


### 4.4 Two-qubit entangling gates

All entangling gates in the simulator are geometric-phase gates on shared motional modes. The builder of Section 4.3 produces them from ordinary drive terms (two tones, or two beams with a difference frequency near a mode); nothing below is a separate gate implementation. The equations here are what the calibration layer fits and what the validation suite checks, and they fix the conventions.

#### 4.4.1 The Mølmer-Sørensen interaction

**Microscopic Hamiltonian.** Bichromatic light at ω₀ ± δ on N ions coupled to one mode (Sørensen and Mølmer 1999, 2000; Mølmer and Sørensen 1999) **[verified]**:

H₀ = ħν(a†a + ½) + (ħω_eg/2)Σ_i σ_z^i,  H_int = Σ_i Σ_{t=1,2} (ħΩ/2)(σ₊^i e^{i(η_i(a+a†) − ω_t t)} + h.c.),  ω₁ = ω_eg − δ, ω₂ = ω_eg + δ (the ion index on η, the tone index on ω; the papers' single index j covers both),

exact in η (the simulator integrates exactly this, as the original papers did in their numerics **[verified]**). In the Lamb-Dicke regime, dropping the carrier and the far-off-resonant (ν + δ) terms, the interaction is a spin-dependent force f(t)J_y x + g(t)J_y p whose propagator is exact for any f, g because [x, p] is a c-number (Sørensen-Mølmer 2000 Eqs. 6-9) **[verified]**:

U(t) = e^{−iA(t)J_y²} e^{−iF(t)J_y x} e^{−iG(t)J_y p},  F = ∫f, G = ∫g, A = −∫F g.

The same structure in the notation the simulator adopts (Kirchmair et al. 2009 Eqs. 3-4; Haljan et al. 2005 Eq. 1) **[verified]**: with the tones detuned ε = ν − δ from the sideband,

H(t) = −(ħηΩ/2)(a† e^{iεt} + a e^{−iεt}) S_y,  U(t) = D̂(α(t) S_y) exp[i(λt − χ sin(εt)) S_y²],  α(t) = (ηΩ/2ε)(e^{iεt} − 1),  λ = η²Ω²/(4ε),  χ = η²Ω²/(4ε²),

with Ω the per-tone Rabi frequency of the plan's convention (H_tone = (ħΩ/2)σ₊e^{i(...)} + h.c.; to first order in η the blue tone alone feeds a†e^{iεt} and the red tone alone a e^{−iεt}, each with amplitude ηΩ/2), so each eigenstate m of S_y is displaced along a circle of radius |m|ηΩ/(2ε) that closes at εt = 2πm (Haljan's α₀ = Ω_sb/(2ε) with Ω_sb = ηΩ the sideband Rabi frequency) **[verified]**. The first two revisions of this plan displayed −ħηΩ(...)S_y with α(t) = (ηΩ/ε)(e^{iεt} − 1), a form that is correct only for a Rabi frequency defined without the ½ (Ω′ = Ω/2, the reading under which Roos's Ω_c = |ε|/(4η) becomes |ε|/(2η) in the plan's Ω; whether Kirchmair's and Roos's own Ω is that one was not re-verified here, and the ingest converter carries the factor), a doubled force that the derivation audit of 2026-09-04 caught and direct integration confirmed (`check_ms_closure.py`) **[corrected: derivation audit, 2026-09-04]**. The two-ion decomposition D̂(αS_y) = P₀ + P₂D̂(2α) + P₋₂D̂(−2α) with P₀ = 1 − S_y²/4, P_{±2} = (S_y² ± 2S_y)/8 **[verified]** is how the simulator's tests evaluate thermal averages analytically; note that the observable rotation is O_V = V†OV, not VOV† as printed in both Kirchmair 2009 and Roos 2008 **[corrected]**.

**Effective coupling and thermal insensitivity.** Second-order perturbation theory gives Ω̃ = −(Ωη)²/(ν − δ) for the bichromatic |ggn⟩ ↔ |een⟩ coupling (twice the monochromatic value −(Ωη)²/(2(ν−δ)) of the 1999 PRL's Eq. 3; the papers reuse the symbol **[verified]**), independent of n because the (n+1) path through |n+1⟩ and the n path through |n−1⟩ enter with opposite-sign denominators and interfere destructively **[verified]**. The multiparticle form is H = 4(χ/ħ)J_x² with χ = η²Ω²ν/(2(ν² − δ²)) in a convention where J carries ħ, producing the GHZ state at t = π/(8χ) **[verified]**; the normalization must travel with χ. Loop closure and maximal entanglement: G(τ) = F(τ) = 0 with (ν − δ)τ = 2πK and A(τ) = −π/2, giving ηΩ/(ν − δ) = 1/(2√K) and τ = √K π/(ηΩ) in Sørensen-Mølmer's spin normalization J_y = ½Σσ_y **[verified]**; and the ratio ηΩ/ε is a ratio of physical quantities that cannot depend on whether the spin operator is written J_y or S_y = 2J_y: in the plan's per-tone convention the maximally entangling closure is ηΩ/ε = 1/(2√K) with τ = 2πK/ε = π√K/(ηΩ) whichever generator is written, and only the two-body phase target rescales as the square of the spin normalization, A(τ) = −π/2 on J_y², −π/8 on S_y² and χ = π/4 on σ_yσ_y, so that |λt*| = π/8 on S_y² gives Ω = ε/(2η√K) and Roos's Ω_c = |ε|/(4η) is the same condition for a Rabi frequency defined without the ½ **[corrected: derivation audit, 2026-09-04]**. The second revision of this plan stated the S_y form as ηΩ/ε = 1/(4√K), τ = π√K/(2ηΩ) and Ω_c = |ε|/(4η) in the plan's own Ω and reported that integrating 'the displayed Hamiltonian' gives a Bell state at 1/4 and a product state at 1/2; both statements were true of the doubled Hamiltonian −ħηΩ(...)S_y and false of the plan's drive. Integrating the exact bichromatic Hamiltonian with (ħΩ/2)σ₊ per tone for one loop from |↓↓⟩ (η = 0.05, ν = 2π × 1 MHz, ε = 2π × 10 kHz, d_m = 16) gives concurrence 0.9999 with populations (0.493, 0, 0, 0.507) at ηΩ/ε = 1/2 and concurrence 0.38 with populations (0.038, 0, 0, 0.962), a χ = π/16 pulse, at 1/4, and the effective Hamiltonian −(ħηΩ/2)(...)S_y reproduces both (`check_ms_closure.py`) **[recomputed here]**; the same error had reached the timing-benchmark fixture of Section 11.1, whose 'v2' closure was a χ = π/16 pulse while the withdrawn 'v1' closure at 1/2 was the maximally entangling one. Section 13 carries the row, and the simulator's S_α = Σ_i σ_α^i is the convention of every MS formula in this plan. Validity: η√(n+1) ≪ 1, which allows n well above 1 **[verified]**, plus Ω ≪ ν to avoid off-resonant carrier excitation.

**Corrections beyond the leading order** (Roos 2008) **[verified]**. Keeping the carrier exactly through a frame rotating at F(t) = (2Ω/δ)(sin(δt + ζ) − sin ζ), the effective Hamiltonian at δ = ν − ε is

H_eff/ħ = −ηΩ(J₀ + J₂) S_{y,ψ}(a†e^{i(εt−ζ)} + h.c.) − (η²Ω²/2δ) J₀² S_{y,ψ}² + (2η²Ω²/3δ) J₁² S_{z,ψ}²,

with Bessel functions at argument 4Ω/δ: the force is saturated by (J₀ + J₂), the second term is the counter-rotating spin-spin coupling, the third an unwanted S_z² coupling, and the spin axis is rotated by ψ = (4Ω/δ) sin ζ **[verified]**. These are second-order Magnus terms, not ac Stark shifts **[corrected]**. The exact propagator has α(t) = (ηΩ/ε)(J₀ + J₂)e^{−iζ}(e^{iεt} − 1) and phase rates λ, χ; smooth (non-square) envelopes break loop closure unless the pulse is repeated with the sign of the coupling reversed, which closes the loop at the end of the two-pulse sequence (2τ, not τ) **[corrected]**. Because the simulator integrates the exact Hamiltonian, these appear automatically; the closed forms are test targets and calibration models.

**Thermal-state observables.** For |↓↓⟩ with a thermal mode (Kirchmair Eq. 14) **[verified]**: the populations with two, one and zero ions bright are p₂ = (1/8)(3 + e^{−16|α|²(n̄+½)} + 4cos(4γ)e^{−4|α|²(n̄+½)}), p₁ = (1/4)(1 − e^{−16|α|²(n̄+½)}), p₀ by normalization, with γ = λt − χ sin(εt). Haljan's cat signal P_↓ = ½[1 − exp(−½ṅτ|4α₀|² − (n̄ + ½)|2α(τ)|²)] adds the heating term **[verified]**. The 40Ca+ anchors: ν/2π = 1.232 MHz, η = 0.044, F = 0.993(1) at t_g = 50 µs (ε/2π = 20 kHz), 0.971(2) at 25 µs, and with a Doppler-cooled n̄ = 20(2) at the same 50 µs setting, parity contrast 0.964 **[corrected: gate time attached]**; the paper's fidelity formula as printed has a sign slip (+Im ρ_{dd,uu} should be −Im) **[corrected]**.

**Stark-shift compensation** (Kirchmair Sec. 2.2) **[verified]**: imbalancing the tones, Ω_b = Ω(1 + ξ), Ω_r = Ω(1 − ξ), produces a compensating carrier light shift δ_ac^{(C)} = 2(Ω_r² − Ω_b²)/δ = −8Ω²ξ/δ with ξ = (δη²/|ε|)(φ/π), at the price of an S_z a†a term δ^{(SB)} = (ε/2)ξn that breaks thermal-state operation. Innsbruck's third-tone cancellation (Section 4.3) is the alternative.

#### 4.4.2 Phases and geometry

The bichromatic force couples the mode to σ_φ = e^{−iφ_s}σ₊ + e^{iφ_s}σ₋ with spin phase φ_s = (φ_b + φ_r)/2 and motion phase φ_m = (φ_b − φ_r)/2 in Haljan's convention **[verified]** (Lee 2005 writes φ_S = −(half-sum) with the opposite sign convention **[corrected]**). With co-propagating beat waves a shared optical phase fluctuation enters φ_s (phase sensitive); with counter-propagating beat waves it enters only φ_m, and slow, global fluctuations of the motion phase are harmless because they cancel at loop closure **[corrected: the qualifier "slow global" is load-bearing]**. Quantinuum's wrapper-pulse construction and the phase-insensitive geometries of Section 4.3.4 are scheduler options. Asymmetric detunings μ₊ ≠ μ₋ produce a Stark term s_i = μ₊ − μ₋ **[verified]**.

#### 4.4.3 Multi-mode gates and pulse shaping

For a chain, every mode of the driven axis participates. In the Lamb-Dicke limit the propagator is (Choi et al. 2014 Eqs. 1-3; Zhu, Monroe, Duan 2006 Eq. 4) **[verified]**

U(τ) = exp[Σ_i φ̂_i(τ)σ_x^i + iΣ_{i<j} χ_{ij}(τ)σ_x^iσ_x^j],  φ̂_i = Σ_m(α_{i,m}a_m† − α*_{i,m}a_m),

with the pair sum over unordered pairs, so that the coefficient of σ_x^aσ_x^b is χ_ab itself (the source's Σ_{i,j} would double it); the sign relating Choi's +iχ_ab to XX(χ) = exp(−iχσ_xσ_x) of Section 7.6 is set by the detuning side and is the sign s the compiler records,

α_{i,m}(τ) = iη_{i,m}∫₀^τ Ω_i(t) sin(μt) e^{iω_m t} dt,  χ_{ij}(τ) = Σ_m η_{i,m}η_{j,m} ∫₀^τ dt′∫₀^{t′} dt [Ω_i(t)Ω_j(t′) + Ω_j(t)Ω_i(t′)] sin(μt) sin(μt′) sin[ω_m(t′ − t)],

with η_{i,m} = b_{i,m}Δk√(ħ/2Mω_m) and the sine beat-note convention (force zero at t = 0) **[verified]**. The XX gate on ions a, b requires |χ_{ab}(τ_g)| = π/4 modulo π/2, the sign s being recorded by the compiler so that a solver landing on −π/4 is not scored as a failure (derivation audit of 2026-09-04), and α_{a,m}(τ_g) = 0 for all N modes (2N real conditions); with Ω_a = Ω_b and 2N + 1 equal segments this is a linear system **[verified]**. The symmetrized envelope product replaces the factor 2 of Choi's printed form 2Ω_i(t)Ω_j(t′): the second-order Magnus term is Σ_{i,j}σ_x^iσ_x^j K_ij with a time-ordered kernel that is odd under t ↔ t′, so grouping (a, b) with (b, a) gives χ_ab = K_ab + K_ba, which equals 2K_ab only when Ω_a(t) ∝ Ω_b(t); the printed form is exact for the equal-envelope gate solver, wrong by ±18% for independently shaped per-ion envelopes (±30% in a three-ion example), and as printed gives χ_ij ≠ χ_ji although σ_x^iσ_x^j is symmetric (derivation audit of 2026-09-04) **[corrected: derivation audit, 2026-09-04]**. Blümel et al. 2021 use a kernel without the pair sum and therefore a maximally entangling target of π/8, so a simulator using the π/4 convention must double their kernel, not halve it **[corrected]**. The simulator adopts χ = π/4 with the symmetrized kernel and tests both conversions.

**Amplitude modulation** (Zhu-Monroe-Duan 2006; Choi 2014) **[verified]**: segment the pulse into m constant-amplitude pieces χ(t) = Ω_p sin(μt); the residual-displacement infidelity for thermal spectator modes is F_in ≈ Σ_k β̄_k[|α_j^k|² + |α_n^k|²]/4 with β̄_k = coth[√(μ_k/4) ln(1 + 1/n̄₁)]. Zhu et al.'s Eq. 8 for the Lamb-Dicke thermal infidelity is inconsistent by a factor 2 with the fidelity formula printed above it, and their mode-frequency relation should read ω_{ξ,k} = √λ_{ξ,k} ω_z (the printed ω_ξ is a typo that is silent for axial modes and tenfold wrong for transverse modes at β_x = 10) **[corrected]**; their single-mode closed form Eq. 7 omits a factor 1/l_ξ **[corrected]**. Choi's anchors: five 171Yb+ ions, transverse COM 2.5-4.5 MHz, axial 310-550 kHz, 355 nm Raman, τ_g = 104 µs with 5 segments for two ions and 190 µs with 9 segments for five **[verified]**.

**Frequency modulation** (Leung et al. 2018) **[verified]**: H_MS = (Ω/2)Σ_k [S^k_{φ,γ} a_k† e^{iθ_k(t)} + h.c.] with θ_k(t) = ∫₀^t δ_k(t′)dt′ (the printed Eq. 5 lacks the parentheses that make it Hermitian **[corrected]**); the Magnus series terminates and the propagator is D̂({α̂_k})Ê(β_{ij}); the trajectory α_k(t) = (Ω/2)(η_{i,k} + η_{j,k})∫₀^t e^{iθ_k}dt′; the residual-displacement error is ε ≈ Σ_k|α_k(τ)|² (the joint-state overlap gives e^{−|α|²}, the traced-out fidelity ½(1 + e^{−2|α|²}) **[corrected]**); robustness to a common detuning drift δ₁ follows from ∂α_k/∂δ₁ ∝ the time-averaged trajectory, so the cost function Σ_k|∫₀^τ∫₀^t e^{iθ_k}dt′dt|² enforces α_{k,avg} = 0 for time-symmetric pulses **[verified]**; β_{ij} = Ω² cos γ Σ_k η_{i,k}η_{j,k}β_k is a weighted sum of trajectory areas, not the bare sum **[corrected]**. Anchors: five 171Yb+ ions at 3.045 MHz radial (an ordinary frequency; the item had dropped the 2π **[corrected]**), 90 µs pulses applied twice, 13 oscillations; the experiment needed Ω = 2π × 600 kHz against 151 kHz predicted, attributed to overestimated Lamb-Dicke parameters **[verified]**. This discrepancy is exactly the kind the calibration layer must be able to reproduce and then correct.

**Amplitude-and-frequency-modulated, stabilized gates** (Blümel et al. 2021) **[verified]**: expand the control g(t) = Σ_n A_n sin(2πnt/τ) (about 300 basis functions for 100 µs), impose closure and its first K frequency derivatives, (∂^k/∂ω_p^k)α_p^i(τ) = 0 for every mode and ion, as a homogeneous linear system MA = 0; the zero-temperature infidelity from residual displacement is f = (4/5)Σ_p(|α_p^i|² + |α_p^j|²) = (4/5)AᵀFA, the entangling angle χ = AᵀKA, and the power-optimal solution is the eigenvector of the projected kernel with the largest eigenvalue, Ω₀ = (π/(8|λ_max|))^{1/2} in their π/8 convention. The printed (S6), (S8), (S9) drop a complex conjugate and are not usable as written **[corrected]**. Their hardware: seven 171Yb+ ions with the middle five as qubits, 12.6428 GHz, rf at 2π × 23.83 MHz; mode frequencies and Lamb-Dicke parameters calibrated by blue-sideband spectroscopy with P = sin²(Ω_i^p t) on resonance, so Ω_i^p is half the usual Rabi frequency **[verified flag]**.

The `control/shaping` module implements all three (segmented AM, vertex-parameterized FM with cosine interpolation, Fourier-sine AMFM with K-th-order stabilization and the extended-null-space relaxation Γ = MᵀM) against the same α and χ integrals, in the simulator's π/4 convention, and verifies every solution by exact integration of the full Hamiltonian through the injected `PulseEngine` protocol rather than by importing `dynamics` (control/ never imports dynamics/, which consumes its Pulse objects; the cycle the 2026-09-04 architect critique found), which also captures what the Lamb-Dicke solvers omit (carrier, Debye-Waller, off-resonant terms).

#### 4.4.4 Light-shift (σzσz) gates

**Field-sensitive qubits** (Ballance et al. 2016) **[verified]**: two Raman beams with beat frequency δ = f_z + δ_g, δ_g = 2/t_g, so K = 2 loops close on the axial COM mode; a two-pulse spin-echo sequence; SPAM-corrected Bell infidelity 2.5(7) × 10⁻³ with gate error 1.1(7) × 10⁻³ at t_g = 100 µs and Δ = −3.0 THz. Error formulas from their supplement: thermal ε_n̄ = (1/4)π²η⁴n̄(2n̄ + 1) = (π²/4)η⁴⟨n²⟩ for n̄ < 1, referenced to an entangling angle calibrated at n = 0 (calibrating the power on the thermal ensemble instead leaves the variance form (π²/4)η⁴n̄(n̄ + 1); the two agree to O(n̄), differing by 2% at n̄ = 0.02 and 50% at n̄ = 1, and the Debye-Waller law χ(n) = χ₀[1 − η²(2n + 1)] behind both was confirmed by the derivation audit of 2026-09-04); heating ε_h = ṅt_g/(2K) with ṅ the gate-mode rate (2.2 s⁻¹ for the two-ion COM mode); motional dephasing with Lindblad operator L = a†a√(2/τ) giving ε_d = α_K t_g/τ, α_K = 1/(2K) + 3/(16K²) = (8K + 3)/(16K²) = {11/16, 19/64, 35/256} = {0.6875, 0.2969, 0.1367} for K = {1, 2, 4}, the 1/(2K) being residual spin-motion entanglement and the 3/(16K²) the randomized geometric phase; Ballance prints {0.686, 0.297, 0.137}, the first being the value at finite t_g/τ = 10⁻³ rather than the asymptotic coefficient (derivation audit of 2026-09-04) **[verified]**, **[corrected: derivation audit, 2026-09-04]** (0.297 × 100 µs/200 ms = 0.15 × 10⁻³ reproduces the paper's line; the heating form ε_h = ṅt_g/2K is an empirical fit from their numerical model, stated only for ε_h ≪ 0.1, with ṅ the gate mode's rate taken as twice the single-ion 1.1 s⁻¹). Parameters **[verified]**: f_z = 1.95 MHz, f_r = 4.5 MHz, η_COM = 0.123, η_str = 0.094, n̄ ≈ 0.02, τ ≳ 200 ms, waist 27 µm; the paper's 2 µK is n̄hf_z/k_B = 1.87 µK, not the Bose temperature 23.8 µK, so simulations are seeded from n̄ = 0.02, never from T = 2 µK. Their carrier Rabi frequency is reduced by 0.83 through axial micromotion; that number is J₀(β) with β = |Δk| × 38 nm = 0.851 for |Δk| = 2π√2/397 nm, J₀ = 0.827, and it brings a first micromotion sideband of relative weight J₁²/J₀² = 0.22, a 22% off-resonant channel the paper does not list **[corrected]**. Zhu-Monroe-Duan's Eq. 2, H = Σ_j ħΩ_j cos(Δk·q_j + μt)σ_j^z, is the lab-frame form **[verified]**.

**Clock qubits** (Baldwin et al. 2021) **[corrected]**: the qubit is |↑⟩ = |F=1, m_F=0⟩ and |↓⟩ = |F=0, m_F=0⟩ of the ²S₁/₂ manifold, |↑⟩ being the only ground state the gate light couples (to the Zeeman-split D₃/₂ |F=1, m_F=±1⟩ levels) and |↓⟩ decoupled by the 12.6428 GHz hyperfine splitting; the paper's opening clause prints the assignment inverted and is contradicted by its own next sentence, its detection counts (about 9 photons for F=1 and 0.1 for F=0) and its optical pumping into F=0. Because clock states have no differential vector light shift, the gate uses a polarization gradient from four couplings with phases {0, 0, −π/2, π/2} through the Zeeman-split D₃/₂ levels of 171Yb+, with the single-photon detuning set by the Zeeman splitting itself, Δ/2π ≈ B × 1.4 MHz/G (7.8 MHz at 5.57 G); after adiabatic elimination H ≈ ηΩΣ_j(−1)^j(1 + σ_z^j)(a e^{iδt+iφ_j} + h.c.) with Ω ≈ g²/Δ, only |↑⟩ shifted, on the axial stretch mode. Two loops in a microwave spin echo give R_x(π)U(π/4)R_{−x}(π)U(π/4) = diag(1, i, i, 1). The printed geometric phase Φ = 2π(Ωη/δ)² is the per-loop coefficient of Ŝ² in Φ_loop Ŝ², with Ŝ = σ_z² − σ_z¹ the force operator of the H two clauses above and Ŝ² = 2 − 2σ_z¹σ_z²: one closed loop of duration 2π/δ gives 2π(ηΩ/δ)² on Ŝ², hence 4π(ηΩ/δ)² on σ_z¹σ_z², and the 8π(Ωη/δ)² of the paper's Eq. 1 is the σ_zσ_z coefficient over the K = 2 loops of the spin echo; the two are consistent once the operator normalization and the loop count are named, which the second revision's bare 'inconsistent, gives 8π' did not do, leaving the calibrated Ω for the two U(π/4) segments ambiguous by 2 or 4, so the fixture integrates the printed H through the echo numerically and asserts diag(1, i, i, 1) (Section 9.17; 2026-09-04 theorist critique) **[corrected]**, **[corrected: critique, 2026-09-04]**; their η carries a factor 4 under the root **[verified flag]**. The paper's simulation kept both D-state levels and all six modes with no Lamb-Dicke expansion, dimension 4² × Π(n_max+1), although the printed total of 5600 does not follow from the stated cutoffs **[corrected]**; in its scattering estimate ε_γ^D the condition Δ ≪ Δ_hf refers to the ground-state hyperfine splitting of 12.6428 GHz, left undefined in the paper, under which |↓⟩ does not scatter at all and every Raman or Rayleigh event off the D state is a which-path measurement of the qubit, the opposite regime from P-state Raman gates **[corrected]**, which is precisely the simulator's JOINT_EXACT construction.

#### 4.4.5 Microwave-gradient gates

With an oscillating magnetic-field gradient at ω_g and two microwave tones symmetrically detuned by δ from the ac-Zeeman-shifted qubit frequency (Srinivas et al. 2021 Eq. 1) **[verified]**:

H_I(t) = ħΩ_g J₂(4Ω_μ/δ)(σ_z1 − σ_z2)(a e^{iΔt} + a†e^{−iΔt}),  Ω_g = (r₀/4)[∇(B_g·r̂_q)·r̂](∂ω₀/∂B),  Ω_μ = (B_x/2ħ)⟨↓|μ̂_x|↑⟩,

with intrinsic dynamical decoupling when J₀(4Ω_μ/δ) = 0, i.e. Ω_μ/δ ≈ 0.601, which suppresses dephasing at frequencies well below δ **[verified]**; realized as eight Walsh-modulated pulses over 740 µs on 25Mg+ with error budget dominated by motional dephasing (5.8 × 10⁻⁴ at a 64 ms motional coherence time) **[corrected minor]**. The simulator's "electronic drive" type builds this Hamiltonian from the configured gradient and field sensitivity.

#### 4.4.6 Smooth gates without ground-state cooling

Hughes et al. 2025 write any geometric-phase gate in the frame ħ(ω_m − δ)a†a as H_g = ħδ(t)a†a + (ħΩ_g(t)/2)Ŝ_α(a† + a) **[verified]**, which splits into forced and null oscillators; the maximally entangling angle in their Eq. 4 is θ_g = π/4 (Ŝ_α² has eigenvalues 0 and 4), not π/2 **[corrected]**. Adiabatically eliminating spin-motion entanglement by ramping δ(t) with β̇/δ ≪ 1 gives θ_g ≃ ∫(Ω_g² + α̇²)/δ dt and a temperature-insensitive gate; the printed Eqs. 9-10 contain an anti-Hermitian residual term (±iħβ̇p̂ should be ±ħβ̇x̂), Eq. 16 mislabels β̇/δ as β̇, and Eq. 19's constant b is dimensionally inconsistent as printed **[corrected]**. Their appendix gives the infidelity from mode-frequency fluctuations as exactly two terms, a thermal spin-motion term ∝ (2n̄ + 1)|α_t|²λ² and a temperature-insensitive gate-angle term **[verified]**, and the SLERB metric ε_2q = (6/5 ε_RB + 4/5 ε_leak) × 6/13 **[verified]**. The simulator implements the detuning ramp as an ordinary FM schedule; these formulas are its tests.

#### 4.4.7 Error model of a gate, as the sources quantify it

Bermudez et al. 2017 give the closed forms that the second revision of this plan took as the noise module's targets for an N-ion MS gate **[verified as transcribed]**, with the caveat that their Γ_d and Γ_I are never assigned values in the paper but fitted to reproduce reported fidelities, and the N² global-noise branch was chosen the same way **[corrected]**: motional error ε_m ≈ [πN(δ − ω_z)/(2ω_z²t_g)]·0.8(n̄ + 1) + [π²N(N − 1)η⁴/(8N²)](1.2n̄² + 1.4n̄) (spectator loops plus Debye-Waller); dephasing error ε_d ≈ 2Γ_d t_g Σ_{ij} e^{−|z_i − z_j|/ξ_c}, which is 2t_gN/T₂ for uncorrelated and 2t_gN²/T₂ for globally correlated field noise; intensity-noise error ε_I ≈ Γ_I t_g η²(n̄ + ½) + Γ_I t_g η²(N − 1)/4; and the recalibration rule t_g(n̄_f) = t_g(0)(1 + η²(2n̄ + 1)/N) after heating **[verified as transcribed]**, together with the mapping of the total ε onto Pauli depolarizing channels for circuit-level simulation **[verified]**. The derivation audit of 2026-09-04 re-derived these budgets from the exact gate dynamics (two blind derivations, a comparer and two adjudicators, one of whom held out for the printed forms) and found them to be scale estimates with fitted coefficients rather than derivable results, so the plan's targets are the derived forms below and Bermudez's are kept as the source's statements: the spectator-loop error is ε_loop = Σ_{m≠g}(2n_m + 1)Σ_i|α_{i,m}(t_g)|² = π²NK Σ_{m≠g}(2n_m + 1)(ω_z/ω_m) sin²(δ_m t_g/2)/(δ_m t_g)² with δ_m = μ − ω_m (Bermudez's πN(δ − ω_z)/(2ω_z²t_g) = π²NK/(ω_z t_g)² is the right scale with the mode sum collapsed into the constant 0.8, and the thermal weight is 2n_m + 1, not 0.8(n̄ + 1)); the Debye-Waller error is (π²/8)N(N − 1)(η⁴/N²)(n̄² + n̄), the bracket being Var(n) rather than 1.2n̄² + 1.4n̄ (28 to 34% high); the dephasing error is ε_d = (Γ_d t_g/2)Σ_ij C_ij Cov_t(σ_z^i, σ_z^j) ≤ (Γ_d t_g/2)Σ_ij C_ij, so Nt_g/(2T₂) for uncorrelated and N²t_g/(4T₂) for global noise with T₂ the single-ion 1/e coherence time, 4 and 8 times below the printed 2t_gN/T₂ and 2t_gN²/T₂ (no single convention for Γ_d rescues both printed forms at once); the intensity-noise spin term is Γ_I t_g η² · 3(N − 1)/(8K), not (N − 1)/4, because the phase accumulates as (1 − cos δt) and ∫(1 − cos δt)² dt = (3/2)t_g, a Γ_I-independent ratio of the two terms (fitted intercept-to-slope ratios 1.003 to 1.010 of the derived form against 1.5/K of the printed one); and the recalibration rule keeps η²(2n̄ + 1) but not the 1/N. Section 9.16 pins the derived forms against exact integration **[corrected: derivation audit, 2026-09-04]**. Kirchmair's budget adds laser frequency noise (2 × 10⁻³ per gate from incoherent carrier excitation), heating ΔF = Γ_h t_g/2, and Rabi-frequency variation δΩ/Ω ≈ 1.4 × 10⁻² **[corrected minor]**; Ballance's adds photon scattering as the largest term (Section 4.3.2). Section 6 turns these into collapse operators and sampled parameters, and Sections 9.4 and 9.16 require the simulated gate to reproduce each derived closed form in its regime of validity.

**Reconciliations established by the recovery consolidation (Appendix D, Run 3).** (1) The three published thermal Debye-Waller infidelities are one formula, (π²/4)η⁴⟨(n − n_ref)²⟩ over the thermal distribution, with n_ref = n̄ for Sørensen-Mølmer, who re-optimize the pulse duration to the mean, n_ref = 0 for Ballance, calibrated at n = 0, and n_ref = −½ for Zhu, referenced to η²(2n + 1) = 0; in units of (π²/4)η⁴ they read n̄² + n̄, 2n̄² + n̄ and 2n̄² + 2n̄ + ¼, or 2.000, 3.000 and 4.250 at n̄ = 1, so the apparent factor-2 disagreement is a Rabi-calibration reference that no paper documents, and the simulator records which reference a device's calibration uses **[corrected]**. (2) Multi-mode closure counts must include both transverse families: with 2N radial modes Choi's segment count 2N + 1 becomes 4N + 1 (35 → 69 at N = 17) and Blümel's constraint rows N(K + 1) become 2N(K + 1) (51 → 102 at N = 17, K = 2); a solver built on one family leaves the other family's loops open **[corrected]**. (3) Trout's circuit-level dephasing rate r_d = 15 s⁻¹ and the 0.5 s T₂ it cites were reconciled by the second revision through Bermudez's global-noise branch, T₂ = 2N²/r_d = 0.53 s at N = 2; with the derived global coefficient N²t_g/(4T₂) of Section 4.4.7 the same pair gives T₂ = N²/(4r_d) = 0.067 s, so the reconciliation no longer closes and the pair (15 s⁻¹, 0.5 s) is recorded as unreconciled, the naive 1/(2r_d) = 33 ms being no better **[corrected: derivation audit, 2026-09-04]**. (4) Roos's Table 1 entry κ/θ = 8/(3η) for the σzσz gate contradicts his Eq. 15, which gives (8/3)η, a factor 1/η² = 100 at η = 0.1 (the second revision printed 900; 2026-09-04 theorist critique), and his Eq. 22 sign is inverted relative to its parent Eq. 19 **[corrected]**. (5) Kirchmair's F = 0.993(1), the 2 × 10⁻³ carrier-excitation figure and δΩ/Ω = 1.4 × 10⁻² are attributed by that paper to Benhelm et al. 2008 **[corrected]**. (6) Hughes's Eq. 19 prints δ_max^{j} for δ_max^{−j}, which would freeze the ramp, and Eq. 20 omits a†a **[corrected]**. (7) Arbitrary-angle XX(χ) is covered by no source; the simulator obtains it by rescaling the null-space pulse amplitude by s with s²χ_max = χ and re-checking closure, and labels the mapping as derived **[background]**. (8) Three published residual-displacement infidelities are one quantity in three fidelity measures: with ε_ent = Σ_{j,m}|α_{j,m}|²(2n̄_m + 1) the entanglement infidelity of a two-qubit gate whose spin-dependent force leaves displacement α_{j,m} on mode m for ion j, Landsman's (4/5)ε_ent is the average gate infidelity (the two-qubit conversion d/(d + 1) = 4/5), Leung's Σ_k|α_k|² is ε_ent at n̄ = 0, and Zhu's Σ_k β̄_k(|α_j^k|² + |α_n^k|²)/4 with β̄_k = coth[√(μ_k/4) ln(1 + 1/n̄₁)] = 2n̄_k + 1 is a state infidelity for his chosen input, a factor 1/4 below ε_ent for the same displacements; the simulator reports ε_ent, converts the sources, and Section 9.4 pins the conversions by direct integration rather than by citation **[background; the plan's own reconciliation, added after the 2026-09-04 critique]**.


### 4.5 Atomic structure and the light-atom coupling chain

Every optical quantity in Sections 4.2 to 4.4 and 8 (Rabi frequencies, Stark shifts, scattering rates, branching ratios, repump and detection rates, the qubit frequency and its field sensitivity) is a function of a small set of atomic constants per species and of the beam geometry. The research runs of 2026-09-03 extracted those quantities as the papers quote them, per species and per experiment, and Section 12 recorded that the angular algebra behind them had been worked in the source set for 9Be+ only. This section specifies the layer that derives them for any species, so that a species module holds only measured constants with citations and everything hyperfine-resolved is computed. The conventions are Steck's (Quantum and Atom Optics, and the alkali D-line data notes), chosen because they are stated with every phase and normalization written out and are the ones most of the trapped-ion literature follows. The formulas below are standard atomic physics **[background]** whose transcription against those sources is checked in Run 4 (Appendix D); every number quoted is **[recomputed here]** with the scripts of Appendix D and compared with anchors that earlier runs verified independently.

#### 4.5.1 Fine, hyperfine and Zeeman structure at the operating field

A species module lists, per fine-structure level (n, L, S = 1/2, J): the energy, the lifetime, the magnetic-dipole and electric-quadrupole hyperfine constants A and B, and the Landé factor g_J; per isotope, the nuclear spin I and magnetic moment μ_I. From these the atomic module builds, for every level, the hyperfine-plus-Zeeman Hamiltonian in the |m_I, m_J⟩ basis,

H/h = A I·J + B [3(I·J)² + (3/2) I·J − I(I+1)J(J+1)] / [2I(2I−1)J(2J−1)] + (μ_B/h)(g_J J_z + g_I I_z) B,

with A and B in Hz and μ_B/h = 1.399624 MHz/G, the atomic layer working in Hz throughout and converting to rad/s at its boundary (Section 5.6),

with the quadrupole term present only for I ≥ 1 and J ≥ 1, and the nuclear factor g_I = −(μ_I/(I μ_N))(m_e/m_p) in Steck's sign convention, so that both g factors enter with one sign and g_I is of order 10⁻³ g_J; it diagonalizes H numerically at the configured field. The eigenvectors give the mixing between hyperfine states that the dipole matrix elements of Section 4.5.2 need at intermediate fields; the eigenvalues give every transition frequency, its first and second derivatives with respect to B (the sensitivities that H_int of Section 5.7 and the dephasing model of Section 6.3 consume), and the field-independent points. For J = 1/2 the closed Breit-Rabi form is the unit test of the diagonalization:

E(F = I ± ½, m_F)/h = −ΔE_hfs/(2(2I+1)) + (g_I μ_B/h) m_F B ± (ΔE_hfs/2)[1 + 4m_F x/(2I+1) + x²]^{1/2},  x = (g_J − g_I) μ_B B/(h ΔE_hfs),  ΔE_hfs = A(I + ½) with A in Hz (every term in Hz; the second revision added a joule to two Hz terms; 2026-09-04 theorist critique),

with the stretched states m_F = ±(I + ½) exactly linear in B. Recomputed anchors (Section 9.13): the sixteen 43Ca+ ground levels agree between the two methods to 10⁻¹² MHz at 0, 10, 146.0942 and 500 G; the 43Ca+ |4,0⟩ ↔ |3,+1⟩ transition is stationary at 146.0942 G with f₀ = 3,199,941,076.93 Hz and curvature 2.415 mHz/mG², matching Harty et al. (Section 4.3.3; A = −806.4020716 MHz, g_J = 2.00225664, μ_I = −1.31535 μ_N); 9Be+ |2,0⟩ ↔ |1,+1⟩ is stationary at 119.446 G with f₀ = 1,207,495,853.5 Hz against the 119.45 G and 1,207,495,843 Hz of Langer et al. 2005 (A = −625.008837048 MHz, μ_I = −1.177432 μ_N, g_J = 2.00226; the 10 Hz residual is the precision of the constants used, which fixes the test tolerance and the citation requirement); 25Mg+ |3,+1⟩ ↔ |2,+1⟩ is stationary at 212.78 G with f₀ = 1.686462 GHz against the 212.8 G and 1.686 GHz of Srinivas et al. (Section 4.4.5); and the 171Yb+ clock transition's quadratic shift is 310.9 Hz/G² both numerically and from the closed form (g_J − g_I)²μ_B²/(2h²A) with A = 12.642812118 GHz in Hz (the second revision printed 2hA, one factor of h short; 2026-09-04 theorist critique), against the 310.8 Hz/G² of Section 6.3.
