The coefficient moves by 0.03% across the three g_J values the second revision carried in different rows (2.00225664, which is 43Ca+'s; 2.00254; 2.00292, giving 310.76, 310.85 and 310.97 Hz/G²), so the plan adopts one cited value for the 171Yb+ ground state, g_J = 2.002615(70) (Han et al., arXiv:2501.09973, 2025, a multiconfiguration Dirac-Hartree-Fock and multireference configuration-interaction determination whose own second-order coefficient is 31.0869(22) mHz/µT² = 310.869 Hz/G²; the only other value in the literature is a spectroscopic 1.998 of larger uncertainty), which with g_I from μ_I = +0.49367 μ_N gives 310.87 Hz/G² and sets the test tolerance at ±0.02 Hz/G² from the g_J uncertainty, with the quoted 310.8 inside it (`check_critique_v3.py`) **[verified]**, **[corrected: critique, 2026-09-04]**, **[recomputed here]**. The negative A of 43Ca+ is what places F = 3 above F = 4 (Section 8.4); the module derives that ordering rather than storing it.

Two consequences for the rest of the plan. First, the field-sensitivity numbers of Section 6.3 (1.4 MHz/G per unit g_F m_F at low field, 2.8 MHz/G for a Δm_J = 1 Zeeman qubit; 310.8 Hz/G² as the coefficient of B² for 171Yb+, and for 43Ca+ at 146 G Harty's d²ν/dB² = 2.4 mHz/mG², i.e. 1.21 mHz/mG² as a coefficient of (δB)², the two conventions of the Section 13 curvature row) are now derived: the low-field slope is g_F μ_B m_F/h with g_F = g_J[F(F+1) + J(J+1) − I(I+1)]/(2F(F+1)) + g_I[F(F+1) − J(J+1) + I(I+1)]/(2F(F+1)), and the quadratic coefficients come from the diagonalization. Second, the operating field is a `Field` object with magnitude, direction and noise spectrum: its direction is the quantization axis of Section 4.5.3, and a field offset sampled per shot (Section 6.3) is converted into per-ion, per-level frequency offsets through the computed sensitivities, never through a hand-entered MHz/G.

#### 4.5.2 Dipole matrix elements, lifetimes, branching and saturation

The decay rate of an upper fine-structure level J′ to a lower level J fixes the reduced dipole matrix element, in Steck's normalization,

Γ_{J′→J} = ω³/(3πε₀ħc³) · (2J+1)/(2J′+1) · |⟨J‖d‖J′⟩|²,

and when J′ decays to several lower levels (P₁/₂ → S₁/₂ and D₃/₂ in Yb+, Ca+, Ba+ and Sr+) the level's total Γ is the sum of the partial rates and the fine-structure branching ratio is their ratio, a radial-integral quantity that the species module stores with its citation (0.501(15)% for 171Yb+ P₁/₂ → D₃/₂, Section 8.1). Everything below the fine-structure level is angular algebra and is computed. The hyperfine-resolved element factorizes by the Wigner-Eckart theorem (Steck's convention; the Rb87 notes' Eqs. 35-37),

⟨F m_F|d_q|F′ m_F′⟩ = ⟨J‖d‖J′⟩ (−1)^{F′+J+1+I} √((2F′+1)(2J+1)) {J J′ 1; F′ F I} · (−1)^{F′−1+m_F} √(2F+1) (F′ 1 F; m_F′ q −m_F),

with a 6j symbol in braces and a 3j symbol in parentheses, nonzero only for m_F = m_F′ + q. Two sum rules that the ingest test checks numerically, both printed by Steck (Rb87 data Eqs. 39-40) **[verified]**: Σ_{m′,q}|⟨J m|d_q|J′ m′⟩|² = |⟨J‖d‖J′⟩|² (the normalization convention itself) and Σ_{q,F}|⟨F (m_F′+q)|d_q|F′ m_F′⟩|² = (2J+1)/(2J′+1) |⟨J‖d‖J′⟩|², so the total decay rate out of any upper sublevel |F′ m_F′⟩ equals Γ_{J′→J} whatever its hyperfine quantum numbers, the relative strength S = (2F′+1)(2F+1)(2J′+1) (3j)² (6j)² is the branching ratio of that decay channel and sums to one over the lower manifold, and the total absorption strength out of any lower sublevel, summed over upper states and polarizations, equals |⟨J‖d‖J′⟩|². Steck's alternative reduced element (J‖d‖J′) = √(2J+1)⟨J‖d‖J′⟩ is the conversion the ingest test applies to sources that use it. Recomputed (Section 9.13): the 171Yb+ P₁/₂ F′ = 1 level decays 1/3 to S₁/₂ F = 0 and 2/3 to F = 1, and F′ = 0 decays only to F = 1, which is the branching that Section 8.1's leakage rate R_d and Section 4.2.6's optical pumping (1/3 per excitation into |0⟩) assumed; for I = 1/2 the stretched ground state's absorption strength to P₃/₂, summed over polarizations, lies half in the cycling |2,2⟩ component and entirely there for σ⁺ light, which is why that transition is closed.

At intermediate fields the |F m_F⟩ states of Section 4.5.1 are mixtures, and the module evaluates the element between the actual eigenvectors, Σ c^{(a)*}_{F m_F} c^{(b)}_{F′ m_F′} ⟨F m_F|d_q|F′ m_F′⟩, which is what makes the 43Ca+ shelving probabilities of Section 8.1 (ground states other than the stretch state shelving with up to about 30% probability at 146 G) computable rather than tabulated.

**Intensity, saturation and the Rabi frequency.** A beam of intensity I has field amplitude E₀ = √(2I/(ε₀c)) and polarization components ε_q in the spherical basis of Section 4.5.3; the Rabi frequency of the transition |a⟩ → |b⟩ that it drives, in the plan's convention H = (ħΩ/2)σ₊ + h.c. (Section 4.3.1), is

Ω_{ab} = E₀ Σ_q ε_q ⟨b|d_q|a⟩ / ħ.

The saturation intensity I_sat = πhcΓ/(3λ³) (Section 13) is a property of a two-level transition whose dipole element squared is 3πε₀ħc³Γ/ω³; for such a transition, and only for such a transition, Ω = Γ√(I/(2I_sat)) and the saturation parameter s = I/I_sat equals 2Ω²/Γ². Recomputed (Section 9.13): 171Yb+ 369.5 nm has |⟨J‖d‖J′⟩| = 1.751 e a₀ and I_sat = 50.77 mW/cm² (the 50.8 mW/cm² of Section 9.12); 40Ca+ 397 nm 2.045 e a₀ and 45.11 mW/cm²; 40Ca+ 393 nm 2.972 e a₀ and 50.25 mW/cm². Which transitions qualify is decided by the angular algebra, and the two readout schemes of Section 8.1 fall on opposite sides of the line. The stretched-state σ⁺ cycling transition |I+½, I+½⟩ → |I+3/2, I+3/2⟩ through P₃/₂ has relative strength exactly (2J+1)/(2J′+1) = 1/2 of |⟨J‖d‖J′⟩|², the same factor that appears in Γ_{P₃/₂→S₁/₂}, so Ω = Γ√(I/(2I_sat)) holds and Acton's s = I/I_sat is exact. The 171Yb+ S₁/₂ F = 1 → P₁/₂ F′ = 0 detection line has J = J′ and relative strength 1/3 per Zeeman component, so with the full-line Rabi frequency Ω = Γ√(I/(2I_sat)) each ground sublevel |1, m⟩ couples to |0, 0⟩ through one polarization component with |⟨0,0|d_q|1,m⟩|² = |⟨J‖d‖J′⟩|²/3, so its Rabi frequency is Ω_m² = |ε_q|²Ω²/3, and since Σ_q|ε_q|² = 1 for any single beam the three sublevels share Σ_m Ω_m² = Ω²/3 however the beam is polarized, the magic-angle choice |ε_q|² = 1/3 giving each Ω_m² = Ω²/9; in a rate model with the three ground states equally populated the excited fraction is Σ_m (1/3)(Ω_m²/Γ²)[1 + (2Δ/Γ)²]⁻¹ = (Ω²/9Γ²)[1 + (2Δ/Γ)²]⁻¹ = s_∘/18 at low intensity with s_∘ = 2Ω²/Γ² for the full-line Ω, which is the Γ/18 prefactor of Section 8.1 (the second revision wrote Ω/√3 per sublevel and s_∘/6, counting the hyperfine factor but not the polarization share; derivation audit of 2026-09-04), and the saturation ceiling of Γ/4 follows from four equally populated levels. Noek's prefactor is therefore derived, not fitted, his s = 2Ω²/Γ² in the (Γ/6, 2/3) form being s_∘/3, and the factor between Crain's quoted I/I_sat and the saturation inferred from his measured rate (Section 8.8) must be intensity calibration, detuning or polarization, all of which the level-C Bloch solve of Section 8.1 takes as inputs **[corrected: derivation audit, 2026-09-04]**. The code never accepts "I/I_sat" for a transition it has not classified as closed two-level.

#### 4.5.3 Polarization in the atomic frame

The quantization axis is the direction B̂ of the static field (Section 4.5.1). A beam with wavevector k̂ and complex unit polarization vector ε̂ in the laboratory frame is decomposed as ε̂ = Σ_q ε_q ê_q with ê_{±1} = ∓(x̂′ ± iŷ′)/√2 and ê₀ = ẑ′ = B̂, where x̂′ and ŷ′ complete a right-handed frame with B̂; ε_q = ê_q*·ε̂, and q = +1, 0, −1 drive σ⁺, π and σ⁻ absorption |F m_F⟩ → |F′ m_F + q⟩; the rotation of the laboratory Jones vector and k̂ into the B̂ frame is the module's own construction (no source in Run 4 supplies it, though every sign in the layer depends on it) and it asserts |ε₋₁|² + |ε₀|² + |ε₊₁|² = 1 at beam construction **[background]**. This q is the photon-helicity label. In Steck's matrix-element formulas (Section 13) the index on the operator component is q_op = m_F − m_F′ = m_lower − m_upper, because the lower state is the bra, so a σ⁺ absorption appears in his Eqs. 34-37, in his sublevel Rabi frequencies Ω(m_e, m_g) = ⟨F_g m_g|F_e m_e; 1 −(m_e − m_g)⟩Ω_{−(m_e−m_g)} and in his tables under q = −1: the two labels are opposite because d·ε̂ = Σ_q (−1)^q d_q ε_{−q}, so the field component ε_{+1} couples through d_{−1}. The code stores the helicity label, converts at the one place the Wigner-Eckart routine is called, and a test asserts that a pure ε_{+1} beam drives only m → m + 1 (the 2026-09-04 verifiers found the extracted Steck items with the two labels interchanged; Steck's Rb87 Eq. 57 for the polarization-resolved scattering branching also omits Γ as printed and returns a population rather than a rate) **[corrected]**. Since ε̂ ⊥ k̂, a beam along B̂ carries no π component, a linearly polarized beam perpendicular to B̂ with ε̂ ⊥ B̂ carries equal σ⁺ and σ⁻ components (|ε_{±1}|² = ½), and one with ε̂ ∥ B̂ is pure π. Every beam in `light/beams.py` stores k̂ and ε̂ in the laboratory frame and the atomic module returns its q components at each ion, so that the polarization purity on which Harty's ac Zeeman shift (Section 4.3.3), the optical-pumping efficiency (Section 4.2.6), the coherent-population-trapping factor of Section 8.1 and the polarization-gradient gate of Section 4.4.4 all depend is a computed number with a documented laboratory-frame origin, not a per-transition fudge factor. Polarization impurity is a device parameter (an ellipticity and a misalignment angle), sampled quasi-statically when a spectrum is given.

#### 4.5.4 Raman couplings and light shifts from the level structure

For two beams j = 1, 2 with frequencies ω_j, field amplitudes E_j and polarizations ε̂_j, and the set of intermediate levels |e⟩ (all hyperfine and Zeeman sublevels of the P₁/₂ and P₃/₂ manifolds at their field-dependent energies), each far detuned, Δ_e^{(j)} = ω_j − ω_{e,g} with |Δ_e| ≫ Ω, Γ_e, adiabatic elimination of the excited manifold gives, in the plan's convention (Section 13: Ω = −Ω₁Ω₂/(2Δ_R)), the effective two-photon Rabi frequency between ground states g₁ and g₂ and the light shift of each ground state:

Ω_{g₁g₂} = Σ_e Ω^{(2)*}_{e g₂} Ω^{(1)}_{e g₁} / (2Δ_e^{(1)}),  δ_g = Σ_j Σ_e |Ω^{(j)}_{e g}|² / (4Δ_e^{(j)}),

with Ω^{(j)}_{eg} = E_j ⟨e|d·ε̂_j|g⟩/ħ the single-photon Rabi frequencies of Section 4.5.2 and Δ_e = ω_L − ω_eg the plan's detuning sign (Section 13), so that red-detuned light lowers a ground level and the Raman coupling is the coherent sum over all intermediate sublevels with their signs; the second-order derivation gives these signs directly (E_g^{(2)} = |ħΩ/2|²/(E_g − E_e) = ħ|Ω|²/(4Δ)), and the minus sign in Wineland 2003's Eq. 2.3 and in the Section 13 row comes from that paper's definition Ω ≡ −E₀⟨↑|d·ε̂|↓⟩/(2ħ), a phase convention that the drive phase φ absorbs. Both are evaluated by explicit summation over the level structure of Section 4.5.1, and the closed forms of Sections 4.3.2 and 4.4.4 are their tests: Ozeri's clock-qubit Ω_R = (g_b g_r/3)(b₋r₋ − b₊r₊) ω_f/[Δ(Δ − ω_f)] is what the sum reduces to when the excited hyperfine splittings are neglected against Δ and the P₁/₂ and P₃/₂ paths are combined with their 6j weights (Ozeri's g_b and g_r are each beam's peak field times the fully m_F-resolved stretched cycling element |⟨P₃/₂, F = I + 3/2, m = F|d·σ̂₊|S₁/₂, F = I + ½, m = F⟩| over 2ħ, not a reduced matrix element, from which they differ by a Clebsch-Gordan factor of order 30% in Ω_R; and his τ_π = π/(2Ω_R) is a half-Rabi convention converted on ingest **[corrected]**) (the two paths enter with opposite signs, which is the origin of the ω_f/[Δ(Δ − ω_f)] structure and of the 1/Δ² fall-off); the 9Be+ eight-term differential shift of Wineland 2003 Eq. 2.11 and its polarization null; the vanishing differential vector shift of clock states that Baldwin's gate exploits. The decomposition into scalar, vector and tensor polarizabilities is an equivalent representation offered for reporting **[background]**, not a separate computation.

Two approximations enter here by default and are the only ones added to the optical rotating-wave approximation of Section 1.2, both reported with their size: the adiabatic elimination itself, whose residual excited-state population Σ_e |Ω^{(j)}_{eg}|²/(4Δ_e²) is exactly the quantity the scattering channels of Section 4.5.5 use, so the two are consistent by construction; and the counter-rotating two-photon pairing (beam 2 exciting g₁ and beam 1 de-exciting to g₂), which oscillates at about twice the qubit splitting and would need picosecond steps to integrate, and is instead replaced by its second-order frequency shift of order |Ω_{g₁g₂}|²/(2ω₀) (about 2π × 40 Hz for a 1 MHz Raman Rabi frequency on 171Yb+), which the calibration layer absorbs like any other static shift. A frequency-comb drive is the same computation over the set of tooth pairs, with the single-photon couplings replaced by the comb amplitudes g_k = g₀√(πν_rep τ) sech(2πk ν_rep τ) obeying Σ_k g_k² = g₀² and the two-photon Rabi frequency becoming the tooth-pair sum Ω^{(l)} = Σ_k g_k g_{k+l}/(2Δ) at beat-note separation l; the residual excited-state population is then the same sum over teeth, so the scattering channels of Section 4.5.5 stay consistent with the comb drive by construction (Section 4.3.7).

#### 4.5.5 Scattering amplitudes: Raman, Rayleigh and differential Rayleigh decoherence

Off-resonant light also scatters, through the same intermediate sum. The amplitude for scattering a photon of beam j from ground state a to ground state b with emitted polarization q′ is the Kramers-Heisenberg sum A^{(j)}_{a→b,q′} ∝ Σ_e ⟨b|d_{q′}|e⟩⟨e|d·ε̂_j|a⟩/Δ_e^{(j)}, and the rate is Γ_e times the far-detuned excited population times the branching, combined coherently over e: Γ^{(j)}_{a→b} = Σ_{q′} |Σ_e √Γ_e c_{e→b,q′} Ω^{(j)}_{ea}/(2Δ_e^{(j)})|², with c_{e→b,q′} the normalized decay amplitude of Section 4.5.2 (Σ_{b,q′}|c_{e→b,q′}|² = 1) and √Γ_e inside the coherent sum, one factor per intermediate path (strictly √(Γ_e(ω_j/ω_e)³), a 10⁻⁴ fine-structure correction), because the emission element ⟨b|d_{q′}|e⟩ = √(3πε₀ħc³Γ_e/ω_e³) c_{e→b,q′} carries it; the second revision placed Γ_e outside the sum, which is exact only when every intermediate level has the same total rate, true for 9Be+ under one radial integral and false for the species whose P₃/₂ branches to D levels, by up to 11× per channel (derivation audit of 2026-09-04) **[corrected: derivation audit, 2026-09-04]**. This is the general form of Ozeri 2005's Γ_{i,f} = g²γ|a^{(1/2)}_{i→f}/Δ + a^{(3/2)}_{i→f}/(Δ − Δ_f)|² (Section 4.3.2), and the tabulated 9Be+ amplitudes of Section 9.10 (Raman a^{(1/2)} = −√2/3, a^{(3/2)} = +√2/3; Rayleigh 2/3 and 1/3 for σ⁺ on m = −1/2, and so on) together with Ozeri 2007's P_total, ε_S and ε_D are its tests; the module produces the corresponding amplitudes for 43Ca+, 171Yb+, 133Ba+ and 88Sr+ from their level structures, which closes the "worked for 9Be+ only" gap of Section 12. Four things Run 4 fixed in Ozeri's paper as a source: the sentence after his Eq. 18 defines λ_{3/2} = c/ω_{3/2}, contradicting his Eq. 18, Table I and every Table II value, and taken literally inflates the required laser power by (2π)² = 39.5, so λ = 2πc/ω is used (his Table II reproduces to two figures for eight ions with it; the Yb+ row does not: it prints 0.2 × 10⁻⁴ and 2 mW where the paper's own Eq. 17 gives 0.29 × 10⁻⁴ and 2.9 mW, and it is excluded; the first revision of this plan had the printed and recomputed pairs transposed, caught by the errata check of 2026-09-04); his P_total has two equally deep minima, 2√2 πγ/ω_f at Δ = (√2 − 1)ω_f and at −(√2 + 1)ω_f, with P_∞ = 3πγ/ω_f, and P_min is not a gate-error floor because P_Raman falls as 1/Δ²; the Raman scattering rate falls as 1/Δ⁴ and the per-π-pulse probability as 1/Δ², two statements his text leaves implicit; his 5/12 recoil average is isotropic, the dipole-weighted value for fixed polarization being 9/20 (8% more, 27/25; the second revision wrote 16%), his D(β) = exp(βa − β*a†) is the conjugate of QuTiP's convention (harmless only because his β is imaginary), his ε_S is an upper bound (Eq. 11 discards a positive term) and his P_Rayleigh silently includes the D-level Raman channel for Ca+, Sr+, Ba+ and Yb+, overstating the elastic rate by fP_total **[corrected]**.

Three kinds of channel follow, all Lindblad operators on the joint space (Section 5.7):

- **Raman scattering** (b ≠ a): √Γ_{a→b} |b⟩⟨a| ⊗ R, with R the recoil operator of Section 4.2.8. When b is outside the qubit pair this is **leakage**, the channel that Section 12 recorded as absent from every source: for a hyperfine qubit the leaked population sits in the other Zeeman sublevels of the same or the other F manifold, and its fate at readout (bright or dark) follows from the manifold (Section 8.1; for 171Yb+ the F = 1 sublevels are bright and F = 0 is dark); for an optical qubit it sits in other D₅/₂ or S₁/₂ sublevels with their own readout polarity and lifetimes. Leakage is therefore simulated, not estimated, whenever d > 2, and its rate is the sum of the out-of-pair Raman rates otherwise.
- **Rayleigh scattering** (b = a) with **equal** amplitudes for the two qubit states, r_↑ = r_↓ for every emitted mode: the collapse operator is proportional to the identity on the spin and acts only on the motion through R, so it heats but does not decohere; this is the regime of Section 4.3.2's statement that Rayleigh scattering at large detuning leaves a hyperfine (clock) qubit's coherence intact.
- **Differential Rayleigh scattering**, r_↑ ≠ r_↓ (Uys et al. 2010): the operator r_↑|↑⟩⟨↑| + r_↓|↓⟩⟨↓| = ½(r_↑ + r_↓)𝟙 + ½(r_↑ − r_↓)σ_z has a σ_z part that dephases the qubit at a rate proportional to Σ_modes |r_↑ − r_↓|², an error absent from Ozeri 2007's Raman-only estimate. It vanishes for clock states far from the fine-structure levels, where the two amplitudes coincide by symmetry, and does not vanish for Zeeman qubits or at detunings comparable with the fine-structure splitting. The module computes r_↑ and r_↓ from the same amplitude sum, so the channel comes for free once the amplitudes exist. Uys et al. 2010 give the rate **[verified]** (Eqs. 4, 5 and 7 transcribed by Run 4 and re-read from the e-print by a three-verifier panel in Run 5, which confirmed them and sharpened four conventions; see the closing paragraph of this subsection): with Kramers-Heisenberg amplitudes A^{i→j}_{J,λ} = b_λ⟨j|d·ε̂*_{λ+(i−j)}|J, λ+i⟩⟨J, λ+i|d·ε̂_λ|i⟩/(δ_{iJλ} μ²) normalized to the largest dipole element μ of the manifold, so that the scattering rates are Γ_{ij} = Ω_R²γ Σ_λ (Σ_J A^{i→j}_{J,λ})² with Ω_R = μE₀/(2ħ) (a coherent sum over the intermediate fine-structure levels J inside the square, an incoherent sum over the driving polarization components λ outside it), the elastic decoherence rate is

Γ_el = Ω_R² γ Σ_λ (Σ_J A^{d→d}_{J,λ} − Σ_{J′} A^{u→u}_{J′,λ})²,

the square of the difference of the two states' elastic amplitudes, and it enters the master equation as 𝓛_el ρ = −(Γ_el/4)[ρ − σ_z ρ σ_z], so that the qubit coherence decays at (Γ_Ram + Γ_el)/2 while populations relax at the Raman rates alone **[verified]**. Four conventions in Uys's formula that Run 4 fixed: the amplitudes A^{i→j}_{J,λ} carry units of inverse angular frequency and are signed, not dimensionless; μ = ⟨3/2, 3/2|d·ε̂₁|1/2, 1/2⟩ is a fixed normalization tied to Ω_R = μE₀/(2ħ), not a free choice; Uys's δ = ω_transition − ω_laser is positive for red detuning, the opposite of this plan's Δ; and Γ_el is a dephasing rate, not an elastic scattering rate, vanishing for equal amplitudes however large the elastic rates and reaching 2(Γ_dd + Γ_uu) for equal and opposite ones, so the rate-difference estimate (Γ_uu − Γ_dd)²/(Γ_uu + Γ_dd) of Ozeri 2007 under-predicts the measured decoherence at the detuning where the two rates are equal, where the estimator vanishes identically while the measured rate does not (the second revision quantified this as 'a factor of five', a ratio to zero; 2026-09-04 theorist critique) **[corrected]**. Because the detunings in the two amplitudes can have opposite signs the terms can add, and the earlier rate-difference estimate ½Γ_el ≈ (Γ_uu − Γ_dd)²/(Γ_uu + Γ_dd), which is Ozeri 2007's ε_δ = (dΓ/Γ_Rayleigh)² P_Rayleigh, underestimates the measured decoherence fivefold at the detuning where the two elastic rates are equal; Rayleigh decoherence is negligible only when both conditions hold, detuning large compared with the qubit splitting and clock states, in which case Γ_el ∝ 1/Δ⁴ against 1/Δ² in general. Their anchor is a 9Be+ electron-spin qubit at 4.5 T (Ω_z = 2π × 124.1 GHz, 313 nm light, P₃/₂ resonances at −79.4 and −37.7 GHz from the cycling transition, equal elastic rates near −56 GHz). Ozeri 2007's closed forms for linearly polarized beams of equal coupling are the tests of the amplitude sum: Γ_total = (γ/3)[g_b²(b₋² + b₊²) + g_r²(r₋² + r₊²)][1/Δ² + 2/(Δ − ω_f)²], Γ_Raman = (2γ/9)[same bracket][ω_f/(Δ(Δ − ω_f))]², P_Rayleigh = (πγ/ω_f)(3Δ² − 2Δω_f + ω_f²/3)/|Δ(Δ − ω_f)|, with g_{b,r} = E_{b,r}|⟨P₃/₂, F = I+3/2, m_F = F|d·σ̂₊|S₁/₂, F = I+1/2, m_F = F⟩|/(2ħ) the half-convention Rabi frequency of the stretched cycling transition and γ/g² = 4ħω³/(3πε₀c³E²) the atomic-constant ratio that removes the dipole element from the scattering error, g being the stretched-state coupling defined above (Ozeri Eqs. 14-18, verified by derivation from his Eqs. 1, 3 and 12 and numerically against the Rb87 D2 line) **[verified]**; their z₀ = √(ħ/(4Mω)) is the two-ion normal-mode extent, the factor-4 flag of Section 13, and their Rayleigh recoil ⟨|β|²⟩ = (5/12)η² is the isotropic average for their crossed-beam geometry, a special case of the kernel of Section 4.2.8.

**What the Run 5 verifier panel established for the two transcriptions Section 12 flagged** **[verified]**, **[corrected]**. Uys's Eqs. (1)-(8) and Wineland 2003's Eqs. (2.13)-(2.18) were re-read from the cached e-prints by a three-verifier panel in Run 5; the forms above stand, with their conventions sharpened. The Uys amplitudes A^{i→j}_{J,λ} are **signed reals**, because δ_{iJλ} = Ω_{J,λ+i} − Ω_i − ω₀ changes sign across the intermediate levels and per-term moduli destroy Γ_el, a squared difference of amplitude **sums** that is largest precisely where the two elastic rates coincide; λ indexes the **incident** component, carrying b_λ, fixing M_J = λ + i and entering δ_{iJλ}, the scattered index being the derived λ + (i − j), so the two readings coincide only for the elastic terms Eq. (7) uses (refuted 3-0 as a "scattered-photon index", fatal only for a polarization-asymmetric drive) **[corrected]**. The μ² normalization must pair with an Ω_R = μE₀/(2ħ) from the **same** stretched-state μ, half the plan's single-photon Ω, so feeding a plan-convention Ω into Γ_{ij} = Ω_R²γ Σ_λ(Σ_J A)² is a factor 4, and since the source prints no normalization for b_λ the drive builder asserts Σ_λ|b_λ|² = 1. The three collapse operators are ½√Γ_el σ_z, √Γ_du|u⟩⟨d| and √Γ_ud|d⟩⟨u| in the plan's 𝓓[L]ρ convention, and the asymmetry is deliberate: the Raman operators carry the plain rate under the root, the Rayleigh one Γ_el/4, and that prefactor is correct **and must not be "fixed" to Γ_el/2** even though the observable coherence decays at Γ_el/2 - a `mesolve` run with c = ½√Γ_el σ_z alone leaves populations exactly unchanged and returns Γ_el/2, while √(Γ_el/2)σ_z decays twice as fast. The three-operator generator reproduces Eq. (8) elementwise with zero trace derivative, and the spin-echo readout ρ_uu = ½(1 − e^{−(Γ_Ram + Γ_el)τ/2}) has initial slope (Γ_Ram + Γ_el)/4, so twice the fitted short-time slope is the quoted total decoherence rate; it reads the transverse Bloch length, not the population block **[recomputed here]**. On the Wineland side the eight-channel Eq. (2.14) rationals 2/3, 1/3, 1, 1/6, 1/2, 5/6 are relative dipole **line strengths** per polarization component, summed over the excited sublevels of a fine-structure level and normalized to the stretched σ₊ element that defines g_{b,r} - not branching ratios; each bracket sums to 1 for ²P₁/₂ and 2 for ²P₃/₂, which is why one shared γ is exact in LS coupling, and the per-beam polarization amplitudes are unit-normalized, a constraint the paper never prints but every reduction needs **[corrected]**. Wineland's Ω is a half-angle amplitude with a π pulse at Ωτ_π = π/2, so τ_π = π/(2|Ω|) and any formula written in the symbol converts on ingest while the π **time** is convention-independent; Δ is referenced to ²P₁/₂, so the ²P₃/₂ pathway detuning is Δ − ω_F and the two carry opposite signs at the optimum, +82.01 and −115.99 GHz for 9Be+ **[recomputed here]**. The optimization is of the **ratio** R_SE/|Ω| and never of R_SE: minimizing |x(x − 1)|[1/x² + 2/(x − 1)²] with x = Δ/ω_F gives x = √2 − 1 = 0.414214 and minimum 2√2, hence R_SE/|Ω| = 4√2 γ/ω_F and P_SE = 2√2 πγ/ω_F = 8.885766 γ/ω_F = 8.706 × 10⁻⁴ for 9Be+ at γ/2π = 19.4 MHz and ω_F/2π = 198 GHz, whereas the negative test of minimizing the bracket alone returns the different and wrong x = ω_F/(1 + 2^{1/3}) = 0.442493 **[recomputed here]**. The two per-π-pulse figures near 10⁻³ must not be conflated: 2√2 πγ/ω_F is the m_F = 0 clock line with b₋ = r₋ = b₊ = −r₊ = 1/√2, the |2,2⟩ ↔ |1,1⟩ Zeeman-type qubit with b₀ = 1, r₊ = r₋ = 1/√2 giving (8π/√6)γ/ω_F = 10.260399 γ/ω_F = 1.0053 × 10⁻³ **[recomputed here]**. Eqs. (2.17) and (2.18) share one (g_b² + g_r²)/3 prefactor and bracket, so δ_{0↔0} = −(ω₀/γ)R_SE holds exactly for any Δ and polarization: compute the bracket once, reuse it for the dissipator weight and for the shift, added as −(δ_{0↔0}/2)σ_z, and note that unlike the |2,2⟩ ↔ |1,1⟩ shift it is polarization-independent and therefore unnullable **[recomputed here]**. Two caveats travel with R_SE - it over-estimates decoherence when |Δ| ≫ ω_F, where elastic events dominate, and at large fine structure Raman scattering from further levels must be added - but Table 1 sits at Δ = 0.414 ω_F, so neither discounts the tabulated numbers, the far-detuned replacement being the Γ_el above **[contested, reconciled]**. What Run 5 does **not** close is recoil: these dissipators stay internal-state only, so the kernel of Section 4.2.8 is the only operator form available and its transfer here is untested (Section 12).

#### 4.5.6 What the species database stores and what it derives

| Stored per species, with citation | Derived by `species/atomic.py`, tested in Section 9.13 |
|---|---|
| Mass; nuclear spin I; nuclear moment μ_I | g_I; the full ∣m_I, m_J⟩ hyperfine-Zeeman spectrum of every level at any field; state labels by adiabatic connection |
| Per level: energy, lifetime, A, B, g_J | Zeeman energies and mixings; every transition frequency with dB and d²B sensitivities; clock points; the level ordering (sign of A) |
| Per fine-structure transition: wavelength, Γ, fine-structure branching (S versus D) | reduced dipole element; I_sat for closed two-level transitions; every hyperfine and Zeeman-resolved dipole element, decay branching and absorption strength |
| Nothing about polarization | q components of every beam at every ion from the laboratory-frame geometry and B̂ |
| Nothing about Raman couplings | Ω_{g₁g₂}, per-level light shifts, differential shifts, comb sums, adiabatic-elimination residuals |
| Nothing about scattering | Raman, leakage and Rayleigh amplitudes and rates per beam; differential-Rayleigh dephasing; the Lindblad operators of Section 5.7 |
| The designated qubit pair, cycling, repump and shelving transitions | classification of each as closed two-level or not; bright and dark manifolds for the readout POVM |

**Conventions Run 4 settled for the species layer** **[verified]**, **[corrected]**. Three normalizations of the dipole element coexist and stack: Steck's Brink-Satchler reduced element ⟨J‖d‖J′⟩ (no 1/√(2j + 1)), the fully m_F-resolved stretched cycling element that Ozeri, Uys and Wineland use as their anchor, smaller by √(1/2) on a J = 1/2 → 3/2 line (87Rb D2: 2.989311 against 4.227524 e a₀ at τ = 26.2348(77) ns, the elements being derived from the stored lifetime with its uncertainty and never typed in, since the rounded 26.24 ns already gives 4.227104 e a₀; so substituting one element for the other moves every rate by 2), and the Edmonds round-bracket element, larger by √(2j + 1); one level up, Steck's Γ_{J_gJ_e} = ω³(2J_g + 1)|⟨J_g‖d‖J_e⟩|²/(3πε₀ħc³(2J_e + 1)) is the total, m_F-independent decay rate out of any sublevel of J_e into the fine-structure level J_g and equals b(J_e → J_g)/τ, so on a unit-branching alkali D2 line it is 1/τ, Ozeri's, Uys's, Wineland's and Olmschenk's γ exactly, with no second factor 2 (the second revision of this plan claimed one; the degeneracy ratio inside the formula is the same factor already spent in converting the reduced element to the stretched one, and applying it again is a factor 4 on a D2 line; caught by the derivation audit of 2026-09-04) **[corrected: derivation audit, 2026-09-04]**; every stored element and rate carries a convention tag and is converted once at ingest, and the degeneracy ratio is never applied on top of a 3j-normalized element. The two hyperfine factors are directed quantities built from one 6j symbol with swapped prefactors, S_FF′ = (2F′ + 1)(2J + 1){…}² summing to one over excited F′ and b(F′ → F) = (2F + 1)(2J′ + 1){…}² summing to one over ground F, never one "hyperfine factor". The sign of A_hfs is an independent signed input that sources rarely print (9Be+, 43Ca+ and the 171Yb+ ³D[3/2]₁/₂ level are all inverted multiplets); with A > 0 the 43Ca+ clock point exists nowhere between 1 and 4000 G and the 9Be+ one nowhere between 8 and 16 mT, which is the module's ingest test of the sign, inferred from μ_I when not printed. The measured g_J replaces the Landé formula (5.9 ppm off for 87Rb; g_J = 2.000 exactly moves the 43Ca+ clock point by 160 mG and 47 Hz), Steck's g_S = +2.0023193 is opposite in sign to CODATA's g_e and the module uses Steck's convention throughout, and the three self-consistent nuclear-Zeeman forms (Steck's μ_I = −μ_B g_I I/ħ with a plus sign, Langer's g_I = −μ_I/(Iμ_B) with a plus, Harty's g_I^{(N)} = μ_I/(Iμ_N) with a minus) must not be mixed: Harty's g with Langer's sign is an 83.76 kHz error in f₀ and 0.207 G in B₀ for 43Ca+. The exact 2 × 2 fixed-M diagonalization is primary and the Breit-Rabi closed form a cross-check because the radicand collapses to (1 ± x)² at the stretched states and the closed form returns the wrong branch beyond |x| = 1 (2437.6 G for 87Rb); eigenstates are labelled by adiabatic continuation from B = 0, and drive matrix elements are computed in the field-dressed eigenbasis, since Steck's drive Hamiltonian assumes degenerate sublevels and at 146 G the mixing is what creates the clock point. "Curvature" is stored twice under distinct names, the second derivative d²ν/dB² (Harty prints 2.4 mHz/mG²) and the Taylor coefficient ½d²ν/dB² (Langer prints 0.305 Hz/µT²), and accumulated phase is 2π∫[ν(B) − ν(B₀)]dt with the bracket in Hz (both of Langer's figures fix the 2π: 75.8 µs against his printed 80 µs, 52.2 s against his 50 s). The second-order sums carry a three-index detuning Δ_i(m_S, beam) = ω_beam − (ω_i − ω_{m_S}), and a code with a single Δ_i per intermediate level and beam returns exactly zero for the 0 ↔ 0 clock light shift, the sharpest test of that convention (Section 9.13); the sums have no iγ/2 in their denominators and are used only far from resonance, the multi-level Bloch solve of Section 4.2.8 taking over within a few linewidths. I_sat = πhcΓ/(3λ³) is the cycling value with σ₀ = 3λ²/(2π), exactly one third of the random-orientation ħω³Γ/(4πc²) that is also widely quoted (87Rb D2: 1.669325 against 5.007975 mW/cm²), λ is the vacuum wavelength, and for a degenerate manifold the scalar saturation parameter is abandoned for per-sublevel Rabi frequencies. Apparatus figures must not become species constants: the 9Be+ 1.81(2) Hz trap-rf ac Zeeman shift, the −5 Hz 43Ca+ trap shift already subtracted from f₀, the −1.0 Hz microwave ac Zeeman shift acting only during π/2 pulses, the 14.7 s Ramsey contrast time, the 4.2 kHz differential Stark detuning, the F₇/₂ filling rate, the 5 G quantization field, beam powers and waists and the collection efficiency are all settable device scalars defaulting to zero or none; and two constants carry conditional provenance as a field, not a comment: the 171Yb+ branching 0.00501(15) holds only with τ(P₁/₂) = 8.07 ns, because the fit measures the product γR, and the 43Ca+ μ_I = −1.31535 μ_N is a thesis value 0.17% from the handbook number that fits f₀ nine times better, effectively a fitted constant. Steck's QAO Eq. 7.132 prints the hyperfine operator with half-value ħ exponents, still unfixed in revision 0.16.10 of 27 June 2026 (use the dimensionless S = I·J/ħ² form), his Rb87 Eq. 60 drops a prime, and his uncoupled-basis elements QAO 7.328-7.331 are the A-only J = 1/2 or I = 1/2 case, not a general intermediate-field kit (B needs I, J ≥ 1 and C needs I, J ≥ 3/2), so the general-J diagonalizer of Section 4.5.1 is the plan's own construction built from powers of I·J **[background]**.

The species modules of milestone M0 (171Yb+, 40Ca+, then 43Ca+, 133Ba+, 137Ba+, 9Be+, 25Mg+, 88Sr+) therefore shrink to a table of cited constants each, and the anchors of Section 9.13 are the acceptance test of the shared algebra. The Run 4 extractions fix the first entries **[verified]**, **[corrected]**: for 171Yb+ (Olmschenk et al. 2007) the S₁/₂ **zero-field** splitting 12,642,812,118.5 Hz with quadratic shift 310.8 Hz/G², recomputed from first principles as taylor_c2 = (g_J − g_I)²μ_B²/(2h²ν₀) = 310.76 Hz/G² and therefore confirmed to be the Taylor coefficient and not d²ν/dB² **[recomputed here]** (the splittings 12.642815, 12.642819 and 12.642821 GHz printed elsewhere in the literature are this same second-order shift at 3.0, 4.7 and 5.3 G and must never be stored as zero-field constants), the ground-state g-factor g_F(F = 1) = g_J/2 = 1.001128 with g_Fμ_B/h = 1.4012 MHz/G - the ubiquitous "1.4 MHz/G" being that product and not the dimensionless g_F, whose misuse returns an adjacent-m_F splitting of 11.6 MHz instead of 8.27 MHz at 5.9 G, a 40% error in the quantity that gates the coherent-population-trapping window of Section 4.2.4 **[recomputed here]**, the P₁/₂ splitting 2.105 GHz, D₃/₂ 0.86(2) GHz and ³D[3/2]₁/₂ 2.2095(11) GHz, the P₁/₂ lifetime 8.07(9) ns from Pinnington et al. (γ/2π = 19.7 MHz, an inference from the shared citation rather than a printed symbol) with branching 0.00501(15) to D₃/₂ (measured on 174Yb+, isotope independent), the D₃/₂ lifetime 52.7 ms with its 935.2 nm repump and 3.07 GHz sideband (the P₁/₂ → D₃/₂ decay channel itself lies at 2.438 µm, the 935.2 nm line being D₃/₂ → ³D[3/2]₁/₂, a conflation that changes the inferred P₁/₂ → D₃/₂ reduced element by 4.2 times and that one derivation of the 2026-09-04 audit fell into, so the species table stores the channel wavelength next to its element), and the F₇/₂ trap state cleared at 638.6 nm; two glosses the extractors added and the verifiers removed: the detection error is dominated by off-resonant excitation of the bright state through the 2.1 GHz P₁/₂ splitting, not by the 14.7 GHz dark-state detuning (Section 8.1's R_b), and the insensitivity of detection to 935 nm power above 500 µW follows from the 0.005 branching ratio, not from repump saturation; for 43Ca+ (Harty et al. 2014) μ_I = −1.31535 μ_N with the zero-field splitting from Arbes et al., to which the Breit-Rabi fit needs a third constant g_J that the paper never prints (2.00226; g_J = 2.000 moves the field-independent point by 0.16 G and f₀ by 48 Hz, so Section 9.13 cites it), a 16-state ground manifold spanning 3.6 GHz at 146 G with adjacent Zeeman splittings of about 50 MHz (Zeeman spreads 409 MHz within F = 4 and 306 MHz within F = 3, the paper's "~500 MHz"), lasers at 397 and 866 nm for Doppler cooling plus 393, 850 and 854 nm for readout and reset, the M-resolving 393 nm shelving in which ground states other than the stretch state shelve with at most about 30% probability, and the two-frequency σ⁺ 397 nm pumping with microwave clean-up; for 9Be+ (Langer et al. 2005) the clock point B₀ = 11,944.6 µT and 1,207,495,843 Hz with curvature 0.305 Hz/µT² and a 1.81(2) Hz ac Zeeman correction, the 17.6 kHz/µT slope of the |2,2⟩ ↔ |1,1⟩ magnetometry transition at B₀, the 21 kHz/µT slope of the field-sensitive |2,−2⟩ ↔ |1,−1⟩ qubit, and further field-independent points at 0.01196 T and 0.02231 T, whose state pairs the paper conveys only graphically and the diagonalization resolves as |2,1⟩ ↔ |1,0⟩ at 119.643 G and |2,1⟩ ↔ |1,1⟩ at 223.073 G (recomputed slopes 17.64 and 21.0 kHz/µT, curvature 0.3049 Hz/µT²) **[recomputed here]**.

#### 4.5.7 Electric-quadrupole coupling for optical qubits

The 40Ca+ S₁/₂ ↔ D₅/₂ transition at 729 nm and the 88Sr+ S₁/₂ ↔ D₅/₂ transition at 674 nm are dipole-forbidden electric-quadrupole (E2) lines, so the dipole algebra of Sections 4.5.2 to 4.5.5 does not give their Rabi frequencies. A dedicated run (Appendix D, Run 4Q) read James 1998 Sec. 4-5 and Appendix, the Roos 2000 thesis and Schindler et al. 2013 for this coupling; 18 items were checked, 11 were refuted on transcription or source errors and corrected, and the forms below carry the verifiers' corrections.

**The drive has the dipole form with one beam's k.** The E2 drive on a Zeeman component |S₁/₂, m⟩ ↔ |D₅/₂, m′⟩ is the travelling-wave Hamiltonian of Section 4.3.1 unchanged, H = (ħΩ_{mm′}/2) σ₊^{(m,m′)} e^{i(k·x̂ − Δ_{mm′}t + φ_L)} + h.c., with k the single beam's optical wavevector, |k| = 2π/λ_vac, and σ₊^{(m,m′)} = |D, m′⟩⟨S, m|; the exponential, the Lamb-Dicke parameters η_p = (k·ê_p) c_i^{(p)} √(ħ/(2Mν_p)) and the sideband ladder are exactly those of the dipole and Raman drives, only the scalar Ω and the level pair change **[verified]** (Roos 2000 Eqs. 3.3, 3.6, 3.17; Schindler 2013). The interaction-picture sign is â(t) = a e^{−iνt}, so ηâ†σ₊ is the blue sideband at Δ = +ν; the thesis prints the opposite sign once, contradicted by its own later equations **[corrected]**. James's standing-wave node and antinode construction, in which E1 and E2 couplings swap roles between carrier and sideband, applies to a mirror-formed standing wave only and its printed node and antinode positions in Sec. 4 (Eqs. 4.9 and 4.13; Sec. 5 and the Appendix contain none) are each a factor 2 too large (nodes at lλ/2, antinodes at (2l − 1)λ/4; the printed antinode (2l − 1)λ/2 is a node of the paper's own sin(kζ) field) **[corrected]**; it is not ported into the travelling-wave builder.

**The Rabi frequency factorizes exactly** **[corrected]** (James 1998 Eqs. 4.8, 5.2-5.4; Roos Eq. 3.17, and Eq. 3.26 with the factor k that its printed second line drops restored, as Roos's own Eq. 7.12 has it):

Ω_{mm′} = |(eE₀/(2ħ)) ⟨S₁/₂, m|(ε̂·r)(k·r)|D₅/₂, m′⟩| = (eE₀k/(2ħ)) |⟨S₁/₂‖r²C^{(2)}‖D₅/₂⟩| |Λ(m, m′)| |g^{(q)}|,

with E₀ = √(2I/(ε₀c)) the peak field amplitude, ⟨S‖r²C^{(2)}‖D⟩ the reduced matrix element of the rank-2 Racah tensor C^{(2)}_q = √(4π/5) Y_{2q} (not r²Y_{2m}, a √(4π/5) trap), Λ(m, m′) the Wigner 3-j symbol (1/2 2 5/2; −m q m′), and g^{(q)} = c^{(q)}_{ij} ε_i n_j the contraction of the beam polarization ε̂ and propagation direction n̂ (k = k n̂) with the rank-2 spherical basis tensors of James's Appendix. Three conventions the code asserts: q = m − m′ = −Δm (forced by the 3-j bottom row, the opposite of the common q = Δm, harmless for linear polarization because |g^{(q)}| = |g^{(−q)}| but load-bearing for circular polarization); the rank-2 tensors are normalized to Σ_ij |c^{(q)}_{ij}|² = 2/3, not 1, so a library that unit-normalizes them inflates every quadrupole Rabi frequency by √(3/2), and they are c^{(q)} = (2/3)b^{(q)} with b^{(q)}_{ij}r_ir_j = r²C^{(2)}_q (b^{(0)} = diag(−½, −½, 1), b^{(±1)} = ∓√(3/8)[x̂ẑ + ẑx̂ ± i(ŷẑ + ẑŷ)], b^{(±2)} = √(3/8)[x̂x̂ − ŷŷ ± i(x̂ŷ + ŷx̂)]), not the conjugate duals (2/3)b^{(q)*}, which share the 2/3 norm but turn q = m − m′ into q = m′ − m for circular light, so the module asserts c^{(q)}_{ij}r_ir_j = (2/3)r²C^{(2)}_q at construction, which is what fixes the sign of q for circular polarization (derivation audit of 2026-09-04) **[corrected: derivation audit, 2026-09-04]**; and Ω follows the plan's convention (carrier π pulse at t = π/Ω), which is Roos's and Steck's and twice the Boulder value. The 3-j selection rule |Δm| ≤ 2 admits 10 of the 12 S₁/₂-D₅/₂ Zeeman pairs, excluding (−½, +5/2) and (+½, −5/2) **[verified]**. In the frame with B̂ = ẑ, k̂ = (sin φ, 0, cos φ) and a linear polarization ε̂ = (cos γ cos φ, sin γ, −cos γ sin φ), the geometric factors are g^{(0)} = ½|cos γ sin 2φ|, g^{(±1)} = (1/√6)|cos γ cos 2φ + i sin γ cos φ| and g^{(±2)} = (1/√6)|½ cos γ sin 2φ + i sin γ sin φ|, where γ = arcsin|ε̂·ŷ| is the angle between the polarization and the k-B plane (Roos's printed wording, "the angle between the polarization and the field projected into the plane of incidence", is degenerate and reproduces his own vectors only at φ = 90°) **[corrected]**; they satisfy (g^{(0)})² + 2(g^{(±1)})² + 2(g^{(±2)})² = 1/3 for every geometry, with maxima g^{(0)} = ½ at (45°, 0°) g^{(±1)} = 1/√6 at φ = 0 for every γ, where only |Δm| = 1 is driven, and g^{(±2)} = 1/√6 at (90°, 90°), where only |Δm| = 2 is driven, a clean two-level system by construction (the second revision's sentence gave both maxima at both geometries, which its own sum rule forbids, 2/3 against 1/3; 2026-09-04 theorist critique), while Δm = 0 is never driven with k ⊥ B **[verified]**. Circular or elliptical polarization is handled by the complex contraction, for which no source gives a closed form.

**The reduced matrix element comes from the D-state lifetime** **[corrected]** (James Eqs. 5.6-5.10; Roos Eq. 3.27): A^{(E2)} = cαk⁵|⟨S‖r²C^{(2)}‖D⟩|²/(15(2j′ + 1)) with j′ = 5/2 the upper level (2j′ + 1 = 6; the lower-level value would make the element √3 too small), cα = e²/(4πε₀ħ) = 2.187691 × 10⁶ m/s in SI, and A the branch rate into S₁/₂, equal to 1/τ only at unit branching (true to 10⁻⁵ for the Ca+ D₅/₂ level and to about 10⁻⁴ for Sr+, whose M1 branch to D₃/₂ is estimated below; false for Ba+ D₅/₂ or any P₁/₂ level with a D branch). k must be the vacuum transition wavenumber: James's Fig. 4 quotes the calcium wavelengths in air (729.147 nm; vacuum 729.347 nm) and the strontium wavelengths in vacuum, and Roos's table row is labelled λ_air, so an air value used as vacuum biases k by 274 ppm and the rate by 0.14%, and gives the wrong laser frequency outright **[corrected]**. With the current 40Ca+ D₅/₂ lifetime 1.168(7) s (Barton et al. 2000, quoted by Schindler, concordant with Kreuter et al. 2005's independent single-ion 1168(9) ms; Roos's 1.045 s and James's 1.14 s are superseded compilations) the element is 2.724 × 10⁻²⁰ m² = 9.73 a₀², against 10.29 a₀² with Roos's lifetime **[corrected]**; for 88Sr+ the modern measured lifetime is 390.8(1.6) ms (Letchumanan, Wilson, Gill and Sinclair, Phys. Rev. A 72, 012509 (2005), read on 2026-09-04 as Chapter 5 of Letchumanan's 2004 Imperial College thesis because the paper is paywalled, quoted as 0.3908(16) s by Jiang et al. 2009 and adopted by the NIST Sr II compilation as A = 2.559(10) s⁻¹ at accuracy AA), giving 13.81(3) a₀², 6.06% below the 14.70 a₀² that the superseded 1987 ion-cloud value 345(33) ms of the first two revisions gave, so every 674 nm Rabi frequency falls by 6.06% and every π time lengthens by 6.45% (`check_sr_lifetime_quad.py`) **[verified]**, **[corrected]**, **[recomputed here]**. The measurement is the most direct of five (Gerz 1987, 345(33) ms from a pressure-extrapolated ion cloud; Madej and Sankey 1990, 372(25); Barwood 1993, 347(11) from 5120 decays; Biémont 2000, 408(22) from a storage ring with 10% loss corrections; Letchumanan 2005 from 160,000 single-ion shelving periods); its applied corrections total 0.11% of the rate (collisional deshelving 2.2(2.2) × 10⁻³ s⁻¹ from 8 observed collisions per hour and off-resonant 1033 nm quenching by the 1092 nm repumper 6(6) × 10⁻⁴ s⁻¹) and its residual error is statistical; the thesis prints 390.3 ms because its Table 5.2 repeats an uncorrected July-2002 rate, and the weighted mean of the corrected rates is the published 390.8 ms **[verified]**. Its blackbody correction is not a correction: the only blackbody deshelving channel is D₅/₂ → P₃/₂ at 1032.7 nm, whose rate (1 − b)[(2J_P + 1)/(2J_D + 1)]A_PD/(e^{ħω/k_BT} − 1) is 4 × 10⁻¹⁴ s⁻¹ at 300 K against the 2.559 s⁻¹ decay rate, eleven orders of magnitude below the measurement's uncertainty, and reaches the decay rate only near 1000 K, which is why a filament or an operating ion gauge in view of the ion matters (`check_sr_lifetime_bbr.py`); the blackbody frequency shift, a leading clock systematic, is a different quantity **[verified]**, **[recomputed here]**. Unit branching into S₁/₂ holds to 10⁻⁴ for Sr+, not 10⁻⁵: the M1 channel D₅/₂ → D₃/₂ is estimated at 2.4 × 10⁻⁴ s⁻¹, a 9 × 10⁻⁵ branch, 98 times Ca+'s because the D fine-structure splitting is 4.6 times larger and M1 rates scale as σ³, so the D₅/₂ collapse operators carry a D₃/₂ channel at that weight **[background: LS-coupling estimate, no source]**. Theory values bracket the measurement (Poirier 1993, 396 ms; Sahoo 2006, 357(12) ms; Jiang 2009, 394(3) ms); Nichol et al. 2022 quote about 400 ms citing Sahoo, a number that matches neither, and it must not be propagated. The NIST clock frequency 444 779 044 095 484.6 Hz gives λ_vac = 674.025591 nm, the plan's value to 1.3 ppb, while NIST's air Ritz wavelength 673.8392 nm for the same line is the 276 ppm trap of the previous paragraph **[verified]**. The E2/E1 amplitude suppression is k a₀/2 ≈ 2.3 × 10⁻⁴ ≈ α/16 at optical wavelengths, not the often-repeated α/2, which overstates E2 couplings by 10 to 40 times **[corrected]**.

**Where the metastable lifetimes come from, and which number the species table stores** (Kreuter et al. 2005, verified in Run 5) **[verified]**, **[corrected]**. The 40Ca+ D lifetimes exist in two flavours the table must not merge. **Measured**, single ion: τ(D₅/₂) = 1168(9) ms and τ(D₃/₂) = 1176(11) ms statistical, each with one-sided systematic corrections of a few ms (repumping, detection error, and for D₃/₂ motional heating) that the source never quadrature-combines with the statistical error, so they are stored as separate signed bounds at about 1% tolerance **[verified]**. **Theoretical**, all-order: τ(D₃/₂) = 1196(11) ms and τ(D₅/₂) = 1165(11) ms, ratio 1.0259(9), following end to end from the single-channel E2 rate A_vw = (1.11995 × 10¹⁸/λ[Å]⁵)|⟨v‖Q‖w⟩|²/(2j_v + 1) s⁻¹ on the reduced quadrupole elements 7.939(37) and 9.740(47) a.u. at the NIST **vacuum** wavelengths 7325.91 Å and 7293.48 Å with the **upper**-state degeneracy: recomputed, 1195.7 ms, 1165.5 ms and 1.025957, with 2 dQ/Q reproducing the printed ±11 ms and so confirming both the |Q|² and the 1/(2j_v + 1) normalization, and with the competing M1 D₃/₂-S₁/₂ channel 10.1 orders smaller, which is what justifies one collapse operator per D level rather than assuming it. That prefactor is Johnson's (1/15)(ω/c)⁵ normalization and is not portable: the competing (1/75) convention gives exactly one fifth of it, so its matrix elements are √5 larger **[recomputed here]**. The two theoretical errors are pure 2 dQ/Q and therefore correlated, so they must not be quadrature-combined into the ratio error, whose ±9 × 10⁻⁴ comes from the spread of calculation variants **[corrected]**. Three further channels are implemented and default off, as physical inputs rather than fudge factors. Blackbody M1 mixing between the D levels is W₁₂ = A₁₂ n̄(ν, T) with n̄ = 1/(e^{hν/kT} − 1) - **multiply** by the Bose occupation, since dividing by it is low by n̄² = 8.75× at 300 K - giving 7.249 × 10⁻⁶ s⁻¹ at 300.0 K but 7.230 × 10⁻⁶ s⁻¹ at 299.3 K, which is what the printed 7.23 × 10⁻⁶ back-solves to, from A₁₂ = 2.45 × 10⁻⁶ s⁻¹ (Ali and Kim 1988 theory, not a measurement) and ν = 1.82 THz; at 8.5 × 10⁻⁶ of the natural rate the default is to omit the channel and expose T, and the upward rate carries g_{5/2}/g_{3/2} = 6/4 **[recomputed here]**, **[corrected]**. Collisional quenching and j-mixing are pressure-driven collapse operators with per-ion rates R = Σ_s Γ_s p_s/(k_BT) from Knoop et al. 1995's four coefficients as quoted by Kreuter, j-mixing running 7.7× quenching; the recomputed total at 300 K and 10⁻¹¹ mbar per partner is 4.36 × 10⁻⁴ s⁻¹ against the printed "< 3 × 10⁻⁴ s⁻¹", so that bound is rounded and not reproducible from its own coefficients, though either value is below the 10⁻³ neglect threshold **[verified]**, **[contested]**. Reshelving is ṗ_D = −Γp_D + R(1 − p_D), whose long-delay offset R/(Γ + R) is the experimental signature; the bias direction is the subtle part, since Γ′ = Γ + R is the **faster** rate yet a bare exponential fitted to a decay-plus-floor returns a **longer** apparent lifetime, which is why the source enters reshelving as a negative correction, and recomputation confirms that sign but not its magnitude **[recomputed here]**. **Reconciliation with what this subsection stores:** the plan's 1.168(7) s (Barton et al. 2000, via Schindler) and Kreuter's 1168(9) ms are independent measurements agreeing to their quoted precision, and Kreuter's own offset-model refit gives 1165(10) ms with R_fit = 3(2) × 10⁻³ s⁻¹, consistent with zero, so the `QuadrupoleTransition` record keeps 1.168 s as its τ_upper with Kreuter's signed budget attached and Barton as the concordant second measurement, while the theoretical 1165(11) ms is stored separately as a cross-check on the Racah-normalized reduced element and never as a lifetime. For 88Sr+ Run 5 itself adds nothing: it never reached Letchumanan et al. 2005 (the cached PDF was a publisher access wall), so its own brief still lists a modern measured 4d ²D₅/₂ lifetime as the one item with no source at all. That gap was closed independently on 2026-09-04 from Letchumanan's thesis at 390.8(1.6) ms, which is the value the paragraph above already carries, and Run 5 neither corroborates nor disturbs it - so nothing here is re-flagged, and the contrast with Ca+ stands on the Sr+ side too: its own blackbody deshelving channel, D₅/₂ → P₃/₂ at 1032.7 nm, is eleven orders below the decay rate at 300 K, not the percent-level correction the Run 5 brief assumed when it demanded one **[verified]**.

**Closed-form anchor and worked numbers.** On the stretched |Δm| = 2 component at φ = γ = 90°, |Λ|g = (1/√6)(1/√6) = 1/6, the global maximum of |Λ|g over all ten components and all geometries (runner-up Δm = 0 at (45°, 0°) with 0.31623 × 0.5 = 0.15811), and the reduced element cancels: Ω = (eE₀/ħ) √(5λ_vac³A/(64π³cα)) **[verified flag]** (Roos p. 31; James Eqs. 5.11 and 5.13; 90/(12² × 8) = 5/64 exactly). Roos's worked example, 100 mW in a 30 µm waist (E₀ = 2.309 × 10⁵ V/m, printed 2.3 × 10⁵, "30 µm²" a typo for the waist), gives Ω/2π = 1.1495 MHz with his inputs (printed 1.2 MHz, a rounding) and 1.088 MHz with the vacuum wavelength and the current lifetime, a 460 ns π time, with ηΩ/2π = 57.7 kHz for the sideband **[corrected]**. James's alternative master formula Ω₀ = (e|E|/(ħ√(cα)))√(A/k³) σ with σ^{(E2)} = √(15(2j′+1)/4)|Σ_q (3j) c^{(q)}_{ij}ε_i n_j| is the same object; its k³ against the rate's k⁵ comes from the explicit k/2 prefactor of his E2 Rabi definition, not from anything hidden in the dimensionless σ, and his worked σ values are for P₁/₂ and D₃/₂ and are not unique to a geometry **[corrected]**.

**Zeeman structure, sensitivities and the qubit pair** **[verified flag]**. With I = 0 the LS Landé factors g_J = 2 (S₁/₂), 2/3 (P₁/₂), 4/3 (P₃/₂), 4/5 (D₃/₂), 6/5 (D₅/₂) place the component (m, m′) at ν₀ + (g_D m′ − g_S m)μ_B B/h (μ_B/h = 1.399624 MHz/G); the ten allowed components span 5.6 μ_B B/h (31.4 MHz at 4 G, Roos's "approximately 30 MHz"), the extremes being S(+½) → D(−3/2) and S(−½) → D(+3/2), not the stretched pair; the Δm = 0 qubit S(−½) ↔ D(−½) used by Innsbruck has coefficient 0.4 (0.560 MHz/G, tied with S(+½) ↔ D(+½)) and the stretched S(−½) ↔ D(−5/2) sideband-cooling line 2.0 (2.799 MHz/G), exactly 5 times more sensitive with the LS factors (the measured g_S ≈ 2.00226 and g_D ≈ 1.20033 shift this at the 0.1% level and were not verified here). The general hyperfine-Zeeman machinery of Section 4.5.1 reproduces these as its I = 0 case. Schindler's paper labels its flopping formula p_{|1⟩} although |1⟩ is the bright pumped ground state; the sin² sum is the D₅/₂ excitation probability **[corrected]**.

**Dissipation, dephasing and crosstalk for the optical qubit** **[corrected]**. The collapse operator is √(A · w(m, m′)) |S, m⟩⟨D, m′| with the same A that fixed the Rabi frequency, so drive and decay cannot drift apart, and with the per-component weights w from the same 3-j squares (Σ over m and q at fixed m′ equals 1/6); a lumped √A σ₋ is adequate at A ≈ 0.86 s⁻¹ for microsecond gates. Dephasing is dominated below about 1 ms by laser-frequency noise and above it by the components' Zeeman sensitivities; Schindler's measured decay is not exponential, so the simulator does not default to a √(1/(2τ₂)) σ_z Lindblad term but routes the coherence through Section 6.9's `filter_function` with the free-induction filter F = 4 sin²(ωT/2) and S_b = S_δ/4, the two-sided PSD of the σ_z coefficient (half the laser-frequency fluctuation), so that C(T) = exp[−(2/π)∫₀^∞ dω S_b(ω) F(ω)/ω²] in Section 6.9's normalization and under no second convention (the second revision printed a local form exp[−∫dω A(ω)² sin²(ωT/2)/ω²] with no prefactor, no sidedness and A undefined, which differs from the hyperfine path by 2/π or by 4 depending on how A² is read, the factor-4 trap Section 6.9 warns against, on the only default dephasing model of the 729 and 674 nm qubits; 2026-09-04 theorist critique) **[corrected: critique, 2026-09-04]**, or a sampled phase trajectory with that spectrum (Section 6.3). Shot-to-shot intensity noise (0.41(6)% measured on the ions, 1.5% source specification, on second-to-minute timescales) is a per-shot Rabi-frequency sample, with ΔΩ/Ω = ½ΔI/I for a resonant carrier drive and ΔI/I for an ac-Stark operation. Addressing crosstalk on the 729 nm beam is the amplitude ratio ε_{ij} = Ω_j/Ω_i of Section 6.6 (Schindler prints it inverted and squared; the paper's 22/121 = 18% is the amplitude ratio, 3.3% the intensity ratio that matches its stated < 3% bound), the spectator ac-Stark rotation carries ε² in exp(−iθ/2 Σ_j ε_{ij}² σ_z^{(j)}), and because the error is unitary a round-trip test inverts the addressing matrix and recovers the ideal gate.

**What the species table gains.** A `QuadrupoleTransition` record per (isotope, lower, upper) holding λ_vac, τ_upper, the branching fraction b, j_lower, j_upper: 40Ca+ (729.347 nm, 1.168 s, 1.0, 1/2, 5/2) and 88Sr+ (674.02559 nm, 0.3908 s, 1.0 less the 9 × 10⁻⁵ M1 branch, 1/2, 5/2), from which the module derives k, A, the reduced element (asserting 9.73 a₀² and 13.81 a₀², with 14.70 a₀² from the superseded 0.345 s as the negative control), the ten Rabi frequencies for any beam geometry, and the Lamb-Dicke parameters from the same k (η = 0.0969 for a single 40Ca+ at 1 MHz along the beam; Schindler's quoted 6% and 2.5% at 22.5° and 67.5° to the axis are mutually consistent with the cos-projection of one k but 1.49 times smaller than the single-ion 1 MHz values, and refer to an unnamed mode or frequency, so the simulator takes only the geometry from that source). The ac Stark shift of a driven component by the nine off-resonant components inside the 30 MHz Zeeman span is computed by second-order perturbation from the same Ω(m, m′) table, unvalidated against any published number (Section 12).

### 4.6 Transport, splitting, merging and junctions (specification for milestone M12)

Transport is a non-goal of the first release (Section 1.3). Run 5 read the transport, splitting and junction literature to the equation level anyway, because the phenomenological per-transport quanta budget has to be replaced eventually and because the crystal solver, the S_E model and the scheduler of Sections 4.1, 4.1.5 and 7.3 are what a transport module would consume. This subsection fixes the equations and the printed source errors for milestone M12 (Section 10); nothing in Sections 5 to 9 depends on it. Its conventions are Section 13's new rows, its numbers are recomputed in `validation/scripts/check_transport.py` and tabulated in Section 9.15, and its gaps are in Section 12.

**The exact reduction to a driven, parametrically modulated oscillator.** For an ion of charge Q and mass m on the axis in a potential φ(q, t) whose minimum follows a prescribed transport function q₀(t), set φ_res = φ − φ_local(q − q₀) − φ₀(t) and a_res = −(Q/m) ∂φ_res/∂q. In u = q − q₀(t) the equation ü + ω₀²u = −q̈₀ + a_res(u + q₀) is exact; expanding a_res about q₀(t) and truncating at first order (the source assumes a_res^{(n)} = 0 for n ≥ 2 throughout) gives **[verified]** (Reichle et al. 2006, Eqs. 2-7)

ℋ(t) = p²/2m + (m ω²(t)/2) u² − m f(t) u,  ω²(t) = ω₀²(1 − a′_res[q₀(t)]/ω₀²),  f(t) = −q̈₀(t) + a_res[q₀(t)],

with p = mu̇ the *moving-frame* momentum and f an acceleration. Eq. (7) follows from the lab Lagrangian only after discarding d/dt(m q̇₀ u), which converts the inertial cross term into −m q̈₀ u, so expectation values of p are moving-frame and the coherent amplitude is built in that gauge. Second-quantizing with u = √(ħ/2mω₀)(a + a†) at fixed ω₀ gives H/ħ = ω₀(a†a + ½) + [(ω²(t) − ω₀²)/(4ω₀)](a + a†)² − f(t)√(m/(2ħω₀))(a + a†): the (a + a†)² term is the squeezing channel, vanishing identically only at constant curvature, and the linear term alone makes the propagator a pure displacement **[verified]**. Two solver conventions follow: ω²(t) may go negative when a′_res > ω₀², a momentary well inversion, and the ladder operators are referred to the instantaneous well centre, never the lab origin **[background]**.

**The exact quantum solution, and the Ermakov normalization its source contradicts.** ℋ being quadratic, the Lewis-Riesenfeld invariant gives the exact propagator U(t) = e^{iΦ} D(α(t)) S(ξ(δ, θ)) - a displacement always, a squeeze only if ω was modulated - with variances ħρ²/2m and ħm(ρ⁻² + ρ̇²)/2 that depend on ρ alone, never on f **[verified]** (Reichle Eqs. 30-42). The classical system is ρ̈ + ω²(t)ρ = 1/ρ³ with ρ²μ̇ = 1, Green function G(t, t′) = θ(t − t′)ρ(t)ρ(t′)sin(μ(t) − μ(t′)), driven solution u_p = ∫_{−t₀}^{t} G f dt′ and suppression amplitude Ξ = u̇_p + iω u_p with transferred energy 𝒲 = m|Ξ|²/2, the last valid only where f(t₁) = 0 and for a start at rest, since it omits the −m f u term. The printed ρ²μ̇ = 1 makes ρ numerically ω^{−1/2}, [ρ] = √(s/rad) - the only assignment consistent with the closed-form solution, the adiabatic expansion and ρ(−t₀) = 1/√ω₀, and the only one making u_p a length, Ξ a velocity and 𝒲 a joule **[corrected]**. The paper's footnote, that the Eq. (11) constant "has the SI units m² rad/s", describes a second bookkeeping in which ρ is a pure length and the constant C is carried explicitly; each is self-consistent and the two are never mixed **[corrected]**. Only the sign of ρ is free and it cancels, and ρ and μ depend on ω(t) alone, so cache them per frequency waveform **[background]**.

**Excitation: the Husimi-Kerner parameter and two conjugate integral forms.** At constant ω = ω₀ under a homogeneous force, P_mn = (μ!/ν!) γ^{ν−μ} e^{−γ}[L_μ^{ν−μ}(γ)]² with ν = max(m, n), μ = min(m, n), so the added quanta are exactly γ and P_m0 is Poissonian **[verified]** (Reichle Eqs. 27-29, after Husimi and Kerner). The parameter is γ = m|u̇_c + iω₀u_c|²/(2ħω₀) = m|Ξ|²/(2ħω₀) = 𝒲/(ħω₀) = |α|². The paper prints the prefactor unparenthesized as "m/2 ħ ω₀"; the literal reading is wrong by (ħω₀)² and dimensionally invalid, so code writes m/(2ħω₀) **[corrected]**. γ = |α|² ties the two published pictures together: Bowler's α(t) = √(mω/2ħ)(−e^{−iωt} ∫₀^t ż₀(t′)e^{+iωt′}dt′) is the accumulated well-frame displacement **[verified]** (Bowler et al. 2012, Eq. 1, credited to Lau and James 2011) and Reichle's Ξ(t₁) = −e^{+iω₀t₁} ∫ e^{−iω₀t′} q̈₀ dt′ is its integration by parts. The two carry the *conjugate* phase convention (velocity form e^{+iωt′} inside with e^{−iωt} outside, acceleration form the reverse), so fix one globally before chaining segments; magnitudes agree exactly **[verified]**. Bowler's Eq. (1) is exact for all t only when α pairs the well-centred coordinate with the *lab* momentum; built instead in Reichle's moving-frame p = mu̇ gauge it acquires a boundary term in ż₀(t) − ż₀(0)e^{−iωt}, so the two coincide only when the well is at rest at both ends **[corrected]**.

**Waveform design: the Fourier criterion on the well acceleration.** In the well-controlled regime (1 ≫ |a′_res|/ω₀², |q̈₀| ≫ |a_res|) the excitation is the Fourier component of the well *acceleration* at ω₀ **[verified]** (Reichle Eqs. 43-45, 51). Ξ is that transform only up to the unimodular factor −e^{iω₀t₁} and only over the finite window [−t₀, t₁]; the infinite-window identification is exact when the acceleration vanishes outside, and the phase is kept when segments add coherently **[corrected]**. Splitting q₀ about t = 0 gives, on the symmetric interval t₁ = t₀, |Ξ(t₀)|² = |∫ sin(ω₀t′) q̈₀^A dt′|² + |∫ cos(ω₀t′) q̈₀^S dt′|², two non-negative independent terms, so purely antisymmetric transport is a design *choice* justified by that decomposition, not an approximation **[verified]**. Tilding by the half-distance b/2 so that q̃₀(±t₀) = ±1 gives γ(t₀) = (m b² ω₀/(8ħ))|Ξ̃(t₀)/ω₀|²; the prefactor is 5.3498 × 10⁷ for 9Be+ over b = 400 µm at ω₀/2π = 3 MHz, so γ ≤ 1 needs |Ξ̃/ω₀|² ≤ 1.8692 × 10⁻⁸, printed as 2 × 10⁻⁸ *without* the tilde, and the sudden limit Ξ̃/ω₀ → 2 returns ħω₀γ = ½ m ω₀² b² exactly, the full potential energy and the normalization test **[recomputed here]**. Eq. (51)'s asymptotic form makes the excitation depend on the transport function's derivatives at the *start* point, and by antisymmetry the end, which is why smooth ramps beat a sine ramp **[verified]**.

**Closed forms for the sine and erf ramps, and the quanta figures that do not reproduce.** For q̃₀ = sin(πt/2t₀), Ξ̃(t₀)/ω₀ = −i e^{ix}·2cos x/(1 − (2x/π)²) with x = ω₀t₀; the factor 2 and the phase were re-derived from Eq. (43) and stand as printed **[verified]** (Reichle Eq. 46). |Ξ̃|² ∝ (1 + cos 2x) under the envelope 4/(1 − (2x/π)²)², whose zeros are at x/2π = (2n + 3)/4 = 0.75, 1.25, 1.75, … (the n = 0 root x = π/2 cancelled by the vanishing denominator) and which crosses γ = 1 at x/2π = 30.238 for b = 400 µm and 52.373 for b = 1.2 mm, 60 and 105 total cycles **[recomputed here]**. For q̃₀(s) = Erf(2s/t_p)/Erf(2t₀/t_p) with y = ω₀t_p, Ξ̃/ω₀ ∼ 2 e^{−y²/16} Re{Erf[2x/y + i y/4]}/Erf[2x/y] → 2 e^{−y²/16} as 2x/y → ∞ **[verified]** (Reichle Eqs. 48-50; the source writes "b" for y/4 inside Eq. 50 while b is the transport distance elsewhere). Its two erf quanta claims fail, however: the asymptote squared, 4 e^{−y²/8}, gives γ = 3.259 at y = 12, b = 400 µm where the paper says the criterion is met, and the exact Eq. (49) at y = 13, x/2π = 4 gives γ = 4.322 at b = 2.2 mm where it claims less than a quantum **[recomputed here]**. Both are sub-quantum only if the verified factor 2 is dropped, so its numerics almost certainly lost it: the equations are the regression targets and the printed quanta are not, and γ = 1 needs y = 12.39 and 13.44, still an order of magnitude shorter than the sine ramp **[corrected]**, **[recomputed here]**.

**The impulsive limit as an analytic test case.** For a rectangular velocity pulse (ż₀ = v on [0, t_T], zero outside), α(t_T) = √(mω/2ħ)(i v/ω)(1 − e^{−iωt_T}) and n̄ = (2mv²/ħω) sin²(ωt_T/2), with exact recapture at ωt_T = 2πN and minima spaced by 1/t_T in ordinary frequency **[verified]** (Bowler Eq. 2, the 2πN condition credited to Couvert et al. 2008). It is a test case only: at Bowler's 370 µm in 8 µs it gives n̄ = 2.05 × 10⁴, and the paper calls sudden starts and stops impractical **[verified]**, **[recomputed here]**.

**Splitting: the quartic double well in volts, and two printed defects in its equations of motion.** The separation potential is Φ(x) = β x⁴ + α x² + γ x, an electrostatic potential in *volts* about the centre of the centre segment C, with β > 0 required once α ≤ 0, α swept α_i > 0 → 0 → α_f < 0, and γ a tilt whose axial field is E = −γ **[corrected]** (Kaufmann et al. 2014, Eqs. 1-2; the cubic term vanishes only for an axially symmetric trap, terms above quartic are dropped). The two-ion total is Φ_tot = Φ(x₀ + d/2) + Φ(x₀ − d/2) + κ/d with κ = e/(4πε₀) = 1.4400 × 10⁻⁹ V m, *not* e²/(4πε₀); the energy is eΦ_tot, and reading Φ_tot as an energy breaks every downstream prefactor **[corrected]**, **[recomputed here]**. Bowler's U(z) = a(t)z² + b(t)z⁴ is the same object as an energy with a = eα, b = eβ, and a third normalization V = ½αx² + ¼βx⁴ differs by 2 and 4 **[background]**. The printed equations of motion carry two defects neither author states: the right-hand sides have units V/m against N on the left, so one factor of e is missing, and because the printed left side is −mẍ the ± pair goes "+" with x₁ and "−" with x₂ for x₁ < x₂. Implement m ẍ₁ = −e[4β(t)x₁³ + 2α(t)x₁ + γ] − eκ/(x₂ − x₁)² and m ẍ₂ = −e[…] + eκ/(x₂ − x₁)², ions at rest initially; the opposite pairing makes the interaction attractive and collapses the crystal, the equilibrium condition 2α(d/2) − κ/d² = 0 having no root at all **[corrected]**, **[recomputed here]**.

**Splitting: segment voltages to coefficients, with the antisymmetric half-amplitude tilt.** α = Σ_n U_n α_n and β = Σ_n U_n β_n over n ∈ {C, S, O} are strictly linear in the segment voltages, while γ = ΔU_S γ_S + ΔU_O γ_O + γ′ is affine through the stray-field offset γ′, with α_n = ½ f_n φ″_{n,C}(0), β_n = (1/24) f_n φ^{(4)}_{n,C}(0), γ_n = f_n φ′_{n,C}(0) and multiplicities f_C = 1, f_{S,O} = 2 for the symmetric flanking pairs, applied to the Taylor coefficients only and never inside the electrode sum **[verified]** (Kaufmann Eqs. 3-5, 7). ΔU_n is the *antisymmetric half-amplitude*, +ΔU_n right and −ΔU_n left, so the physical right-minus-left difference is 2ΔU_n; this is the only reading consistent with f_{S,O} = 2, a full-difference reading being wrong by −2, and tilt compensation sets ΔU_O = γ/γ_O **[corrected]**. Maximizing β at the critical point pins U_O = +U_lim, U_S = −U_lim and eliminates U_C, giving β_CP = 2.570 × 10¹⁴ V/m⁴ for trap A at U_lim = 10 V **[recomputed here]** (Eqs. 33-34, Table 1). The piecewise static sets on the α mesh (Eqs. 35-38) are self-consistent only with their α domains restored: without them the four expressions are two pairs of contradictory definitions, U_O sits on a plateau at +U_lim spanning the critical point, and U_C is re-solved at every mesh point **[corrected]**. Nor is the recipe geometry-general: a surface trap with a segmented ground plane has β_C and β_S sign-flipped against every three-dimensional trap in the source's own table, and there the quartic truncation breaks down **[verified]**.

**Splitting: the critical point, and the two-ion mode structure the plan derives there.** At α = 0, γ = 0, d_CP = (2κ/β)^{1/5} and ω_CP = β^{3/10}(3e/m)^{1/2}(2κ)^{1/5} **[verified]** (Kaufmann Eqs. 14-15, the scaling credited to Home and Steane 2006), which for trap A is 25.70 µm and 176.4 kHz against its two-figure tabulated 0.18 MHz; the printed form, the equivalent √(3eβ/m) d_CP and the Eq. (9) external curvature agree to machine precision, while κ = e²/(4πε₀) would return about 31 Hz **[corrected]**, **[recomputed here]**. The perturbative separation d(α) ≈ d_CP − (1/5)(16/(β⁴κ))^{1/5} α + (2/25)(4/(β⁷κ³))^{1/5} α² is *linear*-dominated, ∂d/∂α|_CP = −2/(5β d_CP) ≠ 0 with the quadratic term only 4.0% of the linear one at the edge of the stated range |α| ≪ β d_CP²; calling d quadratic in α zeroes out the impulsive mechanism the paper is built on **[corrected]**, **[recomputed here]**. Neither source names or quantizes the two-ion normal modes, so the plan derives them from the Hessian of eΦ_tot: at the critical point ∂²(eΦ_tot)/∂x₀² = 6eβd_CP², ∂²/∂d² = (5/2)eβd_CP², the cross derivative vanishes, and with effective masses 2m and m/2 this gives ω_COM = √(3eβ/m) d_CP = ω_CP *exactly* (Coulomb does not couple to x₀) and ω_str = √(5/3) ω_CP = 1.29099 ω_CP, confirmed by building the two-ion potential and diagonalizing the mass-weighted Hessian numerically **[background]**, **[recomputed here]**. So ω_str = √3 ω_COM does *not* hold near the critical point, and the centre-of-mass mode does not soften to zero because the quartic still supports it; Bowler's simulated 700 and 880 kHz minima for a different trap give 1.257, corroboration rather than confirmation, so this stays a derivation to re-check **[verified]**.

**Splitting: the final well and the two distance ramps.** Past the critical point the Coulomb term is dropped and the geometry is the quartic double well: d_f = √(−2α_f/β) and ω_f² = −4eα_f/m, the factor 4 being the quartic curvature 12βx_min² at a minimum **[corrected]**. At trap A's α_f = −1.70 × 10⁷ V/m² this is ω_f/2π = 2.038 MHz and d_f = 363.7 µm, while the factor-2 form gives 1.441 MHz, a √2 error straight into every n̄ = ΔE/(ħω_f) **[recomputed here]**. Note the asymmetry: *before* the critical point the quartic is neglected and ω² = 2eα/m, *after* it the Coulomb is, so neither limit is ordinary harmonic and every phonon number carries its reference frequency - the same energy gives 2.0 × 10⁴ quanta against ω_f and 2.3 × 10⁵ against ω_CP **[corrected]**, **[recomputed here]**. Of the printed ramps, d = d_i + (d_f − d_i) sin²(πt/2T) stands as printed but has non-vanishing endpoint acceleration d̈(0) = (d_f − d_i)π²/2T² **[verified]**, while the quintic carries a global sign typo: the printed bracket (−10 t³/T³ + 15 t⁴/T⁴ − 6 t⁵/T⁵) equals −1 at t = T, terminating at 2d_i − d_f, about −358 µm for trap A - a negative separation with the ions crossing and the Coulomb term diverging **[corrected]**, **[recomputed here]**. Implement d(t) = d_i + (d_f − d_i)(10 t³/T³ − 15 t⁴/T⁴ + 6 t⁵/T⁵), the only form with ḋ = d̈ = 0 at both ends and the default; those endpoint conditions are invariant under the flip, which is why the typo survived into print.

**Splitting: the diabatic transfer, its Gaussian suppression, and the incoherent floor.** Diabatically the equilibrium separation cannot follow and the pair takes an impulsive displacement δd_CP ≈ (∂d/∂α)|_CP α̇_CP ξ τ_CP/2 with τ_CP = π/ω_CP, giving δE = ½ m ω_CP²(δd_CP/2)² = (π²/8) ξ² m (β⁴κ)^{−2/5} α̇_CP² per ion with the fitted ξ² ≈ 0.1 **[verified]** (Kaufmann Eqs. 21-25). The printed π²/8 and the fitted ξ² are used *as a pair*: chaining Eqs. (21)-(24) gives π²/32, exactly a quarter, requiring a refit to ξ² ≈ 0.4; Eq. (23) also drops the exact prefactor 16^{1/5}/5 = 0.34822 and the minus sign of ∂d/∂α; the sign is harmless for the quadratic δE and matters only for directional use of δd_CP, but the prefactor is not, since δE ∝ (∂d/∂α)² and restoring it multiplies δE by 0.34822² = 0.1213, turning the chained π²/32 into π²/264, so the plan states one pairing end to end, the printed Eq. (25) with its π²/8 and the fitted ξ² ≈ 0.1 exactly as the source calibrated them (the Section 13 row), and never the chained π²/32 or the prefactor-restored π²/264, each of which needs its own refitted ξ² (about 0.4 and about 3.3, the latter no longer readable as a fractional diabatic impulse); the three pairings are mutually exclusive and the code refuses to mix them (the second revision called the prefactor harmless; 2026-09-04 theorist critique) **[recomputed here]**, **[corrected: critique, 2026-09-04]**. Adiabaticity is χ = d̈_CP/(d_CP ω_CP²), χ < 1 adiabatic, with d̈_CP = (4/25)(4/(β⁷κ³))^{1/5} α̇_CP² for the relative coordinate; the printed 2/25 is short by a factor 2, and 4/25 reproduces the printed χ to one part in 10¹⁵ **[corrected]**, **[recomputed here]**. In the adiabatic branch δE′ ≈ δE exp[c²(1 − 1/χ)]; for a uniform sweep α̇ ∝ 1/T both δE and χ scale as T⁻², so the suppression is *Gaussian* in the splitting duration, not exponential in it, and c is undefined in its source (the string occurs exactly once, inside the formula), an O(1) fit parameter a trap-A reconstruction puts near 0.93 **[corrected]**, **[background]**. The incoherent floor is the Section 4.1.5 rate Γ_h(ω) = e² S_E(ω)/(4mħω) integrated as Δn̄_th = ∫₀^T Γ_h(ω(t)) dt (Kaufmann Eq. 32, identical to Blakestad's Eq. 18, so one S_E model drives the splitting, transport and junction channels); because both ramps depend only on t/T at fixed endpoints, ω(t) is a function of t/T alone and the integral is *strictly proportional to T*, so the source's clause that "the anomalous heating contribution is scaling as 1/T" contradicts its own equation and figure and must not be implemented **[corrected]**. The optimum duration is therefore the crossing of a T⁻²-then-Gaussian coherent term against a linear thermal one, tens of microseconds; the apparatus law Γ_h ≈ 6.3 (f/MHz)^{−1.81} ms⁻¹, 140/ms at the 0.18 MHz critical point, is apparatus- rather than geometry-specific **[verified]**, **[recomputed here]**.

**Splitting: tilt as the dominant practical failure mode, and the trap-size scaling.** A residual tilt opens the second well only at α̃ = −(3/2)β^{1/3}|γ|^{2/3}, with the saddle at x = ½(γ/β)^{1/3} (a double root of 4u³ − 3u + 1 = (2u − 1)²(u + 1) in the reduced coordinate) and the surviving minimum at −(γ/β)^{1/3}. The barrier height is ΔV_c = V(saddle) − V(minimum) = (27/16)(γ⁴/β)^{1/3}, 27/16 = 1.6875 exactly, the printed subtraction order giving −27/16 **[corrected]**, **[recomputed here]**. Above a critical tilt the Coulomb repulsion cannot push the right ion over the saddle and both stay in one well, so the split fails outright: γ̃ = C_γ(κ³β_CP²)^{1/5} with the *numerically fitted* C_γ = 1.06 and the requirement |γ| < γ̃, the source printing Eq. (20) as an inequality on γ̃ itself, which is not its content **[corrected]**. For trap A γ̃ = 3.050 V/m and the tolerance is ΔU_O = γ̃/γ_O = 9.53 mV, matching the paper's "more accurately than about 9 mV"; only the exponent is derived, the source's own saddle-balance heuristic giving (8/27)^{3/5} = 0.482, and since γ̃ ∝ β_CP^{2/5} the *tolerance grows* with the voltage limit, the opposite of its wording **[corrected]**, **[recomputed here]**. Below γ̃ the split is asymmetric: ∂²x_{R,L}/∂α² = γ·52·2^{4/5}/(675 β^{9/5}κ^{6/5}) ± (2/25)(4/(β⁷κ³))^{1/5}, where ±2/25 is *correct as printed*, exactly half of the relative coordinate's 4/25 through its own explicit ±½ (an extracted item proposed "fixing" it to 4/25, doubling the per-ion kick) **[corrected]**. So moderate tilt reduces the transfer to one ion and raises it for the other: the two output qubits get different coherent amplitudes and the model records which was kicked. The acceleration diverges as |γ| → γ̃, with the doubling point at 0.7425 γ̃ against the source's stated ~67%, and Bowler's +3 mV on one outer electrode leaving n̄ ≈ 1.14 in one zone but n̄ > 15 in the other anchors the sharpness **[verified]**, **[recomputed here]**. Attainable quartic confinement scales as β_CP ∝ d_eff^{−4} with d_eff = (w² + h² + d²)^{1/2}, and the *exponent alone* is usable: the printed prefactor 2.2 × 10²⁴ V is 23 orders too large in SI and 8× too small with d_eff in micrometres, against an implied 17.7 V for that trap **[corrected]**, **[recomputed here]**.

**Junctions: the pseudopotential barrier and the sum rule that must be switched off.** The only junction in the source set is one two-wafer three-dimensional X design with rf bridges, so this layer is single-apparatus. The total potential is Φ(r) = φ_ps(r) + Σ_n V_n φ̃_n(r) with φ_ps = q(V_rf ∇φ̃_rf)²/(4mΩ_rf²), *also a potential in volts*, the ion's energy being qΦ and the denominator 4 rather than 2 the *peak* V_rf convention **[verified]** (Blakestad et al. 2011, Eqs. 1-2). φ_ps ≥ 0 identically and is *not* a Laplace solution, so the Hessian of the total Φ is not traceless - only the φ̃_n basis is - while linearity in V_n makes the constrained least-squares waveform solve possible with φ_ps a fixed additive term **[verified]**. The four barriers, one per entrance, are about 0.3 eV for 9Be+ at V_rf ≈ 200 V peak and Ω_rf ≈ 2π × 83 MHz, which fixes |∇φ̃_rf| = 873 m⁻¹, and rescale as 1/m at fixed charge, giving 68 meV for 40Ca+ and 16 meV for 171Yb+; the paper's own boundary-element pseudopotential needed a 1.4× scaling to match its measured rf-noise heating, so that factor is an explicit fit parameter **[verified]**, **[recomputed here]**. Everywhere ω_i² = ω̃_i² + ω_rf,i² with Σ_i ω̃_i² = 0, ω̃_i the control contribution (imaginary means anti-trapping) **[verified]** (Eq. 13); but ω_x² + ω_y² + ω_z² = 2ω_rf² holds only where ω_rf,z = 0, that is in the straight channels, because inside the junction the pseudopotential has axial curvature - *anti*-confining at the apex, confining at the centre - so the solver asserts that sum rule outside and disables it within **[corrected]**. At the junction centre all control voltages are zero and the pseudopotential alone traps; channel equivalence h_x = h_z plus a traceless rf Hessian forces h_y = −2h_x, and with ω_rf,i ∝ |h_i| this predicts ω_y = 2ω_x, 11.4 MHz against the reported 11.3 MHz at ω_x/2π = 5.7 MHz **[background]**, **[recomputed here]**. That x-z degeneracy leaves the principal axes ill-defined and can rotate a two-ion crystal uncontrollably; the reported data lifted it with a shim giving |ω′_x − ω′_z| > 2π × 400 kHz, so reproducing exact degeneracy will not match the measured excitation, the alternative being a dwell tuned to a full mode-mixing cycle **[verified]**.

**Junctions: residual micromotion and the two technical excitation channels.** Residual axial rf field gives axial micromotion z₁^peak = qE₀/(mΩ_rf²) = 2√(qφ_ps/(mΩ_rf²)) with z₁^rms = z₁^peak/√2. At the experiment zone, 880 µm from the junction centre, qφ_ps = 2.9 × 10⁻⁵ eV gives 68 nm peak against 48 nm rms; the paper quotes 47 nm, the rms-field value, while its Eq. (2) is peak-normalized, so a code path returning 68 nm from the stated 2.9 × 10⁻⁵ eV is correct in the peak convention and not buggy, and that figure is a model estimate at a deliberately low-gradient zone, *not* at the apex where the axial rf field and hence micromotion are maximal **[corrected]**, **[recomputed here]**. Along a traversal ω_z/2π is held near 3.6 MHz on approach, ramped to 4.2 MHz at the apex - the maximum attainable there against the anti-confinement within a ±10 V supply - then to 5.7 MHz at the centre, so the co-moving Hamiltonian needs both the parametric and the displacement term and the Fock cutoff is re-checked at the highest frequency **[verified]**. The first technical channel is rf-drive sideband noise on a pseudopotential slope, ṅ_z = q⁴/(16 m³ Ω_rf⁴ ħ ω_z)[∂(E₀²)/∂z]²[S_{V_N}(Ω_rf + ω_z) + S_{V_N}(Ω_rf − ω_z)]/V_rf², one-sided S_{V_N} in the same convention as Eq. (18), both sidebands summed separately and the normalization fractional **[verified]** (Eqs. 15-16). The rate goes as the *square* of the pseudopotential slope - the prose says "proportional to the slope" while the equation has it squared - and it is one of two mechanisms, neither dominant nor intrinsic: the only apportionment puts rf noise at 0.1 to 0.5 quanta per barrier pass, 3 to 30% of the observed excitation, the remainder attributed primarily to DAC update noise, while anomalous surface heating, the genuinely intrinsic channel, is insignificant here **[corrected]**.
