That scalar bookkeeping is **invalid for alternating axes**, so none of the (−1)^j sums, order results or finite-pulse asymptotics carries over, and no source gives the resulting filter functions, the transverse rows of the filter matrix, or a CPMG-against-XY4 comparison. The first-order dynamically corrected gate is likewise **not implementable**: only amplitudes and timings are given, with no segment phases and no amplitude-time normalization, and the naive reading in which the angles sum to π gives a sequence **worse** than a primitive pulse - yet the DCG is the cited remedy for restoring Carr-Purcell's 18 dB/octave under finite pulses, so both must come from Khodjasteh and Viola directly. What the module does instead is implement the **full 3 × 3 toggling machinery** above, which is axis-agnostic by construction, and build XY4, XY8, KDD, CDD and (XY)^N by composing per-pulse R^{P_l} blocks with the accumulated Λ^{(l−1)} matrices rather than with any scalar sign - tagged **[background]**, no source validating the result - then validate it against the single-axis closed forms, which it must reproduce to quadrature precision: Biercuk's Eq. 2 at both parities, the CPMG and UDD leading coefficients, and a mixed-axis four-interval assembly matched to 4 × 10⁻¹⁰ against brute-force propagation.


## 7. Control stack: native gates, compilation, scheduling and calibration

### 7.1 Native gate set

The simulator's native set is the one exposed by current trapped-ion cloud machines, because it maps one-to-one onto physical pulses:

- **GPi(φ)**: a π rotation about the equatorial axis at azimuth φ. Matrix (IonQ documentation [verified]): `[[0, e^{-iφ}], [e^{iφ}, 0]]`. Physical realization: a carrier pulse of area π with optical (or microwave) phase φ relative to the qubit's phase frame.
- **GPi2(φ)**: a π/2 rotation about the same axis. Matrix: `(1/√2)[[1, -i e^{-iφ}], [-i e^{iφ}, 1]]`. Physical realization: carrier pulse of area π/2.
- **RZ(θ)** (virtual): no pulse. Implemented by advancing the phase frame of the qubit so that every later pulse on that qubit carries an extra phase; zero duration, zero error, apart from the bookkeeping. This is exactly how the hardware does it [verified]; the sign of the frame shift is pinned in Section 7.6.
- **MS(φ0, φ1, θ)**: the Mølmer-Sørensen entangling gate between two qubits with per-qubit phases φ0, φ1 and entangling angle θ ∈ [0, π/2] (IonQ specifies angles in turns, 0 to 0.25 turns; the package accepts radians and turns explicitly). Fully entangling at θ = π/2. Physical realization: a bichromatic pulse on both ions with the beat-note phases set from φ0, φ1 and the amplitude or duration set from θ through the calibration table.
- **ZZ(θ)**: `diag(e^{-iπθ}, e^{iπθ}, e^{iπθ}, e^{-iπθ})` with θ in turns [verified, IonQ documentation, Forte-class]. Physical realization: a light-shift geometric-phase pulse, or an MS pulse wrapped in single-qubit pulses (the Quantinuum construction, also used on Forte [verified]).

The exact matrices, including the partially entangling MS form and the turns convention, are pinned by tests against the vendor documentation and against the research-run extraction; the package documents the phase convention it adopts (Section 13) and provides converters.

### 7.2 Compiler

Input IR: a list of operations `(name, qubits, params)` supporting the native set plus a standard set (X, Y, Z, H, S, T, RX, RY, RZ, CNOT, CZ, SWAP, controlled-phase, arbitrary U3). Compilation rules:

1. Single-qubit standard gates become at most two GPi2 pulses with virtual RZ: any U ∈ SU(2) equals, up to a global phase, RZ(α)·GPi2(0)·RZ(β)·GPi2(0)·RZ(γ), a ZXZXZ Euler decomposition that is complete because GPi2(0)·RZ(β)·GPi2(0) is RY(−β)·RX(π) up to phase and RX(π) is a Pauli, so the angles are the ZYZ Euler angles of U·X (checked on 20 random SU(2) targets to a residual of 0 **[recomputed here]**); every RZ is absorbed into the phases of later pulses. Arbitrary-area carrier pulses exist as an internal capability of the pulse layer, used by the Rabi scans and by the composite-pulse templates of Section 7.7, but the exported native set is GPi, GPi2, MS and ZZ only, so the IonQ JSON round trip of Section 9.6 holds. The compiler tracks a phase frame per qubit and rewrites the phase of every subsequent pulse (phase propagation).
2. CNOT and CZ become one MS (or ZZ) plus single-qubit gates using the standard identities; controlled-phase with arbitrary angle becomes a partially entangling MS or ZZ.
3. The compiler verifies each compiled block against its target unitary numerically (up to global phase) before scheduling; this is the one place ideal matrices are used.
4. Measure, reset and recool are schedulable operations of the IR and of `Schedule` from the first release, so the pipeline shape is prep → [gates | measure | reset | recool]* → results; the first release executes measurement only as the terminal stage and refuses a mid-circuit measure or reset with a clear error, but the IR, the scheduler and `Result` already carry the positions, so that adding the physics of Section 8.5 (detection recoil, neighbour Stark shift, depumping, recooling and re-preparation of the measured ion) is a stage implementation and not a pipeline change (Section 1.3 names a QEC stack as a client, and that is the client that needs it; 2026-09-04 experimentalist critique) **[corrected: critique, 2026-09-04]**.

### 7.3 Scheduler

Turns native gates into pulses with absolute times:
- single-qubit gates on distinct ions may run simultaneously if the device model allows parallel addressing, otherwise sequentially;
- MS gates are serialized (one at a time per crystal in the first release);
- between pulses the scheduler inserts idle intervals with the device's minimum gap, during which the evolution module applies free evolution with heating and dephasing;
- pulse parameters come only from the calibration table (Section 7.5), never from the device's hidden "true" values, so that the simulated machine behaves like a machine whose knowledge of itself is imperfect in exactly the way a laboratory's is; the derived values are allowed only as tagged initial guesses for the calibration scans, and an entry the calibration could not establish is `uncalibrated` and refuses to schedule rather than falling back to them.

### 7.4 Pulse representation

A `Pulse` holds: target ions; drive type; tones, each with detuning schedule δ(t), phase schedule φ(t) and amplitude envelope Ω(t) (a callable or sampled array); duration; the effective wavevector; and metadata (which gate, which modes are intended to close). Envelopes include square with smooth (sine-squared or Blackman) edges, and the segmented or continuous AM/FM/PM waveforms produced by the pulse-shaping solvers for multi-mode gates. The shaping solvers implement the closure conditions on all modes and the entangling-phase condition as constraints (linear in amplitude for segmented AM; nonlinear for FM), with the robustness conditions the literature uses (first derivative of the displacement with respect to detuning equal to zero) as options; Section 4 gives the equations they satisfy.

### 7.5 Calibration emulation

The calibration layer runs simulated experiments and fits them the way the laboratory does:

1. **Carrier Rabi frequency per ion**: Rabi flopping scan of pulse duration on the carrier; fit to the expected curve including the Debye-Waller reduction from the thermal motion of the current preparation; yields the π time and the amplitude scale per ion (which also reveals the addressing beam's intensity profile).
2. **Mode frequencies**: sideband spectroscopy (scan detuning near ±ω_m, observe excitation) or Ramsey-type motional spectroscopy; yields ω_m for the modes used and, from sideband asymmetry, n̄ per mode.
3. **Phase frame alignment**: a Ramsey experiment establishes the reference phase for each ion's frame; MS phase scans establish the entangling axis relative to it.
4. **Entangling gate**: for the chosen pair, scan the detuning and amplitude (or duration) of the bichromatic pulse; fit populations and parity oscillations to find the closure point and χ = π/4; store amplitude, detuning, duration and the phase offsets.
5. **Detection threshold and window**: histogram bright and dark photon counts on the simulated readout model; choose the threshold minimizing the average error; store the window length.
6. **Heating rate**: delay-scan of sideband asymmetry; store measured n̄-dot per mode (used to schedule recooling where the device has it).
7. **Stark shift**: a Ramsey experiment with the gate beams on and detuned, per (ion, beam), fitted for the differential light shift that sets the MS phase; stored in `stark`.
8. **Crosstalk**: drive ion i, fit the Rabi rate and phase on every neighbour j; stored in `crosstalk` as ε_ij.
9. **Field**: the clock-point or Zeeman-splitting scan that fixes B and its drift, stored in `field`, run first because every other fit reads the Zeeman sensitivities.

The experiments form a dependency graph that `calibrate()` follows and never reorders: field, then micromotion compensation, then mode frequencies, then carrier Rabi and Stark, then crosstalk, then the entangling-gate scans, then detection and heating, and a downstream fit refuses to run while an upstream entry it reads is `uncalibrated` (a mode-frequency fit on an uncompensated ion carries J₁ micromotion sidebands; the second revision declared the experiments as an unordered list, and its `CalibrationTable` named three entries, `stark`, `crosstalk` and `field`, for which no experiment existed; 2026-09-04 experimentalist critique) **[corrected: critique, 2026-09-04]**.

Calibrations are cached per device configuration and per noise seed; the tests in Section 9 require that calibrated parameters agree with the device's true derived parameters within the uncertainty the fits report, and that the gates built from them reach the fidelity the noise model predicts.

**Bootstrap, fits and failure.** The first calibration has no table to read, so each experiment starts from the device's derived values as initial guesses, tagged `seed` in the table: the carrier scan spans at least ten derived π times and fits Rabi frequency and contrast, with n̄ taken from the sideband-ratio thermometry of Section 4.2.7 rather than from the Debye-Waller envelope, whose decay over about 1/(η²n̄) Rabi periods makes a three-parameter fit to less than one period degenerate; sideband spectroscopy is a coarse scan (±10% about the derived frequency, stepped at the pulse's linewidth) followed by a fine scan fitted with the excitation lineshape in the plan's own convention, P = [Ω²/(Ω² + δ²)] sin²((t/2)√(Ω² + δ²)) (Section 13: carrier π pulse at Ωt = π, half-depth at δ = Ω), rather than a Lorentzian, with a CI assertion that the fitted π time on a noiseless carrier equals π/Ω and that the fitted Ω reproduces the device's derived Ω within the reported uncertainty (the second revision printed here the Boulder half-Rabi form Ω²/(Ω² + δ²/4) sin²(t√(Ω² + δ²/4)) that Section 7.9 flags, unflagged and called the plan's own, which would have written Ω at half the truth into the table and into every π time, η and pulse amplitude derived from it; 2026-09-04 critique, all four lenses) **[corrected: critique, 2026-09-04]**, and must report an uncertainty below the sub-kilohertz need of the FM pulse solvers (the first version of this plan specified scans that could neither identify n̄ nor resolve a 10 kHz sideband with 64 steps over 600 kHz; caught by the 2026-09-04 experimentalist critique); a micromotion-compensation experiment (rf-photon correlation, the sideband-ratio method on the rf micromotion sideband, and two-direction Doppler nulling, Berkeland 1998) scans the shim voltages, returns the residual β per beam direction and stores shims and β in the table, so that compensation is calibrated, drifts with the stray field between calibrations and is re-nulled like a laboratory re-nulls it; a Ramsey-frequency experiment measures each qubit's frequency and stores it, because Section 7.3 forbids the scheduler the true value and every pulse needs a detuning; the entangling-gate scan spans ±30% in amplitude about the closure amplitude the pulse solver predicts at the calibrated mode frequencies and fits populations and parity contrast; the detection calibration draws 10⁴ bright and 10⁴ dark records; the heating-rate scan uses delays up to 10/ṅ_derived. Every fit reports an uncertainty, and a fit that fails to converge or lands at the edge of its scan range marks the entry `uncalibrated`, which the scheduler refuses to use. The calibration package sits above `control` and `dynamics` and drives them through the `PulseEngine` protocol (Appendix E), and `control.schedule` reads only the plain `CalibrationTable` data object, which is what breaks the schedule-calibration import cycle of the first layout. Calibration by simulated experiment is hours of pulse integrations at the rates of Section 11.1, so it is cached, invalidated rather than silently regenerated when a device parameter changes, and run as an explicit job (Section 14.4).

**Age, drift and cost.** Every entry carries the time it was fitted and the noise sample it was fitted under; slow parameters are trajectories with a correlation time (Sections 6.2 and 6.3), so the calibration error starts at the fit uncertainty and grows with elapsed time, `run` takes the table and the run's start time as arguments, and a gate driven with a stale detuning is a reproducible failure mode rather than an impossibility. An optional servo (frequency feedforward, or periodic re-lock of a parameter with a stated bandwidth) high-passes the drift into the residual band, which is the mechanism behind the 50 Hz feedforward residual of Section 6.2; the first version of this plan sampled raw quasi-static drift against a once-computed table, which double-counts the slow band that every laboratory servos away (caught by the 2026-09-04 experimentalist critique). Because a full calibration by simulated experiment does not fit the compute budget of Section 11 beyond two ions (about 400 detuning-by-amplitude points at 2 s each per pair at joint dimension 256 is 13 minutes per pair and 94 hours for the 435 pairs of a 30-ion device, and the per-pair joint space for N ≥ 4 exceeds the ceiling), the default calibration is a surrogate: the closed-form α_m and χ_m integrals of Section 4.4.3 on the derived mode structure, corrected by a small number of exact spot checks (one pair per distinct pair class, at the resolved-mode set of Section 5.4), with the full simulated-experiment path an opt-in audit that reports the surrogate's error; Section 9.8 likewise validates GATE_LOCAL against JOINT_EXACT only where JOINT_EXACT is affordable **[corrected]**.


### 7.6 Native gates, exactly

Two equivalent native sets are supported; the simulator stores both and converts.

**Research notation** (Debnath et al. 2016; Maslov 2017; Trout 2018; Egan 2021) **[verified]**:

R_φ(θ) = [[cos(θ/2), −i sin(θ/2)e^{−iφ}], [−i sin(θ/2)e^{iφ}, cos(θ/2)]] = exp[−i(θ/2)(cos φ σ_x + sin φ σ_y)],  R_x = R₀, R_y = R_{π/2};

XX(χ) = [[cos χ, 0, 0, −i sin χ], [0, cos χ, −i sin χ, 0], [0, −i sin χ, cos χ, 0], [−i sin χ, 0, 0, cos χ]] = exp(−iχ σ_x⊗σ_x),

with |χ| ∈ [0, π/2] set by laser power and the sign α_ij = sgn(χ) fixed per ion pair by which modes the pair couples to most strongly and the detuning chosen; χ = ±π/4 is maximally entangling **[verified]**. The optical-phase-insensitive variant is ZZ_ij(χ), an XX flanked by π/2 single-qubit pulses on each side driven by the same beam pairs (Chen et al. 2023; the Quantinuum wrapper construction) **[verified]**.

**IonQ cloud notation** (IonQ documentation) **[verified]**, all parameters in turns (1 turn = 2π rad):

GPi(φ) = [[0, e^{−2πiφ}], [e^{2πiφ}, 0]] = cos(2πφ)X + sin(2πφ)Y (a π rotation about the equatorial axis at azimuth 2πφ, up to the global phase i);

GPi2(φ) = (1/√2)[[1, −ie^{−2πiφ}], [−ie^{2πiφ}, 1]] = exp[−i(π/4)(cos 2πφ X + sin 2πφ Y)], so φ = 0 gives R_x(π/2), φ = 0.25 gives R_y(π/2), and φ = 0.5 gives R_x(−π/2); the documentation's prose statement that φ = 0.5 gives R_x(+π/2) contradicts its own matrix **[corrected]**;

MS(φ₀, φ₁) = (1/√2)[[1, 0, 0, −ie^{−2πi(φ₀+φ₁)}], [0, 1, −ie^{−2πi(φ₀−φ₁)}, 0], [0, −ie^{2πi(φ₀−φ₁)}, 1, 0], [−ie^{2πi(φ₀+φ₁)}, 0, 0, 1]], and MS(φ₀, φ₁, θ) = exp[−iπθ GPi(φ₀)⊗GPi(φ₁)] with θ ∈ [0, 0.25] turns, reducing to the fully entangling form at θ = 0.25 **[verified]**;

ZZ(θ) = exp(−iπθ Z⊗Z) = diag(e^{−iπθ}, e^{iπθ}, e^{iπθ}, e^{−iπθ}) **[verified]**;

VirtualZ(θ) = diag(e^{−iπθ}, e^{iπθ}) = RZ(2πθ), implemented by shifting the phase parameter of every later gate on that qubit and discarding the Z rotation before measurement. With the documented matrices and the gates read in time order, the shift is φ → φ − θ for every later GPi, GPi2 and MS phase (recomputed here: RZ(θ), GPi(0.5), GPi2(0) equals GPi(0.5 − θ), GPi2(−θ) followed by RZ(θ)); the documentation's printed rule with +θ holds when its diagram is read in matrix order, right to left. The sign is therefore a circuit-ordering convention, and the simulator pins it with this test rather than with the prose **[corrected]**. The frame update is free, but the scheduler still charges the physical dead time the hardware inserts between pulses (14 µs of DDS phase switching in Harty's system, where identity gates are physical delays and a zero-duration Z would change the detuning sensitivity that the measured 1.0(3) × 10⁻⁶ error depends on), and the residual frame phase commutes through ZZ but must be folded into the φ₀ and φ₁ arguments of every later MS gate **[corrected]**. Note that the phase-type parameters appear as e^{±2πiφ} while the angle-type parameters appear as cos(πθ), sin(πθ), because one is a phase and the other a half-angle **[corrected]**.

Conversions: XX(χ) = MS(0, 0, θ) with χ = πθ; R_φ(θ) with θ = π is GPi(φ/2π) up to the global phase i. The `control/native` module holds these matrices with unit tests against the documentation and against `ionq_core`'s helpers.

**JSON encoding of the native set** (IonQ OpenAPI v0.4 specification and the `qiskit-ionq`, `ionq-core-python` and `PennyLane-IonQ` client code, read 2026-09-04) **[verified against the client sources]**: the gate names are `gpi`, `gpi2`, `ms`, `zz` and `nop`; `gpi` and `gpi2` take a scalar `target` and a scalar `phase` in turns; `ms` takes `targets` (two qubits), `phases` (two entries, turns) and `angle`, the entangling angle in turns with default 0.25 (maximally entangling), a range 0 to 0.25 that only the PennyLane plugin's docstring states and the specification does not enforce, and an "in units of π" wording in `ionq_core` that describes the same number (its matrix uses cos πθ, sin πθ); `zz` takes `targets` and `angle` and no phases. The job body is `{"type": "ionq.circuit.v1", "backend": …, "shots": …, "input": {"gateset": "native" | "qis", "qubits": N, "circuit": […]}}`, with `noise: {model, seed}` for the vendor simulator and `settings` for error mitigation and compilation. The specification's other circuit format is OpenQASM 3 (`ionq.qasm3.v1`), not OpenQASM 2, so the package's OpenQASM 2 importer serves general interchange while the IonQ path is native JSON or OpenQASM 3; the OpenQASM 2 export of the client SDKs declares `gpi`, `gpi2`, `ms` and `zz` as custom gates built from `u`, `rz`, `rxx` and `rzz`, which the importer accepts.

### 7.7 Compilation templates, verified

- **CNOT from one XX and four pulses** (Maslov 2017 Fig. 1; Debnath 2016 Fig. 2a) **[verified]**. In time order: RY(vπ/2) on the control; XX(sπ/4); RX(−sπ/2) on the control together with RX(−vsπ/2) on the target; RY(−vπ/2) on the control, with s = sgn(χ) fixed by the ion pair and v = ±1 free. This equals CNOT up to the global phase e^{iπvs/4} for all four sign combinations (recomputed here; the source writes the phase as (−1)^{−vs/4}) **[corrected: phase]**. Debnath's operator-ordered template R_{−z}(π/2)_i R_{−αy}(π/2)_i R_{−x}(π/2)_j XX(απ/4) R_{αy}(π/2)_i equals e^{iπ/4}CNOT for both signs of α **[verified]**. Cost (2τ_1q + τ_2q, 4ε + E) against two XX gates and eight pulses in the older construction of Nebendahl, Häffner and Roos **[corrected reference]**. Trout et al.'s Fig. 8 template with its printed rotation signs is not a CNOT at all, equalling (Z⊗I)·CNOT for s = +1 and (Z⊗X)·CNOT for s = −1; the compiler tests keep it as a negative control **[corrected]**.
- **Controlled phase CP(θ)** from a partially entangling XX(α_ij|θ|/4) plus single-qubit rotations (Debnath Fig. 2b), not exact as drawn: the template is diagonal with the right conditional phase but carries an extra R_z((sgn(θ)π − θ)/2) on both qubits, with overlap 0.854 with CP(θ) at θ = π/2 and 0.691 at π/4 (both reproduced by that rotation; the first version of this plan described the defect as R_z(sgn(θ)π/2), which would give an overlap of 0.5 for every θ), so the compiler appends R_z((θ − sgn(θ)π)/2) on both ions **[corrected]**. The Methods sentence that puts χ_ij = π/4 at θ = ±π/2 contradicts the figure caption's χ = sgn(θ)θ/4, which gives π/8; building CP(π/2) from XX(π/4) yields CZ **[corrected]**.
- **Sign table.** Debnath's published α_ij list covers nine of ten pairs and puts α₂₅ in both sign groups; Maslov's list for the same machine is complete. The compiler takes s from the simulator's own mode structure and detuning and uses neither table **[corrected]**.
- **Controlled X^α** for any real α ∈ [−1, 1] from one XX(sαπ/4) plus four single-qubit pulses (three on the control, one on the target), halving the two-qubit gate count of the two-CNOT construction; controlled Y^α and Z^α by conjugating the target **[corrected: four pulses, not five]**.
- **Arbitrary single-qubit unitary in at most two pulses**: U ≡ R(−π, −c − π/2)·R(2b + π, a − c − π/2) for U = e^{id}[[e^{ia}cos b, e^{ic}sin b], [−e^{−ic}sin b, e^{−ia}cos b]], where "·" is matrix multiplication and the circuit applies the right factor first; degenerate to one pulse when 2b + π ≡ 0 **[corrected: ordering]**.
- **RZ** needs no pulse (virtual) or, where a physical realization is wanted, two: R(π, x)·R(π, x − θ/2) with a free phase x, exact up to a global sign (±1 depending on θ and x, recomputed here) **[verified]** (Maslov's free x is not the virtual-Z technique **[corrected]**).
- **Cost model** for the compiler's choices: single-qubit duration |θ|τ_1q/π with error ∝ |sin θ|ε; two-qubit duration τ_2q with error ∝ |sin 2χ|E (Maslov) **[verified]**; circuit fidelity as Π(1 − e_i) for a single-qubit wire **[verified]**.

Every template is checked numerically against its target unitary, up to global phase, at compile time.

### 7.8 Pulse-shaping practice

Published shapes the scheduler reproduces: nine-segment AM at τ_g = 235 µs for a five-ion chain with ν_x = 3.07 MHz and ν_z = 0.27 MHz, one precomputed shape per pair, gate time scaling roughly as ν_x/ν_z² ∝ n^{1.7} (Debnath 2016) **[verified]**; sixteen-segment AM with linear interpolation between segments (chosen to avoid exciting axial motion) at 225 µs with η ≈ 0.08 for a 15-ion chain at (0.193, 3.077, 3.234) MHz, robust to mode-frequency errors below 1 kHz; the centre ion of an odd chain participates only in spatially symmetric modes (b_{8,2n} = 0) and so needs high power in most of its gates (Egan 2021) **[verified]**; FM designed from only the six highest of 17 transverse modes with AM suppressing unwanted Fourier components (Landsman 2019) **[verified]**; AMFM Fourier-sine designs with about 300 basis functions and K-th-order stabilization on a seven-ion chain (Blümel 2021) **[verified]**; and, on a 36-ion chain with about 10 kHz between neighbouring transverse modes, AM pulses closing all transverse modes with MS durations of 550-883 µs (median 672 µs) and ZZ durations of 770-1103 µs, the detuning placed above the 21st mode (Chen 2023) **[corrected: mode index]**. Single-qubit pulses use SK1 composite sequences with a Gaussian envelope on chip traps to avoid exciting axial motion through light-induced charging (Egan) **[verified]** and are otherwise square with smooth edges. The SK1 phases and order claims of Section 4.3.5 are derived for **square** pulses only, and the exact amplitude filter function of Section 6.9 is pulse-shape independent at fixed total sequence time while the detuning one is not, so a Gaussian-enveloped SK1 keeps its dc amplitude cancellation (the closed polygon Σ_l θ_l ρ̃_a^{(l)} = 0 depends only on the areas and the toggled axes) but not its detuning behaviour; the scheduler therefore emits the shaped pulse as a piecewise-constant approximation whose segment count is chosen by the Section 5.5 convergence policy, and reports the residual against the square-pulse 2 × 2 result.

### 7.9 Calibration recipes, as the sources run them

- **Carrier Rabi frequency**: Rabi flopping on each ion; fit with the thermal Debye-Waller envelope (Section 4.2.7); for chains with tightly focused beams fit Cetina's algebraic (non-exponential) contrast loss and phase lag instead of an exponential **[corrected]**.
- **Mode frequencies and Lamb-Dicke parameters**: blue-sideband spectroscopy, P_{|1⟩} = [Ω_i^{p2}/(Ω_i^{p2} + δω_p²/4)] sin²(t√(Ω_i^{p2} + δω_p²/4)) with Ω_i^p = √(n̄+1)Ω_iη_i^p, remembering that this Ω is half the usual Rabi frequency (Blümel 2021 Eq. S28), a half-Rabi form doubled on ingest and never the fit function of Section 7.5 **[verified]**; the extracted |η_i^p| = |Ω_i^p|/(√(n̄+1)Ω_i) feeds the pulse solvers, and the Leung discrepancy (measured Ω four times the prediction) is the failure mode this step guards against.
- **Entangling gate**: prepare |00⟩, apply XX, measure P₀₀ and P₁₁ and scan the AM pulse height until P₀₀ = P₁₁; measure the parity oscillation P₀₀ + P₁₁ − P₀₁ − P₁₀ under an analysis π/2 pulse of scanned phase; the Bell fidelity F = ½(P₀₀ + P₁₁ + Φ) with Φ the parity contrast is a lower bound (Wright 2019) **[verified]**; for partially entangling angles use Debnath's interference sequence, control in |1⟩, target in (|0⟩ + |1⟩)/√2, CP(θ), then R_x(π/2), giving P(|1⟩) = ½(1 − sin θ) **[verified]**.
- **Randomized benchmarking variants**: Knill-style sequences of π/2 gates with interleaved π or identity gates and a final π/2 into the computational basis, fit to Bp^L + ½ with p the per-π/2-gate fidelity in the authors' convention (Wright) **[verified]**; Clifford RB with the whole random sequence followed by its inverse, fit to a linear or exponential decay (Egan; Mount F = A₀p^L + B₀ with error (1 − p)/2) **[corrected: inverse of the sequence, not of each Clifford]**; direct RB with random layers of XX at probability p_2Q and single-qubit π/2 rotations, fit to a + bp^d, and the two-instance extraction of (r_1Q, r_2Q), whose printed matrix in Chen et al. is singular (both rows [0.75, 0.25]; the equation entered with the November 2024 revision that is the version of record, Quantum 8, 1516, so here the newest text is the one not to copy) and whose right-hand side must be error rates r = (4ⁿ − 1)(1 − p)/4ⁿ rather than raw decay bases **[corrected]**.
- **Detection**: bright and dark histograms, threshold and window (Section 8); Debnath removed detection crosstalk by decomposing into the known 32-state detector response **[verified]**.
- **Application-level**: the Hellinger circuit fidelity F_c = (Σ_i√(p_iq_i))², the #AQ pass criterion F_c − ε_c > 1/e on the QED-C circuits with plurality-vote error mitigation starting at threshold t = 7 and decrementing (Chen 2023) **[verified]**, and the composite expected-fidelity product F_2Q^n F_1Q^{2(n+1)} F_SPAM^{10} used as a crosstalk-free floor (Wright) **[corrected: the caption's qualifier applies to one figure only]**.

### 7.10 The control hardware chain

The pulse a `Schedule` specifies is not the field the ion sees; between them sit a direct digital synthesizer (DDS), an rf amplifier, an acousto-optic or electro-optic modulator and the beam path, and each leaves a signature the simulator must be able to reproduce, because published error budgets attribute errors to them: Harty's 14 µs DDS phase-switching dead time and 5 × 10⁻⁴ amplitude stability (Section 4.3.3), Egan's Gaussian-envelope SK1 pulses chosen so as not to excite axial motion (Section 7.8), Chen's mechanical spin-phase noise (Section 12). None of this has a first-principles source in the research runs; it is standard instrumentation physics **[background]** exposed as user-supplied parameters with documented defaults, and every effect below enters through the drive coefficient Ω(t)e^{−i(μt − φ(t))} of Section 5.2, so no new Hamiltonian term is needed.

- **Frequency, phase and amplitude quantization.** A DDS with a 32-bit frequency tuning word at clock f_clk has a resolution f_clk/2³² (0.23 Hz at 1 GHz), a 14- to 16-bit phase word (2π/2¹⁴ = 0.38 mrad) and a 10- to 14-bit amplitude word; the scheduler rounds the requested tone parameters to the configured grid and the simulator uses the rounded values, so the residual detuning after rounding is part of the physics, not a tolerance.
- **Phase coherence between pulses.** Each tone is generated by an oscillator that runs continuously at its programmed frequency, so the phase of a pulse starting at t₀ is φ_prog + ω_tone t₀ unless the hardware resets the phase at each pulse; the simulator models both, per tone, as `phase="continuous"` or `"reset"`. The frame bookkeeping of Section 4.3.4 and the virtual-Z rule of Section 7.6 assume phase-continuous operation at the nominal qubit frequency, which is the mode the rule was pinned in.
- **Modulator response.** An acousto-optic modulator's rise time, about the beam diameter divided by the acoustic velocity (100 ns per 0.4 mm of beam at 4.2 km/s in tellurium dioxide), low-pass filters the amplitude envelope; the simulator convolves the programmed envelope with a first-order response of that time constant and, optionally, models the amplifier's saturation as a static nonlinearity Ω = Ω_sat tanh(V/V_sat), so that a nominally square pulse acquires the finite edges that determine its off-resonant excitation of neighbouring sidebands. The rf-power-to-Ω map is calibrated by the Rabi scan of Section 7.5 exactly as in the laboratory.
- **Timing.** A per-pulse dead time (DDS phase switching, sequencer latency) and a timing jitter σ_t are scheduler parameters; the dead time is idle evolution under H₀ with the noise channels of Section 6.7 active.
- **Beam pointing and power.** Slow pointing drift changes each ion's intensity through the beam profile of `light/beams.py`, and with it both Ω and the crosstalk ratios ε_ij; it is sampled quasi-statically. Fast intensity noise is the sampled time series of Section 6.4. The optical path difference between two Raman beams sets the beat-note phase, and its mechanical drift is the spin-phase noise that Chen et al. invoke, entered as a user-supplied phase spectrum (Section 12).
- **Detection electronics.** Detector dead time, afterpulsing and SNSPD recovery are the readout-side counterparts (Section 8.8), with the same user-supplied status.

A device preset that omits these parameters gets ideal electronics and says so in its `derived()` report, so a fidelity that depends on them cannot be claimed by accident.


## 8. Readout, SPAM and results

Readout is modelled in four layers that the code keeps separate: an atomic-rate layer, a stochastic photon-record layer, a discriminator layer, and an error-budget layer. Three conventions caused most of the factor errors the verifiers found in the sources and are pinned first: bright/dark polarity is a property of the readout scheme, not of the qubit (direct fluorescence and shelving readout invert it within the same papers); Γ is stored as an angular population decay rate in s⁻¹ with an assertion that Γ/2π reproduces the quoted linewidth; and the system detection efficiency ε_sys enters exactly once, at the conversion from scattered to detected rate **[verified]**.

### 8.1 Atomic-rate layer

**Bright-state scattering rate.** For the 171Yb+ cycling transition ²S₁/₂|F=1⟩ → ²P₁/₂|F=0⟩ with the three ground sublevels destabilized against coherent population trapping, the optimally polarized scattering rate is (Noek et al. 2013 Eq. 1; Crain et al. 2019) **[verified]**

R_∘ = (Γ/18) s_∘ / [1 + (2/9) s_∘ + (2Δ/Γ)²],  s_∘ = 2Ω²/Γ² = I/I_sat,  equivalently R_∘ = (Γ/4) s̃/[1 + s̃ + (2Δ/Γ)²] with s̃ = (2/9)s_∘ = I/(229 mW/cm²),

with Γ the partial rate of the S₁/₂ ↔ P₁/₂ line, Γ_S = 2π × 19.62 MHz (0.99499 of the 19.72 MHz total from the 8.07(9) ns lifetime, Section 9.12; the 19.6 MHz reading the second revision carried here and in the 8.4 preset is retired everywhere, and a CI check greps the tree for retired constants), Ω the full J = 1/2 → J′ = 1/2 line Rabi frequency Ω = |⟨J‖d‖J′⟩|E₀/ħ with no angular factor, I_sat = πhcΓ/(3λ³) = 50.8 mW/cm² at 369.5 nm, saturating at Γ/4 = 30.79 µs⁻¹ (three ground plus one excited level), not Γ/2, with half-maximum at s_∘ = 4.5 **[verified]**, **[corrected: derivation audit, 2026-09-04]**. Noek's printed form (Γ/6)s/[1 + (2/3)s + (2Δ/Γ)²] is the same four-level result written for s = s_∘/3, so a simulator that computes Ω from intensity and a dipole matrix element must apply the angular factors exactly once: the prefactor Γ/18 = Γ × ½ × (1/3) × (1/3) carries two independent factors of 1/3, the hyperfine reduced element |⟨F = 1‖d‖F′ = 0⟩|² = |⟨J‖d‖J′⟩|²/3 and the polarization share |ε_q|² = 1/3 of each ground sublevel's matching component at the magic angle 54.74° to B, which is what 'optimally polarized' means here (pure σ± light, or a polarization parallel or perpendicular to B, leaves a dark state and R_∘ = 0). The second revision of this plan wrote the prefactor as Γ/6 with (2/3)s_∘, folding one factor of 1/3 and not the other, which the derivation audit of 2026-09-04 caught: the exact four-level Lindblad steady state (sixteen-dimensional Liouvillian, magic-angle polarization, pumping rate ≪ Zeeman splitting ≪ Γ) sits at 0.3335, 0.3332 and 0.331 of the second revision's form and at 0.99999, 0.9993 and 0.992 of the corrected one over three decades of s_∘, and maximizing that steady state over polarization, field and detuning at fixed s_∘ reproduces (Γ/18, 2/9) to nine digits and never reaches the old value; Section 4.5.2 derives the prefactor and the level-C Bloch solve computes it. For a general σ⁺ stretch-state scheme on a P₃/₂ cycling transition with arbitrary nuclear spin I, Acton et al. 2006 give the closed forms λ₀ = τ_D η (sγ/2)/(1 + s + (2δ/γ)²) for the mean detected count and the leak probabilities per emitted photon α₁ (dark → bright) and α₂ (bright → dark) with Clebsch-Gordan combinations M₁ = 4I(3+2I)/(9(1+2I)²), M_{2π} = 4I/(9+18I), M_{2−} = 16I/(9(1+2I)³) **[corrected]** (the paper's printed normalization gives M₁ = 8/9 unless C → C/2; the code pins the normalization by reproducing M₁ = 2/9, M_{2π} = M_{2−} = 1/9 at I = 1/2). Coherent population trapping in the F = 1 → F′ = 0 detection cycle caps the 171Yb+ scattering rate at Γ/4, the saturation ceiling n_e/(n_e + n_g) of the three-ground-plus-one-excited manifold, one half of the two-level Γ/2, while leaving leakage unchanged (Olmschenk et al. 2007 quote one third, which is the Λ repump cycle's ceiling and not this manifold's; the lumped 'η → η/3' model of the earlier revisions under-predicts detected counts by 3 and is illegal whenever R_∘ comes from the Bloch solve, whose assertion is R_∘ ≤ Γ/4; at Crain's fitted s_∘ = 0.815 the corrected R_∘ alone gives 0.0880Γ, which times ε_sys = 4.356% is his 472 kcps) **[corrected: critique, 2026-09-04]**; the ≈ 5 G field is applied to destabilize the dark states (Berkeland and Boshier 2002, extracted and verified in Run 5), so the ceiling is reached under optimal destabilization and B → 0 does not recover the full rate but collapses it as two dark states become exact; its origin is now settled rather than unresolved, being the equal-population saturation ceiling n_e/(n_e + n_g) of a closed manifold, which is 1/3 for the three-level Λ repump cycle and 1/4 for the four-state F = 1 → F′ = 0 manifold, so the naive four-level 1/2 was simply the wrong manifold count (paragraph below) **[verified]**, **[corrected]**. The simulator still computes the rate from the level-C Bloch steady state at the configured field and polarization and carries the ceiling as the bound it must not exceed.

**Coherent population trapping and dark-state destabilization, from the source the plan had never extracted** (Berkeland and Boshier 2002, verified in Run 5) **[verified]**, **[corrected]**. A closed manifold J_i → J_f supports dark superpositions |d⟩ = Σ_{m_i} c_{m_i}|J_i, m_i⟩ with ⟨d|(−e r·E)|f⟩ = 0 for every upper sublevel, and their number follows from (J_i, J_f) and the polarization alone: none for J_f = J_i + 1; one for J_f = J_i, at any polarization for integer J_i but only for circular light when it is half-integer; two for J_f = J_i − 1 (Table I) **[verified]**. The operative condition is the null space of M_{m_f m_i} ∝ (−1)^q(−1)^{J_f − m_f}(J_f 1 J_i; −m_f q m_i)E_{−q} with q = m_f − m_i and E_{±1}, E_0 the spherical components of the positive-frequency amplitude, which for J_i = 1 ↔ J_f = 0 collapses to the **straight** pairing Σ_m c_m E_m = 0. Eq. (8) prints the reversed pairing c_{−1}E_{+1} + c_0E_0 + c_{+1}E_{−1} = 0, the same condition written for E*, which returns the dark states of the **conjugate** polarization: identical for real (linear) light, σ⁺ ↔ σ⁻ swapped for circular or elliptical, with Eqs. (9a)-(9b) and Table II inheriting the reversal **[corrected]**. The discriminating test is σ⁺ light (E_{+1} = 0, E_{−1} = √2), which must leave span{|1,0⟩, |1,+1⟩} dark and not span{|1,−1⟩, |1,0⟩} **[recomputed here]**. Two reading traps travel with the paper: it labels E_{+1}, E_0, E_{−1} as σ⁻, π, σ⁺ driving Δm = −1, 0, +1, the reverse of Section 13's helicity label; and the Wigner-3j polarization index must be read off each equation's own column ordering, since the relaxation equation's (J_f J_i 1; −m_f m_i q) forces q = m_f − m_i while the same paper's Wigner-Eckart Eq. (4) forces q = m_i − m_f. Importing the latter into the former zeroes every σ channel, collapses the total feeding rate from γ to γ/3 on the (1,0), (½,½) and (3/2,½) transitions, breaks trace preservation and starves exactly the dark sublevels the treatment is about **[recomputed here]**, **[corrected]**.

**What this fixes in the readout layer.** The factor this section carried as unexplained is a saturation ceiling: in the fully saturated incoherent limit every state of the closed manifold is equally populated, so the excited fraction is n_e/(n_e + n_g) = (2J_f + 1)/((2J_i + 1) + (2J_f + 1)) - exactly 1/4 for the three-ground-plus-one-excited 171Yb+ ²S₁/₂(F=1) → ²P₁/₂(F′=0) manifold, 1/3 for a three-level Λ system, 1/2 for an ideal two-level atom **[recomputed here]**. Both special values are source-backed, 1/4 as the Ω ≫ γ limit of Eqs. (13)-(14) and 1/3 as the R_if, R_df → ∞ limit of the rate equation 1/P_f = 3 + (1 − α)γ/R_if + αγ/R_df (Eq. 21, α the branching fraction into the dark ground state); the general n_e/(n_e + n_g) form is derived here from the equal-population argument and printed by no source, **[background]**. That settles the fork this section recorded: Olmschenk's 1/3 is the Λ ceiling of the S₁/₂-P₁/₂-D₃/₂ repump cycle, and the naive four-level 1/2 is the wrong manifold count. Reaching either ceiling needs the dark state actively destabilized, and with γ, the resonant rms Rabi frequency Ω of Eq. (5) and the dark state's own evolution rate δ the only frequencies in play, P_f is maximal at δ ≈ Ω/2 and, for the idealized g_J = 2, J_i = 1 level whose m_i = ±1 shifts are ±2δ_B, at δ_B ≡ μ_B|B|/ħ = Ω/4 with the linear polarization at θ_BE = arccos(1/√3) = arctan√2 = 54.7356° to B, the angle making the three transition Rabi frequencies equal (misprinted "≈ 63°" once in the source), with Ω ≈ γ/3 buying substantial fluorescence without significant broadening **[recomputed here]**, **[corrected]**. For a real hyperfine level with g_F ≈ 1 the shifts are ±δ_B, so δ = δ_B and the optimum field is twice the idealized value - derived, since the source says only that differing g-factors "mostly result in a simple shift along the δ_B axis", **[background]** - and this section's ≈ 5 G operating point sits about a factor 2 above that optimum (δ_B/2π = 3.27 MHz at Ω = γ/3, that is B ≈ 2.3 G at μ_B/h = 1.3996 MHz/G; `check_critique_v3.py`), where P_f begins to fall as 1/B²; the second revision printed 'two decades', which at 1/B² would have been a factor 10³ in fluorescence and contradicted the same paragraph's claim that the ceiling is reached at this field, so the optimum is a computed output of the CPT solve at the configured Ω and polarization and the 5 G rate is asserted against the Γ/4 ceiling rather than against a scaling (2026-09-04 theorist critique) **[corrected: critique, 2026-09-04]**, **[recomputed here]**. At B = 0 the same fluorescence is recovered instead by polarization modulation, an AOM pair split by δ_AOM = Ω/2 plus an equal-amplitude π beam or a phase modulator with δ ≈ Φδ_mod (Section 13 carries its two corrected mappings); no steady state exists there, so `steadystate` is invalid and the module propagates ≈ 1000/γ then period-averages ρ_ee **[corrected]**, **[verified]**. The Liouvillian is the multi-level optical-Bloch builder of Section 4.2.8 with three additions the printed Eq. (12) omits: the ground-state feeding term carries the product of **two** 3j symbols with the (−1)^{m_i − m_i′} phase, which is what transfers excited coherence into ground coherence, so there is one collapse operator per polarization channel q and never one per excited sublevel; and −γ on excited-excited elements and −(γ/2 + δω_L/2) on optical coherences must be added or the map is not trace-preserving **[corrected]**. The same source's Λ-arm repumper window, corrected to √α Ω_SP < Ω_DP < γ/2 from a printed condition too strong by 1/α², is recorded in Section 12 for Section 4.2 to pick up **[corrected]**.

**Leakage rates.** Off-resonant pumping between the bright and dark manifolds is the dominant error channel for direct hyperfine readout; R_d is the rate at which a bright ion (F = 1) is pumped dark and R_b the rate at which a dark ion (F = 0) is pumped bright, Noek's and Crain's names, so R_d carries the bright state's off-resonant F = 1 → F′ = 1 channel at Δ_HFP with its 1/3 branching into F = 0 and R_b the dark state's only dipole-allowed channel, F = 0 → F′ = 1 at Δ_HFP + Δ_HFS (a reader who takes R_d for the dark-to-bright rate finds the two detunings interchanged, which one comparer of the 2026-09-04 derivation audit did). For 171Yb+ **[corrected]**:

R_d ≈ (2/3)(1/3)(Γ/2)(2Ω²/Γ²)(Γ/(2Δ_HFP))²,  R_b ≈ (2/3)(Γ/2)(2Ω²/Γ²)(Γ/(2(Δ_HFP + Δ_HFS)))²,

with Δ_HFP = 2π × 2.1 GHz (P₁/₂ hyperfine splitting) and Δ_HFS = 2π × 12.6 GHz (qubit splitting). Both are linear in intensity with no saturation denominator, so the ratios R_d/R_∘ and R_b/R_∘ grow without bound at high saturation and the operating point must stay at s_∘ ≲ 1 **[verified]**. The R_d prefactor differs by a factor of 2 between Noek (2/3 · 1/3) and Crain (1/3) and the two papers do not reconcile it; the atomic-rate layer therefore computes the (R_d, R_b) prefactors from the angular algebra of Section 4.5.2, which settles the bookkeeping in Noek's favour, and reproduces the measured values (Crain: R_d = 341(13) Hz at 56.2 mW/cm²; Noek: about 1.5 kHz at about 170 mW/cm²) through the apparatus entries of the calibration table (intensity scale, detuning, polarization purity), never through a species constant (Section 8.8; the second revision's sentence here still carried the retracted fitted species parameter; 2026-09-04 architect critique) **[contested]**, **[corrected: critique, 2026-09-04]**. Their ratio R_b/R_d = 3(Δ_HFP/(Δ_HFP + Δ_HFS))² = 3/49 ≈ 0.061 versus a measured 16.4/341 = 0.048 **[verified]**. For I = 1/2 clock-state readout through P₃/₂ the hard ceiling from off-resonant coupling before reaching the cycling transition is F_max = 1 − (4/9)(γ/(2ω_HFP))², the 4/9 being a product 2 × (1/3) × (2/3) of squared Clebsch-Gordan factors and event multiplicity **[corrected]**; for 111Cd+ this is 99.9375% **[verified]**.

**The metastable manifold during detection** (added after the 2026-09-04 experimentalist critique) **[corrected]**. At Crain's rates a 171Yb+ ion scatters about 10³ photons in a 100 µs window and branches 0.501% to D₃/₂ on each, so it leaves the cycling transition several times per shot and is returned by the 935.2 nm repump (with its 3.07 GHz sideband for both hyperfine components), and the return redistributes population between F = 0 and F = 1 with the branching of Section 4.5.2, a bright-to-dark and dark-to-bright channel that R_d and R_b above do not contain; in 40Ca+ the 6% branch to D₃/₂ and the 397/866 nm dark resonances set the whole photon budget. The Bloch model of this section therefore includes the D manifold and the repump beams, with power, detuning and polarization as device parameters, so that R_∘, R_d and R_b carry the D excursions and a mis-set or failed repump is a simulable fault rather than an assumption; the first version of this plan omitted the repump path.

**Detected rate and background.** R_det = ε_sys R_∘ with ε_sys = ε_geom ε_optics ε_filter ε_det, where the geometric fraction for an objective of numerical aperture NA is (1 − cos(arcsin NA))/2 (10% at NA 0.6) **[verified]**; EMCCD excess noise is represented by an effective quantum efficiency QE/F² with F = √2 **[verified]**. Even isotopes (174Yb+) serve as in-situ efficiency calibrators because they lack a hyperfine dark state, but the fitted ε_sys is a lower bound since the P₁/₂ level branches 0.501(15)% to D₃/₂ and needs a repump **[corrected]**. Background counts R_bg are an additive rate tied to the same intensity knob as Ω² (scattered detection light: 0.075 counts s⁻¹ per mW cm⁻² in Crain's apparatus, 6.5 Hz + 35 Hz/µW in Noek's) **[verified]**.

**Shelving.** For optical qubits and shelved hyperfine qubits the dark state is a metastable D level; its decay during the window is an error ε_decay = 1 − exp(−t_det/τ_D) with τ_D = 1168(7) ms for 40Ca+ D₅/₂ and about 30 s for Ba+ D₅/₂ **[corrected]** (Christensen et al. print the exponent with the wrong sign). Shelving transfer itself is imperfect and state-selective only partially: in 43Ca+ at 146 G, ground states other than the stretch state still shelve with probability up to about 30%, so the dark-outcome operator is Π_dark = |4,+4⟩⟨4,+4| + Σ p_{F,M}|F,M⟩⟨F,M| rather than a rank-one projector, and the readout is not quantum non-demolition **[corrected]**. 133Ba+ shelves |1⟩ through ²P₃/₂ with branching 0.74 / 0.23 / 0.03 to S₁/₂ / D₅/₂ / D₃/₂, reaching 0.9998 shelving with repumps and π-polarized 455 nm light; its residual error is off-resonant excitation detuned by the 623(30) MHz P₃/₂ hyperfine splitting **[corrected]**.

**Optional full optical-Bloch check.** The rate layer is cross-checked, per species, by a QuTiP steady-state solution of the S₁/₂(F=0,1) ⊗ P₁/₂(F=0,1) manifold with the drive on the cycling transition, the off-resonant couplings at Δ_HFP and Δ_HFP + Δ_HFS, collapse operators √(Γ·BR_q)|g_q⟩⟨e| per polarization branch, and a Zeeman term to break the coherent dark state; Γ Tr(ρ_ss P_e) must reproduce R_∘ and the leakage rates follow from the populations' slow dynamics **[recipe from the research run]**.

### 8.2 Photon-record layer

Given the joint internal outcome sampled after the circuit (Section 5.7), each ion's count record over a window t is a piecewise-constant-rate Poisson process driven by the bright-dark(-shelf) continuous-time Markov chain plus background; the fast path samples that chain exactly, alternating Exp(R_d) and Exp(R_b) holding times until the window closes and drawing Poisson counts at the piecewise rates, so that windows with kτ not small (Myerson's 420 µs, the 133Ba+ 4.5 ms) carry as many transitions as the physics gives them, and the single-jump forms below are the analytic oracles in their small-kτ regime (the first version of this plan restricted the record to one jump; caught by the 2026-09-04 critique). The single-jump count distribution is (Crain et al. 2019 Eq. 1 with its missing no-jump branch restored **[corrected]**):

P(n; t) = e^{−R_T t} P_p(n; R₁ t) + Σ_{k=0}^{n} ∫₀^t dτ P_p(k; R₁ τ) R_T e^{−R_T τ} P_p(n−k; R₂(t−τ)),

where R₁, R₂ are the detected rates before and after the jump, R_T the jump rate, and P_p the Poisson pmf; as printed the equation sums to 1 − e^{−R_T t} and carries only about 1% of the probability in the paper's own regime. Acton's Poisson-exponential mixtures for the dark and bright states with leak probabilities α₁/η and α₂/η per detected photon are the equivalent closed forms **[verified]**, normalized to machine precision by the verifiers. The zero-threshold error formulas for a hyperfine qubit with background (Crain Eqs. 2-3) are used with ε_sys restored in the final exponent, e^{−(ε_sys R_∘ + R_bg)t} **[corrected]**; the printed omission changes the bright-state error at 11 µs by a factor of about 9. The idealized limit of all this is Wineland's P_N(0) = (1 − η_d)^N ≈ e^{−n_d} **[verified]**, a binomial over a fixed photon number that needs only η_d ≪ 1.

The simulator generates records two interchangeable ways and asserts they agree with the closed forms: a fast path that samples the Markov chain exactly as above (or per-sub-bin Bernoulli(t_s/τ) to match the discriminators' linearization) and then Poisson counts at the piecewise rates plus Poisson(R_bg t); and a trajectory path that runs `mcsolve` on the reduced bright/dark(/shelf) space with a photon-counting collapse operator, recording arrival times. The mean-count calibration curve for a bright-prepared ion, n̄(τ) = ε R_∘ [(R_b/k) τ + (R_d/k²)(1 − e^{−kτ})] with k = R_b + R_d, follows from the two-state rate equation ṗ₁ = R_b p₀ − R_d p₁ **[verified]** and is the fit the calibration layer uses to extract (R_d, R_b, ε R_∘).

### 8.3 Discriminator layer

Implemented as a strategy interface over the same record:

- **Threshold.** Sum counts over t_b and declare bright iff n > n_c with n_c half-integer; scan (n_c, t_b) for the interior optimum, which exists because the overlap error falls with t_b while leakage, shelf decay and background rise **[verified]**. Myerson's 40Ca+ optimum: n_c = 5.5, t_b = 420 µs, ε = 1.8(1) × 10⁻⁴ **[verified]**.
- **Time-resolved maximum likelihood** (Myerson et al. 2008 Eqs. 1-2) **[verified]**. With sub-bin counts n_i, p_B = Π B(n_i) and p_D = (1 − t_b/τ) Π D(n_i) + (t_s/τ) Σ_j Π_{i<j} D(n_i) Π_{i≥j} B(n_i), evaluated by the exact O(N) recursion M_k = M_{k−1} D(n_k), S_k = (S_{k−1} + M_{k−1}) B(n_k), in the log domain with log-sum-exp and a common rescaling of the running p_B (M_N underflows near N ≈ 227 sub-bins at the paper's rates). The mixture weights are first-order in t/τ and normalize exactly; ties go to bright as in the source. This reaches 0.87(11) × 10⁻⁴ on 40Ca+ **[verified]**.
- **Adaptive (Bayesian early termination).** After each sub-bin compute e_D = p_B/(p_B + p_D), e_B = p_D/(p_B + p_D) and stop when the smaller falls below a cutoff or at a worst-case time; the posterior form assumes equal priors, and the printed middle expression omits the prior factor **[corrected]**. Myerson: ε = 1.0(1) × 10⁻⁴ at 145 µs average; Noek's first-photon protocol (0 counts → dark, ≥ 2 → bright, 1 count before τ_c = ln(R_d/R_dc)/(ε R_∘) → bright) gives 99.85% at 28 µs **[verified]**; Crain's stop-on-first-photon with SNSPDs gives 99.931(6)% at 11 µs **[verified]**. The per-shot posterior error is exposed as a soft-decision confidence for clients.
- **Camera (spatial) discriminators** (Burrell et al. 2010) **[verified]**: per-pixel Poisson likelihoods in brightness-sorted order, R = |ln(p_B/p_D)|, register error ≈ Σ_k e^{−R_k}; a spatio-temporal variant with shelf decay across exposures; and neighbour-conditioned likelihoods B_{νki}, D_{νki} for the four nearest-neighbour states, decoded by iterated conditional modes seeded all-dark **[corrected]**. Crosstalk then emerges from PSF overlap without a free parameter; the source's finding that time resolution gives no improvement on a camera, unlike a PMT, is a test target **[verified]**. Camera SNR uses read noise as (kr)², linear in the number of readouts **[corrected]**, and the Airy scale 1.22 λ/NA **[contested: the source prints 1.22 λ/tan α]**.

### 8.4 Error-budget layer and the measurement operator

The budget is reported as named channels, never one scalar: preparation (optical pumping, mapping pulses), shelving or mapping transfer, shelf decay during the window, discrimination (count overlap plus background), and register crosstalk; ε_B and ε_D are reported separately and averaged only at the end as ε = ½(ε_B + ε_D), because real systems are strongly asymmetric (Burrell's threshold optimum has ε_B = 0) **[verified]**. Two figure-of-merit conventions exist in the sources, Acton's worst-case min(F_B, F_D) and the unweighted mean used by Myerson, Burrell, Harty and Christensen, and tables in those papers are state-averaged so single-state errors appear halved **[verified]**; the simulator states which it reports. Validated budgets: Harty's 43Ca+ 6.8(5) × 10⁻⁴ closes exactly from its Table I rows (1.8 + 1.8 + 1.7 + 1.5, ×10⁻⁴) **[verified]**; Christensen's 133Ba+ table totals 3.4 × 10⁻⁴ against a measured 2.9(6) × 10⁻⁴ **[verified]**; for 133Ba+ the threshold overlap at bright mean 39 versus dark mean 1 contributes below 10⁻⁶, so the simulated SPAM must be dominated by shelving and pumping errors, not counting statistics **[verified]**. Register scaling is F_s = (1 − ε_s)^{N_q} under i.i.d. errors, with usable size N_q < ln 2/ε_s **[verified]**.

The whole readout can be summarized as a classical POVM applied after the coherent evolution: E_bright and E_dark built from the per-prepared-state confusion probabilities, verified to sum to the identity, with a non-rank-one dark element for shelving schemes; the post-measurement state is discarded because shelving readout is not QND **[recipe]**. The POVM is the summary of the record and discriminator layers, computed once per device by the calibration layer, and applying it directly to the joint state is the fast path that replaces those layers (Section 5.7); the full path and the fast path must agree in confusion matrix, and a Bell state's bit correlations must survive both, which Section 9.5 tests. Species presets: 40Ca+ PMT (R_B = 55,800 s⁻¹, R_D = 442 s⁻¹, ε = 0.19%, τ_D = 1168 ms); 43Ca+ (50,000 detected counts/s, ε = 0.3%, B₀ = 146.094 G, inverted hyperfine ordering with F = 3 above F = 4 **[corrected]**); 171Yb+ (Γ_S = 2π × 19.62 MHz partial, 19.72 total, Δ_HFP = 2.1 GHz, Δ_HFS = 12.642812118 GHz, branching P₁/₂ → D₃/₂ 0.00501(15), τ(D₃/₂) = 52.7 ms, ε_sys = 2.2% Noek / 4.356% Crain); 133Ba+ (shelving through ²P₃/₂, τ_D ≈ 30 s, window 4.5 ms, threshold n ≤ 12) **[verified]**.

### 8.5 Readout crosstalk and mid-circuit measurement

Wineland's mechanism is a degradation of state-discrimination efficiency by a neighbour's scattered light of different polarization, not a change in the instrumental η_d **[corrected]**; it is modelled as added counts on a dark neighbour plus depumping-reduced N. Radiative pumping of a neighbour by a bright ion is bounded by I_ion/I_sat = 3λ²/(8π²x²) with I_sat = πhcΓ/(3λ³) (Acton's printed I_sat has ħ for h and is 2π too small, making the printed ratio and its 7 × 10⁻⁴ estimate 2π too large; the corrected 111Cd+ value at 4 µm is 1.1 × 10⁻⁴) **[corrected]**. Camera PSF overlap gives about 4.0% nearest-neighbour and 0.9% next-nearest signal leakage at 14 µm spacing **[verified]**. During mid-circuit readout a spectator qubit dephases with a Gaussian, not exponential, coherence decay, exp(−τ²/α²) with α = 94 ms at 200 µm and 814 ms at 370 µm from the detected ion **[verified]** (Crain 2019); the simulator implements this as a quasi-static frequency offset sampled per shot, since a constant-rate σ_z Lindblad term would give the wrong lineshape. Mid-circuit measurement itself is out of scope for the first release (Section 7.2), and its extension is not a scheduler change: 10³ to 10⁴ scattered photons at the 1.1 × 10⁻³ quanta per photon of Section 4.2.8 heat every mode the detection beam projects on by 1 to 10 quanta and leave the measured ion in a bright-manifold mixture, so a mid-circuit measurement requires recooling and re-preparation of the measured ion in the schedule, and the physics for it (the recoil channel, optical pumping, sympathetic cooling) is what Sections 4.2 and 4.5 supply, and the IR and the scheduler carry measure, reset and recool as operations from the first release (Section 7.2), so implementing the stage changes no pipeline shape; the spectator model here covers the neighbours (the first version of this plan called the extension a scheduler change; caught by the 2026-09-04 experimentalist critique) **[corrected]**.

### 8.6 Results

A `Result` carries per shot: the bitstring, the photon-count record per ion (and per sub-bin when time-resolved), the discriminator's posterior confidence when available, and the sampled quasi-static noise parameters of that shot's dynamical sample. Aggregates: counts and probabilities per bitstring; per-qubit ε_B and ε_D estimated from calibration runs; the diagnostics block (fidelity level, truncation-boundary populations, included and dropped modes, calibration table used, solver tolerances, seeds). Optional heavy outputs: the final density matrix or the trajectory ensemble. The IonQ exporter emits the cloud formats exactly as the vendor SDKs consume them, established from the IonQ OpenAPI v0.4 specification and the `qiskit-ionq`, `ionq-core-python` and `PennyLane-IonQ` client code read on 2026-09-04 **[verified against the client sources]**: the legacy probability result (`ionq.result.probabilities.json.v1`) is a flat object keyed by the **decimal integer of the measured register with qubit 0 as the least-significant bit**, for example `{"0": 0.5, "1": 0.25, "3": 0.25}`; PennyLane reverses the bit order because it is big-endian and Qiskit does not because it is little-endian, and the pinned client test maps `{"5": 0.5, "7": 0.5}` on three qubits to the Qiskit keys `101` and `111`. The histogram variant carries shot counts with the same keys, the per-shot format is an ordered list of the same decimal strings (`["6", "1", "0", "7"]` on three qubits is `110, 001, 000, 111`), and the v2 register-nested format is keyed by bitstrings per named register. The simulator's `Result` stores bitstrings with an explicit `bit_order` attribute and converts at the exporter, with round-trip tests on these examples (Section 9.13), so that client code written against the cloud API runs unchanged against the simulator.

### 8.7 Gaps the sources leave

No source gives a continuous-time arrival-record likelihood (all discriminators are sub-bin based); detector dead time, afterpulsing and SNSPD recovery are excluded everywhere; state preparation is quantified per apparatus but never as a first-principles rate model; correlated SPAM errors across a register are unmeasured; the neighbour-state assignment for end ions and the decoder's initialization for large registers are unspecified; and the temperature dependence of shelving fidelity through Doppler broadening has no functional form in the sources. Each is a documented limitation of the first release, with the preparation-error model built from the optical-pumping rates of Section 4.2 as the first planned extension.

### 8.8 Additions from the Run 2A readout consolidation

The cross-source consolidation of the readout brief (Appendix D) recomputed the contested numbers and adds five requirements to the layers above:

- **Two saturation parameters, never aliased.** Noek's s = 2Ω²/Γ² in his (Γ/6, 2/3) form is s_∘/3 = I/(3I_sat) in the plan's full-line convention (Section 8.1): pinning it from Crain's measured detected rate (472 kcps at ε_sys = 4.356%) gives s = 0.815, that is s_∘ = 2.45, against the quoted I/I_sat = 1.10 at 56.2 mW/cm² (the two-level I_sat = 51 mW/cm² convention), a factor 2.2 in intensity (the second revision, with a prefactor one factor of 1/3 too large, made it 1.35 in the other direction). Acton's s is I/I_sat. Because the corrected R_∘ is the maximum of the exact four-level steady state over polarization, field and detuning at fixed intensity, a measured rate above it can only mean more light at the ion or a higher ε_sys than stated, so the residual is an apparatus quantity (intensity calibration of a focused beam, polarization impurity, detection efficiency), not a missing factor in the physics; the device model stores both parameters and the intensity-to-Ω chain is marked uncalibrated until the optical-Bloch check (Section 8.1) closes the gap **[corrected: derivation audit, 2026-09-04]**.
- **Leakage-rate prefactors are derived, and the apparatus residual is calibrated.** With that s_∘, Crain's R_d prefactor predicts 364 Hz against the measured 341(13) Hz and Noek's predicts 243 Hz; the (2/3)(Γ/2) form of R_b predicts 14.9 Hz against 16.4(5) Hz; the measured ratio R_d/R_b = 20.8 sits between the two predictions (24.5 and 16.3). Neither prefactor pair is fully consistent with the measured triple, so the atomic-rate layer computes the (R_d, R_b) prefactors from the angular algebra of Section 4.5.2, which settles the Clebsch-Gordan bookkeeping in Noek's favour, and reports the residual against Crain's measured triple as a discrepancy attributed to apparatus inputs (intensity calibration, detuning, polarization purity) that the device model exposes as calibration parameters with provenance, never as species constants; the first version of this plan carried a fitted species scale here, which contradicted Section 3.1 **[corrected]**.
- **Closed-form angular factors** for the general σ⁺ stretch-state scheme, normalized so the cycling line has unit strength: M₁(I) = 4I(3 + 2I)/(9(1 + 2I)²), M_2π(I) = 4I/(9 + 18I), M_2−(I) = 16I/(9(1 + 2I)³), giving 2/9, 1/9, 1/9 at I = 1/2; the clock-state P₁/₂ scheme has M₁′ = M₂′ = 2/9 with α₂′ > α₁′ always **[corrected]**.
- **Detector non-idealities are a named gap, not a negligible one.** The corrected closed-form optimum for Crain's apparatus is 5.84 × 10⁻⁴ against the measured 6.9 × 10⁻⁴; dead time, afterpulsing and SNSPD recovery at a 2.1 µs mean inter-arrival time are the plausible remainder, and the photon-record layer therefore carries an optional dead-time and afterpulse model with parameters the user must supply **[extracted]**.
- **Motion-to-readout coupling has no functional form in any source.** Harty et al. observe that heating degrades 393 nm shelving in 43Ca+ through Doppler broadening but give no equation; the simulator implements the coupling from first principles through the thermal Doppler profile of the shelving transition and flags it as untested against data **[background]**.
- **Micromotion on the detection beam.** R_∘ carries J₀(β)² and a first-sideband channel J₁(β)², like every other drive (Section 4.1); no readout source applies it **[corrected]**.
- **Doppler broadening from residual motion, made computable.** With v_rms = √(Σ_m ħω_m(2n̄_m + 1)/(2m)) over the modes the detection k-vector projects onto, k v_rms/2π for 171Yb+ is 257 kHz at n̄ = 0.1, 425 kHz at n̄ = 1, 1.57 MHz at n̄ = 20 and 3.48 MHz at n̄ = 100 against the 19.7 MHz natural linewidth, so detection or shelving error from first-order Doppler broadening is negligible until n̄ ≳ 100 and the Lamb-Dicke guard η²(2n̄ + 1) fails first; derived, not sourced **[background]**.


# Part IV. Validation, delivery and provenance

## 9. Validation suite

Every module ships tests that reproduce published, verified numbers or closed forms. The suite is the definition of "correct" for this project: a change that moves any of these outside tolerance fails CI. Two kinds of rows share the tables and have different CI semantics. **Pinned tests** (closed forms, recomputed values, identities, conversions) fail the build; where a row states no tolerance, the default is 10⁻⁹ relative for closed forms and exact identities, the printed number of significant digits for recomputed values, and 10⁻¹² for operator identities. **Consistency anchors** (laboratory numbers quoted from a paper) are tracked metrics with the acceptance band stated in the row or, by default, the published uncertainty, or 20% where none is given; they report but do not fail the build, because Section 12 records that published fidelities cannot be reproduced from first principles without recomputing the mode structure and pulses under stated assumptions. Every row is written in the simulator's own conventions and names the Section 13 row it depends on; a source's convention appears only as a labelled conversion test. Multi-ion rows run with the resolved-mode set their device preset declares and the frozen-spectator contribution of Section 5.2 reported. All tests run in JOINT_EXACT mode unless marked.

### 9.8 GATE_LOCAL against JOINT_EXACT

| Test | Expected |
|---|---|
| Three- and four-ion circuits of 3 to 5 gates at the resolved-mode sets of Section 9.6 | final probabilities agree within the residual-displacement bound Σ∣α∣²(2n̄ + 1) that GATE_LOCAL reports; disagreement above it fails; longer circuits (10 to 30 gates, about an hour per sample at dimension 512 at the Section 11.1 rates) are compared in GATE_LOCAL against the analytic budget only |
| Motional bookkeeping | tracked n̄ per mode after each gate matches the joint simulation's reduced motional state to 5% |
| Dropped-mode policy | modes dropped by the contribution criterion (∣α_m(τ)∣²(2n̄_m + 1) < 10⁻⁶ and ∣χ_m(τ)∣ < 10⁻⁴ from the Section 4.4.3 integrals with the pulse's detuning schedule) change two-qubit fidelity by less than the summed dropped contribution the report states; a mode with η = 10⁻³ two kilohertz from a tone is kept; a mode above the drop pair but with ∣χ_m∣ < 0.05 rad is classed frozen, never dropped, and its χ_m loss appears in the calibration target and in `Diagnostics.mode_class` (Section 5.2) |

### 9.9 Numerical convergence (every case above)

Tightening tolerances by 10× (and, for the integrator ladder, loosening by 10× and comparing) and raising truncation caps by 2 (where the doubled cap stays within the dimension ceiling; otherwise the check runs on the reduced mode set) changes reported probabilities by less than the stated test tolerance; the truncation-boundary population stays below the per-test threshold of Section 5.5 and the cap keeps the Section 5.1.1 margin above the populated range in every pulse; frozen-spectator runs reproduce the joint run's entangling angle χ and final probabilities within the reported bound; the same run over 1 and 18 workers agrees to 10⁻¹² with per-trajectory identity under the same seed (a bitwise assertion fails at 4.4 × 10⁻¹⁶ on 5.3.1 because trajectory sums accumulate in completion order, Section 3.4); shots drawn from `mcsolve` with and without `improved_sampling` converge to the `mesolve` histogram on a two-ion dissipative case.

### 9.11 Application consistency (Section 14)

These tests live in the separate application package and run in its CI, not the core's.

| Test | Expected |
|---|---|
| Coarse-graining identity | populations and Pauli expectations shown at Level 1 after gate k, computed from the recorded Level 3 joint state, equal the reduced-density-matrix values to 10⁻¹² |
| Channel derivation | an app-side channel-replay run of a two-ion Bell circuit matches JOINT_EXACT within the channel-derivation residual it reports; a deliberately hotter motional state widens the residual and the discrepancy together |
| Downward propagation | changing the rf amplitude at Level 4 changes β, ν, η and the recalibrated gate durations consistently with Section 4.1 and the pulse solvers; the device card updates without manual steps |
| Request semantics | XX(0.3) requested at Level 1 yields a pulse whose simulated unitary is XX(0.3) within the calibration tolerance; a detuning set by hand at Level 2 shows the actual unitary at Level 1 |
| Provenance coverage | every quantity in the view-model catalogue resolves to a tagged Part II item (static test over the catalogue; the build fails on an untagged quantity) |
| Convergence badge | an under-truncated run turns the badge red and fails the halving test; the same run at the policy's caps passes |
| Record round trip | export then import reproduces bitstrings, traces, seeds and hashes bitwise |
| Re-simulation cache | zooming into a pulse twice recomputes once; the cached trace equals a fresh one to solver tolerance |
| Navigation | a Bell-state job is followed from a histogram bar to a Hamiltonian matrix element in at most six clicks (`flet test`) |
| Presets | each Section 9 preset loads, runs and displays the published number beside the simulated one with the correct tag |

### 9.13 Atomic structure, numerics and interface targets recomputed for this revision (2026-09-04)

Recomputed on this machine (`check_atomic.py`, `bench_numerics.py`, `bench_ms_timing.py`, Appendix D); each becomes a test. The atomic anchors were computed from the Breit-Rabi formula and from a general hyperfine-plus-Zeeman diagonalization with Steck's conventions (Section 13) and compared with published values that the earlier runs had verified independently.

| Fitted-temperature conversion | n̄ = [exp(ν/ν_T) − 1]⁻¹: 1.6021 at ν_T = 2 MHz, ν = 0.97 MHz (T = h ν_T/k_B = 96.0 µK); 1.0373 and 0.9864 at ν_T = 4 MHz, ν = 2.7 and 2.8 MHz; 0.1678 at ν_T = 0.5 MHz, ν = 0.97 MHz; 0.6105 at ν_T = 1 MHz, ν = 0.97 MHz. Negative test: ν_T/ν = 2.0619 against n̄ + ½ = 2.1021 in the first row, so the Rayleigh-Jeans shortcut is high by about half a quantum in every row | Pino et al. 2021 Table II [corrected], [recomputed here] |
| Four-ion crystal span | ℓ = (e²/(4πε₀ m ω_x²))^{1/3} = 2.7966 µm for 171 u at ω_x/2π = 0.97 MHz (2.7970 µm at 170.9363 u); the N = 4 dimensionless equilibria are ±0.45438, ±1.43680 and the span 2.87360 ℓ = 8.0365 µm (8.0375 µm at the isotope mass), matching the reported "8 µm" | Pino et al. 2021 Methods [verified], [recomputed here] |
| Junction micromotion, peak against rms | qφ_ps = 2.9 × 10⁻⁵ eV gives E₀ = √(4mΩ_rf²φ_ps/q) = 1716.6 V/m, z₁^peak = qE₀/(mΩ_rf²) = 67.58 nm (identical to 2√(qφ_ps/(mΩ_rf²)) to rtol 10⁻⁷) and z₁^rms = 47.78 nm against the paper's 47 nm. The static shift from the 8.7 × 10⁻⁸ eV/µm axial gradient in the 3.6 MHz well is 1.82 nm, which rules it out as the explanation. A code path returning 68 nm is correct in the peak convention | Blakestad Sec. II [corrected], [recomputed here] |
| Junction barrier from the pseudopotential | 0.3 eV for 9Be+ at V_rf = 200 V peak, Ω_rf/2π = 83 MHz requires ∣∇φ̃_rf∣ = 873.0 m⁻¹, that is a peak axial rf field of 1.746 × 10⁵ V/m; the 1/m rescaling at fixed charge and drive gives 67.7 meV for 40Ca+ and 15.8 meV for 171Yb+ | Blakestad Sec. II, Fig. 4 [verified], [recomputed here] |
| Junction-centre Laplace identity | With the channel axes equivalent (h_x = h_z) and the rf Hessian traceless, h_y = −2h_x, and since ω_rf,i ∝ ∣h_i∣ this forces ω_y = 2ω_x exactly: 11.4 MHz predicted against the reported 11.3 MHz at ω_x/2π = 5.7 MHz (measured ratio 1.982). Assert ω_x² + ω_y² + ω_z² = 2ω_rf² in the straight channels and disable it inside the junction | Blakestad Eq. 13 [background], [recomputed here] |
| DAC resonance ladder | f_z/J at f_z = 3.6 MHz for J = 7 to 14: 514.286, 450.000, 400.000, 360.000, 327.273, 300.000, 276.923, 257.143 kHz; only J ≥ 8 sits within the 500 kHz hardware cap, which is why the observed set starts at J = 8, and R_DAC = 480 kHz gives f_z/R_DAC = 7.500, half-integer between J = 7 and 8. Per-update advances from Table I after subtracting the 20 µs dwell: 11.1, 8.2 and 6.4 µm at mean speeds 5.33, 3.96 and 3.05 m/s; a 5 µm/update rule would give 2.4 m/s and 733 µs for the 330 µs E-C-E move | Blakestad Sec. V B, Table I [corrected], [recomputed here] |
| Sideband coupling ladder | g_m = (−iη)^m g₀/m! with g_nm = g_m √(n!/(n−m)!) reproduces the printed g_n1 = −iηg₀√n and g_n2 = −½η²g₀√(n(n−1)) exactly (complex equality to machine precision at m = 1, 2 and n = 2, 3, 5) | Sterk et al. 2022 Eq. 2, Methods [verified], [recomputed here] |
| Pseudo-energy zero at the ground state | With η = 0.23, M_{0,+1} = η e^{−η²/2} = 0.22400, P_{↑,+1}(3π) = cos²(3πM/2) = 0.2428, red and second-red dark, so E_p = 2(0.2428 − 1 − 1) + 7/2 = −0.0144; the exact zero is at η = 0.22808 (2/9 = 0.22222). Companion: 1 − η²n = −0.0580 at n = 20, so the linearized Debye-Waller factor is invalid there | Walther et al. 2012 Eq. 3 [corrected], [recomputed here] |
| Loss-functional weight placement | With α₁ = α₂ = 2 × 10⁻³ Hz⁻¹ and illustrative I₁ = 1.5 kHz, I₂ = 0.4 kHz: ℒ = α₁I₁ + (α₂I₂)² = 3.640000 (dimensionless) against α₁I₁ + α₂I₂² = 323.000000, the second terms differing by exactly α₂. Thermal envelope ordering: ⟨√n⟩/√n̄ → √π/2 = 0.88623 (0.88600 at n̄ = 1000) and ⟨√(n(n−1))⟩/n̄ → 1 (0.99950 at n̄ = 1000), so the two terms grow as √n̄ and n̄² and their ratio as n̄^{3/2} | Sterk et al. 2022 Eq. 1 [corrected], [recomputed here] |
| Convolution against the exact displaced thermal state | At n̄_th = 0.1: p₀ = 0.822579 (convolution) against 0.830092 (Laguerre), max∣Δp∣ = 1.297 × 10⁻² and variance 0.21000 against 0.23000 at n̄_α = 0.1; p₀ = 0.334436 against 0.366264, max∣Δp∣ = 3.183 × 10⁻² and variance 1.11000 against 1.31000 at n̄_α = 1. The variance difference is exactly 2n̄_th n̄_α in both. The printed index range m = n..N sums to e^{−n̄_α} = 0.36788 at n̄_α = 1 | Walther et al. 2012 Eq. 2 [corrected], [recomputed here] |
| End-to-end junction transport (integration target, not a unit test) | A designed adiabatic junction waveform must return essentially **zero** coherent excitation, since the measured 0.053 ± 0.003 quanta per E-C-E round trip (350 µs, 1.76 mm, two barrier crossings) and 0.18 ± 0.02 per E-C-F-C-E and E-C-V-C-E are noise-limited rather than adiabaticity-limited; anomalous heating alone contributes 0.007 to 0.014 quanta | Blakestad Table I, Secs. V C-E [verified] |
| End-to-end splitting (integration target) | The quartic-plus-Coulomb model on Bowler's schedule (2.6 MHz initial, minima near 700 kHz COM and 880 kHz stretch, final 2.85 and 2.77 MHz) over 55 µs with a 17 µs first segment must give about 2 quanta per ion, dominated by the coherent component, with about 0.2 quanta from ambient heating, and must remain partially de-excitable (2.1 → 1.4, 1.9 → 1.6) under a resonant uniform-field displacement | Bowler et al. 2012, two-ion paragraph and Fig. 5 [verified] |
| End-to-end fast transport (integration target) | Sweeping the dwell of an 11.2 µs out-and-back 280 µm sin²-position ramp at 1.41 MHz must give n̄_α(τ_d) periodic with period 1/ν_ax = 709.22 ns, minima ≤ 0.1 quanta and maxima of order 100; sweeping the kick delay of a 4 µs transport plus one 400 ns square kick must give a sinusoidal n̄_α(τ_k) whose two kick polarities are offset by half a motional cycle | Walther et al. 2012 Fig. 3 [verified] |
| Round-trip time reversal (self-consistency) | In a noiseless constant-ω simulation, applying the exact time reversal of a forward waveform after a hold of an integer number of secular periods returns α → 0 and n̄ to its initial value up to integrator error, and the excitation-versus-hold-offset curve is flat for an optimized waveform and sinusoidal for an unoptimized one | Sterk et al. 2022 (phase independence); Pino et al. 2021 (combine as the time reverse of split) [background] |

### 9.16 Derivation-audit targets (Appendix D, 2026-09-04)

The blind re-derivation audit of Appendix D confirmed 37 of 58 load-bearing equations and corrected 16; the rows below are the numerical checks its derivers, comparers and adjudicators ran to discriminate the plan's forms from the corrected ones, written as the test suite must reproduce them. Each row names the equation by its audit id (section-index of the harvest). Two of them are re-derived by committed scripts (`check_c0_floquet.py`, `check_ms_closure.py`); the rest were computed by the audit's agents in the run's own scripts and are recorded here as targets an implementer reproduces, not as **[recomputed here]** numbers, since Appendix D's rule is that the tag requires a committed script.

| 6-4: Fuzz the 8-dimensional MS-crosstalk model over (chi, phi_beam, theta_13, theta_23) with 4000 samples, comparing F and P_ion3 to the plan's closed forms; then evaluate at theta_13 = theta_23 = pi/2 and at (0.1644, -0.2763). | max∣numeric - plan form∣ = 1.33e-15, independent of both chi and phi_beam; F = 0.900790 and P_ion3 = 0.097217 at the quoted angles (against the printed 0.974422 / 0.025450); at theta_13 = theta_23 = pi/2 the crosstalk unitary reduces to -X1X2, giving P_ion3 = 0 where the halved-argument form gives 0.5. | derivation audit 2026-09-04 (the audit agents' scripts live in the run transcript and are not committed; the row is the implementer's target) |
| 8-1: Solve the exact 4-level 171Yb+ F=1 -> F'=0 Lindblad steady state (16-dim Liouvillian, magic-angle polarization, pump << omega_Z << Gamma) over three decades of s_o and compare to both the plan's (Gamma/6, 2/3) form and the (Gamma/18, 2/9) form; also check the saturation ceiling and half-max. | exact/corrected = 0.999991, 0.999284, 0.991954; exact/plan = 0.333478, 0.333243, ... (a flat factor 3). Ceiling Gamma/4 = 30.79/us confirmed; half-max at s_o = 4.5, not 1.5. Sum_m Omega_m^2 = Omega^2/3 for any single-beam polarization; Sum_q∣eps_q∣^{-2} >= 9. | derivation audit 2026-09-04 (the audit agents' scripts live in the run transcript and are not committed; the row is the implementer's target) |

The Poisson convolution collapse Sum_k P_p(k; R_1 tau) P_p(n-k; R_2(t-tau)) = P_p(n; R_1 tau + R_2(t-tau)) is exact. | derivation audit 2026-09-04 (the audit agents' scripts live in the run transcript and are not committed; the row is the implementer's target) |
| 8-5: Compute eps_geom = (1 - cos(arcsin NA))/2 symbolically and by isotropic Monte Carlo at NA = 0.2, 0.4, 0.6, 0.9, and compare to the paraxial NA^2/4; also derive the EMCCD excess-noise factor from the cascade recursion. | Closed form = sin^2(theta_max/2) identically; exactly 1/10 at NA = 0.6 (MC 0.099927 ± 0.00015). Paraxial errs -1.0%, -4.2%, -10.0%, -28.2%. F^2 = (2G + p - 1)/(G(1+p)) -> 2 - 1/G -> 2, so QE_eff = QE/F^2 = QE/2 belongs in the variance only, never in the mean rate. | derivation audit 2026-09-04 (the audit agents' scripts live in the run transcript and are not committed; the row is the implementer's target) |

## 10. Roadmap and milestones

Each milestone ends with tests that pass in CI. Nothing in a later milestone is started until the earlier milestone's validation cases pass in JOINT_EXACT mode. The ordering follows the physical chain, so at every stage a runnable, physically meaningful subset exists.

### M0. Scaffolding and public interfaces (1 to 2 weeks)
- `uv`-managed project, Python 3.13, dependencies pinned (qutip 5.3.x, numpy, scipy; pytest, hypothesis; matplotlib optional).
- GUI dependency declared as the optional extra `gui`: `flet[all]>=0.86,<1.0` and `flet-charts>=0.86,<1.0` (Flet 0.86.5 of 2026-08-01, Apache-2.0, Python 3.10 or later; the 0.86 line is the announced last release before 1.0, so the pin is reviewed when 1.0 ships). Flet is the Flutter-based Python UI framework that will carry the separate application package of Section 14: device configuration, live experiment scans and run monitoring in a native window (`flet run`) or a browser served from the host (`flet run --web`). Nothing in the physics core imports it.
