- **The inter-segment state hand-off is unspecified.** The closed-form amplitude solution holds only at constant frequency and the squeezing parameters depend on the past evolution, but no source prescribes how to propagate the Ermakov state (ρ, ρ̇, μ), the accumulated squeezing (δ, θ) and the coherent phase across a transport, a split, a junction and a merge - which is exactly what accumulating squeezing over a multi-segment schedule requires. Section 4.6 specifies that carry-across as the plan's own construction.
- **Junction coverage is single-apparatus and dated.** One source family supplies the rf pseudopotential barriers, the apex axial anti-confinement, the frequency schedule and two technical noise channels, and its junction is a two-wafer three-dimensional design with rf bridges rather than a modern surface-electrode junction. Missing: surface-trap junction barrier structure, an explicit-Ω_rf or Floquet treatment of micromotion at the apex, the excitation cost of crossing a large-micromotion region, and two-dimensional transport with rotating principal axes. The DAC-comb artefact that dominated the measured excitation is 2011 hardware and is specified as an optional term.
- **The splitting voltage recipe is not geometry-general, and its counterexample is a surface trap.** Its source assumes that "any reasonable segmented trap geometry will exhibit similar characteristics", yet its own tabulated surface trap with a segmented ground plane has the centre and splitting quartic coefficients sign-flipped relative to every three-dimensional design, and the authors warn that the quartic Taylor truncation itself breaks down there. This bears directly on Section 4.1.6, which is the plan's surface-electrode layer. Relatedly, the quartic expansion is used at or past its own stated validity edge: it holds while the well separation is small against the segment width, but the critical-point separation is already a sizeable fraction of the segment spacing and the final separation exceeds it, with no error estimate supplied.
- **Splitting excitation has no external validation and N > 2 is unquantified.** The measured budgets present are 1.4 to 2.1 quanta per two-ion split with partial de-excitability and a simulated floor near one phonon at tens of microseconds; a nine-ion partitioning result reports reliability across all combinations but no excitation numbers, and one two-ion transport result is qualitative only. Ruster et al. 2014, Kaufmann et al. 2017, Kaushal et al. 2020 and the Quantinuum QCCD reports would be the external checks and were not read.
- **Transport-induced decoherence and recooling are unmodelled.** Every Run 5 transport entry is unitary apart from an ambient-heating rate: no dephasing rate during transport, no spin-motion entanglement from the waveform, no magnetic-field-gradient phase along the path, and no coherence budget per shuttle beyond one spin-echo 1/e time and one qualitative Ramsey verification. No cooling-time budget as a function of the excitation to be removed is given, and no source reports a post-cooling occupation for any cooling stage, so the residual n̄ entering a gate is a free parameter.
- **Transport failure and reordering rates are unquantified, with a known undetected path.** The sources describe such events only as "rare" and "automatically detected and scrubbed", and the crystal-mode-structure probe used to catch them is explicitly blind to events that swap the qubit ions and to multiple reorderings that restore the correct order before the probe. The heralded-erasure channel of Section 4.6 therefore has no rate and a known undetected-error path with no bound.
- **None of the Run 5 transport corrections could be checked against a version of record.** Every verification was against arXiv preprints, publisher pages having been unreachable during the run, and the splitting source exists as arXiv v1 only. About ten printed defects are corrected across Section 4.6; each should be re-checked against the journal text before it enters a code comment, and each is brief-derived rather than a published erratum.

**What the derivation audit does not establish (2026-09-04).** The audit re-derived equations from the plan's stated conventions and confirmed or corrected them numerically; it did not check citations against the source PDFs, so the provenance claims about printed errors (Wineland's Eq. 62, Brownnutt's Eq. 14, Turchette's Eq. 2) rest on the errata check and on internal consistency; it left every empirical species input unverified (ε_sys presets, background rates, measured branching ratios, reduced-element values are ingest data, not derivable quantities, and both derivers of the readout rows said so); it audited equations, not the code that will implement them; four rows rest on one blind derivation and one on one adjudicator, and about fifteen setups were judged insufficient, so a handful of confirmations rest on a guessed convention that happened to match. Five items it left open are recorded here so that they are not mistaken for settled: which triangle of the displacement matrix ⟨n′|D|n⟩ a plan sentence calls 'above' the diagonal (now stated explicitly in Section 5.1.1, but the modulus is transpose-symmetric and nothing computable depends on it); the 171Yb+ P₁/₂ → D₃/₂ reduced element, whose value hinges on using the 2.438 µm channel wavelength rather than the 935 nm repump (Section 4.5.6 now pins it); the number of digits at which the 87Rb reference elements are quoted, which follow the stored lifetime (Section 4.5.6); the sign branch of the entangling angle, which Section 4.4.3 now states as |χ| = π/4 modulo π/2 with the compiler's sign; and the apparatus quantities of Section 8 (detection efficiencies, background rates, the D₃/₂ branching), which no derivation can supply. The two equations that lost their comparer to the output caps, the transverse Hessian of Section 4.1.3 and the emission angular factors of Section 4.2.8, keep their earlier tags and the committed check scripts behind them (`check_recoil.py`, `check_surface_mixed.py`), and are the first candidates for a second audit pass.

**What the 2026-09-04 four-lens critique changed and what it left open.** The critique of Appendix D (four lenses, 64 findings) was folded on 2026-09-04 as edit batches 19a to 19e; every fix is marked **[corrected: critique, 2026-09-04]** where it lands and Section 9.17 carries its regression targets. What it did not settle is recorded here so that none of it is mistaken for done: the theorist's overall paragraph names a q-for-Q and a b-for-β slip in displayed equations of Section 4.1 without a line, and neither was located; three of the numerics critic's measurements are quoted from the critique and not yet reproduced by a committed script (the `mesolve`/`sesolve` wall-time ratio 69.5 at dimension 64, the `PicklingError` under `map="parallel"`, and the ENR displacement's ⟨n₀⟩ = 2.2412 against 2.25), so they carry no **[recomputed here]** tag; the Monroe 1995 beam geometry behind the Lamb-Dicke anchor is inferred from the quoted numbers (a 90° crossing reproduces them, a counter-propagating pair does not) and is to be confirmed against the paper's figure; the Doppler acceptance test of Section 4.2.1 (three n̄ from one Δ) is stated as the design and not yet computed; the 171Yb+ g_J the plan now adopts is a theoretical determination (Han et al. 2025) with no modern measurement behind it; and the cost model of Section 11 was rebuilt on the v4 benchmark, whose findings Section 11.2 and Appendix D record, including that the v3 table had timed a dense operator through four unmerged coefficient terms.


## 13. Conventions the simulator fixes

Every quantity below has at least two conventions in the primary literature, and the research runs found published factor errors that trace to mixing them. The simulator adopts one convention per quantity, records the source it follows, restates it in the docstring of every module that touches the quantity, and pins the conversions with tests.

| Quantity | Simulator convention | Follows | Alternatives seen in sources |
|---|---|---|---|
| Frequencies | Internal: angular, rad/s. Public API: Hz, converted at the boundary with an explicit 2π | RMP 2003 | RMP writes ν for an angular frequency; papers quote "γ = 2π × 19.6 MHz", "γ/2π = 60 MHz" and "19.7 MHz" for γ/2π interchangeably [corrected]; Leung quotes 3.045 MHz as an ordinary frequency and Zhu's ω_{ξ,k} refers to ω_z, not ω_ξ [corrected] |
| Mathieu equation | d²x/dξ² + [a − 2q cos 2ξ]x = 0, ξ = ω_rf t/2, a = 4Z∣e∣Uα/(mω_rf²), q = 2Z∣e∣Ũα′/(mω_rf²) | Leibfried et al. 2003 | Wineland 1998 and Berkeland 1998 use +2q cos (a half-period shift); factor-4 q definitions elsewhere [verified] |
| Characteristic exponent | Monodromy matrix; β ≈ √(a + q²/2) as a check | Floquet theory | RMP Eqs. 10-11 carry a printed index-shift error [corrected] |
| Ladder operators | x̂ = x₀(a + a†), x₀ = √(ħ/2mω) | RMP Eq. 26 | James uses the Kittel convention with an explicit i [verified]; Brownnutt arXiv v1 prints â with √(ħmω) [corrected]; Ozeri 2007 and Baldwin 2021 put a factor 4 under the root of z₀ or η [verified flag] |
| Lamb-Dicke parameter | η_{j,k} = b_j^{(k)}(Δk·ê_k)√(ħ/2mω_k) per ion and mode for the drive's effective Δk | Wineland 1998 Eq. 33; Monroe 2020 | James's s_m^{(p)} = √N b_m^{(p)}μ_p^{−1/4} against the single-ion frequency [verified]; single-beam k in some papers |
| Rabi frequency | H = (ħΩ/2)σ₊e^{…} + h.c., carrier π pulse at Ωt = π | RMP 2003; Monroe 2020; Lee 2005 | Wineland 1998 (ħΩ = −μB₁/4), Monroe 1995 and Blümel 2021 Eq. S28 use P = sin²(Ωt), half the usual Rabi frequency [verified flag] |
| Two-photon Rabi frequency | Ω_R = Σ_e Ω₂*Ω₁/(2Δ_e) with Ω_j = E_j⟨e∣d·ε̂_j∣g⟩/ħ the single-photon Rabi frequencies in the ħΩ/2 convention and Δ_e = ω_L − ω_eg; light shift δ_g = Σ∣Ω∣²/(4Δ_e) (Section 4.5.4) | Wineland 2003 Eqs. 2.3, 2.10 read as the matrix-element normalization they are; second-order perturbation theory | Wineland's overall minus sign is his Ω ≡ −E₀⟨d⟩/(2ħ) definition, a phase convention; RMP A13 and Wineland 2003 read as Ω₁Ω₂/Δ_R are 2× too large; Monroe 1995's Ω = g₁g₂/Δ goes with P = sin²(Ωτ) [corrected] |
| Detuning symbols | δ = ω_L − ω₀ from the carrier; μ = beat-note detuning from the carrier; δ_{i,m} = μ_i − ω_m always two-indexed; δ̂ = dimensionless composite-pulse detuning error | Run 2B consolidation | Landsman's δ measured from a mode; Mount's dimensionless δ [corrected] |
| Effective wavevector | Δk = k₁ − k₂ with beam 1 coupling to the upper level; the magnitude 2k sin(θ_cross/2) from the configured geometry; beam labels fixed at ingest | RMP; Wineland 1998; Lee 2005 | Landsman's k = k₂ − k₁; Lee's arXiv v1 Eq. 2 inverts its own beam-to-level assignment, the journal version being correct [corrected: preprint only] |
| Optical phase factor on σ₊ | e^{+i(Δk·X̄_i − Δφ_i)} globally; beam-path and ion-position drift stored as one scalar per ion per pulse | Monroe 2020 Eqs. 16, 18 | e^{−i(…)} in Monroe Eqs. 3, 5 and Lee Eqs. 3, 5 [corrected] |
| Participation factor | counted once, in b_j^{(k)}, with ξ_m = √(ħ/2Mω_m) | Monroe 2020 | Lee's 1/√2 inside η for two ions; Ozeri's z₀ = √(ħ/4Mω) [corrected] |
| Rotation generators | R(θ, φ) carries θ/2 in the exponent, XX(χ) carries χ with no ½; no shared generator helper; p_xx = sin²α is the two-qubit case only | Maslov 2017; Trout 2018 | [corrected] |
| Displacement integral | Choi's form with sin(μt) inside the integrand and bare e^{iω_m t}, or δ_m = ω_m − μ with a slow envelope, never mixed; FM uses θ_m(t) = ∫δ_m dt′ | Choi 2014 | Landsman's printed bare-ω_m form with a slow envelope integrates to nearly zero [corrected] |
| Micromotion correction | η_eff = ηC₀ with C₀ the e^{iνt} Fourier coefficient of the exact Floquet function normalized by its Wronskian, Im(u* u̇) = ν, from the monodromy module: C₀ = 1 + 3q²/16 + O(q⁴) (1.007741 at q = 0.2), no first-order term; excess micromotion as phase modulation with index φ_Ω | derivation audit of 2026-09-04 (`check_c0_floquet.py`); Wineland Eqs. 71-72; Berkeland Eq. 21 (misprint corrected) | the RMP's (1 + q/2)⁻¹, whether attached to Ω₀ or to η, is the u(0) = 1 normalization at one rf phase, an rf-clock artifact [corrected: derivation audit, 2026-09-04]; "Debye-Waller" is a different factor [corrected]; pdftotext renders φ_Ω as φ_V [corrected] |
| Lamb-Dicke base | single-ion mass, unit-norm b, participation never pre-folded: η_{i,m} = (Δk·ê_m) b_{i,m} √(ħ/2m_ion ω_m); for mixed species the same form with the mass-weighted orthonormal eigenvector component c_{i,m} and the ion's own mass (Home 2013 Eq. 19; Morigi-Walther Eq. 12) | Monroe 2020; trap and Hamiltonian briefs; Section 4.1.7 | Sørensen-Mølmer's and Leung's per-ion COM η already contains 1/√N (0.1103 single-ion against 0.0493 at N = 5 and 0.0268 at N = 17 for 171Yb+ at 355 nm, 3.045 MHz); Srinivas puts the total two-ion mass inside r₀ [corrected] |
| Scattering-rate object | one multi-level optical-Bloch steady state supplies the cooling rate W(Δ), the readout rate R_∘ and ρ_ee | Run 3 consolidation | the two-level Lorentzian saturates at Γ/2 where 171Yb+ detection saturates at Γ/4 with ρ_ee → s_∘/18 at low intensity for s_∘ = I/I_sat (s_∘/6 in the second revision, one polarization factor of 1/3 short; derivation audit 2026-09-04) [corrected] |
| Cooling coefficients A_± | bare coefficients with units of rate, A_± = W(Δ ∓ ν) + (η̃²/η²)W(Δ) with η̃² = α(k_em x₀)² the emitted photon's Lamb-Dicke parameter, the Fock rate equation carrying the drive's η²; the carrier weight reduces to α for one resonant beam along the mode axis (Stenholm's case) and vanishes only for EIT at the dark resonance [corrected: derivation audit, 2026-09-04] | Stenholm 1986 Eqs. 5.49-5.54; Cirac 1992 Eq. 30; Marzoli 1994 Eq. 2; Eschner 2003 Eq. 6; Morigi 2000; Lechner 2016 | RMP Eqs. 117-119 put η² inside; Cirac's printed Eqs. 50 and 52 correlate the sideband sign with the rate index and lack 2η²; the first version of this plan excluded the carrier term, which changes the steady state [corrected] |
| Mode addressing | every mode is a (family, index) pair; axial ascending in μ, transverse descending in γ; eigh output reversed for transverse | Marquet 2003; James 1998 | 'N modes' written for a 3N-mode chain [corrected] |
| Heating-rate meaning | a quoted rate is d⟨n⟩/dt at n = 0, that is ΓN̄; thermal diffusion is Γ(2N̄ + 1); rates of one bath object add; ṅ ∝ ω^{−(α+1)} when S_E ∝ ω^{−α} | Brownnutt 2015 | Trout's symmetric infinite-temperature pair {√ṅ a, √ṅ a†} [verified] |
| Micromotion factors on drives | C₀ on η and J_n(β) on every drive: gates, cooling and detection | Run 3 consolidation | applied to gates only in the cooling, two-qubit and readout sources [corrected] |
| Debye-Waller statistics | shot-to-shot: Ω constant within a shot and distributed across shots; never a within-shot damping | Wineland 1998; Cetina 2022 | [corrected] |
| Doppler limit | computed from the force model; the closed forms ħΓ/2 (ξ = 1) and 0.35ħΓ (RMP's ξ = 2/5) differ by 1.43 | RMP Eq. 106; Berkeland Eq. 13 | [corrected] |
| Sideband-cooling trapping condition | Ω_{n−1,n}t = 2mπ in this plan's Rabi convention | Section 4.2 | Ωt = mπ in Wineland 1998 and Monroe 1995 [corrected] |
| Zero-point energy | dropped, H = Σħω_k a†a | Wineland 1998; Marquet 2003 | James keeps +ħω_k/2 per mode; a convention, not an error [verified] |
| Mode eigenvectors | Orthonormal, axial eigenvalues increasing, last component positive | Marquet 2003; James 1998 | Transverse modes numbered by decreasing frequency [verified] |
| Electric-field noise density | Single-sided S_E; Γ_h = e²S_E/(4mħω) | Brownnutt Eqs. 11-12 | Two-sided gives e²S_E/(2mħω); the arXiv preprint of Turchette prints Eq. 3 inconsistent by 2, the published PRA version is consistent [corrected: preprint only] |
| Heating master equation | rates Γ(N̄+1) on a, ΓN̄ on a†; Γ_h = ΓN̄ | Wineland Eq. 62 (typo fixed); Brownnutt Eqs. 15-17 | the journal prints 2aρ†a†, a typesetting artifact absent from both arXiv versions of the paper [corrected] |
| Motional dephasing operator | L = a†a√(2/τ), coherence of ∣n⟩ + ∣n′⟩ decays at (n − n′)²/τ | Ballance 2016 | other normalizations of γ_φ |
| Qubit dephasing operator | L = √(γ_φ/2) σ_z, coherence decays at γ_φ, so γ_φ = 1/T₂ for the white component; a measured T₂ is split into its quasi-static (Gaussian) and white (exponential) parts before use | Section 5.7; Ramsey test in Section 9.7 | conventions giving 2/T₂ elsewhere [corrected: row added after the 2026-09-04 critique] |
| Spin operator in MS formulas | S_α = Σ_i σ_α^i (eigenvalues 0, ±2 for two ions) for the displayed propagator, the P₀, P_{±2} decomposition and Kirchmair's envelopes; the force on S_y is −(ħηΩ/2)(a†e^{iεt} + h.c.) for the plan's per-tone Ω; closure ηΩ/ε = 1/(2√K) in every spin normalization, with the phase target −π/2 on J_y², −π/8 on S_y², π/4 on σ_yσ_y | Sørensen-Mølmer 2000; Kirchmair 2009; Roos 2008; derivation audit 2026-09-04 (`check_ms_closure.py`) | Sørensen-Mølmer's J_α = ½Σσ_α with closure 1/(2√K) is ingested unchanged (the second revision halved it to 1/(4√K), a factor-2 force error caught by the derivation audit); Roos's Ω_c = ∣ε∣/(4η) reads ∣ε∣/(2η) in the plan's Ω [corrected: derivation audit, 2026-09-04] |

Field FWHM = 1.6768 τ, intensity (sech²) FWHM = 1.1222 τ (`check_critique_v3.py`; the earlier 0.8384 τ and 0.5611 τ are the widths of sech(πt/τ)). A `PulseShape` field records which envelope τ parameterizes [corrected: critique, 2026-09-04] | Section 4.3.7; Lee Eq. 1; Mizrahi Eq. 20 | Hayes' field-sech τ is 2× the Lee-family τ for one physical pulse, which is the whole origin of the 1 − x²/24 against 1 − x²/6 quadratic coefficients; measured autocorrelation widths passed as τ [corrected] |
| Comb sech arguments | a single tooth counted from the carrier carries sech(2πk ν_rep τ); a tooth **pair** indexed by separation l carries sech(πl ν_rep τ); the comb Stark sum carries sech²((j+k)πν_rep τ). | Comb saturation intensity | I_sat namespaced by chain: the plan's πhcΓ/(3λ³) with the CG-resolved dipole chain of Section 4.5.2, or the paper-specific 0.15 W/cm² (2.9545× larger) with g = γ√(I/(2I_sat)); a test asserts the two are never combined | Section 4.3.7; Section 13 saturation-intensity row | Mizrahi hardcodes 0.15 W/cm² for the non-cycling Yb+ D1 line, a lumped Clebsch-Gordan repair; pairing it with the resolved chain counts the line strength twice and makes every absolute Rabi frequency wrong by ~3 [corrected] |

Ω_{n,a} carries no sech factor | Section 4.3.7; derived by reindexing Lee Eqs. 9-10 | the printed C_{n,a} form invites a j ≠ 0 guard, which deletes the j = 0 Zeeman terms that make the elliptical-polarization cancellation work; the source's "only the closest few beat notes matter" is a heuristic, not a truncation licence, and partial sums plateau falsely a factor 2.093 low near ∣k∣ ~ 10 [corrected] |

II B; Cummins 2003 Eq. 7; Merrill and Brown Eqs. 10-11 | Low-Yoder-Chuang's F = ‖UV†‖ is identically 1 for a qubit under its own Eq. 2 norm and Brown-Harrow-Chuang's 1 − ‖V†U‖ identically 0 for unitaries; both replaced [corrected]. Keep separate from the RB row and the depolarizing row |

| Static control-error primitive | M(θ, φ; ε_a, ε_d) = exp[−iθ{(1 + ε_a)ρ̂(φ)·σ + ε_d σ_z}/2] with ρ̂(φ) = (cos φ, sin φ, 0); ε_a scales the rotation **angle**, ε_d is a detuning in units of the instantaneous Rabi rate, and the +ε_d σ_z term does **not** reverse with the drive phase | Kabytayev 2014 Sec. II A; Merrill and Brown Sec. 2.2.1; Cummins Eq. 5 | Cummins' "error in the pulse power" g must be implemented against the Rabi amplitude, not the optical power; the addressing channel ε_N has the **inverted** zero (ε_N = 0 ideal, U_T = I, M = R(θε_N, φ)), so R(θ(1 + ε_N), φ) is wrong [corrected] |

II-V and includes it in Sec. VI |
| Filter-function normalization stack | four pieces pinned together: S two-sided in angular frequency with the e^{−iωt} kernel (per rad/s, not per Hz); prefactor 1/(2π) two-sided or **1/π** one-sided; weight 1/ω²; and an explicit **−iω** inside R_ij(ω) = −iω∫₀^τ dt R_ij(t)e^{+iωt}. The −iω and the 1/ω² are a matched pair, so only (F = ω²∣FT[R]∣², 1/ω²) and (F = ∣FT[R]∣², ω⁰) are self-consistent | Green 2013 Eqs. 22-24; Kabytayev Eq. 3 | mixing a 1/(2π) prefactor with a one-sided integral is a factor 2 in χ; dropping the explicit 1/ω² is a factor ω²; a 1/ω⁴ weight appears nowhere [corrected] |
| Dephasing noise variable | H_deph = b(t)σ_z with **no** ½, so b is **half** the qubit-splitting fluctuation and the accumulated relative phase is φ(t) = 2∫b dt′; S_b is the two-sided PSD of b. A supplied qubit-frequency spectrum S_δ for δ(t)σ_z/2 converts as S_b = S_δ/4 | Green 2013 Eq. 4; reconciled with Biercuk 2009 Eq. 1 | Biercuk's S_β is described as the splitting-fluctuation PSD, which is 4S_b; reading one for the other is a factor 4 in χ, and Green's own Sec. 6.2 numerics silently switch to β σ_z/2 [corrected] |

The total analytic estimate is max[(1 − F)_FF, (1 − F)_dc] | Kabytayev Eq. 5 and Fig. 3 caption | the source prints Φ₂ and ĉ₂ in Ω = 1 units, so pairing ĉ₂ = 22.83 with a moment in (rad/s)⁴ errs by Ω^{2(m+1)} = 5.06 × 10²⁴ [corrected] |

| Splitting potential units | Φ(x) = βx⁴ + αx² + γx is an electrostatic potential in **volts** about the centre of the centre segment C; the energy is eΦ, so κ = e/(4πε₀) = 1.4400 × 10⁻⁹ V m, not e²/(4πε₀). Bowler's U(z) = a z² + b z⁴ is the same object as an **energy** with a = eα, b = eβ; a third normalization V = ½αx² + ¼βx⁴ differs by 2 and 4. γ is a coefficient in a volt-potential, so the axial field is E = −γ | Kaufmann et al. 2014 Eqs. 1-2, read as the volt-potential they are; Section 4.6 | Reading Φ_tot as an energy breaks every prefactor of Eqs. 14-18; substituting e²/(4πε₀) into Eq. 15 returns about 31 Hz instead of 176 kHz [corrected] |

| Sideband excitation lineshape | P = [Ω²/(Ω² + δ²)] sin²((t/2)√(Ω² + δ²)), half-depth at δ = Ω, π time π/Ω; the calibration fit function of Section 7.5 | Section 13 Rabi-frequency row; Section 7.5 | Wineland's and Blümel's Ω²/(Ω² + δ²/4) sin²(t√(Ω² + δ²/4)) is the half-Rabi form, doubled on ingest and never fitted [corrected: critique, 2026-09-04] |
| Virtual-Z propagation | RZ(θ) shifts every later pulse phase φ → φ − θ, gates read in time order | Section 7.6 (recomputed) | IonQ's printed +θ holds in matrix order; the second revision's 5.2 printed +θ in time order [corrected: critique, 2026-09-04] |
| Single-photon coupling symbol g | not a plan symbol: the code stores Ω (the ħΩ/2 convention) and two ingest maps, g = Ω/2 for Monroe 1995, Wineland 1998, Ozeri and Section 4.5.5's g_{b,r} = E⟨d⟩/2ħ, and g = Ω for the Lee comb chain (g₀ = γ√(Ī/2I_sat), Section 4.3.7); Monroe's Ω = g₁g₂/Δ is Ω₁Ω₂/(2Δ) in plan units | Sections 4.2.2, 4.3.7, 4.5.5 | one symbol meaning Ω/2 in two subsections and Ω in a third inside two-photon formulas that differ by exactly the factor 2 the plan hunts [corrected: critique, 2026-09-04] |
| Mode index | every `mode: int` is a position in `Crystal.modes`, ordered axial, transverse_1, transverse_2, ascending frequency within a family; eigenvectors unit-norm with the last component positive | Appendix E; Section 4.1.3 | eigensolver output order, frequency order across families, or (family, index) pairs, none declared in the second revision [corrected: critique, 2026-09-04] |
| Filter-function infrared cutoff | ω_min = 2π/T_total in rad/s, always reported with d ln χ/d ln ω_min beside χ | Section 6.9; Appendix E | 1/T for a rad/s field (the second revision); on an IR-divergent spectrum the two differ by 10⁴ on the free-induction fixture [corrected: critique, 2026-09-04] |
| Saturation-intensity rate | I_sat = πhcΓ_partial/(3λ³) with Γ_partial = 2π γ_hz × branching, ANGULAR: 171Yb+ 369.5 nm 50.83 mW/cm² | Section 9.13; Appendix E `Transition.partial_rate_rad_s` | `gamma_hz` fed in unconverted gives 8.09 mW/cm², a 2π in every saturation parameter, scattering rate and comb Rabi frequency [corrected: critique, 2026-09-04] |
| Composite-pulse durations | τ = Σ_l θ_l/Ω: SK1, BB1 4π + θ; CORPSE 4π + θ − 4k; CinSK, CinBB 8π + θ − 4k | Section 4.3.5; `check_composite.py` | the second revision's 8π + 2θ − 4k for the concatenations, 12% long at θ = π [corrected: critique, 2026-09-04] |
| 171Yb+ ground-state g_J | 2.002615(70) (Han et al. 2025, arXiv:2501.09973, MCDHF and MRCI), giving 310.87 Hz/G² with g_I from μ_I = +0.49367 μ_N | Section 4.5.1; `check_critique_v3.py` | 2.00225664 (43Ca+'s), 2.00254 and 2.00292, three uncited values in three rows of the second revision; the old spectroscopic 1.998 [corrected: critique, 2026-09-04] |

**Run 5 amendments to the rows above** (2026-09-04; the row named in bold is the one amended, and the check scripts of Section 9.15 pin each number).

- **"Rotation generators"** (currently: R(θ, φ) carries θ/2 in the exponent, XX(χ) carries χ with no ½). Amend the "Alternatives" cell to add: the composite-pulse literature depends on the θ/2 through R(2π, φ) = −I exactly; halving the tabulated angles to "pulse areas" of θ/2 silently turns every 2π filler into a 4π pulse and breaks SK1, BB1, P2 and the whole Trotter-Suzuki ladder, and the plan's own inverse-pulse rule R(θ, φ + π) is an **ideal**-pulse identity that fails for M under detuning error **[corrected]**.
- **"Detuning symbols"** (currently: … δ̂ = dimensionless composite-pulse detuning error). Amend to identify δ̂ with ε_d of the new primitive row, ε_d = Δ/Ω, so that the open normalization question of Section 12 ("reproducing the published curves requires guessing δ̂ = 2πf/Ω") is now closed by the Kabytayev/Cummins identification ε_d = f = δ/ν₁ rather than by a guess **[corrected]**.
- **"RB error rate"** and **"Depolarizing normalization"**: no change, but add a cross-reference note that neither is F_C, F_K or F_avg, and that a composite-pulse c-coefficient must be converted through the new fidelity row before it is compared with an RB or depolarizing number.
- **"Electric-field noise density"** (single-sided S_E). No change, but note that the filter-function stack above uses **two-sided** spectra in angular frequency, so S_E and S_b live in different conventions and the conversion is made explicit at the boundary; this is exactly the sidedness trap the row already warns about, in a second place.
- **Recoil angular factor** (row already present): add to the Alternatives column that Joshi et al. 2020's α = 1/3 is the isotropic value in its semiclassical heating term while its 2/5 and 1/5 are the second moments of its three-point recoil kernel, so the three numbers are three patterns under the plan's single α_m definition and never rival values; substituting 2/5 for α in the linearized heating rate fails the internal-consistency test by 5% **[recomputed here]**.
- **Scattering-rate object** (row already present): add to the Convention column that the same Bloch steady state supplies the coherent-population-trapping dark-state physics, and that its photon rate is asserted against the equal-population ceiling n_e/(n_e + n_g) as an upper bound.
- **Curvature naming** (row already present): add 171Yb+ as a second anchor - the quoted +310.8 Hz/G² is `taylor_c2`, recomputed as 310.76 Hz/G² from (g_J − g_I)²μ_B²/(2h²ν₀), and three printed hyperfine splittings differ from the zero-field value by exactly that shift at 3.0 to 5.3 G **[recomputed here]**.
- **Sign of A_hfs and the g-factors** (row already present): add that the ubiquitous "1.39-1.40 MHz/G" is the product g_Fμ_B/h and not the dimensionless g_F (171Yb+ ²S₁/₂ F = 1: g_F = g_J/2 = 1.001128, g_Fμ_B/h = 1.4012 MHz/G), that δ_B as printed by the polarization-gradient sources is the adjacent-m_F splitting rather than the full span, and that 9Be+'s negative nuclear moment puts F = I − ½ = 1 above F = 2, which is what assigns the (Δ + ω₀) and (Δ − ω₀) denominators of Wineland's eight-channel scattering rate to their bracket groups **[recomputed here]**, **[corrected]**.
- **Saturation intensity** (row already present): add that Ejtemaee and Haljan's 51 mW/cm² for 171Yb+ at 369.5 nm is this same convention to 0.4% (recomputed 50.78 mW/cm² at Γ_e/2π = 19.6 MHz) **[recomputed here]**.
- **Rabi frequency** and **Two-photon Rabi frequency** (rows already present): extend the Alternatives column with the two Run 5 half-conventions, Uys's Ω_R = μE₀/(2ħ) and Wineland's g = E∣⟨stretched⟩∣/(2ħ) with Ωτ_π = π/2, both exactly half the plan's Ω, plus Joshi's Ω referenced to the S₁/₂-P₃/₂ stretched transition (a code normalizing on the actual D1 σ transition is off by √(3/2) in Ω and 3/2 in s); the π **time** is convention-independent, so what breaks is any formula written in the symbol **[corrected]**.
- **Symbol collisions** (closing paragraph of Section 13): add W (polarization-gradient cooling rate versus blackbody-stimulated transition rate), H (heating rate in s⁻¹ versus Hamiltonian), Γ (natural linewidth versus Sisyphus cooling rate Γ_c versus reshelving-broadened Γ′ = Γ + R versus a collisional specific coefficient in cm³ s⁻¹), n̄ (mean phonon number versus mean thermal photon occupation), α (dark-state branching fraction versus the isotropy average versus a π-pulse degradation slope) and q (3j polarization index versus recoil momentum class versus the Mathieu parameter).
- **"Electric-field noise density"** and **"Heating master equation"**: add that the same single-sided Γ_h = e²S_E/(4mħω) drives the splitting, transport and junction channels of Section 4.6, made time-dependent through ω(t) along a trajectory, and that the equal-rate pair {√Γ a, √Γ a†} used by the transport sources is the N̄ ≫ 1 limit of these rows rather than a competing convention - it must not be imported with a finite N̄, which adds unphysical damping on microsecond timescales.
- **"rf amplitude and pseudopotential"**: add Blakestad's φ_ps = q(V_rf ∇φ̃_rf)²/(4mΩ_rf²) to the alternatives column as the volt-potential form of the same peak convention (Ψ = q φ_ps), and add the note that φ_ps is a square and therefore not a Laplace solution.
- **"Surface-electrode geometry"**: add that a segmented ground plane can sign-flip the centre and splitting quartic Taylor coefficients relative to every three-dimensional segmented trap, so that the Section 4.6 splitting voltage recipe is not geometry-general and the quartic truncation itself can fail there.
- **Symbol collisions renamed in code**: add to the list γ (Husimi-Kerner excitation parameter versus the splitting tilt coefficient in V/m versus a linewidth versus a phenomenological readout decay), α (coherent amplitude versus the splitting quadratic coefficient in V/m² versus the recoil angular factor), β (splitting quartic coefficient in V/m⁴ versus the Floquet exponent versus the erf-asymptote y/4), b (transport half-distance versus Bézier coefficient versus the energy-form quartic coefficient), d (ion-ion separation versus effective trap size versus layer separation), m (ion mass versus final Fock index versus sideband order), μ (Ermakov phase versus min(m, n)), and C (the CENTRE segment, not the splitting segment, which is S).

Symbol collisions renamed in code: nuclear spin versus laser intensity (I), pulse area versus polarization (π), laser detuning versus Zeeman splitting (Δ, δ), Ramsey arm versus shelf lifetime versus acquisition window (τ), mode index versus ion mass (m, M in Landsman and Monroe) [verified], "even modes" meaning spatially symmetric (odd index) in Egan [verified], the Mathieu parameter versus the ion charge (q in Sections 4.1.1 and 4.1.2, 4.1.5; `q_mathieu` and `Q_charge` in code) and the mode eigenvector component versus the characteristic exponent (b in Sections 4.1.3 and 4.1.5; `b_mode` and `beta_mathieu`) [corrected: added after the 2026-09-04 critique], and n_max (highest Fock index) versus d_m (mode dimension, n_max + 1); across the Run 4 sources also α (recoil geometric factor, Laguerre order, dc radial asymmetry, mode index, Madsen's aspect ratio, the double-thermal weight), β (Floquet exponent, mass-weighted eigenvector, Chiaverini's depth prefactor, the quartic axial coefficient, Wübbena's amplitude ratio), κ (Nizamani's rf depth factor, Madsen's dc efficiency), d (ion height, layer separation, compensating ground width, half separation), U₀ (Wesenberg's energy scale, Madsen's dc voltage), ω₀ (optical transition frequency in Steck, qubit frequency in Ozeri, Wineland and Uys) and γ (five distinct decay rates: population, optical coherence, dressed-state width, radiative Raman width, phenomenological contrast decay), each namespaced by source at import.


## 14. The zoomable application (milestone M11)

### 14.1 The idea and the contract

The application presents one simulated trapped-ion quantum computer at five levels of abstraction. The user starts where a cloud customer stands, with a circuit and a histogram, and zooms in step by step until the screen shows the Hamiltonian terms, matrix elements and collapse operators that the simulator is actually integrating. Every level is runnable: the user can execute the machine while looking at it from any height. The application is a separate package, `qutip_trap_app`, that depends on the released `qutip_trap` and uses only its public API. Nothing in the core library is written, scheduled or changed for the GUI: milestones M0 to M10 do not depend on M11, the core's CI does not run the app's tests, and when the app needs something the core lacks, that need is filed as a core feature request and judged on the core's own merits while the app works around it on its own side. Two axes are kept separate in the design. The **abstraction of the view** is what the screen shows. The **fidelity of execution** is which engine of Section 5.4 computed it. The zoom ladder pairs a default engine with each level, but any level can display a run made by a deeper engine, and shallower engines are always labelled as derived.

Four rules make the ladder honest rather than decorative.

1. **One record, many views.** Every level is a view over the same run record (Section 14.3). Nothing on any screen comes from a separate illustrative model. A histogram bar is the count of recorded shots; a Bloch vector is the reduced state of the recorded joint state; an ion's drawn position is the equilibrium solver's output at scale.
2. **Coarse-graining commutes with simulation, within a stated bound.** Running at a shallower fidelity must reproduce the deeper engine within the bound the shallower engine reports (the residual displacement of GATE_LOCAL, the channel-derivation residual of the app-side channel replay). The app can check this on demand, and Section 9.11 tests it.
3. **Every displayed formula and number carries its provenance.** The four tags of Section 1 appear as chips on the screen, linked to the Part II item and its source. A quantity without a tag is a bug.
4. **Every result carries its convergence report.** The truncation-boundary population and the tolerance-halving check of Section 5.5 are shown with every trace, and a failing check colours the result rather than hiding it.

### 14.2 The zoom ladder

| Level | What the user sees | What the user can change | Default engine | Zoom-in targets |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 0, Machine | The device card (species, ion count, native gate set, calibrated gate errors, SPAM), a circuit editor with OpenQASM 2 and IonQ JSON import, shot count, the results histogram with counts and probabilities, and the compiler's target distribution shown beside the simulated one and never in its place | Circuit, shots, qubit-to-ion mapping | Channel replay (app-side, built from the core's Section 6.8 channel summaries), labelled as derived | A histogram bar opens the shots behind it; a shot opens its photon-count records; a gate opens Level 1; the device card opens the Level 4 pages; the SPAM figure opens the readout page |
| 1, Circuit | The compiled native-gate timeline on ions (GPi, GPi2, MS or XX, ZZ, virtual-Z frame updates), each gate with its target unitary, calibrated parameters (χ, phases, duration) and the error channel derived for it; the register state after each gate as reduced density matrices, Bloch vectors and Pauli expectations; the phase register per ion | Gate parameters, compiler template choices, idle intervals | Channel replay or deeper | A gate opens its pulses at Level 2; a channel opens its derivation (process tomography of the simulated pulse); the phase register opens the virtual-Z rule with its pinning test (Section 7.6) |
| 2, Schedule | Pulses on a time axis: which ions each beam illuminates and from which direction, the tones with their detunings drawn against the mode spectrum beneath, phases, amplitude and frequency waveforms with their segment tables, durations and dead times, the crosstalk light on neighbours, and loop-closure indicators per mode | Waveforms, detunings, amplitudes, durations, crosstalk parameters | GATE_LOCAL | A pulse opens Level 3; a tone opens the Hamiltonian term it produces; a mode opens the crystal page at Level 4 |
| 3, Dynamics | Inside one pulse for one dynamical sample: qubit populations and coherences against time, Fock distributions per mode as heatmaps, the spin-dependent phase-space trajectories α_m(t) closing or failing to close, the accumulated entangling angle χ(t), the Debye-Waller factor, quantum jumps (heating, scattering) marked where they occurred, the process matrix of the finished pulse, and the quasi-static noise values drawn for this sample | Initial motional state per mode, noise rates, truncation caps and solver tolerances (with the convergence check re-run) | JOINT_EXACT, re-simulated on demand for the zoomed pulse | A Hamiltonian term opens its matrix elements and the Ω_{n′,n} table at Level 4; a jump opens the collapse operator and the derivation of its rate |
| 4, Physics | The first-principles pages: species (level diagram with the driven transitions, branching ratios, hyperfine structure, the qubit pair), trap (electrode potentials to Mathieu a and q to β and ν, pseudopotential, the stability diagram with the operating point, micromotion), crystal (equilibrium positions to scale, normal modes with animated eigenvectors, η per ion, mode and Δk), light (beams as fields with k-vectors and polarizations, the Raman configuration, Ω from intensity, Stark shifts, scattering rates against detuning), noise (the S_E model and heating rates, dephasing spectra, the crosstalk matrix), cooling (stages and n̄(t) per mode from the rate or master equations), readout (R_∘, R_d and R_b against saturation, count histograms, the discriminator and its threshold, the error budget), and the Hamiltonian builder page showing the H(t) terms and collapse operators assembled for a chosen pulse | Any device parameter: voltages and rf amplitude, laser powers, detunings and geometry, temperatures, noise densities, detection window | JOINT_EXACT with the preparation and readout layers, so a run is the whole process from cooling to photon records | Zooming out re-derives every level above through the calibration emulation |

A **numerics panel** accompanies every level: Hilbert-space dimensions and truncation caps in use, boundary population, the tolerance-halving result, trajectory count, wall time, and the engine that produced what is on screen.

### 14.3 One run record, many views

The run record is the single data structure the levels read. Job (device-model hash, seed, package version, convergence policy) → compiled circuit → schedule → per-sample dynamics traces → readout records → results. A dynamics trace stores, at configurable sample times, expectation values, reduced internal-state density matrices, per-mode occupation distributions, the α_m(t) trajectories and the jump list; full joint kets are stored only while the joint dimension is below a configurable cap. Anything not stored is recomputed on demand: zooming into a pulse re-simulates that pulse alone from its recorded initial state and its recorded noise sample, which by the measured timings of Section 11.1 takes about half a second for a 100 µs entangling pulse at joint dimension 48, two seconds at 256 and minutes at 2048; the record therefore stores the traces of the run itself, re-simulation is immediate only for small joint spaces, larger ones are filled in by a background job, and every result is cached in the record. Readout records hold photon counts per ion per sub-bin and the discriminator's posterior, so a shot can be opened down to its count trace. Records export to a JSON document with binary arrays (HDF5 optional) and re-import bitwise, which is what makes a published screenshot reproducible.

### 14.4 Knobs propagate downward

The device model at Level 4 is the single source of truth. A change there, a trap voltage, a beam power, a detection window, re-derives the analytic layer immediately (mode structure, η, pulse-solver solutions, the closed-form estimates of the gate channels, the device card's estimated columns) and invalidates the calibration table, whose regeneration by simulated experiment (Section 7.5) takes hours at the measured rates of Section 11.1 and runs only as a user-initiated background job; dependent views show a stale badge until that job completes, and the device card states which of its numbers are analytic estimates and which are calibrated. A change at a shallower level is a request rather than an edit: asking for XX(0.3) at Level 1 invokes the pulse solver; setting a detuning by hand at Level 2 is applied as written, and the gate at Level 1 then displays its actual unitary, not the requested one. Requests the device cannot satisfy are shown with the reason, for example loop closure impossible within the power limit or a requested angle outside the calibrated range. At no point does the app let a level float free of the device model.

### 14.5 Correctness affordances

- **Provenance chips** on every formula and number, generated from a provenance ledger: a YAML file kept beside the plan with one record per quantity (`id`, `symbol`, `tag`, `section`, `source`, `equation`, `corrected_form`), which the core's `Device.derived()` provenance and the app's chips both consume, so the Section 9.11 static test is a set difference over ids rather than a hand-maintained index; hovering a chip shows the tag, the source and the corrected form where the plan corrects the source.
- **Convergence badges** on every result and trace, red when the halving test of Section 5.5 fails.
- **Verify deeper.** At Levels 0 to 2 one action runs the same job at the next-deeper engine, on a subset of shots when the full run would be slow, and shows the discrepancy against the bound of Section 9.8 or the channel-derivation residual.
- **Target beside simulated.** Levels 0 and 1 show the compiler's target semantics next to the simulated result, never as the result.
- **Units and conventions.** Frequencies are displayed in Hz with the 2π conversion visible; the convention row of Section 13 that governs a displayed quantity is one hover away.
- **No cartoons.** Every drawn element is computed: ion positions from the equilibrium solver, beams from the configured geometry, mode vectors from the Hessian, spectra from the built Hamiltonian. The few illustrative elements, such as a level diagram's vertical spacing, are labelled "not to scale".
- **Reproducibility.** Seed, device hash, package version and convergence report travel with every exported record.
- **Presets from the validation suite.** Any entry of Section 9 can be loaded as a device configuration plus experiment, for example Harty's microwave randomized benchmarking, Kirchmair's thermal Mølmer-Sørensen scan or Myerson's readout optimum; the app runs it and places the published number beside the simulated one with the provenance chip.
- **Explain panel.** At every level, the Part II subsection that governs what is on screen is shown beside it.

### 14.6 Flet implementation

- **Routes mirror the ladder** with the declarative `ft.Router` of Flet 0.85: `/job/{id}` for Level 0, `/job/{id}/circuit/{gate}` for Level 1, `/job/{id}/schedule/{pulse}` for Level 2, `/job/{id}/dynamics/{pulse}/{sample}` for Level 3, and `/device/{page}` for the Level 4 pages, with a breadcrumb zoom bar and keyboard zoom in and out. Browser history works in the served web mode because routes are real.
- **Core contract.** The app calls only public core API that exists for the core's own reasons: the device model and its hash, `run(circuit, shots, level)` and the `Result` with its diagnostics and convergence report, the experiment API, the per-pulse evolution and process-tomography entry points that the core's validation suite and Section 6.8 reporting already use, and the compiler's target unitaries. The channel-replay engine (`replay.py`) is built app-side from the Section 6.8 summaries. If a zoom view needs data the core does not expose, the app records the gap and shows the view as unavailable rather than patching the core.
- **Layering.** `viewmodel/` is pure Python with no Flet import: it turns run records into view data, performs the coarse-graining, and resolves provenance; it is where the correctness tests run. `views/` holds the Flet controls per level. `workers.py` runs core calls in a `ProcessPoolExecutor`, streaming progress and partial results through `page.pubsub`; view updates are scheduled with `page.run_task`, so the UI never blocks on a solver.
- **Charts.** `flet-charts` LineChart and ScatterChart for time series, calibration scans and phase-space loops; BarChart for histograms; MatplotlibChart for level diagrams, mode eigenvectors and the stability diagram; `ft.RawImage` (Flet 0.86) for streamed heatmaps such as Fock distributions and Wigner functions; DataTable for matrix elements, segment tables and parameter tables.
- **Testing.** pytest on the view-models (coarse-graining identities, provenance coverage, record round trips); `flet test` integration tests for navigation and run flows; the physics itself is never tested through the UI.
- **Packaging.** `flet run` for the desktop window during development, `flet run --web` for the served browser mode, `flet build macos`, `windows` or `linux` for distributable bundles with the physics dependencies installed as binary wheels. The static Pyodide web build is not a target (Section 1.5).

### 14.7 Interactive budgets

| Action | Budget | Basis |
|---|---|---|
| Zoom into a pulse: one trajectory of a 100 µs entangling pulse with the recorded noise sample and jump record | ≤ 1 s at joint dimension ≤ 256 (two modes, d_m = 8); ≤ 10 s at ≤ 864 (three modes, d_m = 6) | Section 11.1 (v4) measured 0.6 s and 6 s per 100 µs gate |
| Zoom into a pulse: jump ensemble and process matrix | background job with progress (tens to hundreds of trajectories; 16 tomography inputs) | Section 5.4 |
| Level 4 parameter change: recalibration by simulated experiment | hours; user-initiated background job with a stale badge until it completes | Section 7.5 at the rates of Section 11.1 |
| Zoom into a pulse at larger dimension | recorded traces shown at once; re-simulation as a background job with progress | Section 11.1 (v4) measured 6 s at dimension 864 and 2.1 min at 2048; Section 11.2 cost model |
| Level 0 or 1 run by channel replay, 20 qubits, 100 gates | seconds | density matrix to 12 qubits, sampling beyond |
| Level 4 parameter change, re-derivation of modes and η | immediate | closed-form and small eigenproblems |
| Level 4 parameter change, pulse re-solve per pair | ≤ 10 s in background with stale badges | AM and FM solvers of Section 4.4.3 |

### 14.8 Out of scope for M11

Live hardware submission, multi-user server deployment, and mobile bundles (possible with `flet build`, but wheel availability of the physics stack on Android and iOS is not part of this plan).


## 15. Sources

Primary sources read by the research runs (R1 = the general deep-research run of 2026-09-03, wf_55100f76-ac4; R2 = the simulator-physics extraction runs, wf_4eccb03e-072 and wf_5d918595-430; R3 = the recovery run wf_489f7283-103; R4 = the gap-filling run of 2026-09-04, wf_0495300f-081; R5 = the source run of 2026-09-04 on frequency-comb drives, composite pulses and decoupling, named single-source constants and transport physics, wf_9734a0eb-744). Items from R2 to R4 were checked against the source text by fidelity verifiers; items from R1 by a three-vote adversarial panel.

**Reviews and foundations**

**Software.** QuTiP 5.3.1 (introspected on this machine: NumPy 2.5.2, SciPy 1.18.1, Python 3.13, Apple Accelerate BLAS, 18 CPUs). Flet 0.86.5 and flet-charts 0.86.5 (PyPI, released 2026-08-01, Python 3.10 or later, Apache-2.0), documentation at flet.dev read 2026-09-04: installation (`uv add 'flet[all]'`; macOS 12 or later, 64-bit Windows 10 and 11, Debian 10 to 12 and Ubuntu 20.04 to 24.04), the Page control (`add`, `update`, `run_task`, `run_thread`, `pubsub`, `Router`), packaging (`flet build` for macOS, Windows, Linux, web, Android and iOS with the Flutter SDK fetched on first build; bundled CPython 3.12 to 3.14; binary wheels only for mobile and web; `flet pack` as the PyInstaller route), web deployment (served mode over WebSockets versus static Pyodide mode), the built-in extension list, and the 0.84 to 0.86 release notes (Docusaurus docs, `ft.Router` and `ft.use_dialog`, dart-bridge in-process communication, data channels, `flet test`, `ft.RawImage`).

A 71, 032504 (2005); arXiv:physics/0409038 (the preprint carries a different title). R5 (Sections 4.5.7, 8.1; Eqs. 1-3 and 7-8, Tables III-V).

**Frequency-comb (mode-locked) Raman drives (R5)**
- Hayes et al., Entanglement of atomic qubits using an optical frequency comb, Phys. Rev. Lett. 104, 140501 (2010); arXiv:1001.2127v2, Eqs. 1-7. R5 (E₀ is fluence- and not peak-normalized, a factor π/2 in Ω₀ and Ī; the θ_p/2 prefactor belongs to the V^N expansion, not the Dirichlet grating factor; the "k" of exp(ikx̂) is the two-photon Δk, since the quoted η = 0.1 closes only with Δk = √2 k; the q-restriction clause is a slip, no configuration in the paper satisfying it as written; the fidelity estimator's Π_C is the full peak-to-peak parity swing and the target state is printed unnormalized. The pdftotext glyph mapping for φ and ϕ in this document is inverted, so those symbols were taken from the arXiv LaTeX only.)
- Islam, Campbell, Choi, Clark, Debnath, Edwards, Fields, Hayes, Hucul, Inlek, Johnson, Korenblit, Lee, Lee, Manning, Matsukevich, Mizrahi, Quraishi, Senko, Smith, Monroe, Beat note stabilization of mode-locked lasers for quantum information processing, Opt. Lett. 39, 3238 (2014); arXiv:1312.6846v1. R5 (a three-page Letter with no numbered equations, so its relations are cited by content. Ω₀ in ε ≈ (π²/2)αΩ₀²T must be an ordinary frequency in Hz; the lock's per-tooth residual is (m − n) on lower sidebands, (m + n) on upper and m on bare teeth, not a single full-n rule; the ν_M1 > ν_M2 inequality is load-bearing and implicit; Fig. 5(b)'s caption lists the sideband labels in reverse order to their frequencies; the printed ν_ab = 12.642819 GHz is a second-order-Zeeman-shifted value, not an atomic constant; the same laser is called Nd:YVO4 and "mode-locked YAG" in different captions.)
- Inlek, Vittorini, Hucul, Crocker, Monroe, Phys. Rev. A 90, 042316 (2014); arXiv:1405.5207v3, Eqs. 1-5 and Appendix Eqs. A.1-A.3. R5 (Eq. 2's tooth orders are n = 160 on the red leg and m = 154 on the blue, swapping them missing each condition by 483 MHz; Eq. A.3's blue reduction flips ν_α, so the printed AWG frequencies are each high by exactly 2ν_α = 5.00 MHz and 111.83 / 38.19 MHz are used; Fig. 7's PLL panel labels are swapped relative to Eq. A.1 and its "12.566 GHz" label conflicts with the text's ν_MO = 12.606 GHz; Eq. 4's two printed lines disagree by a factor 2, both self-consistent readings giving the same φ_S and φ_M; Eq. 3's state map is printed unnormalized; the wave-front tolerance triple does not close, 0.02° over 30 µm giving 15.0° end-to-end against a stated "< 10°".)
- Mizrahi, Senko, Neyenhuis, Johnson, Campbell, Conover, Monroe, Phys. Rev. Lett. 110, 203001 (2013); arXiv:1201.6597v2, Eqs. 1-7. R5 (the Ω/2 prefactor of Eq. 2 and the Bessel argument of Eq. 3 are inconsistent by 2; the path-matching condition is printed as c/τ where cτ ≈ 3 mm is meant; the σ_z sign of the interleaving factor contradicts the companion's free-evolution operator; the "2.5 GHz" eight-pulse train has gaps alternating 418.8 and 342.7 ps; the intro's 1.27 µs trap period contradicts the measured 743 kHz and its own figure ticks.)
- Mizrahi et al., Appl. Phys. B 114, 45 (2014); arXiv:1307.0557v1, Eqs. 1-3, 6, 9-13, 18-31 and 32-53, App. A. R5 (Eq. 23's N = 2πω_rep t and Ω₀ = 2πω_rep θ are wrong by (2π)² = 39.478 under the paper's own angular ω_rep; Eq. 1's printed −(ω_q/2)σ_z contradicts Eq. 37's free-evolution operator; Eq. 1 and Eq. 3 form two internally consistent but mutually incompatible intensity-to-Rabi chains differing by exactly 2; Eq. 30's first line needs a plus on the counter-rotating sum, and Eq. 31's null offsets are roots of a three-pole truncation rather than of the exact sum; Eq. 39's printed e^{+iq_−t_k}σ_− should be e^{−iq_−t_k}σ_−; Eq. 53's cos/sin form omits the √(n+1); the same Bessel-argument factor of 2 as the PRL recurs in its appendix. The hardcoded I_sat = 0.15 W/cm² is a paper-specific lumped D1 convention, 2.95× the two-level value.)
- Campbell, Mizrahi, Quraishi, Senko, Hayes, Hucul, Matsukevich, Maunz, Monroe, Ultrafast gates for single atomic qubits, Phys. Rev. Lett. 105, 090502 (2010); arXiv:1005.4144v1, Eqs. 1-6. R5 (H_eff/h carries Planck's h, so ν_HF, Ω_{0,1} and the Stark shifts are ordinary frequencies - the measured 72% single-pulse ceiling reproduces only on that reading; the second-order Stark shift's −2 weight combines a line-strength ratio with a detuning-sign convention valid only for 0 < Δ < ω_FS; Δ_D1 = Δ_FS/3 is the zero of the single-state shift, the differential minimum sitting at (√2 − 1)Δ_FS; the two-delayed-pulse spectrum is a sinusoidal modulation of the whole envelope, not a two-tooth comb, and the paper reports no shaped-π-pulse energy; the measured differential shift 1.1(5) × 10⁻⁴ Ω_{0,1} sits ~1.6σ below the predicted 1.90 × 10⁻⁴.)
- Lee, Smith, Richerme, Neyenhuis, Hess, Zhang, Monroe, Engineering large Stark shifts for control of individual clock state qubits, Phys. Rev. A 94, 042308 (2016); arXiv:1604.08840v1, Eqs. 1-17. R5 (Eq. 6's printed plus sign is inconsistent with its own Eqs. 3-5 and 8, so an overall minus plus the antisymmetry δ_{a,n} = −δ_{n,a} is used; the "assuming that it is nonzero" condition attaches to the minimized quantity δ_{n,a} and never to j, and a j ≠ 0 guard deletes the Zeeman terms that make the elliptical-polarization cancellation work; Eq. 12's 23 and 12 MHz are ordinary frequencies δω^{(4)}/2π and are not reproducible from the stated inputs; the quoted τ ≈ 14 ps and "~70 GHz bandwidth" are mutually inconsistent; the comb factor needs \|k\| ≳ 1000 and its partial sums plateau falsely a factor 2.09 low near \|k\| ~ 10; the paper's equation numbering is disputed between verification passes, so its expressions are cited by content. Only arXiv v1 was reachable, so the journal copy-edit may have repaired some of these - see Section 12.)
- Mount et al., Phys. Rev. A 92, 060301 (2015); arXiv:1504.01440. Already listed under R2; cited again here for the three-term comb error model of Sections 4.3.5 and 4.3.7. It is outside the R5 source set, so the identification of that model with the truncated tone sum is derived rather than checked against the paper.

**Composite pulses, dynamical decoupling and filter functions (R5)**
- Brown, Harrow, Chuang, Arbitrarily accurate composite pulse sequences, Phys. Rev. A 70, 052318 (2004); arXiv:quant-ph/0407022v2. R5 (Eqs. 1-14 and endnotes [24], [25]; the arXiv v2 of 25 Jan 2005 postdates the PRA publication, so the Eq. 10 sign and the Eq. 9-text U_nY defects stand in the final text and no erratum exists).
- Cummins, Llewellyn, Jones, Tackling systematic errors in quantum logic gates with composite rotations, Phys. Rev. A 67, 042308 (2003); arXiv:quant-ph/0208092. R5 (Eqs. 1-27, Tables I-III; source of CORPSE, SCROFULOUS and the quaternion fidelity, and the origin of the BB1 = W1 tabulation. Eq. 9's y-component is missing a factor 2f; whether the published typesetting fixes it is unverified).
- Merrill, Brown, Progress in compensating pulse sequences for quantum computation, Adv. Chem. Phys. 154, 241 (2014); arXiv:1203.6392. R5 (Secs. 2, 4.1-4.3, Eqs. 1-61; the reference treatment of the four systematic error models, of B2/N2/P2 and of the palindromic Trotter-Suzuki ladder. Eq. 38's rhombus phase sign, Eq. 50's recursion factor, the odd-k B2j bottom layer and one intermediate B2 rewrite line are corrected in Section 4.3.5).
- Low, Yoder, Chuang, Optimal arbitrarily accurate composite pulse sequences, Phys. Rev. A 89, 022341 (2014); arXiv:1307.2211v2. R5 (Secs. II-VI, Eqs. 1-22, Tables II-III; the general-θ_T PD_n/AP_n/ToP_n closed forms, the exact equal-area propagator and the ε-free order certificate. Eq. 10's summation limit and the printed PD_n reality domain are corrected).
- Kabytayev, Green, Khodjasteh, Biercuk, Viola, Brown, Robustness of composite pulses to time-dependent control noise, Phys. Rev. A 90, 012316 (2014); arXiv:1402.5174v2. R5 (Eqs. 1-6 and the Appendix; the bridge between the static composite-pulse orders and the filter functions, the closed-form amplitude filter function, and the dc frozen-noise floor. The Ω^{−2(m+1)} normalization of ĉ_{m+1}, the A_l sign, the Magnus prefactor and the 1/(2π) in the noise moments are corrected; Sec. III B of the arXiv version contains an unfilled placeholder where CORPSE's own ĉ₂ should be, so that coefficient is computed here).
- Green, Sastrawan, Uys, Biercuk, Arbitrary quantum control of qubits in the presence of universal noise, New J. Phys. 15, 095004 (2013); arXiv:1211.1163v2. R5 (Eqs. 2-58; the toggling-frame control matrix, the universal-noise spectral overlap, the piecewise-constant control matrix and the dynamical-decoupling filter functions. Eq. 34's bracket sign, Eq. 35's factor 2 in the exponent, Eq. 40's transverse factor 2, Eq. 42's pulse-width subscript and grouping, and Eq. 50's repeated β_y² are corrected).
- Green, Uys, Biercuk, High-order noise filtering in nontrivial quantum logic gates, Phys. Rev. Lett. 109, 020501 (2012). Not read; the only source for the second-order filter function F_zz^{(2)} that Green 2013 Eq. 34 uses, which is why no error bar on the first-order path is available (Section 12).
- Biercuk, Uys, VanDevender, Shiga, Itano, Bollinger, Experimental Uhrig dynamical decoupling using trapped ions, Phys. Rev. A 79, 062324 (2009); arXiv:0902.2957v3. R5 (Eqs. 1-3 and Appendix Eqs. A.1-A.3; the finite-pulse-width filter function valid at both pulse-number parities, the CPMG and UDD timings, and χ with W = e^{−χ}. Four Appendix-A defects are corrected and the printed Ohmic step function Θ(ω − ω_C) is implemented as Θ(ω_C − ω) per the surrounding text; the ambient spectrum has no printed absolute normalization and is not reproducible from the paper alone).
- Wimperis, Broadband, narrowband and passband composite pulses for use in advanced NMR experiments, J. Magn. Reson. 109, 221 (1994). Not read directly; the origin of BB1 = B2 = W1, NB1 = N2 and PB1 = P2, cited through Cummins 2003 and Merrill-Brown 2014, which tabulate all three.
- Khodjasteh, Viola, Dynamically error-corrected gates for universal quantum computation, Phys. Rev. Lett. 102, 080501 (2009). Not read; the only source for the dynamically corrected gate that Green 2013 cites as the remedy for the finite-pulse degradation of Carr-Purcell, and not implementable from what Green prints (Section 12).
- Tomita, Merrill, Brown, Multi-qubit compensation sequences, New J. Phys. 12, 015002 (2010). Not read; credited by Kabytayev for the concatenation construction behind reduced CinSK and CinBB.
- Uhrig, Keeping a quantum bit alive by optimized pi-pulse sequences, Phys. Rev. Lett. 98, 100504 (2007), and Cywinski, Lutchyn, Nave, Das Sarma, Phys. Rev. B 77, 174509 (2008). Not read; the origin of the UDD timings and of the W = e^{−χ} second-cumulant form that Biercuk 2009 imports without deriving, which is why the Gaussian condition behind it is tagged inferred-standard rather than quoted.

**Transport, splitting and junctions (R5)**
- Reichle, Leibfried, Blakestad, Britton, Jost, Knill, Langer, Ozeri, Seidelin, Wineland, Transport dynamics of single ions in segmented microstructured Paul trap arrays, Fortschr. Phys. 54, No. 8-10, 666 (2006); arXiv:quant-ph/0606237. R5. The moving-well Hamiltonian (Eqs. 2-7), the Lewis-Riesenfeld/Ermakov solution (Eqs. 11-23, 30-42), the Husimi-Kerner probabilities (Eqs. 27-29) and the sine and error-function suppression factors (Eqs. 43-51). Its Eq. (11) normalization contradicts its own units footnote, its excitation prefactor is printed unparenthesized, and its two error-function quanta figures do not reproduce from its own equations (Sections 4.6, 9.15).
- Bowler, Gaebler, Lin, Tan, Hanneke, Jost, Home, Leibfried, Wineland, Coherent diabatic ion transport and separation in a multizone trap array, Phys. Rev. Lett. 109, 080502 (2012); arXiv:1206.0780. R5. The velocity-form coherent amplitude (Eq. 1, credited there to Lau and James, Phys. Rev. A 83, 062330 (2011)), the impulsive constant-velocity limit with its 2πN recapture nulls (Eq. 2), the a z² + b z⁴ separation potential (Eq. 3, credited to Home and Steane 2006), the measured transport and two-ion separation excitations and the +3 mV voltage-sensitivity result.
- Walther, Ziesel, Ruster, Dawkins, Ott, Hettrich, Singer, Schmidt-Kaler, Poschinger, Controlling fast transport of cold trapped ions, Phys. Rev. Lett. 109, 080501 (2012); arXiv:1206.0364. R5. The sideband Rabi readout model (Eq. 1), the thermal-coherent convolution whose printed summation index sits on a Gamma pole (Eq. 2) and the pseudo-energy metric E_p with its carrier-referenced pulse areas (Eq. 3), plus the energy-neutral pair and self-neutral kick protocols.
- Kaufmann, Ruster, Schmiegelow, Schmidt-Kaler, Poschinger, Dynamics and control of fast ion crystal splitting in segmented Paul traps, New J. Phys. 16, 073012 (2014); arXiv:1403.0097. R5. The quartic separation potential in volts (Eqs. 1-2), the voltage-to-coefficient map (Eqs. 3-7), the critical-tilt threshold (Eqs. 19-20), the critical-point scaling (Eqs. 14-18), the impulsive transfer and adiabaticity parameter (Eqs. 21-30), the anomalous-heating integral (Eq. 32), the static voltage sets (Eqs. 33-38), the distance ramps (Eqs. 39-40) and the classical equations of motion (Eqs. 41-42). Six printed defects are corrected in Section 4.6: a missing factor of the elementary charge and a Coulomb sign pairing in the equations of motion, a global sign in the quintic ramp, a reversed subtraction in the well depth, an inequality on the wrong quantity in the critical tilt, a factor 2 in the relative-coordinate acceleration, an internal factor 4 in the impulsive chain, a prose T-scaling that contradicts its own equation, and an unusable trap-size prefactor. Only arXiv v1 exists and the version of record was behind a bot wall, so none of the corrections could be checked against it.
- Blakestad, Ospelkaus, VanDevender, Wesenberg, Biercuk, Leibfried, Wineland, Near-ground-state transport of trapped-ion qubits through a multidimensional array, Phys. Rev. A 84, 032314 (2011); arXiv:1106.5005. R5. The rf pseudopotential barrier in volts (Eqs. 1-2), the secular decomposition and Laplace sum rule (Eq. 13), rf-sideband noise heating on a pseudopotential slope (Eqs. 15-16), the shared single-sided anomalous-heating convention (Eq. 18), the DAC update-noise resonance and the measured junction-traversal excitations (Table I). Its prose calls the rf-noise rate proportional to the pseudopotential slope where its equation squares it, and its three zone-E micromotion numbers are not mutually consistent in one peak-or-rms convention.
- Pino, Dreiling, Figgatt, Gaebler, Moses, Allman, Baldwin, Foss-Feig, Hayes, Mayer, Ryan-Anderson, Neyenhuis, Demonstration of the trapped-ion quantum CCD computer architecture, Nature 592, 209 (2021); arXiv:2003.01293. R5. The transport-primitive durations and fitted per-primitive "heat" (Table II), the cooling-stage durations and their cadence (Table III with the paper's own time budgets), the two-qubit error budget (Table IV) and the quantum-volume circuit-level model. Its heating columns are temperatures in frequency units rather than quanta, its Table IV rows sum to 7.3 × 10⁻³ against a printed total of <7.2 × 10⁻³ and its bound falls below its own measured zone average, and transport failures were removed from the data rather than modelled.
- Sterk, Coakley, Goldberg, Hietala, Lechtenberg, McGuinness, McMurtrey, Parazzoli, Van Der Wall, Stick, Closed-loop optimization of fast trapped-ion shuttling with sub-quanta excitation, npj Quantum Inf. 8, 68 (2022); arXiv:2201.07358. R5. The pseudo-energy loss functional with its second weight inside the square (Eq. 1), the m-th order red-sideband lineshape and its Lamb-Dicke coupling ladder (Eq. 2), the Bézier trajectory ansatz (Eq. 3), the sideband-probe Hamiltonian and dressed states (Eq. 4, Methods), and the delivered-waveform chain. The published version alone gives the 300 ns emulated DAC step, the 40Ca+ isotope and the shortcut-to-adiabaticity correspondence.
- Home, Steane, Electrode configurations for fast separation of trapped ions, Quantum Inf. Comput. 6, 289 (2006). R5, cited but not read: the origin of the a z² + b z⁴ splitting model and of the ω_CP scaling, which both Bowler and Kaufmann attribute to it.
- Torrontegui, Ibáñez, Chen, Ruschhaupt, Guéry-Odelin, Muga, Fast atomic transport without vibrational heating, Phys. Rev. A 83, 013415 (2011). R5, cited but not read: the shortcut-to-adiabaticity trajectory that Sterk's published version identifies with the lowest-order member of its Bézier family.
- Couvert, Kawalec, Reinaudi, Guéry-Odelin, Optimal transport of ultracold atoms in the non-adiabatic regime, Europhys. Lett. 83, 13001 (2008). R5, cited but not read: the origin Bowler gives for the ω t_T = 2πN recapture condition.
- Husimi, Miscellanea in elementary quantum mechanics II, Prog. Theor. Phys. 9, 381 (1953); Kerner, Note on the forced and damped oscillator in quantum mechanics, Can. J. Phys. 36, 371 (1958). R5, cited but not read: the closed form for the forced-oscillator transition probabilities that Reichle's Eq. (27) quotes.


## Appendix D. How the physics in this plan was checked

Three multi-agent research runs on 2026-09-03 produced the provenance tags used throughout.

**Run 1** (wf_55100f76-ac4, 111 agents): five web-search angles, 29 primary sources fetched, 145 falsifiable claims extracted with quotes, the top 25 put to a three-voter adversarial panel that fetched the primary PDFs (a claim died on two refuting votes). Result: 15 claims survived, 10 were refuted, mostly on single details (a wrong isotope, an inverted inequality, a dropped digit, a mislabelled mechanism, a superseded record). Those 15 are the **[verified]** facts in Sections 1, 4.1 and 8 that carry vote counts in the companion report "Paul Trap to Fault Tolerance".

**Run 2A** (wf_4eccb03e-072, 295 agents, 23.5 M subagent tokens, 6.9 h) and **Run 2B** (wf_5d918595-430, 398 agents, 32.2 M subagent tokens, 7.5 h): 59 source readings across six topics (trap and motion 8, cooling 8, readout 9, Hamiltonian and single-qubit control 9, two-qubit gates 15, system level 10), chosen by the author rather than found by search, read through ar5iv LaTeX renderings or the PDFs so that equations were transcribed rather than paraphrased. Each source yielded up to six items (equation, definition, parameter, procedure, result or check) with a verbatim quote and location. Every non-tangential item, 354 in total, went to a fidelity verifier instructed to refute on any transcription, convention, condition or numerical error; a refutation or a low-confidence pass escalated to two more verifiers and a majority decision. Outcome by topic at the time of writing:

| Topic | Items | Passed (with flags) | Refuted as stated, corrected | No verdict |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Trap, crystal, modes, micromotion, heating | 48 | 34 | 14 | 0 (13 re-run in Run 3) |
| Laser cooling and state preparation | 48 | 32 | 16 | 0 |
| Readout and SPAM | 54 | 27 | 27 | 0 |
| Spin-motion Hamiltonian and single-qubit control | 54 | 40 | 14 | 0 |
| Two-qubit entangling gates | 90 | 56 | 34 | 0 (14 re-run in Run 3) |
| System level: native gates, compilation, calibration, noise | 60 | 33 | 27 | 0 |

**How the tags map.** **[verified]** in Part II means the item passed its fidelity check (one confident pass or a majority of three) and the verifier's flags were folded into the text. **[corrected]** means a majority refuted the item as extracted and the plan states the corrected form; in roughly two thirds of these cases the error was the source's own (a printed typo, a dropped factor, an inconsistent equation), which is precisely why the plan quotes corrected forms with the correction named. **[extracted]** means the item has a verbatim quote but no agent verdict. The 27 items whose verifiers stalled in the original runs were re-run in Run 3: 19 passed with flags and 8 were refuted on a gloss or symbol definition while their equations stood, so no tag now rests on a stalled verification; the tag remains only on a handful of Run 1 statements that were extracted but never put to the vote (a waveguide-crosstalk figure, one budget line from Pino et al., optical-pumping details, a 32-ion mode-frequency pair). The user had confirmed the 27 items independently before Run 3; the agent verdicts agree with that confirmation on every equation and add corrections to seven surrounding statements. **[background]** is the author's textbook physics, added for coherence and never load-bearing without a test.

**What the checking cannot give.** The verifiers established fidelity to the sources and internal consistency, and in many cases recomputed numbers, but they did not re-derive the physics from scratch; a source that is consistently wrong would pass. The convention table of Section 13 is the author's reconciliation across sources, informed by the verifiers' flags but not itself agent-verified. Run 2A's per-topic synthesis stage produced the readout brief but not the trap or cooling briefs: those two synthesis agents exceeded the 64,000-token output cap and were lost, so Run 2A's consolidation ran on the readout brief alone (its findings are folded into Sections 8 and 12). Run 2B produced the Hamiltonian and system-level briefs; its two-qubit brief was lost the same way. Its consolidation, run on those two briefs, listed 47 cross-source inconsistencies and independently recomputed the eight that change a unitary or an error estimate; those results are folded into Sections 6, 7, 9, 12 and 13 with the tag [corrected]. Run 3 recovered the three missing briefs.

**Run 3** (wf_489f7283-103, 65 agents, 6.5 M subagent tokens, 8.2 h, 2026-09-04) recovered what the output cap and the stalled verifiers had cost. The transcripts held no partial output from the three brief agents, only the API error, but they held every input: the 27 stalled checks were re-run from their original prompts with the same escalation (all 8 refutations were unanimous three-vote decisions, each on a gloss or a symbol definition while the transcribed equations stood: the R′ relation, Brownnutt's misprinted Eq. 14 and the Eq. 24 rate, a misattributed α example, Landa's two separate pseudopotential-recovery conditions, Baldwin's clock-qubit encoding in three items and the meaning of Δ_hf), and the three lost briefs were synthesized from the recorded item-plus-verdict inputs in 5, 5 and 8 parts of at most eleven items each, with one assembly agent per topic (trap: 70 model entries, 86 parameters, 97 checks; cooling: 68, 109, 90; two-qubit: 109, 188, 166). One consolidation agent then read all six briefs and both earlier consolidations and produced 20 cross-topic inconsistencies, 17 glossary decisions, 14 components, 22 tests and 20 open issues, folded into Sections 4, 6, 8, 9, 12 and 13. The recovered briefs and the consolidation are stored beside the plan as brief_recovered_<topic>.json and consolidated_C.json. The numerical checks marked "recomputed here" in Sections 7 and 13 were run by the author in the QuTiP environment on 2026-09-03 (Run 2B's unitary checks) and 2026-09-04 (everything else), not by workflow agents.

**Refutation rate as a design input.** Across all three runs about a third of carefully extracted, quoted claims failed a fidelity check, and a substantial fraction of those failures were errors in the published sources themselves. That is the empirical case for Section 9: a simulator built by transcribing formulas would inherit those errors, so the validation suite pins every implemented formula to a checked number.

**Local numerical checks (2026-09-04, this machine, QuTiP 5.3.1, NumPy 2.5.2, SciPy 1.18.1, run under both Python 3.14 and the pinned 3.13.14 with `uv`, with identical outputs and wall times within 10%).** Six scripts, committed to the repository under `validation/scripts/` as the oracle for every number tagged **[recomputed here]**, produced those numbers (`check_recoil2.py` recomputes the Run 4R checks: quadrature identities, the single-kick expectation, the α → 0 floor, the Doppler optimum, Cirac's Fig. 8 minima, the Franck-Condon index, the 40Ca+ Lamb-Dicke parameters and the unit regressions): `bench_numerics.py` (displacement-operator constructions and their unitarity and interior accuracy, ENR construction, right-hand-side evaluation counts in the Schrödinger and interaction pictures; Sections 5.1.1 and 5.2), `bench_ms_timing.py` (wall time of a two-ion Mølmer-Sørensen pulse at joint dimensions 48 to 2048; Section 11.1), `check_atomic.py` (Breit-Rabi against numerical diagonalization, clock points of 43Ca+, 9Be+ and 25Mg+, the 171Yb+ quadratic Zeeman coefficient, Wigner-Eckart branching ratios, Γ to reduced element to I_sat; Sections 4.5 and 9.13) `check_surface_mixed.py` (Laplace residual and boundary values of the gapless-plane electrode potential, the five-wire null height, the mixed-species two-ion mode formula; Sections 4.1.6 and 4.1.7) and `check_recoil.py` (the recoil angular factors 1/5, 2/5, 2/5, 3/10 and 1/3 by quadrature over the dipole emission patterns, and the 171Yb+ recoil-per-photon numbers; Section 4.2.8). The QuTiP interface facts of Section 9.13 were introspected in the same environment. These checks establish internal consistency and agreement with published anchors that earlier runs had verified; they do not replace the source checks, which Run 4 supplies for the transcribed formulas. The third revision (2026-09-04, afternoon and evening) added nine scripts to the same directory, each described in its README and each with its output committed under `outputs/`: `check_ms_closure.py` and `check_c0_floquet.py` (the two derivation-audit corrections the author re-derived independently, Sections 4.4.1 and 4.1.1), `bench_ms_timing_v3.py` (Section 11.1 at the corrected closure), `check_sr_lifetime_quad.py` and `check_sr_lifetime_bbr.py` (Section 4.5.7), and the four topic scripts of Run 5S, `check_comb.py`, `check_composite.py`, `check_constants.py` and `check_transport.py` (Sections 4.3.7, 4.3.5 and 6.9, 8.1 and 4.2.4, 4.6), whose numbers fill Section 9.15; every **[recomputed here]** number added in this revision names its script.

**Run 4Q** (wf_197c5930-856, 46 agents, 4.0 M subagent tokens, 37 min, 2026-09-04): three sources on the electric-quadrupole coupling (James 1998 Secs. 4-5 and Appendix, the Roos 2000 thesis, Schindler et al. 2013) read by one extractor each, 18 items verified with the same escalation as Runs 2-3 (6 passed with flags, 11 refuted as extracted and corrected, 1 split), and one brief of 17 model entries, 33 parameters and 22 checks, folded into Section 4.5.7 and Section 9.14. The refutations were mostly the sources' own: a factor k dropped from a thesis equation, air wavelengths mixed with vacuum ones in one figure, a superseded lifetime, an inverted and squared crosstalk definition, a degenerate angle definition, and node positions off by a factor 2 in a standing-wave construction.

**Run 4R** (wf_7474024c-6e0, 65 agents, 7.1 M subagent tokens, 61 min, 2026-09-04): the recoil follow-up. Run 4's first attempt found Cirac 1992, Marzoli 1994 and Javanainen-Stenholm 1981 paywalled; this run re-attempted them through the publishers' harvest and DOI routes (the first two obtained, the third not) and added Itano and Wineland 1982, Eschner et al. 2003, the recoil chapters of Steck's Quantum and Atom Optics and the cooling chapter of Roos's thesis as open substitutes. Six extractors produced 36 items; 58 verdicts with the same escalation as Runs 2-3 gave 25 items verified with flags and 11 refuted as extracted, where the refutations fell on the extractors' glosses rather than on the sources' equations (a wrong pair of 40Ca+ transitions named as the recoil pair, an invented "extra factor ½", the phrase "recoil floor" for a steady state from which the recoil frequency cancels, three page locators), each resolved in the brief's contested list; one brief of 21 model entries, 24 parameters, 25 checks, a 15-step recipe, 19 conventions, 7 contested points and 12 gaps was read in full and folded into Sections 4.2.2, 4.2.8, 9.3, 13 and 15. Seven printed errors across five sources were identified by recomputation and are listed in Section 4.2.8. The checks the brief reports were recomputed locally (`check_recoil2.py`, Appendix D scripts) before their rows entered Section 9.3.

**Run 4** (wf_0495300f-081, 333 agents, 29.8 M subagent tokens, 6320 tool uses, 162 min, 2026-09-04): four topics that the first three runs had left unsourced, each with its own source set (atomic structure and light-atom coupling, 8 sources; spontaneous-emission recoil and multi-level cooling, 8; surface-electrode trap potentials, 5; mixed-species crystals and the quantized Floquet frame, 6), read by 27 extractors (24 usable), yielding 144 items and 286 verdicts with the same escalation as Runs 2-3 (73 items verified with flags, 65 refuted as extracted, 6 split), 15 chunk syntheses assembled into four briefs (173 model entries, 340 parameters and 320 checks in total) and one cross-topic consolidation (35 glossary entries, 26 inconsistencies, 18 must-implement items, 36 validation rows, 24 open issues). As in Run 3, most refutations fell on the extractors' glosses (page locators, hedges promoted to assertions, mislabelled conventions such as the polarization index) rather than on the sources' equations, but the run also established about twenty printed errors in the sources, listed where they matter in Sections 4.1.6, 4.1.7, 4.2.8 and 4.5, adjudicated two cross-brief conflicts by computation (Nizamani's q ≈ 0.7 is a height-normalized figure of merit 3.2 times the local Mathieu q; two-node Gauss-Legendre returns α = 1/3 for every quadratic emission pattern), found one identity (House's four-wire coefficient, Wesenberg's optimal-quadrupole bound and the numerically observed geometry-independent maximum depth are one number to 10⁻¹⁶), corrected seven of this plan's own citations (Sections 15), and named what it could not source (Section 12). Its results entered the text in two passes: the verdict corrections while the syntheses were still running (Sections 4.1.6, 4.1.7, 4.2.8, 4.5, 9.12, 9.13 and 13), then the briefs and consolidation.
